# Architecture decision records

One file per significant decision. Small, dated, immutable.

An ADR is never edited to change its decision. If a decision is reversed, write a new ADR that
supersedes the old one and mark the old one `Superseded by ADR-NNNN`. The wrong turns are as
valuable as the right ones — especially for Product B.

## When to write one

Write an ADR when a choice:

- constrains something outside its own module,
- was contested, or had a plausible alternative,
- will make a future reader ask *"why is it like this?"*.

Do not write one for choices the code already makes obvious.

## Naming

`ADR-NNNN-short-kebab-title.md`, sequential, never reused.

## Template

```markdown
# ADR-NNNN — Title

- **Status:** Proposed | Accepted | Superseded by ADR-MMMM
- **Date:** YYYY-MM-DD

## Context
The forces at play. What makes this a real decision.

## Decision
What we are doing. Present tense, unambiguous.

## Consequences
What this buys, what it costs, and what it forecloses.

## Alternatives considered
Each with the reason it was not chosen.
```

| [0087](ADR-0087-forgot-your-password-is-a-door-on-the-sign-in-screen.md) | *Forgot your password?* is a door on the sign-in screen, not a screen of its own | Accepted — names the flow `ADR-0083` §6 deferred here and settles that it costs `ADR-0076` §1's address table no row and `screen.ts` no slug; **coins** one player-facing phrase, which `ADR-0083` did not have to, and records that as the cost; narrows `ADR-0081` §3's grant of a *forgot password* screen's address by leaving it unspent, and leaves `reset`, `verify` and every other sentence of §3 byte-unchanged; applies `ADR-0031` §5's unconditional `202` and `ADR-0078` §Consequences' *honest about a pending state* to the copy; changes no server, wire, schema or ticket |
| [0088](ADR-0088-the-two-browser-proof-is-a-written-hand-check.md) | The two-browser proof is a written hand-check, not a CI job | Accepted — closes `EPIC-03` with no browser runner; fixes the automated ceiling at `ADR-0032` §4's *"still jsdom, still no network"*; names the four things no test covers and accepts them; §1's **heading** amended by 0089 (*"here or in CI"* → in CI and between a pull request and `develop`), its body, §§2–5 and `DEC-024`'s answer byte-unchanged |
| [0089](ADR-0089-a-browser-drives-this-client-for-a-qa-round-never-for-a-gate.md) | A browser drives this client for a QA round, never for a gate | Accepted — amends `ADR-0088` §1's heading **only**, and preserves every cost term §1's ratio rested on as a standing condition: no browser dependency in any module, `build.yml` still two jobs, no pull request or `verify:` waiting on a case, and no citing a QA round as coverage; upholds `ADR-0002` at the test layer by letting the driver read anything and write nothing but `pd.roomCode`; adds the rule `EPIC-12` §Termination lacked — a failure that does not reproduce by hand is a **harness** defect, filed against `EPIC-12`, excluded from `B(N)`, and never repaired in production code; §2b's **heading and one sentence** amended by 0090 (*"No gate"* → *"No gate, and one caller"*; *"a human's command"* → *"the human's own message and nothing else"*), the rest of §2b and §§2a, 2c, 3, 4, 5, 6 byte-unchanged; permission extended to the `uat` focus by 0092, all six sections standing |
| [0090](ADR-0090-a-skill-may-write-the-catalogue-or-run-it-never-both.md) | A skill may write the catalogue or run it, never both in one turn | Accepted — amends `ADR-0089` §2b's heading and one sentence **only**, reading its *"not another skill invoking it as a step"* as a rule about **composition** rather than about automation alone: *"a human's command"* is a condition only if it means the **immediate caller**, and a human-typed composite is a cron whose clock is the length of its first half. Licenses `qa-cases`, an authoring skill that lands catalogue cases through ordinary reviewed PRs, runs no browser, dispatches neither `qa` nor `qa-manager`, and whose terminal act is a **report naming the command the human types next**; extends `ADR-0089` §5 where it was silent with a `source` column, so a case whose `expect` has no merged source is **not written** but registered as a `DEC` for the product owner; amends `docs/test-plan.md` §*Per-epic suites*' *"filled in when an epic is first tested, not before"* to admit an authored-then-tested path whose output is **provisional until its first round**, because a merged decision proves what was decided and not what shipped; gives §2b a one-command check over a declared three-file set and states which half of it no grep can catch; §2's declared three-file set grown to four by 0092 (`agents/uat.md`, mention-only) and to five by 0097 (`agents/audit.md`, mention-only) |
| [0091](ADR-0091-design-gets-no-agent-a-new-screen-owes-a-card.md) | Design gets no agent: a new screen owes a card, and adoption is gated where it is consumed | Accepted — applies `ADR-0024` and amends nothing, all five of its sections byte-unchanged; adds no agent and no skill to the roster; puts the one rule the undesigned screens lacked in the planner's split procedure — a story adding a `Screen` member names its card in `## Design notes` or its split's first ticket *is* the card — and generalises `EPIC-06`'s interactive deviation into mint-interactively / compose-by-dispatched-ticket with the human's visual verdict allowed to trail the merge; keeps `check-drift.sh` out of `web-client/` (adoption is gated in the consumer's own CI job, where three guards already live and a fourth — no raw length in a Tailwind arbitrary value, measured at 25 occurrences of four values — is added); reopens `EPIC-06` with the six-slug retrofit story and records that `ADR-0024`'s generator trigger has fired; §5's collection vehicle narrowed by the human's run-now call recorded in 0092 — UAT rounds may file the missing cards, the retrofit story covers the remainder — §5's register and §§1–4 byte-unchanged |
| [0092](ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) | A UAT round files what a merged source settles, and asks the product owner the rest | Accepted — extends `ADR-0089`'s permission to a `uat` focus of the **same** cycle with all six of its sections standing as written (a `shot` verb captures screenshots over CDP with Node built-ins only, never committed, **no image-diff tooling ever**; §4 transposed to *observable by a human looking*; §2c gains the corollary that no round may be cited as what made the product *ready*); amends `ADR-0090` §2's declared set from three files to four (`agents/uat.md`, mention-only) and nothing else in it; applies `ADR-0024` §3 and `ADR-0091` §§1–4 byte-unchanged, recording the human's run-now narrowing of §5's vehicle; adds the `uat` observer to the roster, keeps one manager, one skill and one ledger; and gives `B(N)` its second and third exclusions — missing cards and decision-born improvements — plus a baseline rule for newly-carded screens and a verdict line qualified inline. Registers `DEC-086` open for the product owner |
| [0093](ADR-0093-ready-for-real-users-is-said-of-the-shipped-artifact.md) | "Ready for real users" is said of the shipped artifact, and the bar is two facts | Accepted — resolves `DEC-086` for the product owner: the bar is **two merged, checkable facts, neither a test result** — the proofs of record (the `ADR-0088` §2 hand-check and the QA/UAT rounds) load the **built bundle** rather than `npm run dev` (mechanism registered as `DEC-087`, the architect's — **answered 2026-09-04 by [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md)**, which supersedes `ADR-0088` §2 step 3 and makes fact a meetable; the deployment's own serving is `DEC-126`), and **recovery is completable by a real user in the offered deployment** (`ADR-0031` §7's *"valid state in development and tests"* applied: an offering to real users is neither). Meeting the bar is a **precondition on the phrase, never a certificate** — readiness stays the human's judgment made by reading, `ADR-0089` §2c and `ADR-0092` §2 standing; the bar gates the phrase, not the act of offering, which stays the human's. The event is `ADR-0063` §5's — strangers beyond *"people the author invited personally"* — so the founding moment is not gated, and that ADR's farm expiry falls due at the same event on its own terms. Five absences (history paging past 10, ladder paging, the third device, the hundred-way tie, real network) are **out** under a written rule: a gap enters the bar only when its failure falsifies the success condition at first contact or destroys what a player cannot get back |
| [0094](ADR-0094-opening-the-invite-is-taking-the-seat.md) | Opening the invite is taking the seat, and the two join cards are corrected to it | Accepted — §1 qualified by 0105 (an opener already in a running duel takes no seat) — resolves `DEC-092` for the product owner: the join path **ships as it is**, and the two cards that draw screens it does not have are what change. Applies `docs/vision.md`'s first success condition — five verbs, none of them *accepts* — with `ADR-0022` (*holding a room code is the invite*; joining is *"one click with zero friction for the invited"*) and `ADR-0060` §§1, 4 (the first screen carries *"the join form"* and *"the room code box"*, which a separate code screen would make false). Amends nothing: no client, server, wire or `PROTOCOL_VERSION` change, and no `docs/test-plan.md` `expect` — `SMK-05`, `CORE-02` and `CORE-05` stand. Constrains `TASK-120907` (now a `module: design` ticket), `design/screens/join-duel.html` and `enter-code.html`, and two rows of the screen table. Raises nothing for the architect: `TASK-120907`'s technical half was conditional on a *yes* |
| [0095](ADR-0095-the-table-states-who-took-the-pot-and-never-names-a-hand.md) | The table states who took the pot, and never names a hand | Accepted — resolves `DEC-095` for the product owner: **yes** to the banner, **no** to the made hand. When the street is `COMPLETE` the table replaces `Pot N` with one line — `You win 4,850` / `Your rival wins 4,850` / `Split pot — you win 2,425` — transcribed from a `PotAwarded` the client already receives, and **no hand is ever named** at the table, at any street, in text or in an `aria-label`. Applies `docs/vision.md`'s *On variance* — *"Luck decides a hand"* — for the banner, and *"Every hand is stored as an event log, so a match can be replayed and analysed **afterwards**"* with *Positioning* and the roadmap (replay is v0.4) for the refusal. Builds on `ADR-0046` §§0, 2, `ADR-0075` (lifetime is a fact, never a timer), `ADR-0008`, `ADR-0002` and `ADR-0094` (the card is corrected, not the product). Amends nothing: no engine, server, wire type, `PROTOCOL_VERSION` step or `docs/test-plan.md` `expect`. `no-derivation.test.tsx`'s `HAND_TALK` gate stands **byte-unchanged** — its fixture is mid-hand, so a correctly-triggered banner leaves it green, and a coder who finds it red has built the wrong trigger. Constrains `TASK-121101` and both banner frames of `design/screens/duel-table-states.html`, which lose their second line. Raises nothing for the architect: `TASK-121101`'s wire question was conditional on a *yes* |
| [0096](ADR-0096-the-audit-judges-a-whole-duel-against-a-frozen-rubric.md) | The audit judges a whole duel against a frozen rubric, and no round may grow it | Accepted — resolves `DEC-096` for the product owner: a **product audit** is a third focus of the one cycle (`/qa-cycle audit <scope>`), and its unit is the **beat**, not the screen — the walk is `docs/vision.md`'s first success condition read one clause at a time, eight beats from the opened link to the rematch, **including the all-in runout `docs/test-plan.md` has never walked**, both browsers observed, at **two shapes** — the whole walk at `phone` **390 × 664** and `R2`/`R3` again at `laptop` **720 × 900**, on the human's *"we have to support phone size"*, with **one bar checked twice rather than two bars**. `ADR-0092` §3's classifier is **amended for this focus only, by relocating the merged source rather than removing it**: a finding contradicts a **criterion** in a merged, closed, five-line **rubric**, and needs no other source — the human's *may file findings that contradict no merged source*, honoured without abolishing the rule §3 protects. §3 stands byte-unchanged for the `qa` and `uat` focuses. **Termination is the rubric**: `A(N)` counts *criteria* answered `not met`, so `A(N)` can never exceed the rubric's size, a ceiling known before the round starts; the rubric is **frozen for the invocation** (`EPIC-12` rule 1, one level up), a proposed criterion routes as `ADR-0092` §5 routes a question and reaches the list no earlier than the next invocation; `PASS` at `A(N) = 0` says the **list** is satisfied and never that the product is finished (`ADR-0089` §2c, `ADR-0093` §2). **No severity and no backlog under this focus** — a finding deferred by the eight-cap stays counted, which is the repair of the seventeen `status: backlog` rows — with `EPIC-12` rule 2 scoped to the other two focuses on the human's call. Applies `ADR-0092` §§6, 8 (one manager, one ledger, no second skill) and `ADR-0093` §2. Registers `DEC-097` — **the architect's**: the declared-file set and the two-viewport mechanism. Its one escalation — **is a phone a supported surface?** — the human answered the same day (*"we have to support phone size"*), so §4 walks **two shapes** and `DEC-097`(b) became the load-bearing half. **`docs/vision.md` is not amended**: *"She opens it in a browser"* and *"Two browsers, one room link"* name a browser and **no device**, so the call resolves a silence rather than contradicting a sentence, and §4 names what would change that. **Phone landscape was left open by §4 and is now answered** — the human's *"we are ok to support only one orientation for mobile form factor"*, recorded by [`ADR-0097`](ADR-0097-a-resize-is-two-numbers-and-the-observer-is-the-fifth-file.md) §5; **the rubric's home is settled by [0099](ADR-0099-the-rubric-is-the-adr-section-and-a-criterion-is-born-merged.md)** — §2 itself, grown only by an amending ADR that annotates this row, and no working document ever exists (`DEC-098`) |
| [0097](ADR-0097-a-resize-is-two-numbers-and-the-observer-is-the-fifth-file.md) | A resize is two numbers, and the audit's observer is the fifth declared file | Accepted — resolves `DEC-097` for the architect, both halves, and unblocks the first audit round. **(b), the load-bearing half: a viewport resize is an *act* under `ADR-0089` §3**, the third of its three categories — *click, type, navigate, reload, clear storage* — not a read and not an application-state write, so §3 stands byte-unchanged and is applied rather than widened. `scripts/qa/drive.mjs` gains one verb, `size <width> <height>`, sending `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` with **`width`, `height`, `deviceScaleFactor: 0`, `mobile: false` and no other field**, reading the viewport back from the page and **exiting 1 if it is not what was asked for**. *The classification is a property of the fields, not of the method name*: measured, `mobile: true` widens the layout viewport 390 → 520 by mobile shrink-to-fit and turns an `R2` failure into a **false pass**, fabricates `screen` and `devicePixelRatio`, and buys none of `pointer: coarse`, `hover: none` or `maxTouchPoints` — unchanged in all three states. The override **survives session detach and navigation** and is **per-target**, so a live tab keeps its JS context (hence its socket and its seat), fires one `resize` and re-resolves `100dvh` — §4's two shapes are two measurements of one tab, not two passes, and `ADR-0018`'s re-seat is never approached. **Both tabs move together and the walk restores itself to `phone`**, so the shape is the only variable. `Browser.setWindowBounds` is **rejected on measurement**: Chrome clamps a window to 500 px wide, so 390 × 664 yields a 500 × 577 viewport, and 87 px of chrome makes 720 × 900 yield 720 × 813 — §4's numbers are viewport numbers and window sizing cannot express them. **(a)** `ADR-0090` §2's declared-file set becomes **five**, `.claude/agents/audit.md` licensed to *mention* the cycle and never to invoke it, on `ADR-0092` §2's precedent and passing `ADR-0092` §8's own test — `ADR-0096` §2 froze `ADR-0092` §3 byte-unchanged for `qa` and `uat`, so one file cannot hold two classifiers; the check gains `audit` and was verified to exit 0 today, 0 at five and 1 at six. `ADR-0092` §8 otherwise applies unamended: one manager, one ledger, no new skill, no `Write` on the observer. **Portrait only** — the human's *"we are ok to support only one orientation for mobile form factor"* recorded where `ADR-0096` §4 left the question, so no rotation handling, no reflow decision, no second mobile shape, and `screenOrientation` is never sent. `ADR-0089` §§2a/2b/2c re-checked one at a time, with the corollary **a shape walked is not a surface supported**. Refines one stated reason in `ADR-0092` §2 and `ADR-0096` §4 without moving either decision: the ~500 px clip belongs to window *sizing*, not to capture — an overridden 390 × 664 captures faithfully at 390 × 664 |
| [0098](ADR-0098-the-wordmark-belongs-to-the-front-door-alone.md) | The wordmark belongs to the front door alone | Accepted — resolves `DEC-099` for the product owner: the coin-and-two-tone lockup renders on the **front door alone** — the first screen's pre-create branch, as `design/screens/create-duel.html`'s front-door frame draws it — and the unconditional `<h1>Poker Duels</h1>` leaves `App.tsx`, so no other screen or state carries on-page product naming. **Mostly a reading of merged sources, and recorded as one**: eleven card files hold one `.mark` between them; the four secondary-screen cards were composed under the shipped global heading and none drew it; even `create-duel.html`'s own waiting frame gives its top to the code. Confirmed by `docs/vision.md`'s *Positioning* (*"dark, quiet, fast, minimal"*) and the success condition, whose match is played on the screens the mark stays off; builds on `ADR-0033` (anatomy card-born, placement deliberately unsettled until now), `ADR-0060` §5 (chrome above the table already refused once) and `ADR-0094` — which makes the named cost real: **the invited half of the product is seated straight into a dealt hand and now never reads the product's name on-page**, only in the tab title. **No card is in arrears — the client was**; the follow-up ticket touches four files (`App.tsx`, `Lobby.tsx`, `App.test.tsx` — whose four plain-title assertion sites cannot stand — and `Lobby.test.tsx`) and §4 names them for the planner. Amends nothing on the wire and raises nothing for the architect |
| [0099](ADR-0099-the-rubric-is-the-adr-section-and-a-criterion-is-born-merged.md) | The rubric is the ADR section, and a criterion is born merged | Accepted — resolves `DEC-098` for the architect: **the audit rubric is `ADR-0096` §2 itself, and no working document ever exists.** The deciding force sat in neither reading's list — **where a criterion is born**: `ADR-0096` §3 routes every proposed criterion to a `DEC` whose answer is *a merged PR either way*, and a merged answer is an ADR (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5), so every future criterion arrives as merged ADR text whatever `DEC-098` said, and a working document could only ever have held a **copy** — a second register from its first line, able to disagree silently with the law it transcribes, guarded by nothing (*two copies of a rule drift*, `ADR-0092` §8, applied not re-argued). Growth is **one amending ADR** in the pattern `ADR-0092` §2 and `ADR-0097` §4 used on `ADR-0090` §2's declared-file set: ids sequential and never reused (`R6` next), the criterion stated in §2's own three-column form, the **resulting priority order restated as ids only — never another criterion's text**, and `ADR-0096`'s index row annotated *rubric grown to N* **in the same PR** — the same-PR clause priced against its precedent's one observed lag, `ADR-0090`'s row reading *grown to four* a day after `ADR-0097` made the set five, repaired in this ADR's PR. **A round record cites `R<n>` and the ADR section that states it — `R2` (`ADR-0096` §2) today — never a document path**; the freeze (`ADR-0096` §3) becomes a commit fact, the rubric in force for an invocation being the chain as merged at the commit its first round names; §6's metric counts from two dated registers, growth ADRs in this index and invocations in the round stories. `TASK-121203` and `TASK-121205` already cite the decided form and **change nothing**; the declared-file set stays at **five**, no file added anywhere; `ADR-0096` §7's *the rubric* deletion and *the ADR that says why* collapse into one act, so reversal got cheaper. Costs named: the rubric is never again one table once it grows (assembly on every invocation, with the reversal — found a document later, transcribe once at that day's size — priced for the day the chain is the demonstrated obstruction); the index annotation is a hand-maintained discipline, not a gate; a sixth criterion is never cheaper than a full merged ADR — slow on purpose |
| [0100](ADR-0100-the-driver-reaches-an-amount-by-pressing-what-a-player-presses.md) | The driver reaches an amount by pressing what a player presses | Accepted — resolves `DEC-100` for the architect: `web-client/src/e2e/drive-duel.tsx`'s `actThroughTheBar` stops setting a control's value and **presses the sizing row, then reads the action button before it clicks** — find the button by the recorded verb (unchanged), read the total it prints, click if it already matches, otherwise press each sizing control in document order re-reading after each press, and **throw** naming the recorded amount and every amount reached if none matches. That is strictly *more* than `fireEvent.change` proved: the old path set a value and trusted it, the new one proves the bar reached the amount before `whole-duel.test.tsx`'s `expect(actsSent).toEqual(recordedActs)` proves it encoded it. **The premise the question was raised on is false, and measuring it made the answer cheap** — the script is generated, not authored: `playDuel` draws every bet/raise amount from `minRaiseTo` or `allInTo` alone, and all four amount-carrying steps in the committed fixture land on a chip (three `allInTo`, one `minRaiseTo`), so **no frame is re-recorded and none of the four failing e2e files is edited**, which is the evidence nothing they prove was traded away. §4 settles a second breakage the two-file budget hid: the sizing row must set `to = allInTo` *without* sending (three recorded raises) **and** an `ALL_IN` button appears whenever the server names `ALL_IN` (one recorded `AllIn`, and `BettingRules.kt` allows `ALL_IN` with no `RAISE` for a short stack facing a bet) — so the card's three-button `.actions` row is one drawn state, not a law over all states, and no card is in arrears. §5 refuses **by name** a driver-only slider, any test-only prop or `data-testid`, and any reach into state or `actFrame`. §6 rules that **a named preset computes the quantity it is named for**: `floor × 4/3` labelled *⅓ pot* does not ship, and the pot reaches the control from `view.pot + both committedThisStreet` through `Lobby.tsx` — no wire change — while *which* quantity `pot` names is `DEC-101`, registered open for the product owner and answered the same day by [0101](ADR-0101-pot-means-a-pot-sized-raise-and-the-fractions-share-its-base.md). Costs named: the e2e can no longer exercise an **interior** amount at all (nothing lost today, foreclosed tomorrow); the bar can read all-in twice; `TASK-120908` becomes a six-file atomic ticket, blocked. Applies `ADR-0002` and reads `ADR-0033`; amends neither |
| [0101](ADR-0101-pot-means-a-pot-sized-raise-and-the-fractions-share-its-base.md) | `pot` means a pot-sized raise, and the fractions share its base | Accepted — resolves `DEC-101` for **the product owner**: every sizing chip sets a street total, and the three fractions are **one rule over one base**. The base is the pot as it will be *after the call* — `P = view.pot + both seats' committedThisStreet` (`ADR-0100` §6's pot, unchanged) plus `toCall = callTo − the acting seat's committedThisStreet` — and the chips set `min → minRaiseTo`/`minBetTo`, `⅓ → callTo + floor(base/3)`, `½ → callTo + floor(base/2)`, `pot → callTo + base`, `all-in → allInTo`, rounding **down** because chips are integers and under-committing is the side the label promises. With no bet outstanding `toCall` is 0 and the base is simply the pot, so one rule gives *half the pot* when betting and a half-pot raise when raising, with no second case. **Licensed by `docs/vision.md`'s *What it is*, first line — *"Heads-up Texas Hold'em. Two players. Never three."*** — the product is the game, so a control's word is the game's word; `ADR-0100` had already recorded in a merged sentence what a poker player pressing *pot* expects, and the two formulas the card admits are not it. **§3: a fraction chip is offered only when its own amount is legal** (`floor ≤ amount ≤ allInTo`), never clamped and never greyed — so `TASK-120908`'s *"a preset the stack cannot afford is not offered"* stands and gains the bottom end, hiding costs a shortcut and never a legal amount, and `min`/`all-in` are legal by construction and never absent, which is what keeps `ADR-0100` §3's *no frame is re-recorded* true of this answer too. **§5: `design/screens/duel-table.html` is in arrears on one number in two places** — the hero frame's stepper and its `Raise to` button read `3,250` with the `pot` chip selected and become **3,650**; 3,250 is the *base*, printed where the raise that sizes to it belongs. Costs named: a merged card is wrong and a design ticket exists that nobody planned; the row changes shape between turns; preflop the button gets `min` and `½` at the same 300 and **no `⅓` at all**, so an ordinary 2.2–2.5bb open is reachable from no chip; and the chips can no longer be defined from `LegalActions` alone. Registers **`DEC-102`** — the stepper's step, which `ADR-0100` mis-filed here and which gates none of §§1–5. `TASK-120908` keeps `ADR-0100` §7's six files |
| [0102](ADR-0102-a-hand-ends-in-steps-and-the-client-owns-the-clock.md) | A hand ends in steps, and the client owns the clock | Accepted — resolves `DEC-105` for **the architect**: a runout's pacing lives in **`web-client`**, and `poker-server`, `poker-engine` and the wire are untouched. When a `Snapshot` says the hand is over, the store paints it as a **sequence of steps** — one per `StreetDealt` in the `Events` frame that immediately preceded it, then a final step carrying the whole snapshot and `ADR-0095`'s award line — and **queues every frame that arrives while steps remain**, in arrival order, applying them when the last step has stood. At step *k* exactly two fields lag: the board is `view.board.cards.slice(0, n_k)` with `n_k = board.length − (cards carried by the steps after k)`, and the street label is that `StreetDealt`'s own `street`. A `Snapshot` that does not end a hand is applied at once, with no timer, so **ordinary play costs nothing**. **The ticket's source reading was confirmed on three points and wrong by omission on a fourth, which is what decides the answer**: `DuelAction.kt`'s `act` calls `advance` in the same call and `deliver` sends both hands' frames in one batch — the merged `DuelActionTest.afoldEndsTheHandAndOpensTheNext` asserts snapshots for hand 1 and hand 2 in one `outbound` — so a client that only sliced the board would be overwritten a millisecond into the first step, and `ADR-0095` §4's banner is today drawn and erased in the same delivery. §6 meets *the server is authoritative* head on and draws the line: **the client may choose when to paint a fact the server sent, never what the fact is** — every value painted is the server's own, the lag is monotone, bounded and self-terminating, it can never influence what the client sends, and `ADR-0095` §2 had already settled that rendering a server-stated value is not asserting a game fact. §3 fixes the invariant: **the only thing ever withheld is the tail of the board** — no card face comes from a `StreetDealt` payload, no hole card is held back, no control is shown early or retracted, and `no-derivation.test.tsx` stays byte-unchanged. §4 sets the step at **600 ms**, named once in `boot.ts`, with **`0` meaning synchronous** so `drive-duel.tsx` boots at `0` and the four recorded-frame e2e suites are not edited. §5: a **reconnecting or reloading client jumps to the end**, structurally — `resumeFrames` sends no `Events`, so there are no street steps to take. §7: `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does **not** move, and would not have moved for the server option either. Costs named: a runout's **stacks settle at the first step**, so the outcome is legible from the seat plates 1.8 s early; every hand ends 600 ms later; screen and server disagree by design for up to 2.4 s, and a queued `OpponentPresence` over-states its grace; the two seats pace independently; and `DEC-104`'s `Pot 0` is held on screen longer, unfixed and still the product owner's. `TASK-121301` becomes one atomic `web-client` ticket, re-cut whole |
| [0103](ADR-0103-the-table-fits-the-phone-and-the-cards-give-before-the-numbers.md) | The table fits the phone, and the cards give before the numbers | Accepted — §1 qualified by 0106 (a true residual under one CSS pixel reads as met) — resolves `DEC-106` for **the product owner**: the duel table **fits a 390 × 664 phone**, whole column, at every beat — `scrollHeight ≤ clientHeight`, no scroll to act — and it is **one table at two widths**, every element shown in the same order with the same words, its measurements narrowing **continuously with the column's own width** (the `100cqi` idiom already in the card), so no width shows a different table and `ADR-0096` §4's three second-surface tests are all missed. **The arm the question offered was already closed**: `ADR-0096` §2 rules scrolling to see the amount to call `R2` `not met` at either shape and forbids a relaxed phone bar by name, so *"a player scrolls"* would have amended a criterion and contradicted the human's *"we have to support phone size"* — not this ADR's to do. **§3, what gives, in order and exhaustively: whitespace → the rival's face-down hand → the player's hole cards (floor: never smaller than a board card) → the board, last of the card groups. Nothing else gives** — stacks, pot, bet lines, the amount to call, the sizing row and the action buttons keep their size, labels and place (`R3`). The bar **may grow** (its rows wrap at 390) but never truncates; running out of the list re-opens a decision rather than accepting a scroll. **§4: `design/screens/duel-table.html` carries it** — amended so the hardcoded `--w:96px` and `--w:40px` narrow like `--bw`, plus a **second frame at 390 × 664 with the same markup**, boxed in height as well as width; a separate phone card is refused, and the line is **markup identity, not file count**. **The card is in arrears and was already**: measured headless this run it is **732 / 664** at 390 × 664 and 900/900 at 720 × 900, so a client transcribing it perfectly still fails `R2` by 68 px — which is why `TASK-121302` could not close this by conformance. **§5: `TASK-121302` is rewritten, not amended, and blocked on the design ticket**; its height-budget half (`min-height: 100dvh`, `flex: 1`, one column not two) survives as necessary-but-not-sufficient, and its file set is measured. **§1 also settles that one frame answers every beat** — the card reserves every slot, so the table's height does not vary by beat. Costs named: the player's own hand is smaller on a phone; the table acquires a **size budget**, so the next thing added must buy its space or not ship; round 1's fix set grows a dependency and `R2` is counted `not met` again if round 2 runs first (`ADR-0096` §5 — filing does not reduce `A(N)`); two frames drift with no gate; and the give order becomes a merged constraint on design taste. Leaves `DEC-103`, `DEC-104` open by name and reopens no orientation question (`ADR-0097` §5). Applies `ADR-0096` §§2 and 4, `ADR-0091` §§1 and 3, `ADR-0024` §3; amends nothing |
| [0104](ADR-0104-a-frame-reaches-the-connection-in-the-room-it-is-about.md) | A frame reaches the connection that is in the room it is about | Accepted — resolves `DEC-107` for **the architect**: `deliver` resolves a seat to a player exactly as it does today and then asks for that player's writer **for this room**; a connection in another room, or in none, is skipped silently, the same idiom `deliver` already documents for a seat mid-reconnect. **§1: `ConnectionDirectory.writerFor(player)` is deleted**, replaced by `writerFor(player, room)`, so there is no unscoped lookup a later call site can reach for; `deliver`'s signature is unchanged and **all seven call sites are untouched**. **§2:** `RoomMembership` moves to `duels.poker.server.session` and is registered with the writer it belongs to, one object shared by reference, its `code` **`@Volatile`** — read by the ticker and the other seat's socket, and a stale read is invisible on one thread. **§3: production does not change** — `disconnect()` still builds the frame, `ADR-0028` §5's emission table stands, and the registry keeps knowing nothing of writers. **§6: `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move** — no declaration changes, `ADR-0047` §2's fingerprint is unchanged, no ledger row, `TASK-121403` is **not** `atomic:`. **The story's reading was confirmed and found to understate the defect**: the widening is in the composition `seat → PlayerId → writer-anywhere`, so it carries every frame `deliver` routes — nine of `ServerMessage`'s eleven subtypes (`Snapshot`, `Events`, `YourTurn`, `DuelFinished`, `Rejected`, `Failure`, `ActedForAbsent`, `RematchOffered`, `OpponentPresence`; only `Welcome` and `RoomJoined` are never routed) — and needs no race, because nothing on the wire says *leave* (`ADR-0072`) and a held seat's frames follow the player. No card can leak: the recipient is always the player the frame was projected for. **§4** scopes the client's own store — a `RoomJoined` naming a **different** room re-initialises it (counters carry over) — and is explicitly **not** load-bearing; **§5** answers the in-flight case in three windows and names the one no server check can close. **§9** treats the unreproduced fold as a mechanism that fits, never as a cause. Costs named: `ConnectionDirectory`'s documented ignorance is retracted; a `@Volatile` obligation nothing in the type system asks for; silent drops gain a second indistinguishable cause with no counter; membership-before-delivery becomes load-bearing ordering; and **the unscoped "send to a player wherever they are" is foreclosed**, deliberately and with no current victim — `writerFor` has one call site, and this is it. **Splits off `DEC-109`** — the product owner's: may one player hold seats in two live rooms at once? Upholds `ADR-0028` §1 rather than reopening it; leaves `DEC-108` untouched |
| [0105](ADR-0105-one-duel-at-a-time-and-the-refusal-hands-back-the-duel.md) | One duel at a time, and the refusal hands the player back their duel | Accepted — resolves `DEC-109` **for the human, who stated the call on 2026-09-02** after review found the vision does not settle it: **no**, a player holds at most one seat in a **running** duel. While they hold a seat in a `PLAYING` room, `CreateRoom` and `JoinRoom` from that player **take no seat** — the room they asked for is untouched, the seat and duel they hold are untouched, and no coin moves. **§2 scopes *running* to `PLAYING` alone**: a `WAITING` seat is not refused, because `ADR-0073` §3 told the host on screen that the room stays open and they may walk away; a `FINISHED` seat is not refused, because `Room.offerRematch` agrees only when **both** seats have offered. A seat inside `ADR-0013`'s grace window is refused with the rest — that is the grace period working. **§3: the refusal hands the player back the duel they are in** — the table as it stands, never a lobby, never a dialog — and repeating it does the same thing every time; both shipped routes in (`Back to the lobby`, and a second `?room=` link that `boot.ts` prefers over the memory) arrive holding no memory of that room. **§4 fixes two sentences and no third** — `You are already in a duel. Finish it to start another.` — with a table of what the copy may not say (a duration, anything about the code that was asked for per `ADR-0022`'s no-oracle rule, a promise the link will last, the casino's furniture); layout stays `EPIC-06`'s. **§5: the player whose invite was clicked sees nothing**, because nothing happened to them. **§6 states what must be true and writes no repair**: nothing moves, the client must be able to name the duel it returns to, and the refusal must not meter as a guess when `ADR-0022` §2's budget is built (`JoinLimits` and `TOO_MANY_ATTEMPTS` do not exist at `develop`). **§7 inherits `ADR-0104` §9**: this explains no fold. Costs named: **a player who wants out of a duel is stuck** — no resign, no turn clock, and a `PLAYING` room is never reaped, so their exit depends on their rival still acting; the product's primary action, *Create a duel room*, can now say no; the friend whose link was clicked and the player who clicked it are both left slightly in the dark, on purpose; one more string with nothing keeping it in voice; and **§2's hole stays open** — several `WAITING` rooms can each become a duel the player is not at, so *never in two duels at once* is what this aims at and not what it guarantees. Qualifies `ADR-0094` §1 by one case; registers `DEC-110` (the architect's — the mechanism) and `DEC-111` (the product owner's — more than one waiting room). **§2's `WAITING` row amended by [0124](ADR-0124-one-waiting-room-and-play-again-hands-it-back.md)** — a `WAITING` seat is still not refused, but a second `CreateRoom` from its holder now hands back the room they hold instead of opening another; §§1, 3–7 and §2's other two rows stand byte-unchanged **§2's `WAITING` row is amended a second time, by [0141](ADR-0141-taking-a-seat-elsewhere-releases-the-room-you-were-holding.md), on its `JoinRoom` half only**: the join is still **not refused** and the row's stated reason still holds, but the room the joiner was holding no longer stands — so that row's *"allowed, exactly as today"* is now qualified on both halves, by two different ADRs, and is no longer a complete statement on its own |
| [0106](ADR-0106-a-sub-pixel-residual-is-a-fit-and-one-pixel-is-the-fence.md) | A sub-pixel residual is a fit, and one pixel is the fence | Accepted — resolves `DEC-112` for **the product owner**: after `TASK-121402`'s 48 px repair the duel-table document measures a true **664.90625 px against 664** (`scrollHeight` 665) with both action buttons at or above the fold, and *fits the phone* **admits it**. `ADR-0103` §1's `scrollHeight ≤ clientHeight` stays the contract; at the one boundary the integers cannot express — a reading of exactly one over — the judgment moves to the true geometry, and **a true excess strictly under one CSS pixel is met**, one pixel or more is `R2` `not met`, a two-integer excess files with no second read. **§2 fences the tolerance at the instrument's own unit and forecloses its widening** — a tolerance that can hold painted content is the *"relaxed phone bar"* `ADR-0096` §2 forbids by name, so widening is the human's, reachable by no vision-derived ADR. **§3: `TASK-121402` merges as scoped** — the implementer's stop is the conduct `ADR-0103` §3 commands, and the 664 / 664 prediction was unreachable from the day it was written (the defect table's 712 was a rounded 712.90625; the integers were never the geometry). **§4: the last 0.90625 px is re-filed once**, ordinary backlog, buying headroom (the column stands 0.09375 px from the fence) and retiring the second read; its closer spends §3.1 whitespace and nothing further down the give list. **§5 is the QA clause**: a sub-pixel overflow may not be filed — not as a finding, a criterion, a `DEC` or precedent — and shields nothing else; clipped content, imperceivable events, unmeasured beats and any full-pixel excess walk the ordinary path. Costs named: the one-`eval` contract is spent at the boundary (two reads and a rule, until a residual ticket nothing forces to land); a citable tolerance now exists; the product ships 0.09375 px from the fence; a criterion merges undischarged as literally written, carried by the PR body's readings plus this ADR; and a classic-scrollbar window at phone width can grow a scrollbar track for a rounded pixel. Qualifies `ADR-0103` §1 by one boundary case; upholds `ADR-0096` §2 |
| [0107](ADR-0107-pot-names-every-chip-committed-to-the-hand.md) | `Pot` names every chip committed to the hand | Accepted — resolves `DEC-114` for **the product owner**: the table's `Pot` names the **total** — `view.pot` plus both seats' `committedThisStreet`, identically `Lobby.tsx:154`'s `potIncludingStreet` and the `P` that [`ADR-0100`](ADR-0100-the-driver-reaches-an-amount-by-pressing-what-a-player-presses.md) §6 already calls *the pot* when it hands the sizing row a quantity its labels may truthfully name — so the strip prints the number the `pot` chip sizes against ([`ADR-0101`](ADR-0101-pot-means-a-pot-sized-raise-and-the-fractions-share-its-base.md) §1), the blinds are in it the moment they are posted (`Pot 150` at 50/100, never `Pot 0`), a bet joins it at the act rather than the sweep, and the table's three money numerals sum to `duel-rules.md`'s asserted invariant at every instant. One figure, one word — no *Total pot* second line. Costs named: the rival's bet-line chips now also sit inside the pot figure, *the table renders and never derives* narrows to **exactly one** derived quantity, and `duel-table.html`'s two `Pot 2,450` nodes read **2,850** while `duel-table-states.html`'s 3,250 already agrees (its bet-line is empty). No wire change, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` move, no engine change; prop-or-sum is the implementing ticket's shape per `ADR-0101` §7's precedent, and the bet-lines' fate stays with item 6's card |
| [0108](ADR-0108-expiry-plays-the-seat-and-the-timebank-replaces-the-grace-window.md) | An expiry plays the seat, never the duel, and the timebank replaces the grace window | Accepted — resolves `DEC-115` for **the product owner**: a seat on turn has **30 s a decision plus one 3 m timebank per duel** (the human's numbers, configuration not literals), and when the clock is exhausted the server gives up **that one decision** with `ADR-0023`'s conduct — fold facing a bet, check when checking is free, read from the engine's legal set, marked on the wire as the server's act. **An expiry never ends the duel**: no count of timeouts forfeits, no coin moves except by `ADR-0014` on the outcome the engine reaches, and a seat that never acts blinds off under the escalating schedule that already guarantees termination — the coin stays *"a counter of duels won"*. **The timebank replaces `ADR-0013`'s grace window** (amends it; the held seat, the filtered resume and the conduct stand): the clock is indifferent to the socket, so disconnecting never gains, stops or freezes time; an away seat whose clock is exhausted is `ABSENT` and played without delay until it reconnects, exactly as today; a connected seat always gets a fresh 30 s, because a reconnect is observable and attention is not. **The duel never pauses** — `DUEL_PAUSED`'s occasion and *"The duel is paused."* go when the clock lands (amends `ADR-0028`/`ADR-0046` by that clause; every word and mark otherwise stands). One countdown, the acting seat's, visible to both, ticking each second toward a server-stated deadline; zero is not an event — the server's act is. Costs named: a connected ghost still spends ~30 s a decision across a blind-off, and a bank-empty disconnector gets 30 s where today's window gave 60. The mechanism — the deadline's frame, the `PROTOCOL_VERSION` step under `ADR-0047`'s lock, the `atomic:` ticket sized by `ADR-0070`'s probe, expiry as synthesised act or new room event — is registered as `DEC-120`, **the architect's**, and not answered |
| [0109](ADR-0109-the-table-marks-the-last-act-and-the-next-deal-clears-it.md) | The table marks the last act, and the next deal clears it | Accepted — resolves `DEC-117` for **the product owner**: the table marks **the most recent act of the hand it is painting** — one mark, any of the six acts, at the seat that made it, never one per seat — saying what the actor's own button said: `actionVerb`'s verb, with the act event's own `to` total on `Call`/`Bet`/`Raise to`/`All in`, `Fold` and `Check` bare, nothing netted or computed. **Within a hand it is only ever replaced**: street ends, snapshots, presence frames and rejections leave it standing — *clears at the street's end* dies on the delivery, since the closing call rides one `Events` frame with its own `BettingRoundEnded` — and **the next hand's deal, as painted through `ADR-0102` §1's queue, removes it**, so a fold's mark stands through `ADR-0095` §4's award window; `DuelFinished` retires it with the table and none survives into a rematch. **No timer, no fade, no dismiss.** `ADR-0075`'s duration rule does not transfer — an act mark has no absence to outlive — but its grounds against timers and welded-frame clearing do. `EPIC-13` item 3's card owes **six states**, one per act, four with a figure; icon-versus-text, placement and any prior-street distinction stay the human's by `ADR-0024` §3. Costs named: most-recent-only (no action log, as `ADR-0046` §4 chose), the opening corner (a seat whose call closes a street opens the next under its own mark, the hidden act's figure still on screen), cross-street staleness, and a refresh loses the mark until the next act (`PlayerView` carries no last-act field — deliberately not repaired, no wire bump). Registers no new `DEC` **Amended by 0122** — §2's figure list loses `Call`: the mark is bare on a call, because `PlayerCalled` carries only `to` and the price the button now names is unrecoverable a tick later |
| [0110](ADR-0110-creating-a-duel-seats-the-host-at-the-table.md) | Creating a duel seats the host at the table, and the promises move with them | Accepted — resolves `DEC-116` for **the product owner**, on the human's stated direction of 2026-09-02 (the report, not the specification): **the dedicated waiting screen is retired** — from the moment `RoomJoined` answers `CreateRoom` the host is at the duel table with the rival's seat standing empty, and the arrival is the table filling, never a screen swap. **§2: the empty seat says `Waiting for your rival`**, byte-identical to the shipped heading, rendered once, at the seat — the human's *"waiting for oppent"* is normalised to the vision's own vocabulary (*rival*), and being overruled costs one string and one frame. **§3: the table states no game fact before the `Snapshot`** — no stack, blind, card, pot, button or action bar; a starting stack read from configuration would be the client's guess wearing the server's voice (`ADR-0002`). **§4: both promises move verbatim and keep being made** — `Back to the lobby` (same words, same act) and *"The room stays open. That link still works for your rival, and it brings you back."*, every clause still true after the move — which is what keeps `ADR-0105` §2 standing on unchanged ground. **§5: the invite moves whole** — the bare code (the read-aloud path `ADR-0022` bought a typable alphabet for, received by `ADR-0094` §2's first-screen box), the selectable link box (the invite where no clipboard exists), and `Copy the link` with its two feedback lines; nothing said *about* the code, no duration printed. **§6: zero new strings** — the state's whole set is ten shipped strings relocated, and a state needing one more is a new ADR (`ADR-0073` §3's rule, carried forward). **§7: the arrival is the `Snapshot` and is silent**; the rival never sees this state (`ADR-0094` §1), and `DEC-111` is exactly as open after this ADR as before it. **§8 names the card's frames** — host-alone in four variants plus the already-carded live table — under `ADR-0103`'s budget, the drawing the human's taste by `ADR-0024` §3. Amends `ADR-0073` §6 only (the *Created — waiting for your rival* frame is retired); §§1–5 stand byte-unchanged in words and behaviour. No Kotlin, no wire type, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, nothing stored. Costs named: the table acquires a non-duel state every future surface must account for; the 390 × 664 fit is asserted, not measured — a card that cannot fit all three invite parts reopens §5 rather than dropping one; and an empty table can read as broken, with §6 forbidding the soothing sentence |
| [0111](ADR-0111-an-illegal-typed-amount-is-refused-in-the-servers-own-numbers.md) | An illegal typed amount is refused in the server's own numbers | Accepted — resolves `DEC-118` for **the product owner**: the typed field is the first control that can express an amount the rules refuse, and when it does **the table refuses the press and says why in the server's own numbers — never clamps, never knowingly sends**. A press with an illegal entry sends no frame, takes no sent-lock and rewrites nothing; under-floor and over-`allInTo` are one case, refused with `rejection-text.ts`'s merged sentences quoting the violated bound from this turn's `LegalActions`; a non-number is a second kind — `That is not an amount.` — and is what rules out *send it and let the rejection land* as a uniform answer, `Bet`/`Raise` carrying `to: number`. §4 fixes the discipline that keeps this a **reading, not a judgment**: the client refuses only what `YourTurn`'s literal fields refuse — `ActionValidation.kt`'s whole amount rule is two comparisons over bounds the server already states — never grows finer than them, and the server path stays load-bearing (`rejection-text.ts` untouched, still earning its keep for races, finer rules and the roadmap's bots). §5: **no act conversion** — a typed `callTo` is not a `Call`, an over-stack amount is not an `AllIn`. §6: **`ADR-0100` §5 stands in full** — the driver gains no typing branch, sets no field's value, and §8 stays the named repair for interior amounts. §7: the card draws the refusal states before item 7's ticket is startable, and the action button may print the proposal or nothing, never a different amount. Costs named: the interval is read in two places under a stated discipline; a too-strict reading blocks a legal amount and no server message will ever say so; the server's two amount rejections become nearly unreachable from this client; and a typo costs a retype, chosen over a silent all-in. Applies `ADR-0100` §6, extends `ADR-0101` §3 to the row's third control, amends neither, registers nothing; `DEC-102` stays open with its ground shifted and said so |
| [0112](ADR-0112-only-a-running-duel-refuses-another-screen.md) | Only a running duel refuses another screen, and the refusal restores the address | Accepted — resolves `DEC-119` for **the product owner**: the human's reported refresh symptom **did not reproduce** — every path tried survives a reload on the frames alone — and the defect that did is decided in halves along [`ADR-0105`](ADR-0105-one-duel-at-a-time-and-the-refusal-hands-back-the-duel.md) §2's own line, *running means `PLAYING`*. **§1: the address names the screen the player chose, and never the room** — `ADR-0076` §§1–2 applied, no `Screen` member for the table, and the address is **not** expected to name a duel. **§2: while the held duel runs, an ask for another screen moves nothing** — the table stays, the fragment is restored to `/`, and refusing and restoring are **one act**, because an address left standing would name a screen the player is not shown; the refusal is silent, and no string is minted. **§3: a `WAITING`, `FINISHED` or `ABANDONED` room honors the ask** — the chosen screen shows, the address names it, and the seat, the tab's memory and the socket are untouched, so `/` is the room's screen as it now stands. **§4 keeps `ADR-0076` §3's frame rule verbatim** — a frame that seats a duel overrules any chosen screen — and **§5 makes the account reachable by the same rule**, resolving a derived collision in [`ADR-0086`](ADR-0086-the-offers-answer-is-one-key-owned-beside-the-predicate-it-feeds.md) §6's favour: the result screen's accept lands on the account screen it names, and a mailed `verify`/`reset` link refused mid-duel must not spend its token. **§6 binds the implementing story to `EPIC-13`'s DoD reproduction attempt**, naming the undriven paths: the result screen, a runout, a genuinely dropped socket, real latency, the `AccountOffer` anchor, and a mailed link over a held room. Costs named: mid-duel confinement is total, silent and unbounded until item 4's clock lands; `ADR-0076` §3's *always* gains a qualification held only in prose; the look-away hides the invite link and an arriving rematch offer; and a waiting host mid-scroll can be yanked by their rival's arrival. Qualifies `ADR-0076` §3 by exactly one case; registers **`DEC-123`** — the architect's — the mechanism (branch order, room-state read, restore promptness, token survival). No wire change, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` move, no new string, no new control |
| [0113](ADR-0113-the-turn-clock-is-derived-state-and-the-sweep-plays-the-seat.md) | The turn clock is derived state, the wire carries what is left, and the sweep plays the seat | Accepted — resolves `DEC-120` for **the architect**: the mechanism under [`ADR-0108`](ADR-0108-expiry-plays-the-seat-and-the-timebank-replaces-the-grace-window.md), which is implemented and not reopened. **§1: one new `ServerMessage`, `TurnClock`** — `seat`, `handNumber`, `actionSequence`, `turnRemainingMillis`, `bankRemainingMillis` (both seats, indexed by seat) — because every existing frame is disqualified structurally: `Snapshot` and `Events` carry engine types and a clock may not enter `poker-engine`, `YourTurn` reaches one seat and the countdown is visible to both, and `OpponentPresence` is about the rival alone. One frame per room write-back that changes the live decision point, to both seats, last in the batch; a resuming seat gets one in the same critical section that already builds its presence frames. **§2: the wire states what is left, never an instant** — `ADR-0028` §2 re-applied unamended (the two sides share no epoch; `ServerClock` is monotonic from an arbitrary origin), and no `ClientMessage` ever gains a time field. **§3: the clock is derived, never armed** — `Room` gains `turnDeadline: TurnDeadline?` (`seat`, the decision point, `bankBeginsAt`, `expiresAt`, both absolute) and `timebankRemainingMillis`, loses `gracePeriods`, and one pure `Room.clocked(runner, now, turnMillis)` at the two write-backs that move a decision point debits the bank, restarts the deadline and nulls it; `RoomTimeouts` swaps `disconnectGraceMillis` for `turnMillis` and `timebankMillis`. **§4: an expiry is a synthesised act down the ordinary path** — `foldAbsent`'s single-seat body is extracted as `giveUpDecision` and reused verbatim, so `ADR-0023`'s conduct and `ActedForAbsent`'s mark to both seats hold by construction; the timed-out seat is not put in the absent set, so an expiry costs one decision; a timeout **while away** latches `ABSENT`, one **while connected** latches nothing. **§5: `ADR-0025`'s ticker keeps two steps** — `expireGracePeriods()` is replaced by `expireTurnClocks()` (`GraceExpiry` → `TurnClockExpiry`), two passes become one through `RoomRegistry.act`, so a duel that ends on a timeout reaches the coin by the same single settle path. **The race is closed by the room's mutex and the engine's sequence guard, with nothing to cancel**: the act wins and the clock restarts, or the sweep wins and the stale `Act` comes back `Rejected` — either way the decision closes exactly once, and the act path gains no deadline check, so a late act inside the sweep's resolution is honoured. **§6: the client anchors on arrival and paints on apply** — which **amends `ADR-0102` §6** by refusing the queue exemption it promised (a countdown for an unseen decision over a running-out table is the contradiction that ADR exists to prevent); FIFO stands, the source is monotonic and injected at `boot.ts`, every tick recomputes rather than decrements, drift is bounded by one trip and never corrected, and zero is not an event. **§7 dismantles the grace machinery row by row** — `isPaused` and the `DUEL_PAUSED` enum entry deleted, `graceRemainingMillis` gone from the frame, `presenceOf` losing its `now`, `absentSeats`/`foldAbsent`/`secondsRemaining` kept verbatim, `presence-countdown.ts` renamed `countdown.ts`. **§8: replay is untouched** — the engine sees only `PlayerAction`s, determinism is over actions not over how they were chosen, nothing time-derived enters `MatchLog`, and `ServerClock` is monotonic and injected. **§9**: a `PROTOCOL_VERSION` step under `ADR-0047`'s lock with **neither number nor fingerprint named here**, an `atomic:` ticket sized by `ADR-0070`'s probe with **no file list published**, `docs/test-plan.md`'s `CORE-23` named as the one artifact no gate will catch, and one seam: card, then the atomic wire+server+store ticket, then the table draws. **§10 lists what a test must prove**, including the race with a count and two dwells for the anchor. Costs named: a paced runout can spend up to 2.4 s of the next decision's 30 s (the screen is honest about it; the time is not recoverable), one more frame per decision, a two-element list used as a tuple, a stated 30 s that is a floor plus one sweep period, and `DEC-108`'s subject leaving the screen unanswered. Amends `ADR-0028` §2 and `ADR-0025`'s step 2 by one clause each; registers no new `DEC` |
| [0114](ADR-0114-one-predicate-answers-every-ask-and-a-mailed-screen-waits.md) | One predicate answers every ask, and a mailed screen waits for the first frame | Accepted — resolves `DEC-123` for **the architect**: the mechanism behind [`ADR-0112`](ADR-0112-only-a-running-duel-refuses-another-screen.md), whose §§1–5 it serves and reopens nothing in. **§1: one pure module, `web-client/src/routing/room-standing.ts`**, reads the room off the frames — `outcome` → `finished`, `view` → `running`, `roomCode` → `waiting`, and `unknown` before any frame has arrived — and rules on the ask: `honour`, `refuse`, `hold`. Every term is a fact the server sent (`DuelFinished`, `Snapshot`, `RoomJoined`, `Failure`); **`running` is `view !== null && outcome === null`**, `ADR-0105` §2's *running means `PLAYING`* read off frames, grace window and runout inside it, and [`ADR-0110`](ADR-0110-creating-a-duel-seats-the-host-at-the-table.md) leaves it untouched because `waiting` is a standing, not a screen. **§2 re-fixes the branch order**: the six chosen-screen branches move **above** the three store branches and test the ruling's `shown`, never the address's `screen` — so a chosen screen *cannot* render without consulting the rule, which is the enforcement [`ADR-0076`](ADR-0076-a-screen-the-player-chose-has-an-address.md)'s own Consequences said did not exist, and `ADR-0076` §3's frame rule needs no separate machinery: **one predicate, re-evaluated every render, and an arriving frame and a player's ask differ only in which input changed**. **§3: the restore is a `useLayoutEffect`, not a `useEffect`** — that is the whole of the promptness half, because *one act, not two* means no paint separates refusing from restoring, and the effect phase is the only thing in a React tree that guarantees it; no number is asserted, and the recorded *within 2 s* was a driver poll interval, not a latency. **§4: `replaceState` via `leave()`, never `pushState` and never `history.back()`** — `back()` would leave the client entirely from an address typed into a fresh tab. **§5: a screen that spends a secret on arrival waits for the first frame** — `VerifyScreen` submits its token in a mount effect, so at boot a tab holding a room would spend it hundreds of ms before the frames that refuse it; `roomAwaited` (computed once in `bootDuelClient` from `?room=` or the tab's memory, carried on `DuelProvider` like `signedIn`) makes `unknown` distinguishable from `none`, and `hold` renders the room's screen while **writing nothing to the address**, so the token stays unspent and unread. The other five screens render at once, which is also what makes [`ADR-0086`](ADR-0086-the-offers-answer-is-one-key-owned-beside-the-predicate-it-feeds.md) §6's accept land. **§6 names what it does not close**: a resume into a `PLAYING` room applies `RoomJoined` and `Snapshot` in two renders, so one render reads `waiting` — a residual the client cannot close without the server naming the room's state in a frame, which `ADR-0112` §7 forbids on its account; if the owed drive observes it, that is a new `DEC` and not a guess made here. It also records that nothing above rests on `ADR-0112`'s *derived, not driven* claim about the `AccountOffer` anchor. **§7 fixes the proofs** under `ADR-0100` §5 — pressed doors, `location.hash` assignment and a boot with a room in memory, no `data-testid` and no test-only prop — including a **call count** on `verifyEmail` (zero before the frames, exactly one from a finished room). Costs named: a third state of knowledge that leaks into the ruling as `hold`; a boot-time boolean threaded through three files; a look-away unmounts the room's screen, losing `RematchControl`'s local *it begins* mark; a refused ask leaves a dead history entry, so *Back* does nothing once per refusal; and `useLayoutEffect`'s guarantee is invisible to any DOM assertion. No router is built — the day `DEC-120`'s clock lands, honouring mid-duel navigation is the deletion of one line of `rulingOn` |
| [0115](ADR-0115-motion-never-carries-a-fact-and-reduced-motion-stills-every-surface.md) | Motion never carries a fact, and reduced motion stills every surface | Accepted — resolves `DEC-124` for **the product owner**: **no fact lives only in motion, and `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is honoured wholesale and automatically** — every fact a surface states (whose turn, what was bet, where the pot went, how long remains) is stated by its **still form**, the same surface at rest, and when the player's system asks, continuous and decorative motion does not run: the pulse holds as a steady mark, the chip flight is skipped and the chips appear where they land — nothing hidden, nothing added, no second design, **no in-product toggle**. The rule stills *how* the screen changes, never *that* it changes: `ADR-0102`'s 600 ms steps, the clock's once-a-second change and `ADR-0109`'s mark replacement are facts arriving and stand unchanged — **amends nothing**. Governed globally, never per surface: a duration, delay, easing or travel distance is born `--pd-motion-*` in `design/tokens/tokens.css` beside the sheet's **one** reduced-motion block (`ADR-0024` §2 reaching the value-kind the product acquires today; the block's exact CSS is minting, worked with the human per `ADR-0091` §3), and a card that gives a surface motion draws it **at rest as a named state**, so the pane verdict covers both forms (`ADR-0024` §3, `EPIC-13`'s every-state rule). Derived from *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"* — the sentence `ADR-0075` already used to rule an animation furniture — and *"free of everything that makes online poker feel like a casino"*, with `ADR-0103` (the phone) as the ownership precedent for a browser-stated fact of the player's context, and the sheet already bending to two such signals (rem type, forced-colors outlines). **Deliberately motion-sized**: no stance on contrast, screen readers, keyboard or any standard — a named accessibility commitment would change what the product *is*, the human's table, and nobody has asked. Costs named: every moving surface is designed twice, motion may never be the sole signal even where cheapest, and the how/that line lives in drawings and judgment, not a grep. `STORY-1303` owes *waiting* / *acting — moving* / *acting — at rest*; `STORY-1306`'s flight is garnish over amounts the pot figure and stacks state still. Registers no new `DEC` |
| [0116](ADR-0116-the-accept-is-a-door-and-a-door-that-does-not-open-spends-nothing.md) | The accept is a door, and a door that does not open spends nothing | Accepted — resolves `DEC-125` for **the product owner**: **pressing *"Keep them with a password"* means one thing — put me on the account screen where a password can be set — and the offer is spent where the player is *delivered*, never by a press that delivers nothing.** A player never loses the offer without reaching that screen; where the product does not put them there the offer stands and is made again after their next win, exactly as an offer nobody answered. This **narrows `ADR-0085` §2 on one clause only** — *"Taking the offer spends it"* is read as *taking is reaching the screen, not pressing toward it* — leaving *"Not now"* spending at the press (a dismissal has nowhere to arrive), the storage, the key, §3's table and every refusal byte-unchanged; a second tab counts as reaching it, so `ADR-0086` §Consequences' modified-click case is untouched. An answered offer is not shown again on the screen behind it. **Nothing is given back**: no un-dismiss, no *"sorry — here it is again"*, no migration and no key rename, because a rename cannot tell a player this defect answered for from one who pressed *Not now*, and the affected population is empty — nothing is hosted (`EPIC-07` *not written*) and every browser that pressed it is a QA profile. The word is chosen: what today's press does is **not a spend** (a spend hands something over) and **not a forfeit** (`ADR-0046` §5) — the offer is *taken and nothing is handed over*. Satisfied by construction because the offer is only ever shown where the ask is honoured (a `FINISHED` room, `ADR-0112` §3), which is stated as a standing condition on any future surface. Derived from *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"* and *"One duel coin per win… a counter of duels won"*, applying `ADR-0036`'s *offer, not a gate* and its claim path *as the prompt's destination*. **Hands `ADR-0114` §6 its open question**: the drive happened, so the repair writes a **regression test**, non-browser, red at `1a09fb46`. Costs named: the accept-and-abandon player is still never re-offered, pre-repair browsers keep a bit they got nothing for with no correction possible, §2 has no enforcement of its own, and this ADR fixes nothing by itself — `STORY-1311` lands the behaviour. Registers no new `DEC`. **Superseded in full by 0125** two days later: the control it rules on is deleted with the offer, on the human's instruction |
| [0117](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md) | The proofs of record load the built bundle, on an origin of its own | Accepted — resolves `DEC-087` for **the architect**, and **supersedes [`ADR-0088`](ADR-0088-the-two-browser-proof-is-a-written-hand-check.md) §2 step 3 alone**, through that ADR's §5 route. **`vite preview` serves `dist/` on `http://localhost:4173`** — no dependency added (`ADR-0026`'s Vite already ships it), `preview: { port: 4173, strictPort: true }` and **no `preview.proxy`**, so Vite's `preview.proxy ?? server.proxy` leaves one proxy table `dev-proxy.test.ts` guards. **The mode is proved off the artifact, never off the port**: the dev server injects `/@vite/client` into its HTML and the built bundle never names it, so the marker *is* the mechanism that broke `P3`. `scripts/qa/stack.sh` gains `build-web` (foreground) and `web-origin`, and **`wait-web [built\|dev]` defaults to `built`** and fails on a marker mismatch — the gate goes where every caller already is. **`npm run dev` stays for development and loses only the proofs of record**: the `ADR-0088` §2 hand-check is `built` always, every round started after this merges is `built` by default, a `dev` round says so and may not be offered toward [`ADR-0093`](ADR-0093-ready-for-real-users-is-said-of-the-shipped-artifact.md) §1a, and every record names its artifact beside its network layout. §6 extends [`ADR-0089`](ADR-0089-a-browser-drives-this-client-for-a-qa-round-never-for-a-gate.md) §4 by one clause — **a `dev` finding that turns on load latency is re-read on `built` before it is filed**, because 102 modules and 3 are not the same measurement under added delay — and creates no gate: §2b stands, `build.yml` keeps its two jobs, no PR waits on a drive. `TASK-131006`/`P3` re-drives; `P4` finishes on the mode it started; `P1`/`P2`/`P5` do not re-drive for this ADR, though `P2`'s and `P5`'s `delayed` findings fall under §6. Costs named: **a stale `dist/` is undetectable** — the marker proves the mode, not the freshness — the origin move leaves every literal `5173` outside production code wrong and arms `DEFAULT_BASE_URL` as a trap, and the production build drops `StrictMode`'s double-invoke so rounds may disagree with their own history for no defect and no repair. Storage partitions move and it is **explicitly acceptable**: a fresh Chrome profile per round is already mandatory (`ADR-0089` §3), so no round ever carried one. Registers `DEC-126` — what serves the bundle in the **offered deployment**, deliberately not pre-decided here |
| [0118](ADR-0118-a-recovering-browser-shows-nothing-it-was-not-told.md) | A recovering browser shows nothing it was not told, and a late screen is not a false one | Accepted — resolves `DEC-127` for **the product owner**, and it is **two answers, not one**, because the recovery sequence `STORY-1310` drove three times is **two windows of different kinds**. **Before any frame** (`ADR-0114` §5's `unknown`) the client paints the front door, which the server stated nothing to support — **wrong-because-false**, and now `/` renders **no element**: the dark shell, empty, no wordmark, no doors, no profile strip, no spinner, **no new string**. What turned this from a flicker into a defect was read out of `DuelSocket.kt`: `replyToCreateRoom` performs **no seat check** (`DEC-110`, open, is that guard), so *Create a duel room* pressed inside the window is honoured, repoints the connection's membership and makes `boot.ts` overwrite the remembered code — the flashed lobby is **armed**, and `docs/vision.md`'s *"We play a full heads-up match… We hit Rematch."* is the sentence it offends. **After `RoomJoined`, before the frame that says what the room is doing**, the stale *Waiting for your rival* is **acceptable-because-late** and is said out loud rather than left to fall out: it is a state the server **did** state (`ADR-0102` §5's standard, met), it is a gap between two frames of one delivery, and the client **cannot** do better — `replyToJoinRoom`'s `ALREADY_SEATED` branch answers a host rejoining a still-`WAITING` room with `RoomJoined` **and nothing after it**, so waiting for a second frame would hang that host forever; closing it exactly is a wire change and the architect's. **No interstitial**, on *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."* plus `ADR-0105` §4's discipline — and the answer **names what it depends on**: if the `built` re-read `ADR-0117` §6 already owes shows `/` empty for **longer than about a second**, the interstitial question is live again and is a new ADR. **Qualifies `ADR-0114` §2 by one condition** — the fall-through is reached on `none`, not on `unknown` — and touches nothing else: no wire, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, no `Screen` member, no card (`ADR-0091` §2: nothing is drawn). **Costs named**: a recovering browser is a **black rectangle**, permanently so against an unreachable server (named, deliberately unregistered, `ADR-0105` §6's route); and the accepted stale waiting screen carries *Back to the lobby*, which forgets the room and hands a running seat to `ADR-0113`'s sweep |
| [0119](ADR-0119-the-name-is-asked-at-the-first-press-and-skipping-plays.md) | The name is asked at the player's own first press, the suggestion promises nothing, and skipping plays | Accepted — resolves `DEC-129` for **the product owner**, in three parts marked separately. **That a player is asked at all is the human's, recorded verbatim** (*"First time you play dialog "choose your name" appears with sugestion(before duel)"*) and not chosen here. **§1: the ask stands at the player's own first press** of *Create a duel room* or *Join the duel*, in this browser, holding no display name — a screen in place of the front door, after which the press goes through — and **never** on the invite path (measured: `main.tsx:160` and `store/boot.ts` auto-join on `Welcome`, so the vision's *"Send a link. She opens it in a browser. We play"* has no press to hang an ask off), never before a rematch, never on a resume, never over the table, and never to a player whose name was **removed** (`ADR-0052` §1 keeps that on the name surface and nowhere else). It is **not a modal** — `DEC-138` owns whether this product gets one. **§2: two ways out and neither can dead-end** — take a name through the shipped `PUT /api/me/name`, or **skip and play**; every refusal `set-name.ts` can settle leaves the skip working, so a name that cannot be set is never a reason a duel cannot start. **§3:** the ask says what a name is for, says it is permanent using the shipped `PERMANENCE_LINE` **for as long as `ADR-0029` §4 stands** (`DEC-131` takes the sentence with it), and reuses `refusalSentence`. **§4: the suggestion is an offer to type and promises nothing** — the field's initial value, never a reservation, never a claim of availability (`ADR-0029` §5 is applied, not reopened), refused at one press's cost, encoding nothing about the player (`ADR-0029` §6, `ADR-0067`). **§5: an answer spends the ask**, `ADR-0085` §§1–2's shape borrowed verbatim for a second surface — skipping is *not again in this browser*, being rendered spends nothing, the bit never travels to the server. **Supersedes and amends nothing**: `ADR-0036`, `ADR-0058` and `ADR-0063` §2 stay true *because* the skip is real, which is the load-bearing half. Registers **`DEC-142`** (the architect's) for the generator. **§3's permanence obligation is superseded by 0130** — `DEC-131` said yes, so `PERMANENCE_LINE` leaves the product and §3 obligation 2 is replaced by 0130 §5's two sentences, exactly as §3 said it would be; §§1, 2, 4, 5 stand |
| [0120](ADR-0120-a-showdown-shows-the-hands-the-rules-showed-and-the-beat-that-shows-them-stands.md) | A showdown shows the hands the rules showed, and the beat that shows them stands | Accepted — resolves `DEC-133` for **the product owner**: a showdown shows **exactly the hands the rules showed** — the winner's, both on a split, none on a fold — and **nothing is disclosed after the hand settles**. [`ADR-0008`](ADR-0008-loser-mucks-at-showdown.md) stands whole; the post-hand disclosure it names as the only sanctioned widening is **not** written, so **you always see the hand that beat you and never see what you beat**, and the wire does not move. The epic's premise is **corrected**: the shown hand is not *"drawn by nothing"* — it reaches the table through the **snapshot** (`Addressed.kt:52,62` → `PlayerView.of`'s `revealed` → `DuelTable.tsx:76-78` → `Hand.tsx`), pinned by the merged `duel-secrecy.test.tsx` — so what is missing is not the drawing but **the time to read it**: today a called-down showdown is **one 600 ms step** (`ADR-0102` §§2, 4) before the queued next hand lands, on a rival hand `ADR-0103` §3.2 draws at 24–40 px. **The last beat of a hand that turned a hand face up the viewer had not seen stands 2,000 ms**; every other ending keeps 600 ms; the hold ends on the next hand's frames arriving, so `ADR-0095` §4's *never on a timer, never on a fade* is untouched. Drawn in the seat's own two card places — **no panel, no overlay, no new string, no *mucked* line** — and read from the snapshot, never from `HandRevealed`, because `ADR-0102` §5 sends a reconnecting client no events at all. Costs named: the **winner learns nothing**, a held player spends 2 s of a 30 s allowance because a queued turn anchors at arrival (`ADR-0113` §6), the two screens step ~1.4 s apart, and a second duration number exists where `ADR-0102` §4 pinned one. Decides nothing about the winning five (`DEC-134`), the pot's travel, or the rival hand's width (`DEC-136`). §5 names the reversal trigger and the **all-in-and-called** widening it would take first. Registers **`DEC-146`**, the architect's — how one beat gets a length different from a step |
| [0121](ADR-0121-the-table-reflows-never-scales-and-the-shape-is-measured-on-the-device.md) | The table reflows, never scales, and the judged shape is measured on the device | Accepted — resolves `DEC-136` for **the product owner**, on the first photograph of this product on a phone. **The table keeps reflowing**: no transform, `zoom` or whole-column font multiplier ever stands between the viewport and the table, and [`ADR-0103`](ADR-0103-the-table-fits-the-phone-and-the-cards-give-before-the-numbers.md) §§2–5 stand **byte-unchanged**. A uniform scale **is** still one table — it passes all three of `ADR-0096` §4's second-surface tests — so the choice was the product owner's; it is refused on **`R3`** (*at or above the body type size*) with `ADR-0106` §1's rule that the **property governs the instrument**, a transform passing `getComputedStyle` and failing the eye, and on `ADR-0103` §3's give order, which spends whitespace, then card backs, then the hero's hand, then the board, and **never the type**. **The fit is measured at the smallest viewport the browser presents** — iOS Safari with the bars **fully expanded** — as the smaller of `documentElement.clientHeight` and `visualViewport.height`; a fit there is a fit at every larger height, so the client owes **no listener and no refit**. The contract becomes **two numbers, one per axis**: `scrollHeight ≤ clientHeight` **and** `scrollWidth ≤ clientWidth`, `ADR-0106`'s sub-pixel reading and one-pixel fence applying per axis. That **adds no criterion** — `R2`'s *"at once, without scrolling"* names no axis and `R3` already says *not clipped*, so the photographed plates are `R3` `not met` today and [`ADR-0099`](ADR-0099-the-rubric-is-the-adr-section-and-a-criterion-is-born-merged.md) §2's path is not entered. **The judged shape stays 390 × 664** with `ADR-0096` §4's provenance, but it is an **assumption about a device**: a reading taken on a real phone that is smaller **on either axis replaces it**, for the round's shape, the headless override and `duel-table.html`'s `.viewport.phone` box — and it **never moves up**, because §4's own text says a taller value *"would let `R2` pass for a player who does not have it"*. Raising it, or relaxing `R3` to permit the scale, is **the human's**. The first reading is a written hand-check ([`ADR-0088`](ADR-0088-the-two-browser-proof-is-a-written-hand-check.md)); the cause is **not** diagnosed here, and if the give list runs out at the measured shape, `ADR-0103` §3's stop rule fires. Costs named: the number can move and take the card and the give order with it, a hand-check enters the evidence chain for the most-checked criterion and goes stale silently, §1's one-`eval` contract is spent, and the table's ceiling comes closer for the showdown work in the same epic |
| [0122](ADR-0122-call-names-the-price-and-raise-to-names-the-total.md) | `Call` names the price, and `Raise to` still names the total | Accepted — resolves `DEC-137`; amends `ADR-0109` §2's `Call` clause |
| [0123](ADR-0123-a-standing-rematch-offer-follows-the-rival.md) | A standing rematch offer follows the rival, and it never takes the screen | Accepted — resolves `DEC-138` for **the product owner**.
