The ***whether*** is the human's, verbatim (*"it shoud appear on any screen"*); the ***what*** is derived from the success condition *"We hit Rematch."* and *Positioning*'s *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."* A standing offer **from the rival** follows a player onto every screen they can be on while they still hold the room — every `Screen` but `first`, no carve-out — and a player who **left** the room (`forgetRoom()`) is followed nowhere. It is a **panel above the screen, never a modal**: nothing beneath it is scrimmed or disabled, it takes no focus, no keypress the player was already making can answer it, and it never retires itself on a timer. It shows only where the room's own screen is not, so one fact never has two live surfaces; it mints **no new string** (*Your rival offers a rematch*, `Rematch`, *Not now*, the dealing sentence, *That duel room is gone.*); it stands until accepted, dismissed, or refused with `UNKNOWN_ROOM`. **Dismissal hides the surface, never the offer** — nothing is sent, the rival is told nothing, `ADR-0044`'s *silence is a decline* stands, and nothing is stored. The accept ends at the table by `ADR-0112` §4 and `ADR-0114` §3, already shipped. Pays `ADR-0112`'s named cost (*"a finished player on the ladder does not see a rematch offer arrive"*) and amends nothing: no wire, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, no server file, no schema, no stored key. The card is **minting** under `ADR-0091` §3. Registers `DEC-144` — the architect's — the mechanism. Unblocks `STORY-1415` |
| [0124](ADR-0124-one-waiting-room-and-play-again-hands-it-back.md) | A player holds one waiting room, and pressing play again hands it back | Accepted — resolves `DEC-139` **on the human's stated direction of 2026-09-06** (*"i go back to lobby and press play again. in this case i shoud be back to the same room"* — the report, not the specification, and this ADR does not choose it) **and `DEC-111` from `docs/vision.md`** — *"Two people, **one link**, one heads-up poker match"* with *"Not a multi-table poker room"* and *"A counter of duels won."* **§1: a player holds at most one waiting room** — while they hold a `WAITING` seat, a `CreateRoom` from them opens **no second room**; nothing is refused and nothing is destroyed, they are handed back the room they already hold, same code, same seat, room left exactly as found. **§2: the browser goes on forgetting, and the server is what returns them** — `ADR-0072` §3 stands byte-unchanged, no second key, no flag, no client clock, **no client change at all**, because a remembered code is what boot rejoins on and keeping it restores `ADR-0073`'s room with no door; because `ADR-0002` makes the seat the server's fact; and because it works on a device that has never heard of the room, which `ADR-0105`'s Context named and nothing answered. **§3: nothing is said, and no string is added** — `ADR-0110` §6's enumeration is untouched, on *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."*, with the reopening condition stated as an observation (a host who sends the same code believing it fresh). **§4**: the surface is `WaitingTable.tsx` unchanged, and `ADR-0073` §3's three clauses are checked one by one against the room returned to — the third is now true twice over; the ten minutes still runs from `Room.open` and a return does **not** restart it. **§5** tables all six ways a player can ask for a room. **§6 writes no repair**: the room is found from the **player** and never from the connection, nothing moves, and **no new frame is needed** — `RoomJoined(code, seat)` already means this — so no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` step is spent on this half. Amends **`ADR-0105` §2's `WAITING` row only** (*"allowed, exactly as today"* — a `WAITING` seat is still not refused, but a second `CreateRoom` no longer opens a second room); §§1, 3–7 and the other two rows stand. Extends `DEC-110` to the `WAITING` case, which now also blocks `STORY-1416` (**`DEC-110` answered 2026-09-07 by [0133](ADR-0133-the-room-a-player-holds-is-scanned-for.md)** — a lock-free scan finds the room from the player, the `WAITING` hand-back moves no wire, and §6's prediction holds for this half; `STORY-1416` is unblocked); registers `DEC-145` (may a holder of a waiting room take a seat in another by invite). Costs named: **a host cannot get a fresh code for up to ten minutes** and the product has no way to close a room; a press that appears to do nothing; two live states answering the same press differently; `replyToCreateRoom` grows a read; and `ADR-0105` §2's table stops being a complete statement on its own (**`DEC-145` answered 2026-09-08** by [0141](ADR-0141-taking-a-seat-elsewhere-releases-the-room-you-were-holding.md) — the seat elsewhere is taken and the held room is **released**, so §5's third row, *"not decided here"*, is filled in and §7's *last route into two duels at once* is closed to the width of one registry operation) |
| [0125](ADR-0125-the-account-screen-names-the-anonymous-profile-and-owns-the-door.md) | The account screen names the anonymous profile, and it is the only door to a password | Accepted — resolves `DEC-130` for **the product owner**, in two parts marked separately. **The retirement is the human's, recorded verbatim**: on `edits4.png` the **entire** account-offer block is struck through with one word, ***remove***, and the message reads *"When you go to profile it says: 'Annonymus Account'"*. **§1: the post-win offer is retired, whole** — the surface, its strings, `offerAccount`'s three terms, `DuelResult`'s `offer` prop, the key `pd.accountOfferSettled`, its module and its row in `one-module-owns-each-storage-key.test.ts`, which go in the same diff or the row reddens naming a deleted file. **§2: nothing replaces it** — no banner, no badge, no reminder, on any screen; the standing door is the `Account` control the front door already renders unconditionally, so a win is two presses from the password form and §2 refuses to shorten it. **§3: the account screen names the profile *anonymous*** and says three things — what the profile is, that it lives in this browser, and that a password keeps **every coin and every duel** because nothing moves (`ADR-0030` §1, `EPIC-14` item 1e's *sentence on a screen*) — with the words the card's, inside three refusals: **not a tier** (no badge, no *real* account, no rank), it states rather than urges, and the heading stays `Account`. **§4: the screen asserts the state only when it has been told** — measured, `GET /api/me` carries no credential field and `signedIn` supports only the sound direction, breaking on a `201` sign-up whose follow-up sign-in failed and on a player signed out here; where it has not been told it says nothing and still offers the form. **§5: `ProfileStrip`'s `no-profile` branch renders nothing** — `No profile yet.` leaves the product, the `profile` branch untouched. **§6: *anonymous* is a state, never a name** — `ADR-0058`'s `No name` and `ADR-0029` §6 stand, and a player may be named *and* anonymous after `ADR-0119`. **Supersedes** `ADR-0036` §Decision's offer block and three §Consequences bullets (its *never required* first paragraph stands, re-applied), and `ADR-0085`, `ADR-0086` and `ADR-0116` **in full** — the first supersession in this repository. Costs named rather than argued away: the product adopts the *"optional forever, with no prompt"* shape `ADR-0036` §Alternatives rejected, and some players will lose coins a prompt would have saved. **Answers `DEC-089` by deletion**; registers **`DEC-147`**, the architect's |
| [0126](ADR-0126-the-table-shows-the-cards-and-marks-none-of-them.md) | The table shows the cards and marks none of them | Accepted — resolves `DEC-134` for **the product owner**: **no**. Every card the table draws is drawn like every other card of its kind — at a showdown, at a fold, at every street, in either seat, on the board and for **both** winners of a split, **the five that made the winning hand are drawn exactly as the two that did not**. A mark is anything that separates one drawn card from another by *what it did* rather than *what it is* — ring, border, glow, tint, shadow, lift, scale, opacity, reordering, badge, connector, keyable class, `data-*`, `title`, or any `aria-label` beyond `card-text.ts`'s rank and suit — and **dimming the cards that did not play is the same statement and the same refusal**. This is [`ADR-0095`](ADR-0095-the-table-states-who-took-the-pot-and-never-names-a-hand.md) §3 **applied to a statement made without words**, not reopened: a highlight prints no string, so `HAND_TALK` could never see it, but the fact is the same kind — a reading of seven public cards, performed by the product, at the table, live — and the distance from *these five played* to *two pair, kings and jacks* is one string. Licensed by [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md)'s *What it is* — *"a match can be replayed and **analysed afterwards**"* — read as `ADR-0095` read it: **the explaining surface is afterwards**, dated **v0.4** by the *Roadmap* while v0.1 is *"one complete duel, rematch"*. The human's annotation (*"winning combination higlited"*) is the report, not the ruling — `ADR-0120`'s reading of the same drawing, the same day. **The winning five stays inside `poker-engine`**: no wire field, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` step, no server file, `no-derivation.test.tsx` byte-unchanged, and the client never computes it (`ADR-0002`). `BestHand.cards` keeps its value and **loses its stated reason** — the KDoc's *"exists because the client highlights the winning five at showdown"* is a commitment the product declines, this ADR is the correction of record, and the one-line repair is **not `EPIC-14`'s** (*"Nothing here opens `poker-engine`"*). `STORY-1412` loses the wire move and becomes **one gate** — *the drawing of a card does not depend on whether it played* — plus one line on `STORY-1411`'s showdown card; **`STORY-1413` no longer waits on it**. Costs named: the player who cannot read seven cards is charged a **second** time (`ADR-0095` took the name, this takes the mark, `ADR-0120` gave 2 s to work it out unaided); something the human asked for in writing is declined; a deferred wire field is a dearer one; and a refusal needs a gate written to hold a nothing. §6 keeps `ADR-0095` §6's trigger — the first duels played by people who are not the author — and requires the reversal to settle **the mark and the name together**, one field and one turn of `ADR-0047`'s lock. **Registers nothing**, and `DEC-148` is not opened: a *no* has no wire question |
| [0127](ADR-0127-a-control-stands-only-for-a-decision-the-server-has-opened.md) | A control stands only for a decision the server has opened | Accepted — resolves `DEC-135` for **the product owner**: **no** — when it is not the player's turn the action bar does **not** stand as disabled controls; it keeps its box, keeps both rows reserved, and holds one sentence. **§1 is the rule the whole ADR is**: a control — pressable or not — is drawn **only** while this client holds a `LegalActions` for a decision the server opened for its own seat and has not closed. Measured: `PendingTurn.legalActions` is written by `case "YourTurn"` alone (`duel-state.ts:282-292`) and cleared by the next `Snapshot`/`DuelFinished`, so outside a turn the client holds **no** legal actions and every verb or figure on a faint bar would be invented or stale — the client *"adding actions it thinks legal"*, which `ActionBar`'s own KDoc forbids and `bar-no-derivation.test.tsx` gates. Heads-up the coming decision is `{CHECK, BET}` or `{FOLD, CALL, RAISE}` and the client cannot know which, so verbs-without-amounts is the **bigger** claim, not the smaller one. **§3: faint controls keep their one shipped meaning** — *Sent — waiting on the server*, the card's third state — so *not your turn* may not borrow the treatment even if it could be made honest. **§2** pins the `off` state as a still form under `ADR-0115` §1 and names what may never say *wait*: animated dots, spinner, pulse, shimmer, progress bar, fading skeleton. **§4 answers `DEC-108` on both halves** — *wait* and *you may act* may **never** be sayable at one moment, and when they conflict **the notice gives way, not the bar**, which is the route `ADR-0113` §7 and `TASK-130911` already took by deleting the pause; `DUEL_PAUSED` is verified absent from every `.kt`/`.ts`/`.tsx` and from `ProtocolError`. **§5** names the one edge it does not close: the sweep race, where a late press may still be `Rejected` — a boundary no screen can be ahead of, not a standing offer the product has decided to refuse. **The human's annotation is recorded verbatim and not followed** (*"show disabled controllers"*, `edits3.png`, 2026-09-06); `EPIC-14` routed it as a `DEC`, and **§7 makes the overrule two sentences** — which of the two action shapes a faint bar draws, and what stands where an amount goes. Costs named: the human is told no; **`STORY-1406` has no work in it** and is retired rather than split; the sentence stays the third statement of a fact the plate already makes twice (`seat-status.ts:24`, the accent border, the countdown); and a player still cannot see what they will be able to do. Forecloses a bar that previews the next decision, a second meaning for the faint treatment, and a skeleton idiom in the bar. Amends nothing, moves no wire, registers no `DEC` |
| [0128](ADR-0128-the-copy-control-is-never-absent-and-a-press-that-cannot-copy-hands-over-the-selection.md) | The copy control is never absent, and a press that cannot copy hands over the selection | Accepted — resolves `DEC-140` for **the product owner**; amends [`ADR-0110`](ADR-0110-creating-a-duel-seats-the-host-at-the-table.md) in two clauses and nowhere else — §5's *"the control absent where `navigator.clipboard` is"* is struck, and §8.1's fourth host-alone variant goes with the state it drew. **`Copy the link` renders in every browser**, under one name, and a press has two visible outcomes: `Link copied.` where a copy happened, or — where the browser exposes no Clipboard API **and** where a write is refused, now one outcome rather than two — the invite-link box takes focus with the whole link selected and the shipped `Copy it from the box above.` renders. **`Link copied.` is said only when a copy actually happened**, never of a selection and never of an attempt whose success cannot be read. **No string is added**: §6's ten-string enumeration stands byte-unchanged as a set and its escape clause is unspent; what changes is that three of the ten become reachable outside a secure context. The card owes **three** host-alone variants, not four, and `design/screens/duel-table.html`'s `Host alone — no clipboard API` frame is retired. Licensed by [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md)'s *"**Send a link.** She opens it in a browser"* with *"Everything else is downstream of that moment"*, and by *"the author wanted to play quick heads-up duels against his sister"* — which happens on a LAN over plain `http`, where `navigator.clipboard` is undefined. Costs named: the button sometimes does not copy and says so; the fallback is one gesture, not zero; `Copy it from the box above.` acquires a second meaning and keeps a spatial claim; and two shipped absence assertions invert. `DEC-126` is untouched in both directions — TLS is not a prerequisite for the invite. Registers **`DEC-150`** (the architect's) for whether a copy is achievable at all without the Clipboard API, which blocks nothing |
| [0129](ADR-0129-the-host-is-told-the-duel-starts-by-itself.md) | The host waiting alone is told the duel starts by itself, and the arrival stays silent | Accepted — resolves `DEC-141` for **the product owner**, derived from `docs/vision.md`'s first success condition (*"Send a link. She opens it in a browser. We play a full heads-up match."*), with the human's annotation *"dual will start automatically when rival joins"* as the source and not the specification. **Amends `ADR-0110` §6 by exactly one string**, through §6's own named route, and **upholds §7 in full** — the arrival stays silent. **§1: the host-alone state renders one sentence**, once. **§2: it must convey two facts** — the rival's arrival is the trigger, and nothing is required of the host — tested by whether a first-time host can answer *"is there anything I still have to do?"* with *no* from that string alone; a line that only restates the waiting fails, because `Waiting for your rival` already says that and saying it is what produced the question. **§3: six refusals** — no duration, countdown or *forever*; nothing about the code; no game fact; no control named, present or absent; nothing about the rival's experience; no instruction. **§4: the product's voice, and the glyphs are the card's** — *rival* and *duel*, never *opponent*, with the exact string fixed by `STORY-1404`'s card and the two string-set tests taking their literal from it. **§5: the set gains one member and stays closed**, its rule unweakened. Costs named: the zero-new-strings property is spent and this ADR is the precedent the next request will cite; it is the first string here that explains rather than states; the promise is coupled to `Room.join` by nothing but this document |
| [0130](ADR-0130-a-name-can-be-changed-and-the-name-it-leaves-is-spent.md) | A name can be changed, and the name it leaves behind is spent | Accepted — resolves `DEC-131` for **the product owner**, in two halves from two places. ***That* a name may be changed is the human's, recorded verbatim** (*"player name can be changes at any time in accounts settings"*), reversing their own `DEC-017` answer; this ADR does not choose it. ***What becomes of the string* is derived** from *"**A leaderboard.** Ranked results over a season."* read as `ADR-0067` §1 already read it — a row is **text and leads nowhere**, so the name is the whole of a row's identity, and history is a **live join** (`ADR-0039`), so a reissued string would print a second player onto the first's finished duels. **§1: a change is a `set`** under every rule a first name is set under — canonicalisation, character rules, the fold, `ADR-0038`'s blocklist — with **no quota, no cooling-off, no confirmation**, and it **never leaves the player nameless** (`ADR-0051` §3's takedown stays the only route to `null`). **§2: the name given up is retired — not released, not held** — spent forever for everyone **including the player who gave it up**, one rule for both ways a name leaves a player; `ADR-0051` §1's *no string ever leaves this table* is what it rests on and is not amended. **§3: history shows the name as it *is*** — the ladder, every finished duel and the strip print the current string, **no row is rewritten because no row holds a name**, the old name appears nowhere, and nothing marks a row that changed; measured, the blast radius is **three surfaces** and a rename is **invisible at the table** (no `ServerMessage` carries a name; the opponent is *Your rival*). **§4: nobody is told**, and a player who renamed themselves is **never** shown `ADR-0052` §1's removal notice. **§5: `PERMANENCE_LINE` leaves the product** and is replaced by two obligations — the name can be changed later, and the one given up is gone for good — said **before** the send. **§6: one form, on the account screen**, doing both acts; `NameSurface` leaves the front door and `ADR-0052`'s notice travels with it. **Supersedes** `ADR-0029` §4's *`name → a different name` raises*, its title's *permanent* and §5's `403`; `ADR-0051` §3's *no exception at all*; `ADR-0119` §3's obligation 2. Costs named: **a rename is one-way**, the namespace burns per `PUT` rather than per profile, a rival can never find you again, and other people's records change under them. Registers **`DEC-151`**, the architect's, for the mechanism |
| [0131](ADR-0131-signing-out-of-your-own-account-hands-the-browser-a-new-profile.md) | Signing out of your own account hands the browser a new profile, and leaves the old one whole | Accepted — resolves `DEC-132` for **the product owner**, the last of `EPIC-14`'s original eleven. **Signing out ends the browser's standing in the account it signed into**: if that account is the profile this browser owns (a promotion, `ADR-0030` §1), the browser is handed a **new, empty anonymous profile**; if it is somebody else's account, the browser returns to the profile it owns, exactly as it left it; a browser that never had a profile stays a first-time visitor. **Both halves are the human's and neither is chosen here** — the abandoning half verbatim on 2026-09-06 (*"if logout you comeback to new annonumus profile"*, the fourth clause of an item whose every other clause is about this browser's own profile and its promotion), the keeping half verbatim on 2026-08-14 (*"i would like to keep anonimous accout if user log out"*, `ADR-0030` §2). What the ADR chooses is **which sentence governs which case**, stated as a reading, with the one sentence that would correct it in §*What this does not settle*. **§2 is the invariant it rests on: a browser is handed a new profile only when the profile it leaves holds a credential** — to be in that case you signed in to it — so `EPIC-14`'s *"a new way to lose coins"* framing does not hold and **no route in this product abandons a profile a player cannot get back to**. **§3: nothing is revoked, deleted, moved or destroyed** — `ADR-0049` §2's `UPDATE` never runs on this path, its trigger never fires, §3 is not entered, the binding left behind stays **live**, and no coin moves in either direction; `ADR-0050`'s *"Revocation is final forever; signing out is the cheapest act in the product"* is why. **That is the whole answer to what `ADR-0049`'s finality means for a browser about to be somebody else: nothing, because a sign-out is not a revocation.** **§4 licenses one abandonment, at one confirmed press, and nothing else** — `ADR-0027` §5's stale-client harm stays forbidden. **§5: the player is told before it happens, on the confirmation `SignOutControl` already renders** — no new surface, two obligations (this browser becomes a new empty profile; the profile left keeps its coins and duels and is reached by signing in), three refusals from `ADR-0125` §3 (states rather than urges, not a warning about loss, words are the card's), and the shipped `SIGN_OUT_WARNING` second sentence is **false the day the behaviour lands**. **§6: the name ask is not asked again to a browser that skipped it** — `ADR-0119` §5's bit is a browser fact and a new profile is not an answer, so the rule is weighed and left unamended, with the `No name` outcome named as a cost. **Supersedes `ADR-0030` §3's outcome clause and its *keeps its device id* bullet, and §8's *not on sign-out* and *clears the token and only the token* — for that one case only**, standing verbatim everywhere else; supersedes nothing in `ADR-0012`, `ADR-0027`, `ADR-0037`, `ADR-0049` or `ADR-0050`. Costs named rather than argued away: a player watches their coin count go to **zero** with one sentence of explanation; **a forgotten password now loses the account in one press that doing nothing used to undo**, a weaker form of the risk `ADR-0037` refused to force on everyone; empty profiles accumulate with no deletion (`ADR-0039`); and two sign-outs behave differently under one label. Registers **`DEC-152`**, the architect's, fenced by §7 |
| [0132](ADR-0132-the-profile-says-whether-it-holds-a-password.md) | The profile says whether it holds a password, and the client derives nothing | Accepted — resolves `DEC-147` for **the architect**: the mechanism [`ADR-0125`](ADR-0125-the-account-screen-names-the-anonymous-profile-and-owns-the-door.md) §4 registered and deliberately chose none of. **§1: `ProfileResponse` gains a seventh field, `hasPassword: Boolean`** — true exactly when a `credential` row exists for this player with `kind = CredentialKind.PASSWORD`; no default value, declared last. Named for the **kind** rather than the concept, because `ADR-0027` §1 keeps `credential` open to a passphrase or a third-party subject, so a `hasCredential` would one day read `true` for a player whose only stated way out is the password form; it freezes nothing about `DEC-027`. **§2: computed on every response that carries the DTO, never a literal** — a fourth correlated `EXISTS` in `PostgresProfileReads.PROFILE_OF_SQL` **and in both statements in `PostgresProfileWrites`**, so `PUT /api/me/name`'s `200` cannot make `reportNameWrite` turn a claimed player anonymous; the kind is **bound** from `CredentialKind.PASSWORD`, never spelled as SQL. No new port, and `ProfileRoutes` gains no `Credentials`. **§3: told only to the player it is about** — no other DTO carries it, no route answers it for a player the caller did not resolve to, nothing filters or sorts on it, and it reaches no log line, no `ServerMessage` and no engine type. **§4: a missing field is `unavailable`, never `false`** — `profileFromBody` requires the boolean, which is what keeps `ADR-0125` §4's *not told at all* case reachable; the screen renders §3's block on `!hasPassword` and consults `signedIn` for it never. **§5: nothing shipped is re-keyed** — `showPasswordRoute`, `showSignUp`, `showSignInDoor` and `showAttach` keep their predicates, per `ADR-0125` §4's own words. **§6: the provider re-reads `GET /api/me` after an outcome the server has confirmed changed the profile, and today that is exactly one** — `signUp`'s `signed-up`, because sign-in and sign-out already `reload` and `sign-up.ts` passes `noReload` on purpose. **§7: HTTP, not the socket** — `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move, no ledger row, no probe, and `STORY-1408`'s ticket is not `atomic:` on that account. **Contradicts nothing in [`ADR-0050`](ADR-0050-revoking-the-device-signs-out-everywhere-but-here.md) §4**, whose *"no `ProfileResponse` field"* inventories its own diff: `hasRecoveryEmail` landed eight days later under `ADR-0031` §6.3, and the screen has read two fields since. Costs named rather than argued away: a fourth `EXISTS` on the hottest authenticated read, one extra round trip per sign-up, and **§3 is a reviewed rule with no gate behind it**. Names a defect for the planner — `PostgresProfileWrites` passes a literal `false` for `hasRecoveryEmail`, and the name write's adoption prints `RECOVERY_OFF` to a player whose recovery is on |
| [0133](ADR-0133-the-room-a-player-holds-is-scanned-for.md) | The room a player holds is scanned for, and only the `PLAYING` refusal spends a frame | Accepted — resolves `DEC-110` for **the architect**, both states in one lookup, as [`ADR-0105`](ADR-0105-one-duel-at-a-time-and-the-refusal-hands-back-the-duel.md) §6 and [`ADR-0124`](ADR-0124-one-waiting-room-and-play-again-hands-it-back.md) §6 asked. **§1: `RoomRegistry.heldRoom(player)`** — one non-suspending query returning the whole `Room`, where **held** means `seatOf` non-null **and** state `WAITING` or `PLAYING`; `FINISHED`/`ABANDONED` are excluded by the method, not by its callers, because a caller that forgot would break `ADR-0105` §2's finished row and `offerRematch`. The answer is **total and independent of iteration order** — `PLAYING` first, then oldest `lastActivityAt`, then lowest code — because a server answer that depends on a hash map's order is neither testable nor explicable. **§2: it is a scan, not an index, and it takes no lock**: it reads each `Holder.room` through the same `@Volatile` field `get(code)` and `reap`'s unlocked pass already read, so it cannot block or be blocked; the rooms are the truth about where a player sits (`ADR-0002`) and a scan cannot disagree with them, where an index becomes a second source of truth whose leak — the failure `recording`'s own KDoc documents — is a player who can never open a room again. **§3: `heldOrOpen(host, format)`** is `heldRoom ?: create`, under a **per-player striped `Mutex`**, `SeatOwnership`'s shape in a second array; without it find-then-create is check-then-act across `ADR-0018`'s asynchronous eviction, and with it `ADR-0124` §1's *one waiting room* is **true rather than usually true**, since `create` is a `WAITING` seat's only source. **§4 answers the deadlock question by construction**: the scan takes nothing, `heldOrOpen` takes one lock across **no suspension point**, the ordering rule is *a player stripe is always outermost* — and it is structurally enforced today, because `mutate`'s blocks are not `suspend` and `Mutex.withLock` is, so nothing inside a room's critical section can take a lock at all. **§5** fixes two things an implementer gets wrong: the seat is `seatOf` off the returned snapshot, **never the literal `0`** (true of a fresh and a returned `WAITING` room, false of a `PLAYING` one), and `RoomMembership.code` is repointed at the room the answer names — which is what disarms `ADR-0118`'s window at the source it named. **§6**: the `JoinRoom` guard fires only when the held room is `PLAYING` **and its code differs from the code asked for**, or every reconnect breaks. **§7 disagrees with nothing `ADR-0124` predicted and spends a version anyway**: the `WAITING` hand-back is `RoomJoined` alone and moves no wire, but the `PLAYING` refusal takes one new `ProtocolError` value, `ALREADY_IN_DUEL`, and `ProtocolError` is a generated declaration, so `ADR-0047` §2's fingerprint moves and `PROTOCOL_VERSION` with it; no existing value can carry it without asserting something false about the room that was asked for, which `ADR-0022`'s no-oracle rule forbids. **§8**: `RoomJoined`, then `resume`'s frames, then the `Failure` **last**, on `ADR-0044` §5's precedent — and `resume`'s `reconnect().touch()` is checked clause by clause against `ADR-0105` §6's *nothing moves* and passes. **§9 splits it**: `STORY-1416` builds the lookup, moves no wire and is **not** `atomic:`, and must leave the `PLAYING` case falling through to `create` exactly as today; `ADR-0105` §1's own ticket is `atomic:` under `ADR-0047`/`ADR-0068` and sized by `ADR-0070`'s probe. Registers nothing; names two questions unregistered on `ADR-0105` §6's rule. Costs named: a linear scan on the product's primary press with nothing measuring it; a second lock class in a class where the rule getting broken hangs the server; `create` stays reachable behind a KDoc note rather than a fence; the answer can name a room the reaper takes a moment later, and no lock closes that; the `PLAYING` half's version step and `ADR-0047` lock; a found-and-ignored branch in the merged tree between the two tickets; and one more `ProtocolError` whose absence from the client's reducer is silent |
| [0134](ADR-0134-a-rename-spends-before-it-replaces.md) | A rename spends before it replaces, and no name is ever released | Accepted — resolves `DEC-151` for **the architect**: the mechanism `ADR-0130` registered and designed none of. **§1: the permanence trigger becomes the never-released trigger.** `player_display_name_is_permanent()` is dropped and re-created as `player_display_name_is_never_released()` with `ADR-0051` §3's body **minus one disjunct** — a name may leave a player only if the registry has already spent it, which stops being an exception to permanence and becomes the whole rule. `NULL → name`, the identical-name no-op and `name → NULL` are unchanged, so `retire_display_name` stays the only route to nameless and is **not edited**. The names change because a schema that says *permanent* to a `psql` operator is `PERMANENCE_LINE` with a smaller audience. **§2: four statements in one transaction** — `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` (the profile is locked before any registry row, the order the takedown already takes, and the interlock that replaces `AND display_name IS NULL`), the `TAKEN` insert, the retirement of the string being left, then the column. `SetNameResult.AlreadyNamed` is **deleted**; `ADR-0051` §2's rollback now protects two strings; statement 3 carries **no** `AND reason = 'TAKEN'`, so an impossible row raises rather than passing in silence. **§3: the vacated row moves `TAKEN → REPLACED`, a fourth `reason`, with `retired_from` populated** — spent for everyone under the fold, which ignores `reason`, while recording *which* of the two ways the name left its holder; the word is not `RELEASED` because that is the answer `ADR-0130` §2 refused. **§4: `ADR-0052` §6's bit reads false for a renamer for two independent reasons** and `PostgresProfileReads`/`PostgresProfileWrites` change not at all. **§5: `PUT /api/me/name` answers `200 \| 400 \| 401 \| 409 \| 429`** — `403` has no cause and leaves, and a case-only self-rename is `409`. **§6: the write is budgeted, and the budget meters spending rather than trying** — `AttemptBudget` keyed by `PlayerId`, `ADR-0074` §2's reserve-then-refund, a refusal refunded because `ADR-0051` §2 says it burns nothing, numbers chosen to be unreachable by a human so it stays a rate limit and not the quota `ADR-0130` §1 refuses. **§7: nothing crosses the socket** — `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move and `STORY-1409`'s tickets are not `atomic:`. Costs named: a player can **never** change the case of their own name; the write holds a `player` row lock across a registry insert; one statement became four; an idempotent retry spends budget; a fourth `reason` is a word every future query must keep true |
| [0135](ADR-0135-the-server-says-which-sign-out-this-is-and-the-browser-forgets-one-key.md) | The server says which sign-out this is, and the browser forgets one key | Accepted — resolves `DEC-152` for **the architect**, the mechanism `ADR-0131` §7 fenced with six constraints and three unchosen shapes. **The server answers one boolean; the browser forgets one key; the new profile comes from the handshake that already ships.** **Nothing crosses the socket** — `Hello`, `Welcome`, `ProtocolCodec`, `protocol.gen.ts` and `PROTOCOL_VERSION` are byte-unchanged, `ADR-0047`'s ledger gains no row, and `STORY-1410`'s implementing ticket is **not** `atomic:` under `ADR-0068` §3 — and no schema, migration or write moves either. **§1: `player.device_id` does not exist** — `V7__device_binding.sql` dropped the column and the constraint (`ADR-0049` §1, §7), so ownership is a `device_binding` row that stays **live** and is never written on this path. **§2: the browser stops owning a profile by forgetting `pd.deviceId`**, through a new `forgetDeviceId` in `device-id.ts` — the one module that may hold that literal — called from `signOut` and nowhere else, in the abandoning case alone, before `reload()` and whatever the server answered. **Exactly three keys move**; the `ADR-0119` §5 skipped bit and every later key are kept (`ADR-0131` §6), and `ADR-0030` §8 still governs every other moment. **§3: the new profile is minted by `Identity.Anonymous` on the next `Hello`** — `ADR-0027`'s third path, already shipped — so it is created at that handshake and **not at the press**: a browser that signs out and closes the tab writes no rows at all, which makes `ADR-0131`'s accumulation cost smaller than that ADR estimated. **§4: the case is `GET /api/me/device`**, session-required on `ADR-0049` §5's reasoning, answering `DeviceStandingResponse(signOutHandsANewProfile)` — `true` **exactly when the `X-Device-Id` presented on that same request does not resolve, through a live binding, to a player other than the caller**. The comparison is one new `IdentityResolver.namesPlayer`; **`resolve` and all five `Identity` cases are byte-unchanged**, so `ADR-0027` §4's precedence is untouched and the socket gains no query. No `409` guard is needed: a session is issued only by sign-in, so **`ADR-0131` §2's invariant is a property of the route's identity rule** rather than a rule to remember. **§5: `true` is unreachable while any live binding names another player**, so the one outcome §2 forbids cannot be expressed — which is why the two more natural framings were refused, and why the revoked-binding browser (already handed a fresh profile today, silently) now reads `true` and is told the truth. **§6: everything unknown is a keep** — an absent field, a `401`, an `unavailable` read or a client too old to ask all read `false`, and the read **must not go through `readFromApi`**, whose no-device short circuit answers *unknown* for exactly the browser whose answer is `true`. **§7: `signedIn` decides whether a sign-out exists, never which one it is**; `signOut` takes the boolean as a **required** field; the words stay the card's and this ADR mints no string. Costs named rather than argued away: **the abandonment is not durable** — a restored `localStorage` keeps the route, as `ADR-0131` §3 already said — and **no undo, grace window or *sign back in as you were* path is added**, so the forgotten-password cost is exactly as sharp as `ADR-0131` left it; a second request on the account screen; a new public route for one bit; a fact read on one screen and spent at a later press; a third route resolving identity without the shared helper; and `STORY-1408` serialised behind or ahead of `STORY-1410`. Registers no decision |
| [0136](ADR-0136-a-beat-declares-its-own-length-and-zero-silences-every-beat.md) | A beat declares its own length, and zero silences every beat | Accepted — resolves `DEC-146` for **the architect**: the mechanism [`ADR-0120`](ADR-0120-a-showdown-shows-the-hands-the-rules-showed-and-the-beat-that-shows-them-stands.md) §3 registered rather than assumed, when it spent `ADR-0102` §4's licence on **one beat** instead of on the step. **§1: a beat carries its *kind*, not its length** — `RevealStep` gains `hold: "step" \| "read"`, set in `layOutReveal` from `ADR-0120` §3's own predicate (`view.seats.some(seat => seat.index !== view.viewerSeat && seat.holeCards.length > 0)`), read off `PlayerView.viewerSeat` rather than `state.mySeat` so the classification is a pure function of **one frame** and a queued hand-ending `Snapshot` classifies itself on drain; `layOutReveal`'s signature does not change and a fold answers `"step"` with no special case. **§2: the store owns both milliseconds** — `DuelStoreOptions.readMillis`, **absent means `stepMillis`**, mapped in `armTick` off the beat standing at the head of the queue, with `REVEAL_READ_MS = 2000` named beside `REVEAL_STEP_MS = 600` at `boot.ts`'s seam, so `ADR-0102` §4's *named once, at the boot seam* is **extended, not amended** and `advanceReveal` is byte-unchanged. **§3: `stepMillis === 0` silences every beat**, gated in the store rather than in boot because that covers all 113 call sites — so `drive-duel.tsx` is **not edited**, is deliberately not given a redundant `readMillis: 0`, and `ADR-0100` §3's four recorded-frame suites are neither edited nor re-recorded. **§4: no component learns the hold** — the widened step is assignable to `DuelTable`'s narrower prop (checked under `tsc --strict`), so the DOM is byte-identical at 600 ms and 2,000 ms and `no-derivation.test.tsx` stays byte-unchanged. **§5: nothing crosses the socket** — the predicate reads two fields that already ship and are already filtered by `PlayerView.of`'s `showCards`, no declaration changes, `ADR-0047` §2's fingerprint cannot move, and **`STORY-1411`'s implementing ticket is not `atomic:`**; the worst this mechanism can do wrong is stand a beat for the wrong length. **§6: reduced motion cannot reach it** — the hold has **no CSS**, mints no `--pd-motion-*` token and lives in an injected `schedule`, so `ADR-0115` §3 holds by construction and the design card draws a still state. §7 names what a test must prove — the same frame answering differently for the two seats, and the **`delayMillis` arguments** rather than a call count. Costs named: a second dial at the boot seam, `stepMillis: 0` overloaded, a hold with no browser-visible proof, and the deciding hand's result screen 2 s later (§8, named on `ADR-0105` §6's route rather than registered). **Registers no decision** |
| [0137](ADR-0137-a-name-suggestion-is-drawn-in-the-browser.md) | A name suggestion is drawn in the browser, and the vocabulary's size is what makes a refusal rare | Accepted — resolves `DEC-142` for **the architect**: the mechanism [`ADR-0119`](ADR-0119-the-name-is-asked-at-the-first-press-and-skipping-plays.md) §4 registered when it fixed what a suggestion *is* and chose no generator. **§1: client-side, and nothing crosses the wire** — no socket frame, no HTTP route, no `docs/protocol.md` edit, no Kotlin file and no migration, so `ADR-0047` §2's fingerprint cannot move and **`STORY-1407`'s tickets are not `atomic:`**; a suggestion consequently **cannot fail to arrive**, which is what removes the loading state and the empty field `ADR-0119` rejected. **§2: `suggestName(random, replacing?)` is a pure function of a random source** — it takes no profile, device id, session, room code or clock, so `ADR-0029` §6 and `ADR-0067` hold **by the signature**; `random` returns `[0, 1)` and defaults to `Math.random` at the call site (`reconnecting.ts`'s `jitter` idiom), the index is clamped so a source returning `1` cannot join `undefined` into a permanent name, `replacing` redraws at most four times, and the draw is **deliberately not cryptographic** — `RandomRoomCodeSource` needs `SecureRandom` because a room code is an invite, and the vocabulary ships in the bundle anyway. **§3: the vocabulary is one import-free data module** (`web-client/src/profile/name-vocabulary.ts`), an ordered array of word lists joined by a single `U+0020`, **bundled and synchronous** — no asset, no dynamic import, no fetch — with the **arity as data**, so two lists or four is a curator's edit and no ADR. **§4: three tests are the whole guarantee** — the product of the list lengths `>= SUGGESTION_SPACE_FLOOR = 1_000_000` (rarity bought with size, not knowledge: about `k / N` refusals, and `k` grows on renames too by `ADR-0130` §2), every entry NFC and free of `Cc`, `Cf` and **all** whitespace, and the longest-entry sum plus separators `<= 32` code points — so a suggestion can draw a `409` and **can never draw a `400`**. **§5: one draw per mount, stored nowhere**, and a reroll fires on `conflict` alone and only into a field still holding, unedited, the string the generator put there; it is free because `ADR-0134` §6 meters *spending* and refunds a `409`, which is what makes `ADR-0119` §4's *one press* true. **§6: `name_registry` is never consulted** — no query, no endpoint, no filter, no hint; `ADR-0029` §5 and `ADR-0051` §9 are applied, not reopened. Costs named: hundreds of words must be authored before `STORY-1407` merges, the list is public and farmable, the floor decays with nothing watching it, and the tests hold shape but never meaning. **Registers no decision**; ships no word |
| [0138](ADR-0138-the-panel-mounts-beside-the-lobby-and-the-dismissal-lives-in-the-mount.md) | The panel mounts beside the lobby, and the dismissal lives in the mount | Accepted — resolves `DEC-144` for **the architect** |
| [0139](ADR-0139-the-read-beat-is-spent-once-and-the-store-already-remembers.md) | The read beat is spent once per hand, and the store already remembers | Accepted — resolves `DEC-153` for **the architect**, and **amends [`ADR-0136`](ADR-0136-a-beat-declares-its-own-length-and-zero-silences-every-beat.md) §1 in three sentences and in those three only**. [`ADR-0120`](ADR-0120-a-showdown-shows-the-hands-the-rules-showed-and-the-beat-that-shows-them-stands.md) §3's rule is *"a hand face up the viewer **has not been shown before**"*; the sentence restating it *"as the client can evaluate it"* **drops the word `before`**, `ADR-0136` §1 shipped the restatement while calling it *"`ADR-0120` §3's sentence and not a paraphrase of it"*, and that claim is **withdrawn** here. **§1: the rule is the sentence with *before* in it** — the restatement is a sufficient test *under an invariant*, not the rule, and where the two disagree the rule governs; no number, string or card moves and `ADR-0120` §3 is applied rather than reopened. **§2: `layOutReveal` gains a third parameter `held: PlayerView \| null` and the `Snapshot` case passes `state.view`**, which on that path is still the *previous* view; `TASK-141104`'s inline expression is lifted out unchanged as `rivalCardsShown(view)`, and the final step is `"read"` only when the rival's cards are **newly** shown — `held.handNumber === view.handNumber && rivalCardsShown(held)` is what *before* means. **§3: nothing new is remembered** — `DuelState` gains no field, `state.view` is written only by the `Snapshot` case and cleared by nothing, and a `COMPLETE` view reaches it only by having been **painted** (a frame arriving while a reveal stands is queued, not applied), so *"no previous view is remembered"* is superseded **as a statement about the argument list and about nothing else**. **§4: the guard lives in the reducer**, which is the only placement `advanceReveal`'s queued-drain path reaches — `ADR-0136` §1's second reason is not merely preserved, it is the argument for where this goes; `advanceReveal` is byte-unchanged. **§5 closes both ways it could be wrong by merged facts**: `HandRevealed` fires only in `reachShowdownAndSettle`, which settles in the same engine step, so rival cards cannot appear before `COMPLETE`; and the hand comparison is what keeps the **next** hand a read. That first bullet is named as this decision's one load-bearing engine fact — `ADR-0120` §5's all-in-and-called widening would need `held.street === "COMPLETE"`, deliberately not added now. **§7: *before* means *in this store's lifetime*** — a reload forgets, accepted as a limit rather than registered, because no wire field records what a browser *painted* and that field is the engine change three documents refuse. **§8 reconciles `ADR-0102` §5**, whose *"that is 600 ms"* `ADR-0136` moved without noticing while claiming §5 *"stands exactly as written"*; §5's **decision** — one rule, no special case for a resume — is what stands. **§9 writes the server invariant down as a fact and deliberately registers no test for it**: after §2 nothing depends on it, and a ticket deferred outside `EPIC-14` is a ticket nothing fails without. Measured, and the reason this was a question rather than a repair: **0** hand-completing `Snapshot`s in **2,220** resume frames over 60 duels, against **602** from the same probe asked what a runner would hand back if `act` did not call `advance`. Costs named: `layOutReveal` is no longer a pure function of one frame; a one-day-old ADR is amended by the story implementing it; **the change is unfalsifiable in the product**, since a symptom no path reaches is one no path disproves; and the repository now carries a client guard against a server behaviour that does not occur. **Registers no decision**; unblocks `TASK-141108`, which stands exactly as written |
| [0140](ADR-0140-the-clipboard-api-is-the-only-copy-this-client-attempts.md) | The Clipboard API is the only copy this client attempts, because it is the only one that reports its result | Accepted — resolves `DEC-150` for **the architect**, and **supersedes and amends nothing**: [`ADR-0128`](ADR-0128-the-copy-control-is-never-absent-and-a-press-that-cannot-copy-hands-over-the-selection.md) §7 delegated rather than decided — *"This ADR neither requires nor forbids such an attempt"* — and its conditional, *"If one exists whose success the client can actually read, `Link copied.` becomes reachable in those browsers under §4"*, is closed by finding its **antecedent false**. **The answer is none**: `navigator.clipboard.writeText` stays the only clipboard mechanism this client uses, and where the API is undefined the press performs `ADR-0128` §3's hand-over — focus the box, select the whole link, render `Copy it from the box above.` — and attempts nothing else. **The reason is the missing read-back, not the deprecation**: `document.execCommand("copy")`'s boolean reports that the command was supported and enabled for that invocation, not that the system clipboard holds the link, and the one in-page read-back (`navigator.clipboard.readText`) lives on the very `[SecureContext]` interface that is absent by hypothesis — so a running client cannot tell a truthful `true` from an untruthful one, and `ADR-0128` §4 is unmeetable by any available mechanism. **The silent-copy compromise is refused on §3, not §4**: it claims nothing, but §3's promise is *"one press leaves the link either in your clipboard or selected under your cursor, and the screen says which"*, and a silent success prints `Copy it from the box above.` on exactly the devices where the mechanism fires; making the screen honest again needs a third outcome, which needs a string, which is [`ADR-0110`](ADR-0110-creating-a-duel-seats-the-host-at-the-table.md) §6's *"a new ADR, not an invented sentence"* and **the product owner's**. **Measured this run**: under the pinned jsdom 24.1.3 `document.execCommand` is **absent** (`"execCommand" in document` is `false`), as are `queryCommandSupported` and `queryCommandEnabled` — so a test of an `execCommand` mechanism could only fake a function jsdom does not have and assert the code called it — and [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md) §1's `http://localhost:4173` is a secure origin where the branch is unreachable. §6 names the two changes that would have to precede any evidence: the bundle served to the drive on a **non-secure** origin, and a **read-back verb** in `scripts/qa/drive.mjs` that pastes rather than reads. **Changes no file and owes the planner no ticket** — `web-client/src/table/InvitePanel.tsx` already behaves this way, and three shipped assertions already pin the negative. Costs named: a copy that would probably have worked on Chrome or Firefox over LAN is not attempted, on an argument about what the client can know rather than on a measurement; `ADR-0128` §3's *"one gesture, not zero"* stops being provisional and `Link copied.` is unreachable outside a secure context until `DEC-126`; the rule shows up in the client as an absence no grep finds; and reversal now costs an instrument before it costs code. **Registers no decision** — the adjacent product question is named, not registered; `DEC-126` is untouched in both directions |
| [0141](ADR-0141-taking-a-seat-elsewhere-releases-the-room-you-were-holding.md) | Taking a seat elsewhere releases the room you were holding | Accepted — resolves `DEC-145` **from `docs/vision.md`, in two halves from two sentences**. **The seat is taken** on the first success condition — *"Send a link. She opens it in a browser. We play a full heads-up match."* with *"Everything else is downstream of that moment."* — the sentence [0094](ADR-0094-opening-the-invite-is-taking-the-seat.md) §1 already read to ship this path, so the invite gains **no second qualification** and a refusal is refused. **The held room is released** on *"One duel coin per win… A counter of duels won."* with *"Two people, **one link**, one heads-up poker match"* and *"Not a multi-table poker room."* — the three sentences [0124](ADR-0124-one-waiting-room-and-play-again-hands-it-back.md) derived `DEC-111` from. **§2:** the released code answers `UNKNOWN_ROOM`, *"indistinguishable from a room that never existed"* (`ADR-0022` §3), and `Lobby.tsx` prints the string it already prints for a reaped room — **a released room is indistinguishable from a reaped one, so no new state, string, frame or `PROTOCOL_VERSION` step exists anywhere**. **§3:** only a room still `WAITING` and holding nobody else is released — a duel already running in it is left alone. **§4:** a join that seats nobody changes nothing, so the release follows *sitting down*, never *trying to*. **§5, the larger half:** nothing here releases a room whose holder merely left, dropped, closed the tab or pressed create again, and **a duel can still begin into a seat that is already away** — only the case where the holder *provably cannot* come back is touched. **§6:** nothing is said, on *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."* and because the only surface available is a dealt table with a decision due (`ADR-0105` §4). **§7** tables every route; **§8** registers **`DEC-154`**, the architect's, for the release's call site and lock order, since `mutate`'s non-suspending block forbids taking a second room's mutex inside it. **Supersedes two sentences of `ADR-0073`** — the header's *"one way to end, an idle timeout, and no other"* and §3's restatement of it — as statements about a `WAITING` room in general, and **amends `ADR-0105` §2's `WAITING` row, `JoinRoom` half only**. `ADR-0022`'s Decision is untouched. Costs named: **a rival sent a live code can meet a dead one, and it is the product that killed it**; a shipped on-screen line can be made false by its reader's own next act; the first act in the product that ends a room ahead of its clock; **two duels at once is narrowed to a window, not closed**; the player is not told; `replyToJoinRoom` grows a write |
| [0142](ADR-0142-a-text-module-is-checked-against-the-rendered-card.md) | A text module is checked against the rendered card, in the client's own suite | Accepted — resolves `DEC-155` for **the architect**, and **amends nothing**: it is [`ADR-0091`](ADR-0091-design-gets-no-agent-a-new-screen-owes-a-card.md) §4's own sentence — *"Adoption is gated where consumption happens — the client's own CI job"*, and *"`check-drift.sh` does not reach into `web-client/`"* — carried out on a second fact. The gate is a vitest file, `web-client/src/design/card-text.test.ts`, reading each card with `readFileSync` off `import.meta.url` exactly as `styles/tokens.test.ts` already reads `design/tokens/tokens.css`; **no workflow line and no dependency**, because `vitest run` globs it — which is also what keeps `ADR-0088` §1's *"`.github/workflows/build.yml` keeps its two jobs"* true. That placement is evidence-led, not stylistic: **`design/check-frame-cards.sh` runs in no workflow at all** — three ticket `verify:` blocks and zero CI — so *a gate only a ticket runs* has already happened once here. §2 reads the card's **rendered text**, comments stripped **first** so a marker cannot satisfy the gate, splitting on a **NUL** sentinel and not a newline (measured: a newline separator also splits the card's own line wraps and made `account.html`'s three `<p class="line">` sentences read as absent from the card that carries them). §3 therefore **deletes** `account.html`'s three `<!-- ANON-BLOCK: … -->` markers rather than extending them to every card — they are a copy of the rendered sentence that nothing checks (`TASK-140810`'s gate counts three and never compares one to the `<p>` beside it), and whitespace normalisation dissolves the wrapping problem they were invented for. §4 pairs a **module to a card**, not to a frame: `name-ask.html`'s four frames carry different subsets of the six strings (the fourth carries two), so frame scope needs a per-string frame register. §5 makes each pair declare `carded` and `notCarded` export names and checks the partition **both ways** — an unclassified new export fails, and a register entry naming a deleted export fails — then matches each carded string's **imported value**, never a `grep` of source, because `SIGN_OUT_WARNING` and `SIGN_UP_THROTTLED` are hand-concatenated and their values occur **0** times in their own file as a literal. §6 globs `*-text.ts` so a new module cannot escape the register. §7 states the direction honestly: names both ways — which `HttpEndpointDocumentationTest` gives **no** DTO, `theDocumentedFieldNamesAllExist` checking documented ⊆ reflected and `theSignUpSectionNamesEveryFieldTheRequestHas` the converse for a different DTO — values one way only, and **card ⇒ module left unenforced** because a card is mostly headings, notes and placeholder data, with the trigger to close it named. Costs named: **~26 bootstrap register lines** a reviewer must actually read (15 `notCarded` for `account-text.ts`, which has 27 exports and 12 on its card; 11 `NO_CARD` modules), one classification per future export forever, **no carded sentence may span an inline element**, and a green gate over a card nobody has looked at means only that two copies agree. Forecloses marker comments as a card convention — the one genuinely closed door, and additive to reopen. It would have been **green** at `TASK-140710`'s merge: all six strings matched then and match now. **Amended by [0148](ADR-0148-a-module-names-every-card-that-draws-it-and-every-card-is-registered.md)**: §4's *"A module names exactly one card"* is replaced by one row per (module, card) pair, §5 gains a defaulted `notCarded` reason and a **checked absence** assertion, and §6 gains a card-side sweep. **§§1, 2, 3 and 7 stand** — including §7's third bullet, which `ADR-0148` §1 re-affirms and re-arms rather than closing |
| [0143](ADR-0143-irreversibility-is-said-last-and-never-coloured.md) | Irreversibility is said last, and never coloured | Accepted — resolves `DEC-156` for **the product owner**, **derived from the vision**: *Positioning*'s *"The reference points are **Lichess** and **Chess.com**, not PokerStars. **Dark, quiet, fast, minimal.**"* **Yes, an irreversible act gets weight — and the weight is words, position and a press, never a treatment.** The premise is corrected by measurement first: the account surface carries **three** irreversible acts, not one — the name, `REVOKE_PERMANENT`'s *"This device will never sign in to this account again. This cannot be undone."*, and `SIGN_OUT_WARNING` — and the product's shipped grammar for the other two is `RevokeControl`'s and `SignOutControl`'s **in-page second press in plain type**. **§1: no danger register** — no accent, tint, border, box, rule, icon, badge, size or weight step, capitalisation or motion marks an act as irreversible, on any screen, and none in the assistive register either (**no `role="alert"`**, no `aria-live` urgency, no hidden *Warning:* prefix). Measured: in this client colour names **what a thing is** — a result, a clock, a turn, your own row, a standing offer, a control — and has **never** named a consequence, all **eight** `role="status"` refusals across seven components being plain; and `tokens.css` reserves the only urgency hue itself — *"Amber exists only for the turn clock running down."* — so a treatment would need a **new** hue, and minting is the human's (`ADR-0091` §3). **§2: three obligations** on a screen of controls — the act says it cannot be undone; that claim is the **last thing read before the control**, with no other sentence between them; and a **second press wherever no merged ADR has refused one**. **§3**: the name form takes the first two and **cannot** take the third, because `ADR-0130` §1 and §7 refuse a confirmation press — **applied, not reopened** — leaving the display name the one irreversible act whose entire guard is a sentence. **§4** tables every irreversible act and finds one unguarded — *give this profile a password* claims a handle nothing gives back and says nothing — **registered as `DEC-157`** rather than answered. **§5**: the card's two name-form frames already conform; it owes **one margin sentence** and no frame changes, and **no gate is bought**. **§7** names what reverses it: **one player asking for a name back**, or the human at the pane. Supersedes nothing; **narrows `ADR-0130` §5's grant of layout to the card by exactly one relation**. Costs named: the skimmer is not helped and the trigger fires **after** somebody loses a name; the least-guarded act carries the largest permanent loss; a refusal with no gate behind it; and a product-wide rule from a base of three acts |
| [0144](ADR-0144-a-negative-route-test-is-a-control-and-the-predicate-resolves-the-device-once.md) | A negative route test is a control, not a prohibition, and the standing predicate resolves the device once | Accepted — resolves `DEC-159` for **the architect**. **The three merged sources were never jointly unsatisfiable**: [`ADR-0049`](ADR-0049-a-device-binding-is-a-row-and-revoking-is-final.md) §5 specifies `DELETE /api/me/device` and **contains no sentence constraining any other method on that path** — the only occurrence of *verb* in it is *"The `auth` family is verbs about a session — sign-up, sign-in, sign-out — and the point of this decision is that revocation is not one"* — so the prohibition was the **test comment's**, which turned *"only DELETE was asked for"*, a statement about an ADR's scope in 2026-08, into *"only DELETE may ever be installed"*, which nothing decided. **§1: the citation is withdrawn and no clause of `ADR-0049` is superseded, amended or narrowed** — a route is forbidden by an ADR saying so and by nothing else. **§2: a negative route assertion is a control for a positive row in the same file and must name a verb no merged ADR has asked for**; it is never a prohibition, and when a later ADR asks for the verb it names, the control **moves** — `theDeviceRouteIsNotInstalledOnAnyOtherVerb` becomes `…OnUnaskedVerbs` and issues `client.put`, keeping both inequalities and `TASK-040611`'s own reason, *"a `get(...)` typed beside the `delete(...)` would otherwise be invisible"*. `PUT` is chosen because `PUT /api/me/name` is live under the same prefix, so the control is **more** discriminating than the `GET` it replaces. **§3: `ADR-0068` does not yield either — it already decides this and says *split***: §4's *"a scope that grew after the ticket was written… it is still a split, still today"* is this case exactly, so `ADR-0135` §8's *not `atomic:`*, `ADR-0068`'s cap and `MAX_FILES_TOUCHED = 3` **agree**, and no linter constant moves. §1's *"A budgeting rule never rewrites a correctness gate"* is met: the control is re-pointed because its **premise** changed, not to fit a budget. **§4: the edit lands before the route**, so every assertion in the intermediate commit is true when it lands — the `GET` row has been stale since `ADR-0135` merged on 2026-09-07, not since `TASK-141003` — and **no replacement positive row is added**, with the argument given rather than the omission left silent. **§5 corrects `ADR-0135` §4, which was wrong and is said to be**: `namesPlayer` answers one boolean, §5's table needs *resolves to nobody* and *resolves to somebody else* to **differ**, and no sign repairs it — `namesPlayer` inverts rows three and four, `!namesPlayer` inverts rows one and two. The function is **deleted** and the predicate comes from the shipped `resolve(token = null, deviceId)`, whose `Identity.Device` / `UnknownDevice` / `Anonymous` map to §5's rows one-and-two, four and three: **no new public surface**, `IdentityResolver` returns to one public suspend function, and the two impossible branches answer `false` because `ADR-0135` §6 makes every unknown a keep. §4's **prose** predicate, §5's table, §6 and §8 all stand. **Supersedes**, by quoted sentence: `ADR-0135` §4's *"It gains one function"* and its `namesPlayer` signature and body; §Consequences' *"`IdentityResolver` grows a second question"* bullet; and `TASK-040611`'s Tests row **in its choice of verb only**. **§6 registers the ticket edits**: new `TASK-141015` (3 files, before `TASK-141003`), and `TASK-141003` stays at **three** files and a plain ticket. Costs named: a ticket that ships no behaviour on the critical path of eleven; four merged unit tests deleted, their properties surviving one level further out; **`TASK-141002` becomes a done ticket whose `verify:` block no longer passes**; one merge with no `GET` assertion and no composition-level one ever; a second `resolve` call per request; and a two-day-old ADR corrected by the story implementing it. **Registers no decision** — the recurrence is named as a **trigger** rather than registered: nothing detects an ADR asking for a verb a merged control denies, and **the second occurrence, on this path or any other, makes the verb list a mechanism decision** |
| [0145](ADR-0145-a-cards-scaffolding-is-composing-and-the-transcriber-names-the-mint.md) | A card's scaffolding is composing; what a coder will transcribe names the mint | Accepted — resolves `DEC-160` for **the architect**. **`ADR-0091` §3 splits on what a card creates for the product, and has never been about what the human has not yet seen** — settled by §3's own *"**Either way** the verdict that matters stays the human's visual one… and it may **trail the merge**"*, which puts the human's sight downstream of **both** branches, so the not-yet-seen reading empties the composing branch and contradicts the paragraph it sits in. **§2: a card holds two kinds of drawing — subject, which a coder transcribes into `web-client/`, and scaffolding (`.wrap`, `.eyebrow`, `.lede`, `.frames`, `.frame`, `.note`, `.stub`, `.screen`), which nothing transcribes. The test is: name the file that will transcribe it.** If a client file will, the ticket mints that drawing; if the answer is *nothing — it exists so the subject reads*, it is scaffolding and **composing at any level of detail**. This is `ADR-0033` §1's consumer test (*"The test is consumers, not units"*) lifted from values to drawings, with §3's *promotion is one shared consumer away* carried over unchanged; the test has a **direction** — a card copying **from** shipped code mints nothing. **§3's mechanical floor, read from the ticket's `## Files` table plus `ls`, on `ADR-0091` §2's floor-plus-judgment pattern**: writing `design/tokens/tokens.css`; creating a file under `design/components/` or `design/graphics/`; or scaffolding that depicts a **named product screen with no card under `design/screens/`** — the clause that actually decides `TASK-141502`, since `leaderboard.html` and `account.html` both exist and the stand-in cannot claim an authority already occupied. **Specificity is explicitly not the axis** — the axis is authority. Measured: `.wrap` in 20 of 21 cards, `1020px` in 19, `.frame` and `.note` in all 12 screen cards, **406 raw `px` outside `:root`**, so scaffolding-is-minting reclassifies all 86 `module: design` tickets. **§4**: `TASK-141502` and `TASK-141501` both stand, nothing owed; the driver's ground is **corrected on the record** — four raw lengths landed, not two, and `ADR-0103` §1 fixes 390 × 664 and **720 × 900**, so `960px` and `420px` have no external source; the `fail` was a finding against the **split**, not the diff, and a `DEC` is its correct destination. **`TASK-141511` is composing**, unchanged. **§5 does not settle what minting costs**: measured, all four `minting`-labelled tickets are `review: standard` and **all four were dispatched**, so §3's *worked interactively with the human* has not been practised once since 2026-08-30 — **registered as `DEC-161`**, not ratified and not repaired. **§6**: `ADR-0091` §2's rule was **never written into `.claude/agents/planner.md`**, the one file §2 names; that file owes §2's trigger and §§2–3's classification. Costs named: the floor misses `.panel`, the very case that produced the story's minting label; licensed scaffolding accretes with **no gate comparing it to the canonical it depicts**; scaffolding reproduces shipped strings and `ADR-0142` §4's pairing is what contains it, not this rule; *nothing will transcribe this* is a prediction; a **third** classification vocabulary; and `DEC-161` blocks nothing |
| [0146](ADR-0146-a-handle-is-held-not-spent.md) | A handle is held, not spent, and signing up owes no irreversibility sentence | Accepted — resolves `DEC-157` for **the product owner**, **derived from the vision**: *Positioning*'s *"The reference points are **Lichess** and **Chess.com**, not PokerStars. **Dark, quiet, fast, minimal.**"*, read as *a quiet screen does not warn about a cost it does not impose*. **No — *give this profile a password* owes no irreversibility sentence, because the act takes nothing from the player.** **§1: no sentence, no second press, and no copy anywhere asserting that a handle is permanent or now unavailable to others.** **§2 writes down the test [`ADR-0143`](ADR-0143-irreversibility-is-said-last-and-never-coloured.md) §4's table left implicit, applying §2 rather than narrowing it: an act owes those obligations when a player who performs it **ends up with less than they had and cannot get it back**, never because the row it writes cannot be deleted — under [`ADR-0039`](ADR-0039-v01-offers-no-account-deletion.md) almost nothing here can be, and a rule firing on permanence alone puts a warning under every control. **§3 answers the register's second half: a handle is *held*, not *spent*** — [`ADR-0130`](ADR-0130-a-name-can-be-changed-and-the-name-it-leaves-is-spent.md) §2's word describes a string that has **left** its owner, and a handle has left nobody; the two name opposite states of the same unique index, and §2's derivation cannot transfer because it is entirely about visibility — measured, `ProfileResponse` carries **seven** fields and no handle, and [`ADR-0031`](ADR-0031-an-optional-verified-recovery-email.md) §1 says *"a handle is **never shown to anybody**"*, so a reissued handle would print a second player onto **no** row. The stranger who cannot have the string is **not owed a sentence on somebody else's form**, and is the one subject this product refuses (`ADR-0029` §5, and the sign-up `409` conflating *taken* with *you already have a password* on `ADR-0027` §6). **The decisive fact is a merged refusal to decide**: `ADR-0031` §1's *"Nothing here changes a handle… **That is not foreclosed** … but it is not built, and no story should assume it"* — so *you cannot change this later* would settle handle permanence in shipped copy, and the product has just paid `ADR-0130` §5's price to delete the identical sentence for names (`PERMANENCE_LINE`, measured **0** occurrences in `web-client/src` today). **§4** completes `ADR-0143` §4's fourth row — *nothing to say* — and measures the screen both ways: `ANONYMOUS_WAY_OUT` is already the last line before the panel in the state the card draws, and `AccountScreen.tsx:106` (`!signedIn`) against `:133` (`!hasPassword`) leaves one reachable state where the form renders with none of the three sentences and can only answer `409`. **§5:** the card owes **one margin sentence and no frame changes**; **no gate is bought**. **§6** leaves *can a handle ever be changed* exactly where `ADR-0031` §1 left it, and names — **deliberately without registering** — whether the account screen should ever show a player their own handle. **§7:** reversed by a merged ADR **deciding** a handle is permanent, by one player asking to change theirs, or by the human. **Supersedes nothing**; corrects one word (*spends*) in `ADR-0143` §Consequences 5, a clause that decided nothing. Costs named: a mistyped handle is still forever and nothing says so; a player with no recovery address who forgets their handle has no path back and is not told to remember it; **the answer is conditional on a decision nobody has made and nothing detects the condition changing**; and the gap is closed by ruling rather than by shipping, so it still looks like a gap |
| [0147](ADR-0147-the-held-room-is-abandoned-after-the-seat-under-the-joiners-own-stripe.md) | The held room is abandoned after the seat, under the joiner's own stripe | Accepted — resolves `DEC-154` for **the architect**, the mechanism [`ADR-0141`](ADR-0141-taking-a-seat-elsewhere-releases-the-room-you-were-holding.md) §8 registered and refused to choose. **§1: the release is `Room.abandon` through `RoomRegistry.mutate`, never a removal from `rooms`** — `Room.join`'s first `when` branch already answers `UNKNOWN_ROOM` for an `ABANDONED` room *"before anything else, so it is indistinguishable from a room that never existed"*, so `ADR-0141` §2's observable and `ADR-0022` §3's no-oracle property are **inherited from shipped, tested code**: no refusal path, no `ProtocolError` value, no `when` arm. `heldRoom` already excludes `ABANDONED` (`ADR-0133` §1), `resume` already answers `null` for it, and `isReapable` already collects it. Removal was rejected because `reap`'s own KDoc says why it sits outside `mutate` — *"teaching one shared critical section two different shapes of 'done'"* — and a release built on it would be the **third** hand-rolled critical section, on the invite path. **§2: the transition is `room.abandon(room.lastActivityAt)`**, the room handing its own stamp back, so `ADR-0141` §8's *"no `lastActivityAt` is written anywhere but in the room being joined"* is honoured **verbatim rather than amended** — and because `finishedMillis` (5 min) < `waitingMillis` (10 min), a released room is reapable **strictly no later** than it would have been as `WAITING`. **§3: the rooms are found by a new private `waitingRoomsHeldBy(player, except)`, not by `heldRoom`** — `HELD_ROOM_ORDER` sorts `PLAYING` first, so by release time `heldRoom(player)` answers with **the room just joined**, and the obvious implementation abandons the duel the player just sat down in. It returns **codes, not rooms**, ordered by `RoomCode.value`, and a **list**: `replyToCreateRoom`'s `PLAYING` fall-through makes two held `WAITING` rooms reachable on `develop`, and `ADR-0141` §7's *"exactly one live seat afterwards"* is false otherwise. **§4: one new public method, `joinReleasingHeld`, takes `stripeFor(player)` outermost** — `ADR-0133` §4's rule, whose §4 named this call site by name as the likely first customer — then `join`, then a guarded private `release` per held room; **no two room mutexes are ever held at once**, so no room-ordering rule is introduced, and `mutate`'s block stays non-suspending so §4's structural guarantee is untouched. The guard (`WAITING` **and** seats the player) sits **inside** the writing critical section, which is §8's *"conditions … at the moment of the write"* made structural; the unconditional public `abandon(code)` is deliberately not reused. **§5** narrows `heldOrOpen`'s second KDoc property to `heldOrOpen` itself. **§6: `replyToJoinRoom` changes by exactly one identifier** — `join` → `joinReleasingHeld` — with **no new branch, no second write and no new failure mode**, and the `resume` and `ALREADY_SEATED` branches untouched; `ADR-0141`'s *"`replyToJoinRoom` grows a write"* cost is paid by the write **not** landing there, since `DuelSocket` cannot take a private `RoomRegistry` stripe. **§7 hands the planner ten test rows**, each naming the `ADR-0141` clause it defends, and requires two codes and two players so `except` cannot pass as a constant. **Registers no decision.** Costs named: **the joiner's first frame now waits on a stranger's lock** — `RoomJoined` is sent only after every held room's mutex has been taken and released, so a rival joining the room being released delays this player's table by that rival's entire `join`; a player stripe is now held **across suspension**; `RoomState.ABANDONED` stops meaning *a duel ran here*; `abandon(room.lastActivityAt)` passes a non-`now` value into a parameter documented as `now`; the two-rooms-live window is narrowed, **not closed**, and still unmeasured; a `WAITING` and a `PLAYING` room can still be held at once, which is `ADR-0105` §1's unbuilt ticket and **nothing fails if it is never written**; and `RoomRegistry` gains its first method that touches two rooms |
| [0148](ADR-0148-a-module-names-every-card-that-draws-it-and-every-card-is-registered.md) | A module names every card that draws it, and every card is registered | Accepted — resolves `DEC-158` for **the architect**, and **amends [`ADR-0142`](ADR-0142-a-text-module-is-checked-against-the-rendered-card.md) §4**. **§1: `card text ⇒ module` gets no register and §7's third bullet stands** — because the drift that fired §7's trigger was not an instance of it. Sorted by hole, `DEC-158`'s three findings are two **cardinality** failures and one direction failure: `RIVAL_OFFERS` and `ROOM_GONE` are `result/rematch-text.ts`'s, which spends §4's one card on `rematch-panel.html` while the same five sentences are drawn on `rematch-states.html`. Replayed at `f20d07ed` against the values `TASK-141503` later moved out of `RematchControl.tsx` verbatim, **three of five pass and two fail** — the merged forward direction catches two of three findings on its own, aimed at the card it was not allowed to name. The same bug is **already producing a false reason**: `account-text.ts` registers `SIGN_IN_REFUSED` `notCarded` as *"no frame on this card draws a refused sign-in"*, true of `account.html` and false of `sign-in.html`, which draws that exact sentence. **§2: a `PAIRS` row is one (module, card) pair and a module may hold several** — six rows, adding `account-text.ts` × `sign-in.html`, `rematch-text.ts` × `rematch-states.html` and × `duel-end.html`. **§3: `notCarded` becomes a checked claim** — a row may give one `notCardedByDefault` reason, and every `notCarded` export, listed or defaulted, is asserted **absent** from that row's card by **whole-unit equality**, which is what makes the default safe. The asymmetry with §5c's substring is deliberate and free: measured, **all 26 carded values match as whole units**. **§4: coverage is total over cards** — six enter `NO_MODULE`, and an unpaired card may carry **no** carded value as a whole unit, which forces `rematch-states.html` to be paired with no judgement at all and finds `duel-end.html` unprompted (measured: it fires exactly once across the seven, and truly). **§5**: `RematchControl.tsx:71`'s bare literal becomes `OFFER_STANDING`, carded on `rematch-states.html` — one string, **not a rule**, because *product copy a card draws belongs in a text module* has no gate and nothing reads JSX. **§6 re-arms the trigger narrower**: a stale sentence on a card a row **already names**, that is no module's export. The reverse register is priced rather than feared — **746 text units** across twelve cards, 153 on the three paired ones, and **386 on `duel-table.html` + `duel-table-states.html`, whose modules export 0 string constants**, so more than half the corpus has no export name to point at. Inference is refused on measurement: `rematch-panel.html` draws `Account` and `Sign out` as an account-screen **stand-in**, and inferring a pair from it fails thirteen of `account-text.ts`'s fifteen. Costs named: the register **roughly doubles** and its second half is duller — one default sentence asserting something about twenty-three exports at once; **N cards means N partitions per module, forever**; two whole-unit assertions **will** false-positive on one-word strings as `ADR-0145` §2 scaffolding accretes; **§5 removes the one live instance of the hole §1 declines to close**, so §6's count restarts at zero and evidence is deferred; and `duel-table.html`'s reason will still read *its modules export functions only* when 386 units drift |
| [0149](ADR-0149-minting-is-dispatched-and-a-ladder-value-waits-for-the-human.md) | Minting is dispatched, and only a value that joins a ladder waits for the human | Accepted — resolves `DEC-161` for **the architect**, and **supersedes [`ADR-0091`](ADR-0091-design-gets-no-agent-a-new-screen-owes-a-card.md) §3's minting branch, and that branch alone** — *"is worked interactively with the human, because taste does not survive a verify block"*. The rest of §3 stands byte-unchanged; `ADR-0091` §§1, 2, 4, 5 and `ADR-0145` §§1–4 are untouched and applied; and **no verdict moves** — `ADR-0024` §3 keeps the human's visual verdict, and what changes is who **drafts** a value, which no merged source ever assigned to the human. Measured on `develop` at `cee03146`: of 86 `module: design` tickets the four labelled `minting` are all `review: standard` and **all four were dispatched and merged as ordinary PRs**, `TASK-130601` quoting §3's interactive sentence in its own body and being dispatched anyway; `ADR-0145` §3's floor has fired **12** times, **9** of them `EPIC-06` tickets under its scoped deviation and the other three dispatched. **§1: the practice is ratified and nothing is owed as repair** — §3's minting branch means a stricter tier and a labelled ticket, not a different authorship channel, and the interactive session is **un-mandated, not abolished**.
