Nothing can execute that branch: there is no `await human` state, and `ADR-0091` §2 makes the card the split's **first** ticket, so the stall is the whole story's — the trade §3 itself refused one sentence later for the verdict. **§2: a dispatched mint may not add a colour, or a step in a named ladder** (`--pd-fs-*`, `--pd-space-*`, `--pd-radius-*`, `--pd-weight-*`, `--pd-lh-*`, `--pd-track-*`), because inserting into a ladder changes what every existing step means while a **new group** falsifies nothing — measured, the eight tokens minted by dispatch contain **no colour at all**, zero hex literals have entered the sheet since 2026-08-15, and both recorded refusals citing §3 (`ADR-0143`'s hue, `STORY-1309`'s `1rem`) are ladder insertions. **The fallback always renders**, so no run stops: draw at the nearest declared step and say so, as `TASK-130906` did. **§3: mint only what the same diff draws. §4: every minted value states what it derives from, in the sheet. §5: one lint clause** — `module: design` plus `minting` ⇒ `review: standard` or `deep`, which fires on four tickets and fails none — while the Files-keyed clause is **deferred with a trigger**, since it would fail **nine merged `EPIC-06` tickets**. **§6: a story that merges a mint records whether the pane verdict has been given**, `EPIC-06`'s own line used four times and by no story since; measured, **15 of 21 cards** have been created or modified since the last recorded verdict of 2026-08-15. **§7** states what `.claude/agents/planner.md` (lines 159–160, carrying the superseded sentence verbatim), `.claude/agents/qa-manager.md` and `lint_tickets.py` owe. Costs named: a drafted value is now transcribed with no eye on it **by rule**; the ladder/group line would not have stopped `--pd-motion-turn-period`; §6 records debt without collecting it; the gate holds the tier only after the judgment is made; a **fourth** classification vocabulary; and nine historical tickets left unlabelled |
| [0150](ADR-0150-back-to-the-lobby-keeps-the-room.md) | *Back to the lobby* keeps the room, and the offer follows the player who pressed it | Accepted — resolves `DEC-162` for **the human**, whose call this records rather than derives: ***Back to the lobby* stops ending the room**, so a player who presses it keeps their finished room while they browse the lobby, the leaderboard and their account, and a rival's standing offer reaches them on any of those screens. **Supersedes [`ADR-0123`](ADR-0123-a-standing-rematch-offer-follows-the-rival.md) §1's carve-out** — *"A player who has left the room is not followed, and cannot be"*, and its *"`Back to the lobby` calls `forgetRoom()` and loads `/`"* sentence as a statement about the **result screen's** control — while §1's **rule** stands byte-unchanged and is what now carries the decision: a player who pressed it **is** still in the room. §Context's premise *"the product now invites a player to leave the only screen on which a rematch can be seen"* is replaced by this ADR, which creates the invitation that premise assumed. **Supersedes one clause of [`ADR-0072`](ADR-0072-a-tab-remembers-its-room-until-the-player-leaves-it.md) §5**, its wiring of the result branch to `forgetRoom`. §1: what the press ended was this **browser's hold** — the server room's life is untouched. §2: the press shows the front door with the room still held, and nothing on it mentions the room. §3: the panel stands on the front door too, and `ADR-0123` §3's *one fact never has two live surfaces* is measured against **the result screen not showing** rather than `shown !== "first"`. §4 lists what still ends a room; §5 mints no storage key, so a reload lands back on the result screen. **Named cost, the human's own:** the finished room's five minutes must now cover a player who has wandered off. Registers `DEC-163` (the mechanism) and `DEC-164` (whether five minutes still fits), both the architect's |
| [0151](ADR-0151-the-press-is-one-fact-and-the-way-back-stops-navigating.md) | The press is one fact above the cascade, and the way back stops navigating | Accepted — resolves `DEC-163` for **the architect**, all four parts of it, and **derives every one**: **§1** the *left the result screen* fact is one boolean in a new `ResultScreenProvider` that `App` wraps its two children in — a screen change does not unmount it and a reload rebuilds it, which is `ADR-0150` §5's lifetime with no key — cleared during its own render whenever the standing is not `finished`, `ADR-0138` §3's construction applied to the press, because a flag that outlived its result would swallow the next duel's verdict. **§2** `Lobby.tsx`'s three store branches are keyed on `ADR-0114`'s `standing` instead of on `outcome`/`view`/`roomCode`, and one new pure function in `room-standing.ts` — `finishedRoomScreen(standing, leftTheResult)` → `"result" | "front-door" | null` — says which of a finished room's two screens shows. Identical rendering for every state that exists today, and it is what makes the front door **reachable at all**: the raw fields can never skip to it, since a finished room's `view` and `roomCode` are both still set, so the obvious `&& !left` lands the player back at the duel table. **§3** the way back becomes a `<button type="button">` — an anchor would re-boot the tab and undo the press — and the handler calls `leave()` before recording the fact, because `href="/"` was quietly stripping an invite link's `?room=`, which `main.tsx:176` reads at every boot and `boot.ts:113` prefers over the room this tab holds. `onLeave` becomes a **required** `onBack`; `forgetRoom` is not called. **§4** the panel's gate names the front door positively rather than negating the result screen, because the negation also admits the **duel table** and would move `ADR-0138` §2's *no panel over a table* from the gate into the reducer. **Supersedes three sentences of [`ADR-0138`](ADR-0138-the-panel-mounts-beside-the-lobby-and-the-dismissal-lives-in-the-mount.md)** (§1's *one import and one element* and *no context is added*, §2's `shown !== "first"` gate) and **one of [`ADR-0072`](ADR-0072-a-tab-remembers-its-room-until-the-player-leaves-it.md) §5** (the anchor), as a statement about the result screen's control only. `roomStanding`, `rulingOn`, `use-screen.ts`, the store, the wire and the waiting screen are **not edited**. **Costs named:** `App` gains a store-reading provider and stops being one import and one element; six of the thirteen `getByRole("link")` assertions move to `role: "button"` while the waiting screen's seven must not, and ~20 `onLeave` call sites become compile errors; the press leaves no history entry, so the browser's own Back stops returning to the result; the cascade's correctness now rests on `roomStanding`'s ordering; and `room-standing.ts` holds its first function whose argument is a press. Of `ADR-0150`'s four costs it **resolves** the *holds a room, shows the front door* debt and **inherits** the other three unchanged. Registers `DEC-166` (**the product owner's**, two sentences about one dead room), which blocks nothing |
| [0152](ADR-0152-the-finished-rooms-five-minutes-stand.md) | The finished room's five minutes stand, because they never measured the holder's screen | Accepted — resolves `DEC-164` for **the architect**: ***nothing changes***, and the reason is that [`ADR-0150`](ADR-0150-back-to-the-lobby-keeps-the-room.md) §1 **opened no new interval**. Measured on `develop` at `d5ef7df8`: a `FINISHED` room's deadline is `now - lastActivityAt >= timeouts.finishedMillis` (`RoomRegistry.kt:836-844`), and **exactly two** things write that field on such a room — `Room.finish(now)` (`Room.kt:238`) and `RoomRegistry.resume`'s `reconnect(seat).touch(now)` (`RoomRegistry.kt:390`), the only `touch(` call site in the whole server. **A wander is neither, and neither was sitting on the result screen**: screens are the URL hash (`use-screen.ts:58-75`) over a socket booted once per tab (`ADR-0032`), and the client's whole message set is `Act`, `CreateRoom`, `Hello`, `JoinRoom` and `OfferRematch` — no ping, no keepalive, no *leave*. So the room's life is `[finish, finish + F)` before `ADR-0150` and after it; what §1 changed is how much of that interval a **presser** can reach — from none of it to all of it, which is what a player who never pressed always had. §1 **spends** room life already allocated rather than asking for more. **§1** leaves the rule, the default and `ROOM_FINISHED_TIMEOUT_MILLIS` untouched and mints no production change; **§2** states the subset argument, so any future case that five minutes is wrong must rest on it having always been wrong; **§3** closes the write set at two, so no ticket may add a third; **§4** rules that a standing offer goes on **not** restamping (`Room.offerRematch`'s `Offered` branch, `Room.kt:330`) and names that branch as where a floor would land; **§5** registers `DEC-165`; **§6** mints one characterisation test in `RoomReapTest` and no production ticket. **Named cost:** the product ships a `Rematch` control that can lie to a player it invited to press it — an offer at `finish + 4:59` leaves under a second to answer and the press returns `Failure(UNKNOWN_ROOM)` — and this ADR declines to make that impossible. Beside it: the answering window's floor is **zero**; finished rooms accumulate a little more, since more tabs now hold one and every reload restamps through `resume`; and the answer rests on structure because no counter exists and this ADR does not build one. Registers `DEC-165` — **the product owner's** — whether an offer owes a minimum answering time |
| [0153](ADR-0153-a-rematch-offer-owes-its-recipient-a-minute.md) | A rematch offer owes its recipient a minute, and no screen mentions it | Accepted — resolves `DEC-165` for **the product owner**: ***yes — sixty seconds, as a floor and never as a ceiling***. Licensed by [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md)'s first success condition, whose last beat is this press — *"We play a full heads-up match. Someone wins. **We hit Rematch.**"*, followed by *"Everything else is downstream of that moment."* [`ADR-0152`](ADR-0152-the-finished-rooms-five-minutes-stand.md) measured the recipient's window as `finishedMillis - (offer - finish)`, **floor zero**, and named the resulting `Rematch` control *"that can lie to a player it invited to press it"* as its first cost. **§1** makes the room outlive a standing offer by at least sixty seconds — `max(finish + finishedMillis, offer + 60s)`; **§2** makes it a floor only, so a generous window is never shortened; **§3** arms it **once**, in the `Offered` branch (`Room.kt:330`) — a repeat press answers `ALREADY_OFFERED` and the other seat's press agrees the rematch — so a finished room's worst case goes from `finish + 5:00` to `finish + 6:00` and **neither player can push it further**; **§4** says nothing on any screen, confirming `ADR-0044` §7's refusal of a countdown and the vision's *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"*; **§5** leaves `RematchNotice` and `RematchControl` exactly as merged, keeping `RematchControl`'s *"A button that can only fail is not offered"* true without the client learning a deadline; **§6** registers `DEC-167`. Sixty is twice the turn allowance (`RoomTimeouts.DEFAULT_TURN_MILLIS`, `ADR-0108` §1) because that thirty seconds is for a decision the player is **watching for**, with the clock visible, and since [`ADR-0150`](ADR-0150-back-to-the-lobby-keeps-the-room.md) this one arrives unannounced on a screen chosen for something else. **Amends** `ADR-0152` §4 — the branch it named as *"where any floor would land"* — and §3's closure at two writes, in the one way §3 provides for. **Named cost:** a rival now holds a lever over a room that is not only theirs, and the product hands it to them — one press extends a stranger's finished room by up to a minute, spending `ADR-0152` §4's *"independent of what either of them does"*. Beside it: this buys **sixty seconds of honesty, not honesty** — past the minute the button lies exactly as before and nothing warns the recipient; the room is held sixty seconds for a recipient who may not be there, since the floor is armed by the offer being *recorded* and not *seen*; a rematch is slightly likelier to be agreed into a room whose offerer walked away; the number comes from a neighbouring number, not evidence, because the counter `ADR-0152` named still does not exist; and a finished room now carries **two** durations where it carried one. Moves no wire type, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, no schema, no stored key and no word on any screen. Registers `DEC-167` — **the architect's** — the mechanism |
| [0154](ADR-0154-the-gone-room-is-the-rematch-surfaces-news.md) | A gone room is the rematch surface's news, and the front door does not repeat it | Accepted — resolves `DEC-166` for **the product owner**: ***the panel's `That duel room is gone.`, and the front door says nothing at all***. That the player reads **one** sentence and not two is [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md)'s *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."*; that the surviving one is the **panel's** is [`ADR-0150`](ADR-0150-back-to-the-lobby-keeps-the-room.md) §2 read literally — *"The only thing the held room may put on a screen is `ADR-0123`'s panel"* — since a room that is **gone** is news about the room this tab holds, and [`ADR-0044`](ADR-0044-a-rematch-is-one-intent-and-one-room-fact.md) §6 had already assigned the frame (*"the client says so and offers the way back to the lobby"*). **§1** on the front door of a tab standing there **under a room it still holds** — `ADR-0151` §2's `"front-door"` answer — no `UNKNOWN_ROOM` line is rendered at all; **§2** every other front door is byte-unchanged, including a mistyped code, a dead invite link and boot's stale-code correction (`ADR-0072` §6); **§3** only `UNKNOWN_ROOM` is withheld and only there — `ROOM_FULL` and the default sentence are untouched everywhere, because the panel states neither; **§4** `RematchNotice` and `RematchControl` are **not edited**, and `Not now` does not give the front door its sentence back; **§5** tabulates what is on screen for each of the four ways in. The asymmetry that decides it: the panel's sentence also **retires `Rematch`** (`ADR-0138` §4), so silencing the panel would leave a live-looking button over a room known to be dead, against `RematchControl`'s *"A button that can only fail is not offered"*. **Costs named:** a press can now go unanswered — a holder of a finished room who types a nonexistent code on the front door reads **nothing** and spends one of `ADR-0022`'s ten joins a minute retrying, and so does a refusal that lands a round trip after the way back with no rival's offer standing; `Lobby.tsx:494` stops being a function of one field; `UNKNOWN_ROOM` becomes the one refusal with a per-screen rule; neither sentence is made *true*, only non-contradictory; and the result screen and the front door now say different things about one event by design. No wire, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, no schema, no stored key, no new string — one conditional, over a fact `Lobby` already computes. Registers `DEC-168` — **the product owner's** — whether the panel goes on saying *That duel room is gone.* for a refusal the player brought on themselves |
| [0156](ADR-0156-the-relay-dials-by-name-and-holds-both-loopback-families.md) | The relay dials its target by name, and holds both loopback families | Accepted — resolves `DEC-169` for **the architect**, and **amends and supersedes nothing**: [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md) §1 stands byte-unchanged, `web-client/vite.config.ts` is **not** touched, and the origin of a proof of record stays `http://localhost:4173`. **The instrument changes, not the origin.** Measured on `develop`: `localhost` resolves `::1` first here (Node's `verbatim` order), a Node server told to listen on `localhost` binds `::1` **only**, and Vite 5.4.21 does exactly that in **both** modes — dev and preview share one `resolveHostname(options.host)` → `httpServerStart` path, so the question's premise that *dev binds both families* is **false**, and how the delayed `dev` drives ever reached Vite on `5273` is recorded nowhere. `scripts/qa/delay.mjs` dials `connect(port, "127.0.0.1")`, which against a `::1`-only listener is `ECONNREFUSED` — so the relay accepts the browser, cannot forward it, and `TASK-131006`'s drive read `cut 0`, a number that looks like a reading. **§1**: the target dial becomes `connect({ port, host: "localhost", autoSelectFamily: true })` — by name, over every address the name has, the way the browser resolves the origin; `autoSelectFamily` is explicit because with it off the name reaches `::1` only, and because a default is a Node-version fact a flag can flip. **§2**: the four other `connect` sites keep `127.0.0.1` under the rule **dial by literal address what you bound, by name what somebody else bound** — a `localhost` `cut` could reach a different process on `[::1]` and report `cut 0` again. **§3**: the relay holds **both** loopback families on its listen port or refuses to start, second bind at the first's resolved port (both self-tests ask for `0`) — measured, `[::1]:P` and `127.0.0.1:P` do not collide, so a `preview` left on its default `4173` would be reached **directly** by the browser and bypass the relay in silence; held both ways it is `EADDRINUSE` at bring-up, which is what §1's `strictPort` already promises. **§4, what stops this recurring**: a start-up dial that exits 1 naming the target and prints the resolved `remoteAddress`, a per-connection failure message where the `error` handler swallows one today, and `--selftest-unreachable` proving both. **§5**: `npm run preview -- --port 4273` behind the relay on `4173`, so the served port moves and the origin a record names does not; readiness is §2's `/@vite/client` marker fetched **through** the relay. Costs named: a machine without IPv6 loopback cannot run the relay at all (deliberate — a silently skipped bind is the silence being deleted); the relay's port becomes exclusive, so bring-ups that used to work by bypassing it now refuse, and `kill` is denied here; dialling by name spends certainty about which server answered, recorded for the start-up dial only; the path is proved at bring-up, not continuously; and `drive.mjs` defaulting to `:4173` while `stack.sh` still probes `5173` leaves two truths about the origin until `ADR-0117` §3's `stack.sh` half lands. **Registers no decision** — the LAN proof origin `ADR-0140` §6 wants is argued in §Alternatives 2 and left to the ticket that needs it |


## Open decisions

Questions deliberately left open are marked `DEC-NNN` in the document they affect.

**Who answers one** — see [`docs/workflow.md`](../workflow.md#who-answers-a-dec):

| Kind | Answered by |
| --- | --- |
| **Technical** — where a type lives, schema shape, wire format, concurrency, failure semantics | the `architect` agent |
| **Product** — what a player sees, what a duel *is*, which risks inside the software are acceptable | the `product-owner` agent, deriving from [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md) |
| **Vision** — money; what the product *is* or *is not*; the roadmap's shape; risk with consequences outside the software | the human, and nothing else |

A `DEC-NNN` can be listed in **four** places: this file, [`tasks/BOARD.md`](../../tasks/BOARD.md),
and the `## Open decisions` table of any epic under `tasks/epics/`. The PR that answers a decision
**strikes every row it answers, in the same PR.** A strike deferred to somebody else's next PR is a
strike nobody makes — `DEC-035` sat listed open and answered simultaneously for weeks that way.

| ID | Question | Where | Due |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DEC-002 | What performance budget does the hand evaluator carry, how is it measured, and does `HandRank` become a packed integer? | `../../tasks/stories/STORY-0103-hand-evaluator.md` | before STORY-0108 |
| DEC-008 | Is the full `MatchLog` persisted in v0.1, and where — a column, a table per hand, or object storage? | `../../tasks/stories/STORY-0209-postgres-schema-and-migrations.md` | before EPIC-08 |
| DEC-032 | **The architect's** — how does the client test browser storage, and is the developer Node version pinned? Node 24+ defines an inert `localStorage` global (absent `--localstorage-file`) that shadows jsdom's under Vitest, so `typeof localStorage === "undefined"` while `sessionStorage` works. `TASK-030304` sidesteps it by injecting a `Storage`, which its own contract already required — but `TASK-030307`, `TASK-030309` and `STORY-0311` each touch storage, and `STORY-0311` sends the same value as an HTTP header. Open: does anything ever exercise a *real* `Storage`, and does the repo pin a developer Node version rather than only CI's (`.nvmrc` says 24, this workstation runs 26)? `DEC-022` foresaw the split; this is the first time it has bitten. | [`TASK-030304`](../../tasks/tasks/TASK-030304-the-device-id-lives-under-one-key-this-module-owns.md) | before STORY-0311 |
| DEC-060 | **The product owner's** — does a **finished** season ever become reachable from a screen, and how is one chosen? Raised by [`ADR-0061`](ADR-0061-a-season-is-a-calendar-month-and-the-coin-never-resets.md) §7, which settles that a finished season is never *gone* — its standings recompute exactly from rows nothing rewrites — and that v0.3 ships no way for a player to ask for one. So on the first day of a new season the previous month's ladder is computable and unreachable, and **nothing records who won it**. Settle whether the product ever shows a past season, and if so how one is named and chosen (a selector, a *last season* line, a single remembered winner, or nothing), given that a selector is a control on a screen [`ADR-0060`](ADR-0060-the-record-is-its-own-screen-and-the-lobby-is-the-door.md) already said will crowd. *Never* is a complete answer and needs saying out loud rather than falling out. Blocks nothing today | [`ADR-0061`](ADR-0061-a-season-is-a-calendar-month-and-the-coin-never-resets.md) | before the first season boundary after the ladder ships |
| DEC-088 | **The product owner's** — when a UAT round's promotion gate has **more than three screens** each offering a question that clears the bar, which screens' questions take the three slots? Raised 2026-08-30 by `TASK-120708`, whose coder declined to invent the rule and named it instead. `ADR-0092` §5 sets the caps — at most three per round, one per screen — but supplies no ordering, and neither `.claude/agents/qa-manager.md` nor `.claude/agents/uat.md` does. Step 4's fix-set ordering (*`blocker` before `high`, then by how much of the product is blocked*) does **not** generalise: every promoted question has already cleared the same single bar, so there are no severity layers to sort by, and no merged source ranks one screen against another. Until it is answered, `qa-manager` improvises — and a UAT round walks 13 screens for 3 slots, so the first round hits this. *The three sharpest, by the manager's own judgment, recorded in the round story* is a complete answer. Blocks nothing — the cycle runs, it just promotes an unordered three | [`ADR-0092`](ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) | before the first UAT round |
| DEC-093 | **The architect's** — does [`ADR-0092`](../docs/adr/ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) §6's **baseline rule** extend to a round in which a screen-state becomes conformance-judgeable for the first time through **any** merged instrument, or only through a **card** merged in the previous round's repairs? Both §6 and `EPIC-12` §Termination rule 4 are written about a card. Round 3 met the other case: two frames of `design/screens/duel-table-states.html` and `rematch-states.html` were unreadable in rounds 1 and 2 — round 2 said so in as many words, *"check (a) on two of `duel-table-states.html`'s three frames is unreachable by any round with the verbs `drive.mjs` has"* — and became readable in round 3 only because `TASK-121008`'s `record`/`frames` verbs merged in round 2's repairs. The rule's stated **purpose** reaches that case exactly (*"the two rounds measured differently-sized judgeable sets"*); its **text** does not. Raised 2026-08-30 at round 3's triage, which **refused to extend the exemption itself**: a manager who widens a rule in the round that rule would save cannot prove it would have widened it anywhere else, and `STORY-1208` is the precedent for repairing this machinery by a merged sentence rather than by a generous reading at triage. **Not one of `ADR-0092` §5's three promotion slots** — those are the product owner's, and this is `CLAUDE.md` rule 5 routing. **Blocks nothing**: round 3's fix set is empty, `B(3) = 0` so rule 4 could not fire at any reading, and the cycle ended `PASS`. Answering it costs a future invocation a wrong `STOP_DIVERGING` or nothing at all. Recorded in [`STORY-1211`](stories/STORY-1211-round-3-uat-the-count-fell-to-zero-and-the-cycle-ends.md) | [`STORY-1211`](stories/STORY-1211-round-3-uat-the-count-fell-to-zero-and-the-cycle-ends.md) | before the next `/qa-cycle` invocation |
| DEC-094 | **The product owner's** — should a control that **no card draws** wear the client's control vocabulary, or is a bare control the intended treatment for navigation the cards do not draw? Concretely: the lobby's `Your duels`, `Leaderboard` and `Account` doors, and the `Back` on each secondary screen, all render with `className: ""` — computing as body text — beside a room-code input, `Create a duel room`, `Join the duel` and `Set my name` that carry the full recipe. No merged source is contradicted, which is why it is a question and not a finding: `design/tokens/tokens.css` is a `:root` sheet with **no selectors**, so an unclassed button contradicts nothing in it; `create-duel.html`'s front-door frame draws neither control and notes *"nothing else on the door — no lobby noise, no tables list"*; and `ADR-0060` §§2–4 settle the doors' **word**, **element** and **placement** and the way out's word, and say nothing about treatment. Raised 2026-08-30 at round 3's triage and promoted by `qa-manager` under [`ADR-0092`](../docs/adr/ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) §5 — **one of the three slots that round had, and the only one spent**, on the `first` screen, so `DEC-088`'s ordering question did not bite. It clears both halves of the bar: a concrete choice answerable in one sentence, and it bears on whether a player can tell that three words under a form are things they may activate. **Two rounds have now spent a finding on it** — the four `Back`s in round 2, the three doors in round 3 — and only a merged answer closes it mechanically, as `ADR-0094` closed the join path. **Blocks nothing** — round 3's fix set is empty and the cycle ended `PASS`; an answer becomes a merged source either way, and any ticket it yields enters the **ordinary backlog now the cycle has ended**, never the round that asked (`EPIC-12` §Termination rule 1). A *no* earns a row in `docs/test-plan.md` §*Settled, and not a finding*, so a later round re-raising it would itself contradict a merged source. Recorded in [`STORY-1211`](stories/STORY-1211-round-3-uat-the-count-fell-to-zero-and-the-cycle-ends.md) | [`STORY-1211`](stories/STORY-1211-round-3-uat-the-count-fell-to-zero-and-the-cycle-ends.md) | before the cycle's next triage |
| DEC-090 | **The product owner's** — on the `account` screen, should *Attach a recovery address* — which asks for a *Current password* — appear on a device that has no password yet, or only once one exists? The section is offered on an unclaimed profile, where the field it requires cannot be filled. `ADR-0050` §4 settles what the screen **reads** (`deviceRouteLive`, and *no `ProfileResponse` field*) and no merged source settles what it **offers**, so the shipped state contradicts nothing and this is a question. Raised 2026-08-30 at round 1 of `/qa-cycle uat regression`'s triage and promoted by `qa-manager` under [`ADR-0092`](ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) §5 — one of the three slots that round had, on one of three distinct screens, so `DEC-088`'s ordering question did not bite. It clears both halves of the bar: a concrete choice answerable in one sentence, and it bears on a player's ability to tell what is going on or what they may do. **Blocks nothing** — it gates no member of round 1's fix set, so the cycle continues (`ADR-0092` §5); an answer becomes a merged source either way, and any ticket it yields enters the **earliest subsequent** round's triage or the ordinary backlog, never the round that asked (`EPIC-12` §Termination rule 1). Recorded in [`STORY-1209`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1209-round-1-uat-the-front-door-was-never-dressed.md) | [`STORY-1209`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1209-round-1-uat-the-front-door-was-never-dressed.md) | before the cycle's next triage |
| DEC-091 | **The product owner's** — on the `sign-in` screen, should *Back* on the sign-in screen return to the account screen it was opened from, or to the lobby? `ADR-0083` §2 settles where a **successful sign-in** lands (`#/account`) and nothing settles where a refusal or a retreat lands. The shipped *Back* returns to the lobby, two steps from where the player was. No merged source is contradicted. It clears both halves of the bar: a concrete choice answerable in one sentence, and it bears on a player's ability to tell what is going on or what they may do. **Blocks nothing** — it gates no member of round 1's fix set, so the cycle continues (`ADR-0092` §5); an answer becomes a merged source either way, and any ticket it yields enters the **earliest subsequent** round's triage or the ordinary backlog, never the round that asked (`EPIC-12` §Termination rule 1). Recorded in [`STORY-1209`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1209-round-1-uat-the-front-door-was-never-dressed.md) | [`STORY-1209`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1209-round-1-uat-the-front-door-was-never-dressed.md) | before the cycle's next triage |
| DEC-102 | **The product owner's** — what does one press of the sizing row's stepper (`+`/`−`) move the dialled total by, and therefore which legal totals can the bar reach at all? Registered open 2026-08-31 by [`ADR-0101`](ADR-0101-pot-means-a-pot-sized-raise-and-the-fractions-share-its-base.md) §6. `ADR-0100`'s alternatives filed the step size under `DEC-101` (*"the step size is undecided and belongs with `DEC-101`'s control design"*); `ADR-0101` §6 declines it, because the question registered was what the **named presets** compute and a stepper is not a named preset — nothing in that ADR implies a step, and neither `docs/vision.md` nor `docs/duel-rules.md` names one, so an answer would be invented rather than derived. **It gates none of `ADR-0101` §§1–5** — the chips' arithmetic, their offer rule and `ADR-0100` §1's driver all stand without it, and the merged `whole-duel.test.tsx` gate needs only `min` and `all-in` — so the rewritten `TASK-120908` is **not** blocked by it. The constraint whoever answers it must weigh: `docs/duel-rules.md` §*Betting* says **No-limit**, the shipped slider reaches every legal total and five chips reach five, so whether the bar must keep a way to reach any legal total — and therefore whether the stepper may ship after the chips or must ship with them — is part of this question. Raised by an ADR, not promoted at a triage, so **not** one of `ADR-0092` §5's three slots | [`ADR-0101`](ADR-0101-pot-means-a-pot-sized-raise-and-the-fractions-share-its-base.md) §6 | before the sizing row's stepper ships |
| DEC-126 | **The architect's** — in the deployment this product is actually offered on, what serves the built bundle, on what origin, and does the Ktor server serve it or does something in front of it? Registered open 2026-09-04 by [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md) §8, which answers the **proof** half of `DEC-087` with `vite preview` on a workstation and **deliberately declines the deployment half** rather than letting a QA instrument imply an answer: `vite preview` is not a statement that anything ships behind Vite. It is genuinely open — [`docs/architecture.md`](../architecture.md#deployment-later) says *"Not decided yet; not needed before v0.2"*, so the topology is unchosen; `ADR-0117` §Alternatives 1 states the strongest case for the Ktor server serving `dist/` (one origin, no proxy, and `socket-url.ts`'s KDoc already claims *"production is same-origin"*) and rejects it **only as the proof mechanism**, on cost and on refusing to pre-decide this. Two things turn on the answer: `ADR-0088` §2 step 3's origin, which becomes the deployment's once one exists and supersedes `ADR-0117` §4; and `ServerConfig.BASE_URL`, whose default still names the old dev origin. Registered as a `DEC` rather than a prose pointer at `EPIC-07` **on purpose** — this repository has twenty-odd ADRs naming an epic that is not written and collects none of them, and a row in an open table with a due event is the one form that gets struck | [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md) | **before anything is offered to real users** — the same event as `ADR-0093` §1b's bound transport, and beside it |
| DEC-128 | **The product owner's** — when a hand ends with an **uncalled bet returned**, should the table's award banner state the amount **awarded from the pot** (`PotAwarded.amount`, what ships) or the **total chips the winner receives at the resolution** (the award plus the returned uncalled bet, which is exactly the `Pot` figure the same slot printed a tick earlier)? Raised 2026-09-05 at the triage of [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md)'s Definition-of-done drive — 31 hands, two fresh Chrome profiles, `866ebc21`. **Measured, three cases:** at 50/100 an uncontested fold made `Pot 150` → `wins 100` with the winner's stack up 150; at 75/150, `Pot 225` → `wins 150`, stack up 225; at a real 100/200 showdown, `Pot 400` → `wins 400`, stack up 400. The gap is exactly the uncalled blind excess the folder never matched, so the shipped banner is a **third** quantity — neither the pot nor net profit, which is +50 and +75 — and *"the banner states the big blind"* is a coincidence of `BB = 2 × SB` that the showdown row already falsifies. **Not a defect, and deliberately not filed:** [`ADR-0095`](ADR-0095-the-table-states-who-took-the-pot-and-never-names-a-hand.md) §2 names the number by name — *"a `PotAwarded.amount` this client received … **not** a sum of what the seats committed, **not** a stack delta, **not** the view's `pot`"* — which refuses all three of the values the drive proposed, and `PotStrip.awardLineFor` prints `formatChips(award.amount)` with no arithmetic, so a bug ticket would contradict a merged ADR. **What is genuinely open** is that `ADR-0095` §3 and its Alternative 4 **deleted** the card's fold-ending second line (*"Nobody shows — your river bet of 800 goes uncalled and returns"*) on the stated ground that *"the printed amount and the stack change already agree to the chip"* — true against the strip of 2026-08-30, which printed `view.pot` (`0` preflop), and **false since** [`ADR-0107`](ADR-0107-pot-names-every-chip-committed-to-the-hand.md) made the strip print the total, its §3 accepting by name that the figure *"includes chips that a fold would hand back to the raiser"*. A player now watches 150 become *wins 100* while 150 arrives, which is the sentence `ADR-0095` deleted because nobody would need it. The answer must also settle whether the fold ending owes that line back. It is cheap either way — `UncalledBetReturned` carries `seat` and `amount` and is already in `narration`, so either shape is a **transcription** and no wire moves — but a banner printing award + return would be a client-computed figure, which `ADR-0095` §2 forbids and `no-derivation.test.tsx` gates, so a **change** hands the *how* to the architect. **Blocks nothing**, and it does not block `EPIC-13` from closing: the banner is not one of the DoD's five visible claims, and *a pot that matches the sizing row's base* was observed directly on the same drive | [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md) §Definition of done | before the first duel played by someone who is not the author — [`ADR-0095`](ADR-0095-the-table-states-who-took-the-pot-and-never-names-a-hand.md) §6's own named trigger |
| DEC-167 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism does a finished room's reaping deadline observe [`ADR-0153`](ADR-0153-a-rematch-offer-owes-its-recipient-a-minute.md) §1's **sixty-second floor**? Registered 2026-09-10 by that ADR §6. This is the ADR [`ADR-0152`](ADR-0152-the-finished-rooms-five-minutes-stand.md) §3 requires before the write set at `lastActivityAt` may grow, and it must settle: whether the floor is a **second timestamp** on `Room` read by `isReapable` or a **write in the `Offered` branch** `ADR-0152` §4 names (`Room.kt:330`); whether the sixty seconds is a constant or a `RoomTimeouts` field with a deployment override beside `ROOM_FINISHED_TIMEOUT_MILLIS`; whether the floor may arm on a room already past its own deadline but **not yet swept**, given the one-second sweep; and the **rewrite** of `ADR-0152` §6's characterisation test, which pins the opposite and which §6 named as *"the thing deliberately rewritten rather than the thing that silently goes on passing"*. **Nothing in it may** move the wire or `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, add a stored key, change any word on any screen, make a room's life a function of presence, or break `ADR-0153` §3's bound — one arming per room, worst case `finish + 6:00`. **Blocks** the first ticket that implements `ADR-0153` §1 | [`ADR-0153`](ADR-0153-a-rematch-offer-owes-its-recipient-a-minute.md) §6 | before the first ticket that implements `ADR-0153` §1 |
| DEC-168 | **The product owner's** — does the rematch panel go on saying *That duel room is gone.*, and retiring `Rematch`, for a refusal the player **brought on themselves**? Registered 2026-09-10 by [`ADR-0154`](ADR-0154-the-gone-room-is-the-rematch-surfaces-news.md) §6, which answered `DEC-166` for the **front door** and deliberately left the panel as merged. `RematchNotice` branches on the same one `state.refusal` field (`ADR-0138` §4), so on the held-room front door a **mistyped room code** now flips the panel to the gone-room sentence and retires the control over an offer that may still be live — for the rest of the room's five minutes, and for at least the sixty seconds `ADR-0153` §1 guarantees it. Newly reachable for exactly the reason `DEC-166` was. It cannot be answered by tracking which frame the `Failure` answered — `ADR-0072` §7 refuses that — and `RematchControl` shares the branch and the words, so any answer must also say what the **result screen** does. **Blocks nothing**: `ADR-0154` §1's conditional ships either way | [`ADR-0154`](ADR-0154-the-gone-room-is-the-rematch-surfaces-news.md) §6 | with the story that implements `ADR-0150`, or the first UAT round that reaches the front door with a room held |

## Answered decisions

| ID | Question | Answered by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DEC-169 | **The architect's** — **by what change does the QA relay reach the preview origin [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md) §1 established?** `scripts/qa/delay.mjs` dials `connect(targetPort, "127.0.0.1")` (line 66, and four more at 105, 127, 208 and 241) while `web-client/vite.config.ts:7` sets `preview: { port: 4173, strictPort: true }` with **no `host`**, so Vite binds `localhost` — which resolves `::1` first on this machine — and preview listens on IPv6 only. The relay accepts the browser's connection and cannot forward it; `TASK-131006`'s drive reported `cut 0`, no app socket to sever, which makes `STORY-1310`'s `P3` unmeasurable. The fork is that `ADR-0117` §1 writes the preview block out verbatim and says `vite.config.ts` *"gains exactly this, and nothing else"*, so a `host` key contradicts a merged ADR and needs an ADR to authorise it — while the alternative changes the instrument every refresh-path drive shares. **Registered and answered in the same pull request, 2026-09-10**: it was found by a drive rather than by a planner and never stood in an open table, which is why it is listed here and nowhere above. It gated `TASK-131006`, `EPIC-13`'s last ticket | [ADR-0156](ADR-0156-the-relay-dials-by-name-and-holds-both-loopback-families.md) — **the relay dials its target by name, and holds both loopback families.** The instrument changes; the origin does not. `vite.config.ts` is untouched, `ADR-0117` is neither amended nor superseded, and the origin of a proof of record stays `http://localhost:4173`. The question's premise was **falsified while answering it**: dev does *not* bind both families — dev and preview resolve their host through one `resolveHostname` and both bound `::1` when run against this repository's own config — so nothing in the repository states, requires or checks the condition those delayed `dev` drives passed on. §1 dials the target `by name` with `autoSelectFamily: true` written explicitly (measured: with it off the name reaches `::1` only and is `ECONNREFUSED` against a `127.0.0.1` listener). §2 keeps the other four sites literal — **dial by literal address what you bound, by name what somebody else bound** — because a `localhost` `cut` could reach another process on `[::1]` and report `cut 0` all over again. §3 holds both loopback families on the listen port or refuses to start, closing the other hop: measured, `[::1]:P` does not collide with `127.0.0.1:P`, so a `preview` left on its default `4173` is reached **directly** by a browser that prefers `::1` and the relay is bypassed in silence — the same fault that cost `P1` a reading. §4 is the recurrence guard nothing supplied before: a start-up dial that exits 1 naming the target, a per-connection failure message in place of a swallowed error, and `--selftest-unreachable` proving both. §5 fixes the relayed layout — preview on `4273`, relay on `4173` — and §6 writes the follow-up ticket for the planner: two files, `delay.mjs` and `drive.mjs`'s `PD_APP_ORIGIN` (`ADR-0117` §3 as merged), with `ADR-0117` §3's `stack.sh` half still owed and blocking nothing |
| DEC-166 | **The product owner's** — when the room behind a standing rematch offer is gone and its holder is standing on the **front door**, which of two merged sentences does the player read: the front door's *No duel room has that code.* (`Lobby.tsx:494`) or the panel's *That duel room is gone.* (`ADR-0138` §4)? Registered 2026-09-10 by [`ADR-0151`](ADR-0151-the-press-is-one-fact-and-the-way-back-stops-navigating.md) §7, which created the state in which they can share a screen. **Answered: the panel's — and the front door says nothing at all.** | [ADR-0154](ADR-0154-the-gone-room-is-the-rematch-surfaces-news.md) — **one** sentence rather than two is [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md)'s *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."*; that it is the **panel's** is [`ADR-0150`](ADR-0150-back-to-the-lobby-keeps-the-room.md) §2 read literally — *"The only thing the held room may put on a screen is `ADR-0123`'s panel"* — because a room that is gone is news about the room this tab holds, and `ADR-0044` §6 had already given that frame to the rematch surface. **§1** on the front door of a tab standing there under a room it still holds (`ADR-0151` §2's `"front-door"`), no `UNKNOWN_ROOM` line is rendered; **§2** every other front door is byte-unchanged — a mistyped code, a dead invite link, boot's stale-code correction; **§3** only `UNKNOWN_ROOM` is withheld and only there, since the panel states neither `ROOM_FULL` nor the default sentence; **§4** the panel and the result screen are **not edited**, and `Not now` does not restore the front door's line; **§5** tabulates all four ways in. What decides it: the panel's sentence also **retires `Rematch`**, so silencing the panel leaves a live-looking button over a room known to be dead. **Costs named:** a press can now go unanswered — a holder of a finished room who types a nonexistent code reads **nothing**, and retries spend `ADR-0022`'s join budget; `Lobby.tsx:494` stops being a function of one field; `UNKNOWN_ROOM` becomes the one refusal with a per-screen rule; neither sentence is made true, only non-contradictory. Registers `DEC-168` for the product owner, blocking nothing |
| DEC-165 | **The product owner's** — does a rematch offer owe its recipient a **minimum time to answer**, and if so how long? Registered 2026-09-10 by [`ADR-0152`](ADR-0152-the-finished-rooms-five-minutes-stand.md) §5, which measured that a finished room dies at `finish + finishedMillis`, that a standing offer does **not** restart that clock (`Room.offerRematch`'s `Offered` branch, `Room.kt:330`), and that the recipient's window is therefore `finishedMillis - (offer - finish)` — **floor zero, ceiling five minutes** — with no screen stating either bound. **Answered: yes — sixty seconds, as a floor and never as a ceiling.** | [ADR-0153](ADR-0153-a-rematch-offer-owes-its-recipient-a-minute.md) — a rematch offer owes its recipient a minute, and no screen mentions it. Licensed by [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md)'s first success condition, whose last beat is this press: *"We play a full heads-up match. Someone wins. **We hit Rematch.**"* — *"Everything else is downstream of that moment."* A control the product invites a player to press must not be able to fail before their hand reaches the mouse. **§1**: a `FINISHED` room on which an offer stands does not end before sixty seconds have passed since the offer was recorded — the window becomes `max(finish + finishedMillis, offer + 60s) - offer`. **§2**: a floor only; an offer at `finish + 0:10` still leaves four minutes fifty. **§3**: armed **once**, by the `Offered` transition — a repeat press by the same seat answers `ALREADY_OFFERED` (`Room.kt:315`) and the other seat's press agrees the rematch and takes the room to `PLAYING` — so a finished room's worst case moves from `finish + 5:00` to `finish + 6:00`, once, and neither player can push it further. **§4**: **nothing on any screen** — no countdown, no new string, confirming `ADR-0044` §7's refusal to build one and the vision's *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"*; a countdown would need a deadline on the wire and a `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump the floor does not. **§5**: `RematchNotice` and `RematchControl` ship as merged — the floor is how `RematchControl`'s *"A button that can only fail is not offered"* becomes true of the panel, without the client learning a deadline. **§6**: registers `DEC-167`. **Sixty** is twice the turn allowance (`RoomTimeouts.DEFAULT_TURN_MILLIS`, `ADR-0108` §1): thirty seconds is what the product gives a decision the player is **watching for**, at the table, with the clock visible, and [`ADR-0150`](ADR-0150-back-to-the-lobby-keeps-the-room.md) built a recipient who is none of those things. **Amends** `ADR-0152` §§3–4 in the one way §3 provides for. **Named cost:** a rival now holds a lever over a room that is not only theirs — one press extends a stranger's finished room by up to a minute, spending `ADR-0152` §4's *"independent of what either of them does"*; and this buys **sixty seconds of honesty, not honesty** — past the minute the button lies exactly as before, and §4 tells the recipient nothing |
| DEC-164 | **The architect's** — does the finished room's five minutes still cover the window [`ADR-0150`](ADR-0150-back-to-the-lobby-keeps-the-room.md) §1 opens, now that the room's holder may spend the whole of it on another screen? Registered 2026-09-10 by that ADR §7, which carried it in §Consequences as the cost **the human accepted with the call**. **Answered: yes — nothing changes.** | [ADR-0152](ADR-0152-the-finished-rooms-five-minutes-stand.md) — the reaping deadline never measured the holder's screen. Measured on `develop` at `d5ef7df8`: `isReapable` reads `now - lastActivityAt >= timeouts.finishedMillis` (`RoomRegistry.kt:836-844`) and **exactly two** writes move that field on a `FINISHED` room — `Room.finish(now)` (`Room.kt:238`) and `resume`'s `reconnect(seat).touch(now)` (`RoomRegistry.kt:390`), the server's only `touch(` call site. A wander is neither, and neither was sitting on the result screen: screens are the URL hash (`use-screen.ts:58-75`) over one socket per tab (`ADR-0032`), and the client's whole message set carries no ping, no keepalive and no *leave*. The room's life is therefore `[finish, finish + F)` before and after `ADR-0150`; §1 changed only how much of it a **presser** reaches, from none to all — the window a sitter always had. §1 spends allocated room life; it asks for none. §§1–2 leave the rule, the default and `ROOM_FINISHED_TIMEOUT_MILLIS` as merged; §3 closes the write set at two against future tickets; §4 keeps the `Offered` branch unstamped (`Room.kt:330`) and names it as where a floor would land; §6 mints one characterisation test in `RoomReapTest` and no production ticket. **Named cost:** a `Rematch` control that can lie to a player invited to press it — an offer at `finish + 4:59` answers `Failure(UNKNOWN_ROOM)` — left that way deliberately; the answering window's floor is zero; finished rooms accumulate as more tabs hold one and every reload restamps. Registers `DEC-165` for **the product owner** |
| DEC-154 | **The architect's** — by what call, at what point in `replyToJoinRoom`, and under what lock order is a held `WAITING` room **released** when its holder takes a seat in another room? Registered 2026-09-08 by [`ADR-0141`](ADR-0141-taking-a-seat-elsewhere-releases-the-room-you-were-holding.md) §8, which fixed the product half and refused the mechanism. **Answered: by `Room.abandon` through `RoomRegistry.mutate`, at the one point `replyToJoinRoom` already calls `join` — because the release moves *inside* the registry — with the joining player's own `stripeFor` stripe outermost and never two room mutexes held at once.** The release is a **state transition, not a removal**: `Room.join` already answers `UNKNOWN_ROOM` for an `ABANDONED` room *"before anything else"*, `heldRoom` already excludes it, `resume` already answers `null` for it and `isReapable` already collects it — so `ADR-0141` §2's observable is **inherited from shipped code** and no refusal path, `ProtocolError` value or `when` arm is added. Removal was rejected on `reap`'s own KDoc — folding it into `mutate` would mean *"two different shapes of 'done'"*, and a release built on it would be the **third** hand-rolled critical section, on the invite path. `room.abandon(room.lastActivityAt)` hands the room its own stamp back, so §8's *"no `lastActivityAt` is written anywhere but in the room being joined"* holds **verbatim**, and a released room is reapable strictly no later than it would have been as `WAITING`. **The rooms are found by a new `waitingRoomsHeldBy(player, except)`, never by `heldRoom`** — `HELD_ROOM_ORDER` sorts `PLAYING` first, so `heldRoom` would answer with **the room just joined** and the obvious implementation abandons the duel the player just sat down in; it returns codes and a **list**, because `replyToCreateRoom`'s `PLAYING` fall-through makes two held `WAITING` rooms reachable. The guard sits **inside** the writing critical section, `mutate`'s block stays non-suspending, and `ADR-0133` §4's *"a player stripe is always outermost"* is answered **yes, a stripe is taken** — the call site §4 named by name. `replyToJoinRoom` changes by **one identifier**, `join` → `joinReleasingHeld`; `resume` and `ALREADY_SEATED` are untouched; nothing crosses the wire. **Unblocks the ticket that implements `ADR-0141`**, which is now writable, with ten test rows handed to the planner. Costs named: **the joiner's first frame now waits on a stranger's lock**; a player stripe is held across suspension; `ABANDONED` stops meaning *a duel ran here*; a non-`now` value is passed to a parameter documented as `now`; the two-rooms-live window is narrowed, **not closed**; a `WAITING` and a `PLAYING` room can still be held at once, which is `ADR-0105` §1's unbuilt ticket; and `RoomRegistry` gains its first method that touches two rooms | [ADR-0147](ADR-0147-the-held-room-is-abandoned-after-the-seat-under-the-joiners-own-stripe.md) |
| DEC-157 | **The product owner's** — does *give this profile a password* owe an irreversibility sentence of its own? Registered 2026-09-09 by [`ADR-0143`](ADR-0143-irreversibility-is-said-last-and-never-coloured.md) §4, which found it by measuring rather than by being asked and declined to close it. **Answered: no — the act takes nothing from the player, and a handle is *held*, not *spent*.** **Derived from the vision** — *Positioning*'s *"**Dark, quiet, fast, minimal.**"*, read as *a quiet screen does not warn about a cost it does not impose* — with the substantive work done by merged ADRs: `ADR-0030` (*a claim adds a credential and moves nothing*), `ADR-0039`, `ADR-0031` §1 and `ADR-0130` §2 between them already settle what signing up costs, which is nothing. **§1: no sentence, no second press, and no copy asserting a handle is permanent or now unavailable to others.** **§2** applies `ADR-0143` §2 and states its trigger: an act owes those obligations when a player who performs it **ends up with less than they had**, never because the row it writes cannot be deleted. **§3** answers the register's second half — **held**: `ADR-0130` §2's word describes a string that has left its owner, a handle has left nobody, and §2's derivation is entirely about visibility, which measurement kills here (`ProfileResponse` has **seven** fields and no handle; `ADR-0031` §1's *"a handle is **never shown to anybody**"*). The decisive fact is `ADR-0031` §1's own refusal to decide — *"**That is not foreclosed** … but it is not built"* — so *you cannot change this later* would settle handle permanence in shipped copy, one document after `ADR-0130` §5 paid to delete `PERMANENCE_LINE`. **§4** completes the fourth row (*nothing to say*) and measures the two screen states; **§5:** one margin sentence on `account.html`, no frame changes, **no gate bought**; **§6** leaves the change-a-handle question where `ADR-0031` §1 left it and names the show-the-handle question **without registering it**; **§7:** reversed by a merged ADR deciding a handle is permanent, by one player asking to change theirs, or by the human. Supersedes nothing; corrects the word *spends* in `ADR-0143` §Consequences 5. Costs named: a mistyped handle is still forever and unmentioned; a forgotten handle with no recovery address has no path back; the answer is **conditional on a decision nobody has made, with nothing detecting the condition changing**; and the gap is closed by ruling rather than by shipping | [ADR-0146](ADR-0146-a-handle-is-held-not-spent.md) |
| DEC-158 | **The architect's** — [`ADR-0142`](ADR-0142-a-text-module-is-checked-against-the-rendered-card.md) §7's named reopening trigger had fired: does the **card text ⇒ module** direction now get a register, and of what shape? **Answered: no — §7's third bullet stands, because the drift that fired its trigger was not an instance of it.** Sorted by hole, the three findings on `rematch-states.html` are two **cardinality** failures and one direction failure. `RIVAL_OFFERS` and `ROOM_GONE` belong to `result/rematch-text.ts`, which spends `ADR-0142` §4's one card on `rematch-panel.html` while the same five sentences are drawn on `rematch-states.html`; replayed at `f20d07ed`, **three of five pass and two fail**, so the merged forward direction catches two of the three on its own once it is allowed to name that card. The same bug already produces a **false passing reason** — `account-text.ts` registers `SIGN_IN_REFUSED` `notCarded` as *"no frame on this card draws a refused sign-in"*, true of `account.html` and false of `sign-in.html`, whose second frame draws that sentence exactly. So `ADR-0148` amends §4 rather than §7: **a row is one (module, card) pair and a module may hold several** (six rows); **`notCarded` becomes a checked claim**, defaultable per row and asserted **absent** from that row's card by whole-unit equality; **coverage becomes total over cards**, with six in `NO_MODULE` and an unpaired card allowed **no** carded value as a whole unit. `RematchControl.tsx:71`'s bare literal becomes `OFFER_STANDING` — one string, not a rule, since nothing reads JSX. The reverse direction is **priced, not feared**: 746 text units across twelve cards, and 386 of them on two cards whose modules export **0** string constants. The trigger is re-armed narrower — a stale sentence on a card a row **already names**, that is no module's export | [ADR-0148](ADR-0148-a-module-names-every-card-that-draws-it-and-every-card-is-registered.md) |
| DEC-161 | **The architect's** — what does **minting** actually require now? `ADR-0091` §3 said a minting card *"is worked interactively with the human, because taste does not survive a verify block"*, and measured across all **86** `module: design` tickets the four labelled `minting` are `review: standard` and were **dispatched and merged as ordinary PRs** — one of them quoting §3's sentence in its own body. **Answered: the merged practice is ratified and §3's minting branch is superseded — minting is an ordinary dispatched ticket at a stricter tier, and the interactive session is un-mandated rather than abolished; nothing is owed as repair.** Nothing could execute that branch: there is no `await human` state, `ADR-0091` §2 makes the card the split's **first** ticket so the stall is the whole story's, and §3 refused that same trade one sentence later for the verdict. **One exception, drawn where the evidence is**: a dispatched mint may not add a colour or a step in a named ladder, because inserting into a ladder changes what every existing step means — and it blocks nothing, since the ticket draws at the nearest declared step and says so. A mint also mints only what the same diff draws and states what each value derives from; `module: design` plus `minting` ⇒ `review: standard`; and a story that merges a mint records whether the pane verdict has been given, which measured is recorded nowhere since 2026-08-15. **No verdict moves**: `ADR-0024` §3 is untouched | [ADR-0149](ADR-0149-minting-is-dispatched-and-a-ladder-value-waits-for-the-human.md) |
