| DEC-151 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism does a display name **change**? Registered 2026-09-07 by [`ADR-0130`](ADR-0130-a-name-can-be-changed-and-the-name-it-leaves-is-spent.md), which fixed what a player sees and designed none of the machinery under it: the permanence trigger, the statements and their transaction, the vacated `name_registry` row under `ADR-0130` §4's constraint on `ADR-0052` §6's derived bit, what `PUT /api/me/name` answers now that `403 AlreadyNamed` has no cause, and **whether the write needs a budget** | [`ADR-0134`](ADR-0134-a-rename-spends-before-it-replaces.md) — **a rename spends before it replaces, and no name is ever released.** **§1**: `player_display_name_is_permanent()` is dropped and re-created as **`player_display_name_is_never_released()`**, carrying `ADR-0051` §3's body **minus one disjunct** — a name may leave a player only if the registry has already spent it — so the exception becomes the rule; `NULL → name`, the identical-name no-op and `name → NULL` are unchanged and `retire_display_name` is not edited. The names change because a schema saying *permanent* to a `psql` operator is `PERMANENCE_LINE` with a smaller audience. **§2**: **four statements in one transaction** — `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` (the profile is locked before any registry row, the order the takedown already takes, and the interlock replacing `AND display_name IS NULL`), the `TAKEN` insert, the retirement of the string being left, then the column. `SetNameResult.AlreadyNamed` is **deleted**, `ADR-0051` §2's rollback now protects two strings, and statement 3 carries **no** `AND reason = 'TAKEN'` so an impossible row raises rather than passing quietly. **§3**: the vacated row moves **`TAKEN → REPLACED`**, a fourth `reason`, with **`retired_from` populated** — spent for everyone under a fold that ignores `reason`, while recording which of the two ways the name left its holder, which is the fact `ADR-0130`'s own reversibility argument needs; the word is not `RELEASED`, because that is the answer `ADR-0130` §2 refused. **§4**: the removal bit reads **false** for a renamer for **two** independent reasons, and `PostgresProfileReads`/`PostgresProfileWrites` change not at all. **§5**: `PUT /api/me/name` answers `200 \| 400 \| 401 \| 409 \| 429` — `403` is deleted rather than repurposed, and a case-only self-rename is `409`. **§6**: **yes, the write is budgeted**, and the budget **meters spending rather than trying** — `AttemptBudget` keyed by `PlayerId`, `ADR-0074` §2's reserve-then-refund, every refusal refunded because `ADR-0051` §2 says it burns nothing, numbers unreachable by a human so it stays a rate limit and not the quota `ADR-0130` §1 refuses. **§7**: nothing crosses the socket — `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move and `STORY-1409`'s tickets are **not** `atomic:`. Costs named: a player can **never** change the case of their own name; the write holds a `player` row lock across a registry insert; one statement became four; an idempotent retry spends budget |
| DEC-152 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism does a browser **stop owning the profile it owned**, at a sign-out and only there, and how does the confirmation learn **before** it acts which of the two sign-outs this is? Registered 2026-09-07 by [`ADR-0131`](ADR-0131-signing-out-of-your-own-account-hands-the-browser-a-new-profile.md) §7, which fixed the product half, named three shapes and chose none | [`ADR-0135`](ADR-0135-the-server-says-which-sign-out-this-is-and-the-browser-forgets-one-key.md) — **the server answers one boolean, the browser forgets one key, and the new profile comes from the handshake that already ships.** §3 forbids every server act that would be the abandonment, so the only lever left is what the browser holds (`ADR-0002` licenses pulling it); §7 says the *case* is a server fact, so the mechanism is necessarily split across the boundary and **must fail safe**, because a browser that keeps a profile it should have left is today's visible defect while one that leaves a profile it should have kept is `EPIC-04`'s original harm. **§1: `player.device_id` does not exist** — `V7__device_binding.sql` dropped it (`ADR-0049` §1, §7) — and nothing is written to `device_binding`, `player`, `credential`, `duel` or `duel_result`; the row left behind stays **live**. **§2: `forgetDeviceId` in `device-id.ts`**, called from `signOut` alone, in the abandoning case alone, before `reload()` and whatever the server answered; **exactly three keys move** and the `ADR-0119` §5 skipped bit is kept (`ADR-0131` §6). **§3: `Identity.Anonymous` on the next `Hello` mints the new profile** — shipped, tested, unchanged — so it is created **at that handshake, not at the press**, and a browser that closes the tab writes no rows. **§4: `GET /api/me/device`**, session-required, answering `DeviceStandingResponse(signOutHandsANewProfile)`: `true` **exactly when the `X-Device-Id` presented on the same request does not resolve, through a live binding, to a player other than the caller**, computed by a new `IdentityResolver.namesPlayer` with **`resolve` and all five `Identity` cases byte-unchanged**. It compares two credentials the caller already holds — both player ids are already readable in two existing requests — so it is no enumeration surface, and a foreign device id can only move the caller's own answer to `false`. **§5: `true` is unreachable while any live binding names another player**, so `ADR-0131` §2's forbidden outcome cannot be expressed; the revoked-binding browser, already handed a fresh profile silently today, now reads `true` and is told the truth. **§6: unknown is a keep** — and the read **must not go through `readFromApi`**. **§7: `signedIn` never answers which sign-out this is**, and the words stay the card's. **§8: nothing crosses the socket** — `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move and `STORY-1410`'s ticket is **not** `atomic:`. Costs: the abandonment is **not durable** (a restored `localStorage` keeps the route, as `ADR-0131` §3 already said) and **no undo or grace window is added**, so the forgotten-password cost stays exactly where `ADR-0131` left it; a second account-screen request; a route for one bit; a fact read on one screen and spent at a later press; `STORY-1408` and `STORY-1410` serialised. Registers no decision |
| DEC-131 | **The product owner's** — may a display name be **changed after it is set**, and what becomes of the one given up: released for anyone to take, held by its former owner, or retired? Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 1c, against an `ADR-0029` whose title says *permanent* and whose permanence is machinery — an index where *"the index is the reservation"* and a trigger — plus `ADR-0051`'s registry that no string ever leaves | [`ADR-0130`](ADR-0130-a-name-can-be-changed-and-the-name-it-leaves-is-spent.md) — **yes, and the name it leaves behind is spent.** Two halves from two places. ***That* a name may be changed is the human's, recorded verbatim** (2026-09-06: *"player name can be changes at any time in accounts settings"*), reversing their own `DEC-017` answer, and the ADR does not choose it. ***What becomes of the string* is derived** from *"**A leaderboard.** Ranked results over a season."* read as `ADR-0067` §1 read it — the row is **text and leads nowhere**, so the name is a row's only identity, and `ADR-0039` makes history a **live join**, so a reissued string prints a second player onto the first's finished duels. **§1**: a change is a `set` under every existing rule — canonicalisation, characters, the fold, `ADR-0038`'s screening — **no quota, no cooling-off, no confirmation**, and it never leaves the player nameless. **§2**: **retired, not released and not held** — spent for everyone including its former owner, which is `ADR-0038`'s *retired forever, including the player it was taken from* covering a second way a name leaves a player. **§3**: **history shows the name as it is**; no row is rewritten because no row holds one, the old name appears nowhere, nothing marks a changed row, and the rename is invisible at the table. **§4**: nobody is told, and `ADR-0052` §1's removal notice never fires for a rename. **§5**: `PERMANENCE_LINE` leaves the product, replaced by *can be changed later* and *the one you give up is gone for good*, said before the send. **§6**: one form on the **account screen**, doing both acts — which answers the seam `ADR-0125` left open. **Supersedes** `ADR-0029` §4's rename clause, its title's *permanent* and §5's `403`; `ADR-0051` §3's *no exception at all*; `ADR-0119` §3's obligation 2. Costs stated rather than argued away: a rename is **one-way**, the namespace burns per `PUT`, a rival can never find you again, and other players' records change under them. Registers **`DEC-151`**, the architect's |
| DEC-132 | **The product owner's** — does **signing out abandon the anonymous profile and issue a new one**? Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 1d. [`ADR-0012`](ADR-0012-device-bound-anonymous-profiles.md) binds a profile to a device id, `ADR-0027` puts the session above it, and `ADR-0037` calls the device *a credential until revoked* — so today a sign-out returns the browser to the profile it already had, coins included. A browser handed a **fresh** profile at every sign-out is the first mechanism in this product that can strand a duel coin, which is the exact loss the offer `ADR-0125` **has** deleted existed to prevent — so nothing in the product volunteers that warning any more, and the account screen a player chooses to open is the only place it is made. `ADR-0049`'s *a device binding is a row and revoking is final* and `ADR-0050`'s *signs out everywhere but here* are what the mechanism has to be written against | [`ADR-0131`](ADR-0131-signing-out-of-your-own-account-hands-the-browser-a-new-profile.md) — **yes, when the account is the profile this browser owns; no, when it is somebody else's.** Both halves are **the human's and neither is chosen**: the abandoning half verbatim 2026-09-06 (*"if logout you comeback to new annonumus profile"*), the keeping half verbatim 2026-08-14 (*"i would like to keep anonimous accout if user log out"*, `ADR-0030` §2). What the ADR chooses is **which sentence governs which case** — the newer one sits in an item whose every other clause is about this browser's own profile and its promotion, and it never mentions signing into an account the browser does not own — and it says so as **a reading**, with the one sentence that corrects it left for the human. **§1's three rows:** own profile → a new, empty anonymous profile; somebody else's account → back to the profile owned, unchanged; no profile at all → a first-time visitor, already true. A browser is handed a fresh profile **at most once per promotion**, because the second sign-out is row two. **§2 is why `EPIC-14`'s *new way to lose coins* framing does not hold**: to be in row one you signed in to that profile, so it has a password — **nothing is ever abandoned that cannot be signed back into**, and `DEC-152` may not build a route that is. **§3: nothing is revoked, deleted, moved or destroyed**; `ADR-0049`'s finality means **nothing here, because a sign-out is not a revocation**, on `ADR-0050`'s own *"Revocation is final forever; signing out is the cheapest act in the product."* **§4** licenses one abandonment at one confirmed press and keeps `ADR-0027` §5's stale-client harm forbidden. **§5: the player is told before it happens**, on the confirmation `SignOutControl` already renders — no new surface (`ADR-0125` §2, *Positioning*), two obligations and `ADR-0125` §3's three refusals re-applied, licensed by `ADR-0037`'s *the account screens must state which routes are live*. **§6** rules `ADR-0119` §5's skipped bit **unamended**: a new profile is not an answer, so a player who skipped is not asked again and plays as `No name` — weighed, not inherited. **Supersedes `ADR-0030` §3's outcome clause and its *keeps its device id* bullet, and §8's *not on sign-out* and *clears the token and only the token*, for row one only**; supersedes nothing in `ADR-0012`, `ADR-0027`, `ADR-0037`, `ADR-0049` or `ADR-0050`. Costs named: a coin count seen falling to **zero** with one sentence of explanation; **a forgotten password now loses the account in one press that doing nothing used to undo** — the strongest argument against the decision, stated as such; `SIGN_OUT_WARNING` false the day the behaviour lands; empty profiles accumulating under `ADR-0039`; and two sign-outs behaving differently under one label. Registers **`DEC-152`**, the architect's |
| DEC-147 | **The architect's** — by what means does the client learn that the profile it holds carries **no credential**? Registered 2026-09-06 by [`ADR-0125`](ADR-0125-the-account-screen-names-the-anonymous-profile-and-owns-the-door.md) §4, which rules that the account screen may name a profile *anonymous* only when it has been told, and never from the absence of a session token. Measured there: `GET /api/me` carries six fields — `playerId`, `coinBalance`, `displayName`, `displayNameRemoved`, `deviceRouteLive`, `hasRecoveryEmail` — and none of them is this one, while `signedIn` supports only the sound direction (a token implies a password, `AccountScreen.tsx`'s own derivation) and is a false positive for anonymity in two reachable states: a `201` sign-up whose follow-up sign-in failed (`sign-up.ts` answers `signed-up` regardless, by design) and a player who signed out in this browser — the same converse-of-`signedIn` mistake `TASK-120601` filed as a defect on this screen on 2026-08-29. Whether the answer is a seventh field on `GET /api/me`, or something else, and what it may be called without becoming an enumeration surface, is the architect's; `ADR-0125` chooses no mechanism and moves no wire. `ADR-0031` §6.3 (no endpoint returns an address) and `ADR-0027` §6 (an over-budget answer is indistinguishable from a wrong secret) are the posture it is written against | [`ADR-0132`](ADR-0132-the-profile-says-whether-it-holds-a-password.md) on 2026-09-07 — **`ProfileResponse` gains a seventh field, `hasPassword: Boolean`, and the client derives nothing.** True exactly when a `credential` row exists for this player with `kind = CredentialKind.PASSWORD`; **no default value**, declared last. Named for the **kind** and not the concept: `ADR-0027` §1 keeps `credential` open to a passphrase or a third-party subject, so a `hasCredential` would one day read `true` for a player whose only stated way out is the password form, and this name freezes nothing about `DEC-027`. It is **computed on every response that carries the DTO and never a literal** — a fourth correlated `EXISTS` in `PostgresProfileReads.PROFILE_OF_SQL` *and in both statements in `PostgresProfileWrites`*, so `PUT /api/me/name`'s `200` cannot make `reportNameWrite` turn a claimed player anonymous — with the kind **bound** from `CredentialKind.PASSWORD` rather than spelled as SQL, because a renamed constant would otherwise leave a statement that compiles and answers `false` for everyone. **Told only to the player it is about**: no other DTO carries it, no route answers it for a player the caller did not resolve to, nothing filters, sorts or counts on it, and it reaches no log line, no `ServerMessage` and no engine type — the anti-enumeration posture is satisfied by `GET /api/me` resolving identity first (`ADR-0027` §4) and `ProfileReads` being `PlayerId`-keyed (`ADR-0030` §4). On the client, **a missing field is `unavailable`, never `false`** — `profileFromBody` requires the boolean, which is the one line that keeps `ADR-0125` §4's *not told at all* case reachable — and the screen renders §3's block on `!hasPassword`, consulting `signedIn` for it never. **Nothing shipped is re-keyed**: `showPasswordRoute`, `showSignUp`, `showSignInDoor` and `showAttach` keep their predicates, per `ADR-0125` §4's own words. **The held profile is re-read, never edited**: the provider re-reads `GET /api/me` after an outcome the server has confirmed changed the profile, and today that is exactly one — `signUp`'s `signed-up`, because sign-in and sign-out already `reload` and `sign-up.ts` passes `noReload` on purpose; without it a field alone would print *anonymous* beside `This profile now has a password.` **HTTP, not the socket**: `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move, `docs/protocol-versions.md` gains no row, `ADR-0047`'s lock and `ADR-0070`'s probe do not engage, and `STORY-1408`'s ticket is **not `atomic:`** on that account. **Contradicts nothing in `ADR-0050` §4**, whose *"no `ProfileResponse` field"* inventories its own diff — `hasRecoveryEmail` landed eight days later under `ADR-0031` §6.3. Costs named: a fourth `EXISTS` on the hottest authenticated read, one extra round trip per sign-up, and **§3 is a reviewed rule with no gate behind it**. Names a defect for the planner rather than fixing it: `PostgresProfileWrites` passes a literal `false` for `hasRecoveryEmail`, so setting a name prints `RECOVERY_OFF` to a player whose recovery is on. Leaves `DEC-090` and `DEC-027` exactly where they were. **Unblocks `STORY-1408`'s anonymous-state ticket** |
| DEC-110 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism is a player who asks for a second room while already holding a seat found, and answered? Registered open 2026-09-01 by [`ADR-0105`](ADR-0105-one-duel-at-a-time-and-the-refusal-hands-back-the-duel.md) §6 for the `PLAYING` case and **widened to the `WAITING` case** on 2026-09-06 by [`ADR-0124`](ADR-0124-one-waiting-room-and-play-again-hands-it-back.md) §6. **Answered: one lookup, `RoomRegistry.heldRoom(player)`, and it is a scan.** §1 defines *held* as `seatOf` non-null **and** state `WAITING` or `PLAYING` — `FINISHED`/`ABANDONED` excluded by the method rather than by its callers, since a caller that forgot would break `ADR-0105` §2's finished row and `offerRematch` — returning the whole `Room`, under a **total order** (`PLAYING` first, then oldest `lastActivityAt`, then lowest code) so no answer depends on hash iteration order. §2: a **scan, not an index**, reading each `Holder.room` through the `@Volatile` field `get(code)` and `reap`'s unlocked pass already read — the rooms are the truth about where a player sits (`ADR-0002`), an index is a second one whose leak is a player who can never open a room again, and the swap stays a one-body change behind the same method. §3: `heldOrOpen` is `heldRoom ?: create` under a **per-player striped `Mutex`** (`SeatOwnership`'s shape, a second array), which makes `ADR-0124` §1's *one waiting room* true rather than usually true. §4 answers the deadlock question: the scan takes **no lock**, `heldOrOpen` takes one across **no suspension point**, *a player stripe is always outermost* — and it is structurally enforced, because `mutate`'s blocks are not `suspend` while `withLock` is. §5: the seat is `seatOf` off the returned snapshot, **never the literal `0`**, and `RoomMembership` is repointed at the room the answer names, disarming `ADR-0118`'s window at the source it named. §6: the `JoinRoom` guard fires only when the held room is `PLAYING` **and its code differs from the one asked for**, or every reconnect breaks. **§7 spends a version on the `PLAYING` half and none on the `WAITING` one**: `ALREADY_IN_DUEL` is a new `ProtocolError` value, `ProtocolError` is a generated declaration, so `ADR-0047` §2's fingerprint moves — and no existing value can carry it without asserting something false about the room that was asked for, which `ADR-0022`'s no-oracle rule forbids. §8 orders the frames `RoomJoined` → `resume`'s frames → `Failure` **last**, on `ADR-0044` §5's precedent, and checks `resume`'s `reconnect().touch()` clause by clause against `ADR-0105` §6's *nothing moves*. §9 splits it: **`STORY-1416` is unblocked, moves no wire, is not `atomic:`** and leaves the `PLAYING` case falling through to `create`; `ADR-0105` §1's own ticket is `atomic:` and sized by `ADR-0070`'s probe. Registers nothing | [`ADR-0133`](ADR-0133-the-room-a-player-holds-is-scanned-for.md) on 2026-09-07 |
| DEC-138 | **The product owner's** — **does a rematch offer follow the rival off the result screen, and as what?** Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 5, on the human's *"rematch flow - when rematch offered it shoud be shown as popup; it shoud appear on any screen"*. [`ADR-0044`](ADR-0044-a-rematch-is-one-intent-and-one-room-fact.md) already settled the intent and the room fact; what was unowned was the **surface** — `RematchControl` is constructed in `Lobby.tsx`'s `state.outcome !== null` branch and appears in no other, and this product had no modal of any kind. The situation was one `EPIC-13` created on purpose: [`ADR-0112`](ADR-0112-only-a-running-duel-refuses-another-screen.md) §3 lets a `FINISHED` room honour an ask for another screen, and booked the resulting silence as a **cost** | [`ADR-0123`](ADR-0123-a-standing-rematch-offer-follows-the-rival.md) on 2026-09-06 — **a standing rematch offer follows the rival, and it never takes the screen.** The ***whether*** is the human's, recorded verbatim; the ***what*** is derived from *"We play a full heads-up match. Someone wins. **We hit Rematch.**"* and *Positioning*'s *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."* An offer **from the rival** follows a player onto every screen they can be on while they still hold the room (every `Screen` but `first`, no carve-out); a player who **left** the room is followed nowhere. It is a **panel above the screen and never a modal** — nothing beneath it is scrimmed or disabled, it takes no focus, no keypress the player was already making can answer it, it makes no sound, and it never retires itself on a timer. Only an **incoming** offer follows, and only where the room's own screen is not showing, so one fact never has two live surfaces. **No new string:** *Your rival offers a rematch*, `Rematch`, *Not now*, the dealing sentence and *That duel room is gone.* are all merged already. It stands until accepted, dismissed, or refused with `UNKNOWN_ROOM` (`ADR-0044` §6). **Dismissal hides the surface, never the offer** — nothing is sent, the rival is told nothing, silence stays the decline, and nothing is stored, so a reload brings it back. The accept ends at the table by `ADR-0112` §4 and `ADR-0114` §3, already shipped. No wire, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, no server file, no schema, no stored key. The card is **minting** under `ADR-0091` §3 and draws every state including the reduced-motion form (`ADR-0115`). Deliberately unbuilt: a decline or withdrawal on the wire, a countdown, a notice for any other room fact, and any notice while a duel runs. Registers `DEC-144` — the architect's — the mechanism. Unblocks `STORY-1415` |
| DEC-139 | **The product owner's** — **does pressing play again return the host to the room they already hold?** Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 6, on the human's report — *"i go back to lobby and press play again. in this case i shoud be back to the same room"* — which is the **direction**, so `ADR-0124` records it rather than choosing it and decides what the report does not reach. **Answered: yes, and the server is what returns them.** While a player holds a `WAITING` seat, a `CreateRoom` from them **opens no second room**: they are handed back the one they hold, same code, same seat, room left exactly as found, and **nothing is refused**, so `ADR-0073` §3's promise is kept rather than broken — *"and it brings you back"* is now true twice over. **The browser goes on forgetting**: `ADR-0072` §3 stands byte-unchanged, and there is **no client change at all** — keeping the memory across `Back to the lobby` would rejoin the room on the next boot and restore `ADR-0073`'s room with no door, and the memory cannot help a device that never heard of the room, which the server can. **Nothing is said and no string is added** (`ADR-0110` §6 untouched, on *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."*), with the observation that would reopen it stated. The lifetime is unchanged — ten minutes from `Room.open`, and a return does **not** restart it. Amends `ADR-0105` §2's `WAITING` row only; extends `DEC-110` to the `WAITING` case, which now also blocks `STORY-1416`; registers `DEC-145`. Costs named: **a host cannot get a fresh code for up to ten minutes** and the product still has no way to close a room; a press that appears to do nothing; and two live states now answering the same press differently | [ADR-0124](ADR-0124-one-waiting-room-and-play-again-hands-it-back.md) |
| DEC-111 | **The product owner's** — may one player hold more than one `WAITING` room at once? Registered open 2026-09-01 by [`ADR-0105`](ADR-0105-one-duel-at-a-time-and-the-refusal-hands-back-the-duel.md), which answered `DEC-109` for the **running** case and named this hole in its own Consequences. **Answered: no.** Derived from `docs/vision.md`'s one-sentence version — *"Two people, **one link**, one heads-up poker match, one winner"* — with *What it is not*'s *"Not a multi-table poker room"* and *What it is*'s *"A counter of duels won."*: a second waiting room orphans the code the host may already have sent, and a rival who clicks it starts a duel the host is not at, which `ADR-0013`'s window, `ADR-0023`'s absent seat and `ADR-0014`'s coin settle against them — the counter then counts a duel nobody played. **Answered by construction rather than by a rule**: a `WAITING` seat has exactly one source (`Room.open` is its only constructor, `RoomRegistry.create` has one production call site, no transition returns a room to `WAITING`, and the invariant keeps `guest` `null`), so what a second `CreateRoom` does *is* how many waiting rooms a player may hold — and it hands back the first. **Not a refusal**, which is what keeps `ADR-0105` §2's stated reason intact. Answered together with `DEC-139` because they are one question read from opposite ends. **The harm is narrowed, not closed**: a holder of a waiting room who takes a seat in **another** room by invite is the one route left into two duels at once, registered as `DEC-145` | [ADR-0124](ADR-0124-one-waiting-room-and-play-again-hands-it-back.md) |
| DEC-130 | **The product owner's** — does the **account screen name the anonymous state** (the human's *"Anonymous Account"*) and become the **only** door to promotion, retiring the post-win offer entirely? Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 1b, on an annotation that strikes the whole offer block through with the single word *remove* | [`ADR-0125`](ADR-0125-the-account-screen-names-the-anonymous-profile-and-owns-the-door.md) — **yes to both, and the answer is two parts from two places.** *That* the offer goes is **the human's, recorded verbatim** and not chosen: the entire block on `edits4.png` is struck through with one word. **§1 retires it whole** — surface, strings, `offerAccount`'s three terms, `DuelResult`'s `offer` prop, `pd.accountOfferSettled`, its module and its row in the one-module gate, which go in the same diff or the row reddens naming a deleted file. **§2: nothing replaces it** — no banner, no badge, no reminder, on any screen; the standing door is the `Account` control the front door already renders unconditionally, so a win is **two presses** from the password form and §2 refuses to shorten it, because a pointer on the result screen is the struck block in a smaller font. **§3: the account screen names the profile *anonymous*** and says three things — what the profile is, that it lives in this browser, and that a password keeps **every duel coin and every duel** because nothing moves (`ADR-0030` §1, which is `EPIC-14` item 1e's *sentence on a screen*) — words to the card, inside three refusals: **not a tier** (no badge, no *real* account, no rank a password promotes you to), it states rather than urges, and the heading stays `Account`. **§4: the screen asserts the state only when it has been told**, never from the absence of a session token — which registers **`DEC-147`**, the architect's, and holds §3's block until it lands. **§5: the front door stops describing an absence** — `ProfileStrip`'s `no-profile` branch renders nothing, `No profile yet.` leaves the product, the `profile` branch untouched. **§6: *anonymous* is a state, never a name** — `ADR-0058`'s `No name` stands and a player may be named *and* anonymous. **Supersedes `ADR-0036`'s offer block, and `ADR-0085`, `ADR-0086` and `ADR-0116` in full** — the first supersession in this repository. The cost is stated rather than argued away: the product adopts the *"optional forever, with no prompt"* shape `ADR-0036` §Alternatives rejected, so some players will lose coins a prompt would have saved. **Answers `DEC-089` by deletion.** Leaves the credential's shape (`ADR-0041`) to the human, and `DEC-090`, `DEC-094`, `DEC-131` and `DEC-132` exactly where they were |
| DEC-089 | **The product owner's** — on the `result` screen, should a post-verdict account-claim nudge share the verdict's own type weight, or should the verdict stay the single largest thing on the screen? | DEC-134 | **The product owner's** — may the table **mark the five cards that won**, in the winner's hand and on the board? Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 2b, on the human's annotation over the duel table — *"winning combination higlited, winning cards + board card that make combination"* — one of three clauses on one arrow, the other two being `DEC-133` (answered by `ADR-0120`) and the pot's travel (a card under `ADR-0115`) | [`ADR-0126`](ADR-0126-the-table-shows-the-cards-and-marks-none-of-them.md) — **no; the table shows the cards and marks none of them.** Every card is drawn like every other card of its kind: at a showdown, at a fold, at every street, in either seat, on the board and for **both** winners of a split, **the five that made the winning hand are drawn exactly as the two that did not**. A mark is anything separating one drawn card from another by *what it did* rather than *what it is* — ring, border, glow, tint, shadow, lift, scale, opacity, reordering, badge, connector, keyable class, `data-*`, `title`, or any `aria-label` beyond `card-text.ts`'s rank and suit — and **dimming the cards that did not play is the same statement**. `ADR-0095` §3 is **applied, not reopened**: a highlight prints no string, so `HAND_TALK` could never see it, but the fact is the same kind — a reading of seven public cards, by the product, at the table, live — and *these five played* is one string away from *two pair, kings and jacks*. Licensed by *What it is*'s *"replayed and **analysed afterwards**"*, dated **v0.4** while v0.1 is *"one complete duel, rematch"*; the human's annotation is the report, not the ruling, exactly as `ADR-0120` read the same drawing the same day. **No wire field, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` step, no server file, no engine file**; `BestHand.cards` keeps its value and loses its stated reason, the ADR being the correction of record for a KDoc wrong since August. `STORY-1412` loses the wire move and becomes one gate — *the drawing of a card does not depend on whether it played* — plus one line on the showdown card; **`STORY-1413` no longer waits on it**. Costs named: the player who cannot read seven cards is charged a **second** time, something the human asked for in writing is declined, the deferred field gets dearer, and a gate must be written to hold a nothing. Reversal keeps `ADR-0095` §6's trigger and must settle **the mark and the name together**. **Registers nothing** — a *no* has no wire question, so `DEC-148` is not opened |
| DEC-135 | **The product owner's** — when it is not the player's turn, does the action bar stand as **disabled controls** rather than the sentence `Waiting for your rival…` (`ActionBar.tsx:290`)? Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 3c, on the human's annotation *"show disabled controllers"* over the boxed panel on `edits3.png` | [`ADR-0127`](ADR-0127-a-control-stands-only-for-a-decision-the-server-has-opened.md) — **no: a control stands only for a decision the server has opened.** The bar keeps its box and both reserved rows and holds **one still sentence**; it draws no button, chip or amount field — live, faint, outlined or placeholder — outside a turn. Measured: `PendingTurn.legalActions` is written by `case "YourTurn"` alone (`duel-state.ts:282-292`) and cleared by the next `Snapshot`, so **the client holds no legal actions outside a turn** and every verb or figure on a faint bar would be invented or stale. Heads-up the coming decision is `{CHECK, BET}` or `{FOLD, CALL, RAISE}` and the client cannot know which, so **verbs-without-amounts is the bigger claim, not the smaller one**: a bar showing `Fold` where nothing is owed teaches the interface wrong, and a greyed `Call 400` is a number for a turn that does not exist (`ADR-0111`, `bar-no-derivation.test.tsx`). The faint treatment already ships with one meaning — the card's *Sent — waiting on the server* — and keeps it. Licensed by `docs/vision.md`'s *Positioning* (***"Lichess and Chess.com, not PokerStars. Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."***) applied through `CLAUDE.md`'s *a client may never assert a game fact*, **which actions are legal** being the fact. **The human's words are recorded verbatim and are not followed**, and §7 makes the overrule two sentences. Costs named: the human is told no; **`STORY-1406` has no work in it** and is retired rather than split; the sentence stays the third statement of a fact the plate already makes twice; a player still cannot see what they will be able to do. Also answers `DEC-108` |
| DEC-108 | **The product owner's** — when the table says *The duel is paused.*, may the action bar stay enabled? Raised 2026-09-01 by [`STORY-1214`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1214-a-duel-played-by-hand-deadlocked-on-presence.md), where the human obeyed the sentence and waited out a duel the server would have accepted actions in, every control reading `disabled: false`. [`ADR-0046`](ADR-0046-the-table-says-away-timed-out-and-back.md) §6 had declined it **by name**; [`ADR-0113`](ADR-0113-the-turn-clock-is-derived-state-and-the-sweep-plays-the-seat.md) then deleted the sentence and recorded that *"whoever reaches it will find its ground gone"* | [`ADR-0127`](ADR-0127-a-control-stands-only-for-a-decision-the-server-has-opened.md) §4 — **answered on both halves, not merely mooted.** *May* wait *and* you may act *be sayable at one moment?* **No** — a screen that tells a player to wait while the server would accept their press is a defect whatever the `disabled` attribute says, and `STORY-1214` measured the cost in a stopped duel a script would not have noticed. *Which gives way?* **The notice, never the bar** — the bar is drawn from the server's own `LegalActions` for an open decision, so a contradicting notice is copy about a state that no longer holds. That is also the route the product already took: `ADR-0113` §7 deleted the pause and `TASK-130911` removed the line that named it, and `DUEL_PAUSED` is verified absent from every `.kt`/`.ts`/`.tsx` and from `ProtocolError`. **No ticket falls out**: nothing in the product exhibits the case today, and the rule makes the next such notice a defect on the day it is written. §5 names the one edge left open — the sweep race, where a late press may still be `Rejected` — as a boundary no screen can be ahead of rather than a standing offer the product has decided to refuse |
| DEC-140 | **The product owner's** — **does the invite offer a copy control in a browser that exposes no Clipboard API**, and if so by what promise? Raised 2026-09-06 while splitting `EPIC-14` item 3b, from the annotation *copy btn* on the invite-link box — where the control **already shipped** and was hidden by `CopyLink`'s `!navigator.clipboard` guard on both of the human's devices, the origin being `http://192.168.0.142:5173` | [`ADR-0128`](ADR-0128-the-copy-control-is-never-absent-and-a-press-that-cannot-copy-hands-over-the-selection.md) — **the copy control is never absent, and a press that cannot copy hands over the selection.** The annotation is the report, not the specification; the answer is derived from [`docs/vision.md`](../vision.md)'s success condition — ***"Send a link. She opens it in a browser. We play a full heads-up match"***, with *"Everything else is downstream of that moment"* — and from *"the author wanted to play quick heads-up duels against his sister"*, which happens on a home network over plain `http`, the exact environment the shipped rule hid the control in. **§1: `CopyLink` renders in every browser** — no origin, browser or permission state leaves the invite as the box alone. **§2:** one name, `Copy the link`, byte-identical everywhere, because two names for one act is the failure `ADR-0046` opens by naming. **§3: two visible outcomes, not three** — `Link copied.` where a copy happened; otherwise the invite-link box takes focus with the whole link selected and the shipped `Copy it from the box above.` renders, and **a missing API and a refused write are one outcome** because one sentence cannot mean two things. The promise is *one press leaves the link either in your clipboard or selected under your cursor, and the screen says which* — keepable in every browser, which is why it is the one made. **§4: `Link copied.` is said only when a copy actually happened**, never of a selection and never of an attempt whose result cannot be read. **§5: no string is added** — `ADR-0110` §6's ten-string enumeration stands byte-unchanged as a set and its escape clause is unspent; three of the ten simply become reachable outside a secure context. **§6:** the card owes **three** host-alone variants, and `design/screens/duel-table.html`'s `Host alone — no clipboard API` frame is retired with the state it drew. **Amends `ADR-0110` in two clauses and nowhere else**: §5's *"the control absent where `navigator.clipboard` is"* and §8.1's fourth variant. Load-bearing reasoning: **no label can promise a copy in any browser** — `writeText` can be refused where the API exists, which is why a second feedback line was written — so the truth belongs after the press, and *it might not copy* would otherwise argue for hiding the control everywhere; and **the absence is invisible to every proof this project runs**, since `ADR-0117` puts them on `http://localhost:4173`, a potentially-trustworthy origin, so the control is present in every instrument and absent on the only device a second player has ever held. Costs named: the button sometimes does not copy and says so; the fallback is one gesture, not zero; `Copy it from the box above.` acquires a second meaning and keeps a spatial claim a frame could falsify; and the two shipped **absence** assertions invert rather than being deleted. `DEC-126` is untouched in both directions — TLS is neither required by the invite nor argued for by it. Registers **`DEC-150`** (the architect's), which blocks nothing |

Derived from `docs/vision.md`'s first success condition — *"Send a link. She opens it in a browser. Verified before deciding: `Room.kt:212–223` seats and calls `MatchState.start` in one expression and `RoomRegistry.join` deals inside the same critical section, so no host press exists on that path. Costs named rather than hidden — the state's zero-new-strings property is spent and this ADR is the precedent the next request will cite; it is the first string here that explains rather than states; and nothing but this document couples the promise to `Room.join`. | DEC-137 | **The product owner's** — does the `Call` button name the total or what the player must add? | [ADR-0122](ADR-0122-call-names-the-price-and-raise-to-names-the-total.md) | **`Call` names the price, and `Raise to` still names the total.** The button prints `legalActions.callTo − committedThisStreet` — the chips the press takes from the stack, which is `ADR-0101` §1's `toCall` character for character and the **engine's own quantity**, since `BettingRules.kt` builds `callTo = committed + toCall(seat)`: the subtraction recovers the term the server added rather than manufacturing one. At the human's own frame — 100 in, rival raises to 300 — the button reads `Call 200`. `Bet`, `Raise to` and `All in` keep their **totals**, told apart by the game's own preposition, and `ADR-0109`'s last-act mark **goes bare on `Call`**: `PlayerCalled` carries only `to`, and after the act the caller's `committedThisStreet` *is* `to`, so the price is unrecoverable and keeping the total would put two figures on one act a tick apart — `ADR-0109` §2 is amended in that one clause, §§1, 3, 4 and 6 byte-unchanged. Licensed by `docs/duel-rules.md`'s own `DEC-003` sentence: the small blind *"owes 10 more to call rather than 50"* because *"the bar a player faces should be a number that is genuinely at stake"*. **No architect `DEC` is owed and none was registered** — `ActionBar.tsx:255` already computes that difference for the sizing base, so no wire moves, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump is needed and item 4 does **not** serialise against the showdown's wire work under `ADR-0047`. `All in`'s bare total is named as uncovered rather than implied. For the implementing ticket: `ActionBar.test.tsx` commits zero in seventeen places under a `?? 0` default and its one non-zero fixture never asserts the `Call` label, so the change lands green against today's suite either way — the proof needs a hand where the two seats' commitments differ |
| DEC-129 | **The product owner's** — is a player **asked for a name before their first duel**, where does the **suggestion** come from, and may the dialog be dismissed without naming? Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 1a, from the human's own instruction after playing the product on two devices | [`ADR-0119`](ADR-0119-the-name-is-asked-at-the-first-press-and-skipping-plays.md) — **the ask stands at the player's own first press, the suggestion promises nothing, and skipping plays.** *That* a player is asked is the human's own words, recorded verbatim and not chosen. *When* and *whether it can be skipped* are derived from the vision's first success condition — ***"Send a link. She opens it in a browser. We play"*** — which is a sentence about the **invited** player, who presses nothing: `main.tsx:160` reads the code out of the URL and `store/boot.ts` sends `JoinRoom` on `Welcome`, so the invite path has no press to hang an ask off and is left untouched end to end. The ask therefore stands at the player's **own** press of *Create a duel room* or *Join the duel*, once per browser, and never before a rematch, a resume or a link. It **may be dismissed**: skip is one of two equally reachable ways out, and no refusal of a name ever prevents a duel — which is what keeps `ADR-0036`, `ADR-0058` and `ADR-0063` §2 describing a state players are really in rather than leaving them formally accepted and quietly false. The **suggestion is an offer to type**: the field's initial value, never a reservation and never a claim that the name is free, because `ADR-0029` §1 makes freedom a database fact and §5 refuses the endpoint that would answer it — and **nothing is written unless the player takes it**, the one irreversible thing in the area, since `ADR-0051` §1 lets no string leave `name_registry` and §9 builds no un-retire. Costs named rather than hidden: **the invited rival is not asked before her first duel** — half the players in a two-player product — and the host meets one interstitial in a product whose positioning sentence is *fast*. Registers `DEC-142` (the architect's) for the generator |
| DEC-133 | **The product owner's** — **how much of a showdown does the table show?** Raised 2026-09-06 by `EPIC-14` item 2a, on the human's *"if we went to showdown villan card shoud be show"* and their drawing's *"villan card shown if required"*, after a duel played to showdown on a laptop and an iPhone. **Answered at the floor, and the floor turned out to be a pacing question.** Measured first: the shown hand is **not** undrawn — `broadcast` passes `revealedSeats` into `PlayerView.of`, the view carries the revealed seat's `holeCards`, `DuelTable.tsx:76-78` hands them to `Hand.tsx`, and `duel-secrecy.test.tsx`'s *"do reach it once the reveal has arrived"* pins it — so `EPIC-14`'s *"drawn by nothing"* is corrected. What is missing is time: a called-down showdown is **one 600 ms step** before `ADR-0102`'s queue releases the next hand. The answer: the table shows **exactly what the rules showed** (the winner's hand, both on a split, none on a fold), **no post-hand disclosure**, `ADR-0008` unamended, no wire move — *you always see the hand that beat you and never see what you beat* — and **the last beat of a showdown stands 2,000 ms**, ending on the next hand's frames rather than on a fade (`ADR-0095` §4 intact). No panel, no overlay, no new string, no *mucked* line. Costs stated: the winner learns nothing (the reporter won 7–0, so their own case gains only pacing), a held player spends 2 s of a 30 s allowance, and the two screens step ~1.4 s apart. Registers **`DEC-146`** for the architect (one beat, a different length); names the **all-in-and-called** widening without registering it (`ADR-0105` §6) | [`ADR-0120`](ADR-0120-a-showdown-shows-the-hands-the-rules-showed-and-the-beat-that-shows-them-stands.md) on 2026-09-06 |
