A uniform scale is still *one* table and was the product owner's to choose, but it is refused on `R3` and on `ADR-0103` §3's give order — the numbers give last, and a transform shrinks them first. The contract gains its width twin (`scrollWidth ≤ clientWidth`) and adds **no criterion**; **390 × 664 stays** as `ADR-0096` §4's stand-in for a device, correctable **downward** by a hand-checked device reading and never upward. Relaxing `R3` for the scale, raising the shape, and any supported-device list are named as the human's |
| DEC-127 | **The product owner's** — a browser recovering a room paints a confident **`Waiting for your rival`** screen, room code and invite link and all, over a room that is actually `PLAYING` or already finished, and then corrects itself. Is a self-correcting false screen **acceptable-because-late**, or **wrong-because-false** and owed a neutral interstitial? Raised 2026-09-04 by `TASK-131009` reading `STORY-1310` whole, on **three independent observations by three different routes** — `P2` mid-runout, `P4` a plain reload, `P6b` a mailed link with no reload at all. **Answered as two, because it is two windows of different kinds**, and the answer to each is the same one sentence: *the client shows what the server told it, and before it is told it shows nothing.* **Before any frame** the client paints the front door on `ADR-0114` §5's `unknown` standing — a screen the server stated nothing to support — and the reading that settled it came out of `DuelSocket.kt`: `replyToCreateRoom` has **no seat check** (`DEC-110`, open, is exactly that guard), so a press inside the window is honoured, repoints the connection's membership and makes `boot.ts` overwrite the remembered code. **Wrong-because-false**, and `/` now renders **no element** at all. **After `RoomJoined`, before the frame that states the duel**, the stale waiting screen is **acceptable-because-late** and is said out loud: the server did state it, the exposure is one delivery's frame gap, and `replyToJoinRoom`'s `ALREADY_SEATED` branch proves the client cannot do better — a host rejoining a still-`WAITING` room is sent `RoomJoined` **and nothing after it**, so waiting for a second frame would hang that host for the whole of their wait. **No interstitial** (*"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal."*, `ADR-0105` §4), with the dependency stated: **longer than about a second** empty on the `built` re-read `ADR-0117` §6 already owes reopens it. Registers no new `DEC`; names two open questions without registering them (`ADR-0105` §6's route) | [`ADR-0118`](ADR-0118-a-recovering-browser-shows-nothing-it-was-not-told.md) on 2026-09-04 |
| DEC-087 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism do the proofs of record load the built bundle a real user would receive: what serves `dist/` (the Ktor server, a static server, a `scripts/qa/stack.sh` mode), on what origin, and what supersedes [`ADR-0088`](ADR-0088-the-two-browser-proof-is-a-written-hand-check.md) §2 step 3's `npm run dev` — through that ADR's own §5 route, since its §2 is immutable prose — so the hand-check describes the same artifact? Raised 2026-08-30 by [`ADR-0093`](ADR-0093-ready-for-real-users-is-said-of-the-shipped-artifact.md) §1a, whose readiness bar is unmeetable until this is answered. **No longer true that it blocks nothing.** On 2026-09-04 `TASK-131006` found the dev server does not merely serve a different artifact — it *prevents a whole class of reading*. Driving `STORY-1310`'s `P3`, `ADR-0112` §6's **genuinely dropped socket**, cuts the app's socket at the transport; but Vite's HMR client is multiplexed on the same port, so the cut takes both and **the page reloads** before `reconnecting.ts` can be observed recovering in place. Reproduced 4 of 4 across both seats and both delays, and **observed rather than inferred**: `performance.getEntriesByType("navigation")[0].type` reads `reload` after the cut where it read `navigate` at first paint; `document.readyState` cycles complete → interactive → complete; and a JS-realm marker planted immediately before the cut, polled inside one shell invocation so the elapsed time is trustworthy, was gone by `t+710ms` at 300 ms and **already gone at the first poll, `t+25ms`, at 0 ms** — inside `retryDelayMillis`'s own first attempt, so the reload pre-empts the reconnect rather than racing it. `reconnecting.ts` was read in full and contains no navigation call, and `location.reload` cannot be stubbed away (`TypeError: Cannot redefine property` — Chrome enforces it `[LegacyUnforgeable]`), so the reload is a browser-level event no page script can suppress and not something product code chose. The question is therefore no longer only *which artifact the proofs of record describe*, but **whether a class of drive is available at all under `npm run dev`** — and it now blocks `TASK-131006` and `STORY-1310`'s `P3` row | [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md) — **the proofs of record load the built bundle, on an origin of its own.** Answered by the architect on 2026-09-04, the day `TASK-131006` turned the question from *which artifact a record describes* into *whether a class of drive exists at all*. **`vite preview` serves `dist/` on `http://localhost:4173`** — Vite is already pinned by [`ADR-0026`](ADR-0026-vite-and-npm-drive-the-web-client.md) so **no dependency is added** and [`ADR-0088`](ADR-0088-the-two-browser-proof-is-a-written-hand-check.md) §1's refusal stands; `vite.config.ts` gains `preview: { port: 4173, strictPort: true }` and deliberately **no `preview.proxy`**, because Vite resolves `preview.proxy ?? server.proxy` and one table cannot disagree with itself — `dev-proxy.test.ts` asserts all three, the `undefined` proxy being the one with teeth. `strictPort` is load-bearing: a leftover listener becomes a refusal to start rather than a silent origin nobody named. **The mode is proved off the artifact, never off the port** — the dev server injects `<script src="/@vite/client">` and the built `index.html` never names it, so grepping for the marker tests for the presence of the exact mechanism that broke `P3`. **`scripts/qa/stack.sh`** gains `build-web` (foreground, dies unless `dist/index.html` exists, so a failed build is an exit code and not a background task that never listens) and `web-origin`, and **`wait-web [built\|dev]` defaults to `built`** and fails on a marker mismatch — the gate goes there because every caller already invokes it; `drive.mjs`'s `APP` and its two target filters read `PD_APP_ORIGIN`. **`ADR-0088` §2 step 3 alone is superseded**, through that ADR's own §5 route, and now reads `npm ci && npm run build && npm run preview` with the marker as a third failure condition; steps 1, 2 and 4–11 stand byte-unchanged, and step 4's two-`pd.deviceId` check is unaffected because it compares two partitions with each other, never with a remembered one. **`npm run dev` stays for development and loses only the proofs of record**: the hand-check is `built` always, every QA/UAT/audit round started after this merges is `built` by default, a `dev` round names itself and may not be offered toward [`ADR-0093`](ADR-0093-ready-for-real-users-is-said-of-the-shipped-artifact.md) §1a, and **every record names its artifact beside its network layout** — `built, delayed 300ms` is complete, `bare` alone is not. §6 extends [`ADR-0089`](ADR-0089-a-browser-drives-this-client-for-a-qa-round-never-for-a-gate.md) §4 by one clause in the same shape: **a `dev` finding whose conclusion turns on load latency is re-read on `built` before it is filed**, because the dev server answers a page in ~102 requests where the bundle answers it in ~3 — `102 / 6 × 600 ms ≈ 10 s` is the arithmetic behind `P2`'s observed 11.8 s first paint, a property of the instrument and not of the client. **No gate is created and none may be read into it**: `ADR-0089` §2b stands unamended, `build.yml` keeps its two jobs, and no PR, `verify:` block or ticket waits on a drive. **Re-drives**: `TASK-131006`/`P3` on `built`; `P4` finishes on the mode it started on; `P1`/`P2`/`P5` do not re-drive for this ADR, though `P2`'s and `P5`'s `delayed` findings against `ADR-0112` §6 fall under §6 and are a separate ticket. **Costs named**: a **stale `dist/` is undetectable** — the marker proves the mode, not the freshness, and the mitigation is ordering, which is discipline rather than a mechanism; the origin move makes every literal `5173` outside production code wrong and leaves `ServerConfig.DEFAULT_BASE_URL` (`ADR-0077`) naming an origin no proof uses, harmless while `NoRecoveryMailer` is bound and a trap for the first drive that can follow a link; the production build drops `StrictMode`'s double-invoke, so a round may disagree with its own history for neither a defect nor a repair and `ADR-0089` §4's classifier has no term for it; and the proxy stays in the path, so this proves the **bytes** and not the serving path. The **storage-partition move is explicitly acceptable**, checked rather than assumed: `ADR-0089` §3 and the `qa-cycle` skill make a fresh `mktemp -d` profile per round mandatory, so no round has ever carried a partition — only a human's own browser loses one, and arriving as a device the server has never seen is what a real user is. Falsifies `ADR-0088`'s *"the built bundle stays unproven"* and spends `ADR-0096`'s *"the audit walks `npm run dev` until `DEC-087` is answered"*; `docs/test-plan.md`'s *"every case runs against `npm run dev`"* is corrected by the answering change. Registers **`DEC-126`** — what serves the bundle in the **offered deployment** — which this ADR declines to pre-decide, because `docs/architecture.md` says the deployment is *"Not decided yet"* |
| DEC-125 | **The product owner's** — pressing the account offer's accept (*"Keep them with a password"*) on the result screen **spends the offer and navigates nowhere**: the click registers, the offer is gone on any later return, and no screen is reached. `ADR-0112` §5 says the press lands on the account screen; its Context says it bounces back after a page load. Driven on 2026-09-04 at both `bare` and `delayed 300ms`, it does neither — three independent signals (unchanged `href`/`hash`, unchanged DOM over 15s, one CDP target, a surviving MutationObserver) show **no navigation at all**. So a player who accepts loses the offer and is told nothing. What should the press do, and what becomes of a player who has already spent one this way? Raised 2026-09-04 by the driver while landing `TASK-131004`. | [`ADR-0116`](ADR-0116-the-accept-is-a-door-and-a-door-that-does-not-open-spends-nothing.md) on 2026-09-04 — **the accept is a door, and a door that does not open spends nothing.** The press means one thing, *put me on the account screen where a password can be set*, and **the offer is spent where the player is delivered** — never by a press that delivers nothing. Where the product does not put them there, the offer stands and is made again after their next win, exactly as an offer nobody answered. `ADR-0085` §2 is **narrowed on one clause** — *taking* is reaching the screen, not pressing toward it — with *"Not now"* still spending at the press, and the storage, the key, §3's table and every refusal untouched; a second tab counts as reaching it, so `ADR-0086`'s modified-click case stands. An answered offer is not shown again on the screen behind it. **Nothing is given back and nothing is built to give it back** — no un-dismiss, no migration, no key rename (a rename re-offers the players who honestly pressed *Not now*), and the affected population is empty because nothing is hosted. The word is chosen deliberately: today's press is **not a spend** and **not a forfeit** (`ADR-0046` §5) — the offer is *taken, and nothing is handed over*. Satisfied by construction, since the offer only ever shows where the ask is honoured, and that becomes a standing condition on any future surface. Hands `ADR-0114` §6 its open question: the repair writes a **regression test**, non-browser, red at `1a09fb46`. Registers no new `DEC` — the mechanism is `DEC-123`'s answer, already merged as `ADR-0114` and landed by `STORY-1311` |
| DEC-124 | **The product owner's** — does a surface this product animates owe a **still form** for a player whose system asks for reduced motion, and what governs it? Raised 2026-09-02 by the planner while splitting [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md) into stories — items 1 and 6 are the product's first continuous motion, and no `prefers-reduced-motion` rule, motion token or accessibility stance existed anywhere merged (verified, not assumed). Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path) | [`ADR-0115`](ADR-0115-motion-never-carries-a-fact-and-reduced-motion-stills-every-surface.md) on 2026-09-02 — **motion never carries a fact, and reduced motion stills every surface.** Every fact is stated by the surface's still form; motion may emphasise one and never carries one alone. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, continuous and decorative motion does not run — the pulse holds as a steady mark, the chip flight is skipped and the chips appear where they land — the same surface at rest, no second design, no in-product toggle. Only *how* the screen changes is stilled, never *that* it changes: `ADR-0102`'s steps, the clock's per-second change and `ADR-0109`'s mark replacement stand, and nothing is amended. Governed in two merged places, never per surface: motion values are born `--pd-motion-*` in `design/tokens/tokens.css` beside the sheet's one reduced-motion block, and a card that gives a surface motion draws it at rest as a named state, judged at the pane (`ADR-0024` §3). Derived from *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"* and *"free of everything that makes online poker feel like a casino"*; deliberately motion-sized — no accessibility programme, which would be the vision's to add. Unblocks `STORY-1303`'s and `STORY-1306`'s implementing tickets |
| DEC-123 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism is a mid-duel navigation refused and the address restored: the branch order, the room-state read, restore promptness, and how a mailed `verify`/`reset` token survives **unspent**? Registered open 2026-09-02 by [`ADR-0112`](ADR-0112-only-a-running-duel-refuses-another-screen.md) §7, which may not move the wire or `PROTOCOL_VERSION` on its account | [`ADR-0114`](ADR-0114-one-predicate-answers-every-ask-and-a-mailed-screen-waits.md) on 2026-09-02 — **one predicate answers every ask, and a mailed screen waits for the first frame.** A new pure module, `web-client/src/routing/room-standing.ts`, reads the room off the frames the server sent (`outcome` → `finished`, `view` → `running`, `roomCode` → `waiting`, `unknown` before any frame) and rules `honour`, `refuse` or `hold`; **`running` is `view !== null && outcome === null`**, which is `ADR-0105` §2's definition read off frames, grace window and runout inside it. The six chosen-screen branches move **above** the three store branches and test the ruling, so a chosen screen cannot render without consulting the rule — and `ADR-0076` §3's frame rule falls out of the same predicate rather than needing its own. The restore is a **`useLayoutEffect`** calling the merged `leave()`, which is what makes refusing and restoring one commit with no paint between them — `replaceState`, never `back()`. The mailed screens **hold** rather than refuse while the standing is unknown, writing nothing to the address, because `VerifyScreen` spends its token in a mount effect and a booting tab reads *no room* for the whole rejoin round trip; `roomAwaited`, computed once in `bootDuelClient` and carried on `DuelProvider` like `signedIn`, is what distinguishes *no room* from *not told yet*, and it is used only to withhold, never to assert. One residual is named and sized rather than assumed away: a resume into a `PLAYING` room reads `waiting` for one render, closable only by a wire change `ADR-0112` §7 forbids, and a drive that observes it is the evidence for a new `DEC`. No wire, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, no server file, no stored key, no new string, no new control, no `Screen` member for the table, and no router |
| DEC-120 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism is [`ADR-0108`](ADR-0108-expiry-plays-the-seat-and-the-timebank-replaces-the-grace-window.md)'s turn clock carried, enforced and resumed? Registered open 2026-09-02 by `ADR-0108` §6, which fixed what must be true and wrote no repair — `ADR-0105` §6's precedent. Open were: which frame states the deadline; whether an expiry is a server-synthesised act or a new room event; what schedules expiry and at what resolution; how a resuming client learns the live deadline; and what becomes of `disconnectGraceMillis`, `GraceExpiry`, the `DUEL_PAUSED` path and `graceRemainingMillis` | [`ADR-0113`](ADR-0113-the-turn-clock-is-derived-state-and-the-sweep-plays-the-seat.md) on 2026-09-02 — **the clock is derived state, the wire carries what is left, and the sweep plays the seat.** One new `ServerMessage`, `TurnClock` (`seat`, `handNumber`, `actionSequence`, `turnRemainingMillis`, `bankRemainingMillis` for both seats), because `Snapshot`/`Events` carry engine types and a clock may not enter `poker-engine`, `YourTurn` reaches one seat, and `OpponentPresence` is about the rival alone — one frame per write-back that moves the decision point, to both seats, and one to a resuming seat in the critical section that already builds its presence frames. It states **what is left, never an instant** (`ADR-0028` §2, re-applied: no shared epoch). `Room` gains `turnDeadline` (absolute, derived from the decision point) and `timebankRemainingMillis`, loses `gracePeriods`, and **arms nothing** — so the race is closed by the room's mutex and the engine's sequence guard, with nothing to cancel: the act wins and the clock restarts, or the sweep wins and the stale `Act` comes back `Rejected`. An expiry is a **server-synthesised act down the ordinary path**, `foldAbsent`'s single-seat body extracted and reused, so `ADR-0023`'s conduct, `ActedForAbsent`'s mark and the coin's single settle path hold by construction; `ADR-0025`'s ticker keeps two steps with `expireGracePeriods()` **replaced** by a one-pass `expireTurnClocks()`. The client **anchors on arrival and paints on apply**, which amends `ADR-0102` §6 by refusing the queue exemption it promised; the countdown recomputes rather than decrements, drift is bounded and never corrected, and zero is not an event. `isPaused` and the `DUEL_PAUSED` enum entry are deleted, `presenceOf` loses its `now`, and `absentSeats`, `foldAbsent` and `secondsRemaining` are kept verbatim. Replay is untouched: determinism is over actions, not over how they were chosen. The bump is claimed under `ADR-0047`'s lock with **neither number nor fingerprint named**, and the `atomic:` ticket is sized by `ADR-0070`'s probe with **no file list published** |
| DEC-119 | **The product owner's** — while a player holds a room, what does the **address** name, and may they leave the table by asking for another screen? Raised 2026-09-02 by the human — *"when page is refreshed user shoud stay on the same page (refresh duel page shoud not redirect to lobby; and same for all other pages)"* — and registered by [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md). The reported symptom **did not reproduce** — a host in a live duel on a bare `/`, a rival on `?room=CODE`, a host on the waiting screen, and `#/leaderboard` on a room-free browser all survive `location.reload()` — and the defect that did is its inverse: a browser **holding a room** has its fragment **erased**, so `duels`, `leaderboard` and `account` were unreachable mid-duel and the address stopped naming where the player is | [`ADR-0112`](ADR-0112-only-a-running-duel-refuses-another-screen.md) on 2026-09-02 — **only a running duel refuses another screen, and the refusal restores the address.** The address names the screen the player chose and never the room — no `Screen` member for the table, and the address is not expected to name a duel (`ADR-0076` §§1–2 applied, its §2 foreclosure reaffirmed). While the held duel runs (`PLAYING`, grace window included — `ADR-0105` §2's definition), an ask for another screen moves nothing: the table stays, `/` is restored, and refusing and restoring are **one act**, performed silently, no string minted. A `WAITING`, `FINISHED` or `ABANDONED` room **honors** the ask — the chosen screen shows, the address names it, and the seat, the tab's memory and the socket are untouched, so `/` is the room's screen as it now stands. A frame that seats a duel overrules any chosen screen exactly as `ADR-0076` §3 wrote it; the account is reachable by the same rule, so `ADR-0086` §6's accept lands on the account screen it names; and a mailed link refused mid-duel must not spend its token. The implementing story owes `EPIC-13`'s DoD reproduction attempt on the undriven paths — the result screen, a runout, a dropped socket, real latency, the `AccountOffer` anchor, a mailed link over a held room. The mechanism is **`DEC-123`**, the architect's |
| DEC-118 | **The product owner's** — when a player **types** a bet amount that is not a legal raise, what does the table do: refuse the press, clamp to the nearest legal amount, or send it and let the server's rejection land? Raised 2026-09-02 by the human — *"player shoud be able to make bet using raw text input"* — and registered by [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md); that the field exists is the instruction, and only the illegal case was open. **Answered: the table refuses the press and says why in the server's own numbers — it never clamps, and never knowingly sends.** A press with an illegal entry sends no frame, takes no sent-lock and rewrites nothing; the two numeric cases (under the floor, over `allInTo`) are one case, refused with `rejection-text.ts`'s merged sentences quoting the violated bound from this turn's `LegalActions`; **a non-number is a second kind, not a third bound** — `That is not an amount.` — and is what rules out *send it* as a uniform answer, since `Bet`/`Raise` carry `to: number` and the client would first have to invent one. The check is **a reading, not a judgment**: `ActionValidation.kt`'s whole amount rule is two comparisons over bounds `YourTurn` already states, so the client refuses only what the server's standing word refuses in as many words, and if the engine's rule ever grows finer the client's check does not grow with it — finer refusals land through the untouched server path, which stays load-bearing. **No act conversion**: a typed `callTo` is not a `Call`, an over-stack amount is not an `AllIn`. **`ADR-0100` §5 stands in full** — the driver gains no typing branch and §8 stays the named repair for interior amounts. Derived from `docs/vision.md`'s *On variance* — *"showing a player… is more interesting than hiding the maths"* — and *Positioning*'s Lichess reference, applying `ADR-0100` §6's *a dialled total is a proposal, and it is the player's* and extending `ADR-0101` §3's *never clamped* to the row's third control. Costs named: the interval is read in two places under a stated discipline, a too-strict reading blocks a legal amount with no server message to say so, a typo costs a retype, and the card owes the refusal states before item 7's ticket is startable. `DEC-102`'s ground shifts (every legal total is now typeable) and stays open | [ADR-0111](ADR-0111-an-illegal-typed-amount-is-refused-in-the-servers-own-numbers.md) |
| DEC-116 | **The product owner's** — does creating a duel land the host **at the table** rather than on the waiting screen, what stands in the rival's seat until they arrive, and what becomes of [`ADR-0073`](ADR-0073-the-waiting-screen-says-back-to-the-lobby-and-the-room-stays-open.md) §3's promise? Raised 2026-09-02 by the human — *"when player create a duel it shoud be redirected to room immidietly; copy link/invite btn shoud be where the table is(on the table, it shoud be drawn) and opponent icon shoud say:'waiting for oppent', after opponent join duel starts"* — and registered by [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md). **Answered: yes, the dedicated waiting screen is retired** — from the moment `RoomJoined` answers `CreateRoom` the host waits at the duel table, the rival's empty seat says **`Waiting for your rival`** (the shipped heading relocated, rendered once, at the seat), and the table states **no game fact** before the opening `Snapshot`. **Both promises move verbatim and keep being made** — `Back to the lobby`, and *"The room stays open. That link still works for your rival, and it brings you back."* — so `ADR-0105` §2's reasoning cites a sentence a surface still renders. The invite moves **whole**: the bare code, the selectable link box and `Copy the link` with its feedback lines — each a shipped fallback for the next, nothing said *about* the code (`ADR-0022`) and no duration printed (`ADR-0072` §6). The state adds **zero new strings**, the arrival is silent, and the rival never sees it (`ADR-0094` §1). `DEC-111` explicitly untouched | [ADR-0110](ADR-0110-creating-a-duel-seats-the-host-at-the-table.md) |
| DEC-115 | **The product owner's** — what happens when a player's clock runs out, and how does the **timebank** meet [`ADR-0013`](ADR-0013-disconnect-grace-period.md)'s **disconnect grace window**? Raised 2026-09-02 by the human — the 30 s move clock and the 3 m timebank were given; open were the consequence of expiry and how the bank meets the window — and registered by [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md). **Answered: an expiry gives up the one decision with `ADR-0023`'s conduct — fold facing a bet, check when checking is free, read from the engine's legal set, marked on the wire as the server's act — and never ends the duel**: no count of timeouts forfeits, no coin moves except by `ADR-0014` on the outcome the engine reaches, and a seat that never acts blinds off under the escalating schedule that already guarantees a duel terminates. Derived from *What it is* — *"One duel coin per win… A counter of duels won."* and *"A duel is a match, not a hand."* — the same derivation `ADR-0105` made and the human endorsed. **The timebank replaces the grace window**, rather than running beside it or after it: one clock answers *"the seat on turn is not acting"*, indifferent to the socket — the human's own *"timebank also work for disconnection case"* — so disconnecting never gains, stops or freezes time. An away seat whose clock is exhausted is `ABSENT` and played without delay until it reconnects, exactly as today's post-window behaviour; a connected seat always gets a fresh 30 s, because a reconnect is observable and attention is not; **the duel never pauses**, so `DUEL_PAUSED`'s occasion and *"The duel is paused."* go when the clock lands. Amends `ADR-0013` (window → clock; seat-holding, filtered resume and configuration stand) and one clause each of `ADR-0028`/`ADR-0046`. Costs named: a connected ghost still spends ~30 s a decision across a blind-off, and a bank-empty disconnector gets 30 s where the window gave 60. The mechanism is **`DEC-120`, the architect's**, registered open above | [ADR-0108](ADR-0108-expiry-plays-the-seat-and-the-timebank-replaces-the-grace-window.md) |
| DEC-114 | **The product owner's** — what does the table's `Pot` name: the **collected** pot, or the **total including this street's commitments**? Raised 2026-09-02 by the human after playing a duel end to end — *"pot size is not correct; it shoud include all bets including the latest one that was just made"* — and registered by [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md). **The shipped behaviour is deliberate, not a defect**, which is why this is a `DEC` and not a bug ticket under `EPIC-12`: `PotStrip.tsx` prints `view.pot` and says so in as many words (*"the pot is `view.pot` and not a sum of what the seats put in"*), so at 50/100 preflop the strip reads `Pot 0` while both blinds sit in front of the seats. **Nothing mechanical stands in the way of either answer** — `PlayerView` already carries each seat's `committedThisStreet` (`PlayerView.kt:108`) and `web-client/src/lobby/Lobby.tsx:154` already sums it into `potIncludingStreet`, so **no wire change, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump and no engine change** is implied by saying *total* (`GameState.potTotal` exists and is not on the view). The sharp fact is that **the same screen already shows both numbers**: the sizing row's `pot` preset is a fraction of the total per [`ADR-0101`](ADR-0101-pot-means-a-pot-sized-raise-and-the-fractions-share-its-base.md) §1 while the strip prints the collected pot, so a player pressing *pot* gets an amount they cannot derive from what they can see. Answering *collected* is complete and needs saying out loud, but then owes the same screen an account of that gap. Bears on `EPIC-13` item 2 and gates it **Answered: the total** — `view.pot` plus both seats' `committedThisStreet`, the same sum `ADR-0100` §6 already names *the pot* and `Lobby.tsx:154` already computes; one line, one word, no *Total pot* second figure. Derived from the vision's *"Heads-up Texas Hold'em"* by `ADR-0101`'s own chain, with the human's report treated as evidence and not specification. | [ADR-0107](ADR-0107-pot-names-every-chip-committed-to-the-hand.md) |
| DEC-117 | **The product owner's** — what does the table say about **the act just made**, and how long does it stand? Raised 2026-09-02 by the human — *"opponent last action shoud be visible on the screen; probably some icon plus info(like for check icon will be ok, for raise/bet icon plus size info)"* — and registered by [`EPIC-13`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-13-the-living-table.md), whose item 3 it gated. **Answered: the table marks the most recent act of the hand it is painting — one mark, any of the six acts, at the seat that made it — saying what the actor's own button said (`actionVerb`'s verb, with the event's own `to` total on `Call`/`Bet`/`Raise to`/`All in`; `Fold` and `Check` bare). Within a hand it is only ever replaced by the next act; street ends, snapshots and presence leave it standing; the next hand's deal as painted (`ADR-0102` §1's queue) removes it and `DuelFinished` retires it — no timer, no fade, no dismiss.** Derived from `docs/vision.md` *Positioning* — Lichess's single last-move mark — with the words taken from the merged `action-text.ts` rather than coined. `ADR-0075`'s duration rule was confirmed not to transfer (no absence to outlive), while its welded-frame ground eliminated street-end clearing on the server's own delivery. The card owes six states; the drawing stays the human's by `ADR-0024` §3 | [`ADR-0109`](ADR-0109-the-table-marks-the-last-act-and-the-next-deal-clears-it.md) |
| DEC-112 | **The product owner's** — does *fits the phone* admit a sub-pixel residual? Raised 2026-09-02 by [`TASK-121402`](../../tasks/tasks/TASK-121402-the-duel-table-column-fits-the-phone-it-is-nested-in.md), whose 48 px repair left the duel-table document at a true **664.90625 px against 664** (`scrollHeight` 665) with both action buttons at or above the fold and every other criterion met — the ticket's acceptance criterion demanded `≤` and predicted 664 / 664, its Out of scope ordered the implementer to stop short of the next give, and the implementer stopped and said so, which is [`ADR-0103`](ADR-0103-the-table-fits-the-phone-and-the-cards-give-before-the-numbers.md) §3's stop rule operating at its first live boundary. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path — it never appeared in an open table). **Answered: yes, at exactly one boundary — a reading of one integer over is judged on the true geometry, and a true excess strictly under one CSS pixel is met**; one pixel or more, or a two-integer excess, is `R2` `not met` as ever. **The fence is the instrument's own unit and cannot widen**: a tolerance that can hold painted content is the *"relaxed phone bar"* `ADR-0096` §2 forbids by name, so widening belongs to the human. **`TASK-121402` merges as scoped**; **the last 0.90625 px is re-filed once** as ordinary backlog, its closer confined to `ADR-0103` §3.1 whitespace; and a future round **may not file a sub-pixel overflow as anything** — while clipped content, unmeasured beats and full-pixel residuals stay filable on the ordinary path. Derived from the vision's first success condition — *"Send a link. She opens it in a browser. We play a full heads-up match."* — read as `ADR-0103` read it: *fits* is for playing unscrolled, and nothing a player must read or press can hide below a fold no glyph fits under | [ADR-0106](ADR-0106-a-sub-pixel-residual-is-a-fit-and-one-pixel-is-the-fence.md) |
| DEC-109 | **The human's** (routed to the product owner, escalated on review) — may one player hold seats in **two live rooms at once**? Registered open 2026-09-01 by [`ADR-0104`](ADR-0104-a-frame-reaches-the-connection-in-the-room-it-is-about.md) §10, which split it off rather than deciding it. **Answered: no — a player holds at most one seat in a *running* duel.** Derived from `docs/vision.md`'s *What it is not* — *"Not a multi-table poker room. No 6-max, no 9-max, no tournaments, no sit & go, no cash games."* — with the **shape** of the answer taken from *What it is*: *"One duel coin per win… A counter of duels won."* While a player holds a seat in a `PLAYING` room, `CreateRoom` and `JoinRoom` from them **take no seat**: the room asked for is untouched, the seat and duel held are untouched, and **no coin moves**. **Scoped to `PLAYING` alone** — a `WAITING` seat is not refused (`ADR-0073` §3 promised the host on screen that the room stays open) and a `FINISHED` seat is not refused (`Room.offerRematch` agrees only when both seats have offered), while a seat inside `ADR-0013`'s grace window **is** refused, which is the grace period working. **The refusal hands the player back the duel they are in** — the table, never a lobby, never a dialog — and is safely repeatable. **Two sentences and no third**: `You are already in a duel. Finish it to start another.` The player whose invite was clicked sees nothing, because nothing happened to them. **No repair is written**: §6 states what must be true and registers `DEC-110` for the architect; §7 inherits `ADR-0104` §9 and explains no fold. **Vacating the first seat was rejected by name** — every coin in the merged tree is settled by a duel the engine ran to a chip holder (one `sink.record` call site, reached only with an outcome), so a forfeit-on-navigation would be the product's first, would be the least reversible option in the set, and is a commitment `docs/vision.md` does not make. Costs named: a player who wants out of a duel is **stuck** — no resign, no turn clock, and a `PLAYING` room is never reaped, so their exit depends on their rival still acting; *Create a duel room* can now say no; two people are left slightly in the dark about a clicked link, on purpose; and **the multiple-waiting-rooms hole stays open as `DEC-111`** | [ADR-0105](ADR-0105-one-duel-at-a-time-and-the-refusal-hands-back-the-duel.md) |
| DEC-107 | **The architect's** — where is a presence frame scoped to the room it is about, so that a client cannot apply one about a room it has left? Raised 2026-09-01 by [`STORY-1214`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1214-a-duel-played-by-hand-deadlocked-on-presence.md) out of a duel the human played by hand and could not play, and measured on the wire: `OpponentPresence(AWAY, 60000)` arriving **320 ms before the recipient sends `CreateRoom`**, with a roomless controlled negative that was clean in both directions. **Answered: a frame is delivered to the connection that is in the room it is about.** `deliver` resolves a seat to a player exactly as it does today and then asks for that player's writer **for this room**; a connection in another room, or in none, is skipped silently — the idiom `deliver` already documents for a seat mid-reconnect. `ConnectionDirectory.writerFor(player)` is **deleted** so no unscoped lookup survives for a later call site to reach for, `RoomMembership` moves to `duels.poker.server.session` and is registered with its writer with a **`@Volatile`** `code`, and **all seven `deliver` call sites are untouched**. **`PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move** — no declaration changes, `ADR-0047` §2's fingerprint is unchanged, no ledger row, and `TASK-121403` is **not** `atomic:`. **Production is unchanged**: `disconnect()` still builds the frame whether or not the other seat is connected to that room, because the registry knows nothing of writers by design — that half of the question is answered *yes, unchanged*. **The story's reading was confirmed and understates the defect**: the widening lives in the composition `seat → PlayerId → writer-anywhere`, so it carries every frame `deliver` routes and needs no race at all; no card can leak, because the recipient is always the player the frame was projected for. The client's store is scoped too — a `RoomJoined` naming a **different** room re-initialises it — and the ADR says in as many words that this half is **not** what makes the system correct. Costs named: a documented ignorance retracted, a `@Volatile` obligation nothing in the type system asks for, a second indistinguishable cause of a silent drop with no counter, load-bearing ordering, and and **the unscoped *send to a player wherever they are* foreclosed** — deliberately, and with **no current victim**: `writerFor` has one call site and this decision is it, so the cost is prospective. Splits off `DEC-109` for the product owner; leaves `DEC-108` untouched | [ADR-0104](ADR-0104-a-frame-reaches-the-connection-in-the-room-it-is-about.md) |
| DEC-106 | **The product owner's** — what does the duel table look like on a phone: does it get a phone treatment at all, what gives at 390 wide, and which card carries it? Raised 2026-09-01 out of round 1 of `/qa-cycle audit smoke` (run 2026-08-31; [`STORY-1213`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1213-round-1-audit-three-criteria-unmet-and-a-table-that-names-the-wrong-winner.md), `R2` `not met` at four decision beats — 885/664 preflop, `bottom: 820.578` facing a raise, 868/664, 866/664) and by [`TASK-121302`](../../tasks/tasks/TASK-121302-the-decision-fits-a-390-by-664-screen.md), which asserted *"No `DEC` is needed here — this is conformance to a merged card"* and could not close it: `min-height` is a floor, `flex-grow` distributes slack, and **the merged card itself measures 732 against 664 at 390 × 664**, so the card describes no phone and a coder conforming to it still fails. **Answered: the table fits the phone, it is one table at two widths, and the cards give before the numbers** — whitespace, then the rival's face-down hand, then the player's hole cards (never smaller than a board card), then the board; nothing else gives. `design/screens/duel-table.html` is amended and gains a same-markup 390 × 664 frame; `TASK-121302` is rewritten and blocked behind that design ticket. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path); it never appeared in an open table | [ADR-0103](ADR-0103-the-table-fits-the-phone-and-the-cards-give-before-the-numbers.md) |
| DEC-105 | **The architect's** — how is a runout's pacing produced, and what does a step cost? Raised 2026-09-01 by [`TASK-121301`](../../tasks/tasks/TASK-121301-the-runout-arrives-street-by-street-on-the-screen-too.md) against `R1` `not met` at beat 5 of round 1 of `/qa-cycle audit smoke`, and routed by [`ADR-0096`](ADR-0096-the-audit-judges-a-whole-duel-against-a-frozen-rubric.md) §2 — *"how a beat is paced is settled by the ticket that repairs it… with the architect where [a still] cannot [hold it]"*. One question in two halves: **where the pacing lives** — `poker-server` sending one `Snapshot` per street, or `web-client` holding the `Snapshot` it already has — and **what a step costs**, in hold time and in what a reconnecting client does. **Answered: a hand's ending is painted as a sequence of steps, and the client owns the clock.** The store paints a hand-completing `Snapshot` as one step per preceding `StreetDealt` plus a final step, queues every frame behind them in arrival order, and lags exactly two fields — a **prefix of the server's own board**, and that `StreetDealt`'s own street. A step is **600 ms**, named once in `boot.ts`, with `0` meaning synchronous so the four recorded-frame e2e suites are untouched; a **reconnecting client jumps to the end** because `resumeFrames` sends it no `Events` to pace against. **The ticket's reading was confirmed on three points and incomplete on a fourth that decides the answer**: `act` calls `advance` in the same call and both hands' frames are delivered in one batch, so board-slicing alone would be erased a millisecond in. `PROTOCOL_VERSION` does not move, and would not have moved for a server-paced answer either — the server option lost on the engine handing out one state per runout, a wall clock in the authoritative path, and its inability to fix the batch it creates. Costs named: the stacks settle at the first step, every hand ends 600 ms later, and the screen trails the server by design. Registered open by `TASK-121301`, answered the day after | [ADR-0102](ADR-0102-a-hand-ends-in-steps-and-the-client-owns-the-clock.md) |
| DEC-101 | **The product owner's** — what amount does each named sizing preset set: what does a chip labelled `pot` (and `⅓`, `½`) promise a player in a heads-up duel? Registered open 2026-08-31 by [`ADR-0100`](ADR-0100-the-driver-reaches-an-amount-by-pressing-what-a-player-presses.md) §6, which settled the technical half — a named preset **computes the quantity it is named for**, and the pot reaches `ActionBar` through `Lobby.tsx` with no wire change — and refused this one, because the card's single worked example (`Pot 2,450`, rival `committed 400`, `Call 400`, `pot` selected, `Raise to 3,250`) is satisfied by **two** formulas that disagree the moment the hero has committed on the street, and excludes the textbook pot-sized raise, 3,650. **Answered: `pot` means a pot-sized raise, and `⅓` and `½` are fractions of the same base** — the pot as it will be after the call — so `pot → callTo + base`, `½ → callTo + floor(base/2)`, `⅓ → callTo + floor(base/3)`, `min` and `all-in` unchanged as the server's own numbers, and a fraction chip whose amount falls outside `[floor, allInTo]` is **not offered** rather than clamped. `TASK-120908`'s `floor × 4/3` does not ship, its file set stays at `ADR-0100` §7's six, and `design/screens/duel-table.html` is in arrears by one number in two places. The stepper's step was split off as `DEC-102` and refused | [ADR-0101](ADR-0101-pot-means-a-pot-sized-raise-and-the-fractions-share-its-base.md) |
| DEC-100 | **The architect's** — how does a scripted duel reach a bet amount once the table's sizing control is the card's discrete presets rather than a range slider? Raised 2026-08-31 by [`TASK-120908`](../../tasks/tasks/TASK-120908-the-tables-sizing-control-is-the-cards-presets.md), whose coder replaced the range input, got all three of its new tests and the whole of `ActionBar.test.tsx` green at 25, then hit **24 failures across four merged e2e files** for a reason outside its two-file budget and routed rather than widening scope (`CLAUDE.md` rules 4 and 5): `web-client/src/e2e/drive-duel.tsx:125` reaches an arbitrary amount with `fireEvent.change(getByRole("slider"))`, and removing the input makes that query throw in `whole-duel.test.tsx`, `duel-secrecy.test.tsx`, `claimed-here-recovered-there.test.tsx` and `drive-duel.test.tsx`. **Answered: the driver presses the sizing row and reads the action button before it clicks**, throwing by name when no press reaches the recorded total — no test-only door, no driver-only slider, no re-recorded frame, and none of the four files edited. Its product half — what `pot`, `⅓` and `½` each compute — was split off as `DEC-101` and refused. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path); it never appeared in an open table | [ADR-0100](ADR-0100-the-driver-reaches-an-amount-by-pressing-what-a-player-presses.md) |

The coder shipped the ticket's other two scope items (PR #1234) and refused to guess this one (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5). **Not** one of `ADR-0092` §5's three promoted slots — raised by a ticket, like `DEC-092` and `DEC-095`. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path) — it never appeared in an open table | [ADR-0098](ADR-0098-the-wordmark-belongs-to-the-front-door-alone.md) |
| DEC-098 | **The architect's** — is the audit rubric [`ADR-0096`](ADR-0096-the-audit-judges-a-whole-duel-against-a-frozen-rubric.md) §2 **itself**, grown by an amending ADR the way [`ADR-0092`](ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) §2 and [`ADR-0097`](ADR-0097-a-resize-is-two-numbers-and-the-observer-is-the-fifth-file.md) §4 both grew [`ADR-0090`](ADR-0090-a-skill-may-write-the-catalogue-or-run-it-never-both.md) §2's declared-file set, or a **working document elsewhere** that §2 founds — and what does a round cite when it records a criterion `not met`: an ADR section, or a document path? Raised 2026-08-31 by the planner splitting [`STORY-1212`](../../tasks/stories/STORY-1212-the-audit-focus-the-observer-the-resize-and-what-a-criterion-costs.md); blocked nothing, and was answered the same day — before any round cited a criterion and before a sixth criterion arrived, the window its own *Due* named. **Answered: the rubric is §2 itself, and no working document ever exists.** Every future criterion is *born* merged — `ADR-0096` §3 routes a proposal to a `DEC` whose answer is *a merged PR either way*, and a merged answer is an ADR (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5) — so a document could only ever have held a **copy**: a second register from its first line, able to disagree silently with the law it transcribes, guarded by nothing (*two copies of a rule drift*, `ADR-0092` §8). Growth is one amending ADR — ids sequential (`R6` next), the criterion in §2's own three-column form, the priority order restated as ids only, `ADR-0096`'s index row annotated in the same PR — and a round cites `R<n>` with the ADR section that states it, never a path. `TASK-121203` and `TASK-121205` already cite the decided form and change nothing | [ADR-0099](ADR-0099-the-rubric-is-the-adr-section-and-a-criterion-is-born-merged.md) |
| DEC-097 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism does [`ADR-0096`](ADR-0096-the-audit-judges-a-whole-duel-against-a-frozen-rubric.md)'s **audit focus** run under [`ADR-0089`](ADR-0089-a-browser-drives-this-client-for-a-qa-round-never-for-a-gate.md) §2's three conditions? Two halves, both about merged conditions on the browser permission. **(a) The declared-file set** — is `ADR-0090` §2's set amended a second time for an audit observer, or does the focus run with no new agent file? **(b) Two viewports inside one live duel** — `ADR-0096` §4 walks the whole duel at 390 × 664 and re-answers `R2`/`R3` at 720 × 900, and relaunching mid-duel re-seats the device (`ADR-0018`) and deals a different board, so the shapes cannot be two passes over the same beat: either the harness resizes a **live tab** — and is `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` a read, or the application-state write §3 forbids? — or §4 is unbuildable as written and its second shape returns to the product owner. Registered 2026-08-31 by `ADR-0096`; **(b) restated the same day** after the human's *"we have to support phone size"* turned one viewport into two. **Answered without returning anything to the product owner**: the mechanism exists, so §4 stands as merged. Blocked the first audit round | [ADR-0097](ADR-0097-a-resize-is-two-numbers-and-the-observer-is-the-fifth-file.md) |
| DEC-096 | **The product owner's, with three halves settled by the human** — what process catches a product that is *raw*, and what authority does it need? Raised 2026-08-31 by the human after playing the product in two browsers immediately following the `/qa-cycle uat regression` that ended `PASS` with `B(3) = 0`: the round **ends immediately after an all-in** with no runout, *"a lot of times it is hard to understand what is going on"*, the pot and bet amounts are not legible, **scrolling** is required to see the opponent's actions, weird copy and text without spacing. Three merged rules made every one of those unreportable, and the ADR diagnoses each: `ADR-0092` §3's classifier has **no merged source of the general kind** to contradict, so a judgment about legibility could only ever be a question; `EPIC-12` §Termination rule 2 puts everything that makes a product feel unfinished in the `medium`/`low` band that is *"never scheduled by this cycle"* — **seventeen such tickets are in the backlog**; and coverage is **per-screen**, so pacing, a property of a sequence, has no row to be reported in. The human settled three halves and they are recorded verbatim rather than chosen: the audit **may** file findings contradicting no merged source, the benchmark is category quality with the vision's aesthetics, and audit findings are repaired **regardless of severity**. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path) — it never appeared in an open table. Registers `DEC-097` for the architect. Its one escalation — **is a phone a supported surface?** — the human answered the same day (*"we have to support phone size"*), so §4 walks **two shapes** and `DEC-097`(b) became the load-bearing half. **`docs/vision.md` is not amended**: *"She opens it in a browser"* and *"Two browsers, one room link"* name a browser and **no device**, so the call resolves a silence rather than contradicting a sentence, and §4 names what would change that. **Phone landscape was left open by §4 and is now answered** — the human's *"we are ok to support only one orientation for mobile form factor"*, recorded by [`ADR-0097`](ADR-0097-a-resize-is-two-numbers-and-the-observer-is-the-fifth-file.md) §5 | [ADR-0096](ADR-0096-the-audit-judges-a-whole-duel-against-a-frozen-rubric.md) |

The card draws both halves of a two-line banner; only one is buildable. `PotAwarded` carries `seat` and `amount`, so *You win 4,850* is a transcription, while *Two pair, aces and sevens* exists on no `GameEvent` and could only be computed by the client — forbidden by `CLAUDE.md`'s non-negotiables and `ADR-0002`, and gated by `web-client/src/table/no-derivation.test.tsx`'s `HAND_TALK` matcher. Raised 2026-08-30 by [`TASK-121101`](../../tasks/tasks/TASK-121101-the-table-says-who-won-the-hand-it-just-finished.md), whose triage refused to guess it (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5) and which is unstartable until this lands; **not** one of `ADR-0092` §5's three promoted slots, so round 3's cap is untouched. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path) — it never appeared in an open table | [ADR-0095](ADR-0095-the-table-states-who-took-the-pot-and-never-names-a-hand.md) |

Raised 2026-08-30 by [`TASK-120907`](../../tasks/tasks/TASK-120907-the-join-path-ships-neither-screen-its-cards-draw.md), which routed it rather than guessing (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5) and is unstartable until it lands; **not** one of `ADR-0092` §5's three promoted slots, so round 1's cap is untouched. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path) — it never appeared in an open table. Three consecutive UAT rounds (`STORY-1205`, `STORY-1209`, `STORY-1210`) filed the same `high` conformance finding against these cards, and would have gone on doing so: `ADR-0092` §3a judges the shipped screen against its merged card, and nothing said which artefact was the product | [ADR-0094](ADR-0094-opening-the-invite-is-taking-the-seat.md) |
| DEC-086 | **The product owner's** — what, if anything, is the written bar for *"ready for real users"*? Raised 2026-08-30 by the human's UAT request, whose stated goal is that after the pass the *"product should look like ready for real users"*. [`ADR-0089`](ADR-0089-a-browser-drives-this-client-for-a-qa-round-never-for-a-gate.md) §2c forbids citing any round as coverage, and [`ADR-0092`](ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) §2 extends the corollary by name: no round record — a `PASS` included — may be cited as the thing that made the product ready, so the bar, if one exists, must be **decided**, not measured into existence; *there is no written bar; the human judges by reading the round records* was registered as a complete answer | [`ADR-0093`](ADR-0093-ready-for-real-users-is-said-of-the-shipped-artifact.md) — **the phrase is said of the shipped artifact, and the bar is two facts, neither a test result.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-30, deriving from the success condition's second clause — *"She opens it in a browser"*: what she opens is the artifact the product serves her, and today no proof of record describes it — every layer, unit to UAT, loads `npm run dev` on `localhost` while `dist/` is opened by nothing (`ADR-0088` gap 3, still true). **Fact a: the artifact under test is the artifact shipped** — the proofs of record (the `ADR-0088` §2 hand-check and the QA/UAT rounds the human reads) load the built bundle; the serving mechanism is **`DEC-087`, the architect's**, registered open by the same PR and **answered 2026-09-04 by [`ADR-0117`](ADR-0117-the-proofs-of-record-load-the-built-bundle.md)**; while it was open the bar was unmeetable — correctly, since a phrase with no referent should be unavailable. **Fact b: recovery is completable by a real user in the offered deployment** — a mail transport bound, never `NoRecoveryMailer` — because `ADR-0031` §7 scoped the senderless build's validity to *"development and tests"* and an offering to real users is neither, `ADR-0087`'s acknowledgement promises a link that must be able to arrive, and `ADR-0031`'s Consequences price the failure as the product's one total, permanent loss. **Meeting the bar does not make the product ready**: it is a precondition on the phrase, never a certificate — readiness stays the human's judgment made by reading (the escape hatch's kernel, kept), no record may ever be cited as the bar being met, no proof is required to have *passed* (that would cite a record), and the bar gates the **phrase**, never the **act** of offering, which stays the human's own with no ADR scheduling it. The event is `ADR-0063` §5's — strangers beyond *"people the author invited personally"* — so the founding moment (*send a link, she opens it*) is not gated, and that ADR's farm expiry falls due at the same event, on its own terms, named beside this bar rather than collected into it. **Five absences are out under a written rule** — a gap enters the bar only when its failure would falsify the success condition at first contact or destroy something a player cannot get back: history paging past 10, ladder paging (`05-04`), the third device (`CORE-03`), the hundred-way tie (`ADR-0065` §1) and real network each stay known absences a round reports without them bearing on the bar, every one decided in a merged ADR and proved at the layer that computes it, with the reopening trigger named (a real user losing something unrecoverable through one of the five means it was misclassified, and the correction is a superseding ADR). **No machine enforces the bar and none may be built** — a `verify:` asserting *ready* is `ADR-0024` §3's opinion-in-a-verify-block one abstraction up. Costs named: the ADR tells the human their UAT pass cannot end at the phrase they aimed it at; the bar waits on `DEC-087` and a transport (a twenty-first pointer at `EPIC-07`, but a **blocking** one, which fails loudly rather than collecting nothing); §1b removes the senderless-deployment freedom and may cost a mail bill; and enforcement is prose plus the review gate, which is weaker than a gate |
| DEC-085 | **The architect's, with two halves settled by the human mid-decision** — may a UAT round exist, what may it judge, and what may it file? Raised 2026-08-30 by the human's request for a second testing pass after `/qa-cycle`, over the same catalogue, focused on UX and design conformance — *"is the main info properly highlighted? is it clear to user what is going on? are all options accessible?"* — with a missing design *"reported as bug → addressed (according to workflow in build-epic) → then testing should resume"* and the goal that the product *"should look like ready for real users"*. Registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path). Two things genuinely blocked it: [`ADR-0089`](ADR-0089-a-browser-drives-this-client-for-a-qa-round-never-for-a-gate.md) §2's permission is *"exactly their conjunction"* — three conditions written for a **QA** round, and a UAT round is a round-kind it never contemplated, where covering it *by analogy* would make §2 satisfiable by relabelling; and [`ADR-0024`](ADR-0024-design-follows-the-code-workflow.md) §3 (*"a verify block cannot hold an opinion, so it does not try"*) with [`ADR-0091`](ADR-0091-design-gets-no-agent-a-new-screen-owes-a-card.md) reserve visual judgment for the human, while the request asks an agent to question exactly that. Mid-decision the human settled the routing — **a UX question raised during UAT is delegated to the `product-owner`, whose decision may create a bug ticket through `build-epic`** — and the sequencing: **run UAT now and let it file the six missing cards** rather than wait for `ADR-0091` §5's retrofit story | [`ADR-0092`](ADR-0092-a-uat-round-files-what-a-source-settles-and-asks-the-rest.md) on 2026-08-30 — **UAT is a second focus of the same cycle: it files what a merged source settles and asks the product owner the rest.** `/qa-cycle uat <scope>` is the human's own message and chains from nothing — the QA focus never runs the UAT focus, neither report prints the other's command, and a preceding QA cycle is practice, never a checked precondition, because the check would cite a round as a gate (§2c). **The three conditions are checked one at a time and hold**: a `shot` verb captures screenshots over CDP with Node built-ins into the round's temp directory, never committed, **no image-diff tooling ever** (§2a); §2c gains the corollary that no round — a `PASS` included — may be cited as what made the product *ready*, with the bar itself registered as `DEC-086`, the product owner's; a screenshot and a rendered card are **reads** under §3; and §4 transposes to *observable by a human looking* — at the screen, and the rendered card beside it, by eye. **The classifier at the boundary is the merged source**: an observation files as a finding only when it contradicts something merged — a card, a token, an owned literal, an ADR section, a vision sentence — and a judgment with no merged source is a **question**, `ADR-0090` §4 transposed from authoring to observing. Three checks per screen-state: conformance to the merged card (transcription, not taste, and not pixel equality), reachability (*"found N matches, all invisible"* is already mechanical), and copy against merged sources. **A missing card is `high`, outside `B(N)`** — registered debt being collected, not decay — its repair **is** the card (composed per `ADR-0091` §3, verdict trailing), its dedupe key is the card's own path, and the screen is walked, not parked: only conformance reads `BLOCKED — no card`. **Questions go through a bounded gate**: the `uat` agent asks at most three per screen and answers nothing; `qa-manager` — the only promoter — registers at most three `DEC`s per round, one per screen, each a concrete choice answerable in one sentence and bearing on a player's ability to tell what is going on; below the bar is recorded unanswered, never a ticket. **An answered question becomes a merged source** either way — a change makes the shipped state a finding at the next triage, a blessing closes the question mechanically. The frozen set survives: decision-born tickets enter the earliest **subsequent** triage or the backlog, and are the **third `B(N)` exclusion** beside harness defects and missing cards, because counting improvement as decay trips `STOP_DIVERGING` on a product getting better. A round that unlocks newly-carded screens is a **baseline round** — rule 4 skips its comparison, rule 5's three-round budget still binds — and every UAT verdict is qualified inline (`PASS (conformance unjudged on 6 of 7 screens)`) so a round that mostly authored cards says so in the one line anyone reads. `STOP_BLOCKED` is scoped to an escalation that **gates the current fix set**; otherwise `notify.py blocked` carries it and the cycle continues. **One new `uat` observer** (opposite refusal lists cannot share a file with `qa.md`, which stands byte-unchanged); one manager, one skill, one ledger — dedupe spans focuses. `ADR-0090` §2's declared set grows three→four (`agents/uat.md`, mention-only, by the clause's own licensed route). Costs named: round 1's conformance is near-tautological until the pane verdicts land on cards composed from the very screens they judge; up to nine Opus dispatches per invocation; three prose exclusions in a count that used to be simple |
| DEC-084 | **The architect's** — does design work need its own agent, or does it stay on the `coder`/`reviewer` workflow? Raised 2026-08-30 by the human; registered and answered in the same PR (the `DEC-039` path — it never appeared in an open table). The tension: the workflow demonstrably ships design — 42 merged commits under `design/`, 43 tickets, four stories `done` — yet `design/screens/` holds only the v0.1 duel flow, `EPIC-04` and `EPIC-05` shipped six of the seven `Screen` members (`duels`, `leaderboard`, `account`, `sign-in`, `verify`, `reset`) with no design card, no rule anywhere makes a new screen ask for one, and `check-drift.sh` scans `design/` against itself and never reads `web-client/` | [`ADR-0091`](ADR-0091-design-gets-no-agent-a-new-screen-owes-a-card.md) on 2026-08-30 — **no designer agent and no design skill; the taste carrier is the card, and a new screen owes one before its build.** An agent is added here when a decision needs an owner, and this one would own none: `ADR-0024` §3 already gave taste to the human at the render and structure to the `light` review, so an agent between them is a hop — and it would have prevented neither recorded failure, since nothing dispatched any design work at all (an agent nobody dispatches designs nothing) and the drift tax was mechanical. The trigger the epics lacked becomes **the plan-story rule, in `.claude/agents/planner.md` alone**: a story whose split adds a `Screen` member (`web-client/src/routing/screen.ts`) names the card it implements in `## Design notes`, or the split's first ticket **is** the card; a new surface behind an existing slug — the account offer was one — is the planner's judgment under the same rule. `EPIC-06`'s interactive deviation generalises instead of expiring with the epic: **minting is interactive with the human, composing is an ordinary dispatched ticket**, and the human's visual verdict may trail the merge — the named price is a screen shipping a look the human later rejects, bought so unattended runs never stall at a pane. The adoption evidence was **corrected before being acted on**: the zero-token grep measured direct references, but `app.css`'s `@theme static` block rebinds every Tailwind namespace to `var(--pd-*)` under three merged client guards (byte-identity, theme references, colour literals), so the real leak is raw lengths in arbitrary values — `380px` ×21, `560px` ×2, `460px`, `1.5em`, fifteen files — and **`check-drift.sh` therefore stays out of `web-client/`**: adoption is gated where consumption happens, by a fourth client guard refusing raw lengths in arbitrary values, while the shell gate keeps guarding `design/` against itself in `tickets.yml`. The debt is registered, not forgiven — `EPIC-06` reopens with a retrofit story for the six slugs, its 2026-08-15 metrics standing as history — and `ADR-0024`'s generator trigger (*"cards multiply or a grep actually fires"*) is recorded as fired, its promised ticket owed. A deliberately deferred piece is named with its own trigger: a slug↔card coverage gate becomes a ticket only if a screen ships undesigned with the rule in force. Nothing in it was the product owner's: that every player-facing screen carries the identity is the vision's *Positioning* sentence and `EPIC-06`'s merged goal already |
| DEC-083 | **The architect's** — may a single human-typed skill both author catalogue content and then run the QA cycle over it, or must authoring and running stay two separate human commands? Raised 2026-08-29 by the human's request for *"one skill"* that writes the missing cases for existing functionality and then runs a full cycle. [`ADR-0089`](ADR-0089-a-browser-drives-this-client-for-a-qa-round-never-for-a-gate.md) §2b — one of the three standing conditions the browser permission is the **conjunction** of — says a cycle is started by *"a human's command — not a merge, not a cron, **not another skill invoking it as a step**"*, and a composite is literally the third while being plainly the first. §2 says a condition that stops holding *"returns the question as a new `DEC-NNN`"*, so this is that `DEC` rather than an argument around the clause. **Blocks the authoring of every suite `docs/test-plan.md` lists as *not yet written*** — `EPIC-04`, `EPIC-05`, `EPIC-06` | [`ADR-0090`](ADR-0090-a-skill-may-write-the-catalogue-or-run-it-never-both.md) — **a skill may write the catalogue or run it, never both in one turn.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-29. **The composition reading is taken and the automation reading is rejected by name.** Four things decide it: *"a human's command"* forbids nothing unless it means the **immediate caller**, since every automated trigger has a human behind it at some distance; `ADR-0089` §Consequences gave §2b a future job — *"the sentence to point at"* when a nightly is proposed — which a clause satisfiable by relabelling cannot do; §2's own closing sentence pre-refuses the argument form (*"condition **b** failing, not a refinement of it"*); and the composite's **sole** value is that the cycle begins while the human is elsewhere, making it a cron whose clock is the length of its first half, so it fails even the attended/unattended test the permissive reading proposes. **§2b's heading and one sentence are amended in the open** — *"No gate"* becomes *"No gate, and one caller"*, and the trigger sentence becomes *a cycle is started by the human's own message and nothing else*, with `/qa-cycle` the first act of the turn it starts; the rest of §2b and §§2a, 2c, 3, 4, 5 and 6 stand byte-unchanged, as do `ADR-0088` §1's body and §§2–5. **The human types two commands**, and the expensive half is still one: `qa-cases` is licensed to plan, write and land the missing suites through `build-epic` and the ordinary review gate, and may not bring the stack up, start a browser, dispatch `qa` or `qa-manager`, or invoke the cycle by any route — **its terminal act is a report naming, verbatim, the command the human types next.** **On the author-and-grade worry**: the `qa`/`qa-manager` separation survives a composite untouched, so the blunt objection is answered by the reviewed PR; what a diff-versus-ticket review cannot answer is whether a case's `expect` is a claim the product ever made, and a composite hands `ADR-0089` §4 — *"a rule an agent follows, not an exit code"* — a whole catalogue of **never-executed** cases in the same unattended stretch that step 4 is merging production diffs. So every case `qa-cases` writes carries a `source` column, and **a case whose expectation has no merged source is not written**: it becomes a `DEC` for the **product owner**. **And what that grounding does not buy is stated**: a merged decision proves what was *decided*, not what *shipped*, so a suite `qa-cases` writes is **provisional until its first round** — `docs/test-plan.md`'s *"filled in when an epic is first tested, not before"* is amended in the same PR to admit the authored-then-tested path, and the honest expectation for that first round is a pile of harness tickets that can end `PASS` while a dozen of its own cases were wrong. Checkable by one command over a declared three-file set, with the half no grep can catch stated rather than claimed. Chosen in the direction whose **mistake announces itself** — forbidding costs a visible wait every round and generates its own evidence for reversal, while permitting costs an invisible precedent that no `git rm` undoes |

[`EPIC-12`](../../tasks/epics/EPIC-12-quality-and-defect-repair.md) proposes something narrower than what §1 refused — **no CI job, no `package.json` dependency, no pull-request gate**, an agent-run harness driving Chrome over the DevTools protocol that a human invokes by command — and the question is whether that is inside §1's refusal or outside it. Any one of the three failing returns the question as a new `DEC`. **§1's reasoning was a ratio and none of its cost terms are incurred here**, while §2 of that same ADR already has a person driving two browsers through this client: the difference between them is the **position** — a hand-check that leaves a receipt, not a gate between a diff and `develop` — and not the browser. **The driver reads anything and writes nothing** but `pd.roomCode`, because a case that seeds state to reach a screen is a client asserting a game fact (`ADR-0002`). **And it adds the clause `EPIC-12` §Termination lacked**: a failure that does not reproduce by hand is a **harness** defect — filed against `EPIC-12`, excluded from `B(N)`, and never repaired in production code — which is what stops a stale catalogue from reading as a product getting worse, or from being satisfied by a merged diff |

Open since `EPIC-03` was written; the last thing standing between the epic and its close, and the reason its two registers contradicted each other. | [`ADR-0088`](ADR-0088-the-two-browser-proof-is-a-written-hand-check.md) — **the two-browser proof is a written hand-check, not a CI job.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-28. **No browser runner, no fifteenth story, no third CI job**: no Playwright, Puppeteer, Selenium, WebDriver or Cypress dependency enters `web-client/package.json`, `build.yml` keeps its two jobs, and the automated ceiling stays where `STORY-0312` and `poker-server/.../e2e/` put it — `ADR-0032` §4's *"still jsdom, still no network"* stands. **`EPIC-03` closes on its stories, fourteen of fourteen**, and its table's *"decides whether a fourteenth story exists"* is answered **no**. **The gap is named rather than waved past, and it is four things measured on `develop`**: `main.tsx`'s root render — seven nested providers into a `#root` no test document has — is executed by no test and `index.html`'s `<div id="root">` is asserted nowhere (delete either and the suite stays green while the page renders blank); `new WebSocket(socketUrl(window.location))` is never executed — `index.test.ts` stubs the constructor and asserts the *string*, `dev-proxy.test.ts` asserts `vite.config.ts`'s *values*, and nothing forces the two to agree — so **no test in this repository has ever opened a TCP connection to the duel server**, the JVM suite included, which runs on `testApplication` in process; it gates no merge and no ticket, and no agent can write it. **Costs accepted and stated**: the regression window is a release rather than a pull request; a failure has no bisect; the built bundle stays unproven by anything including the hand-check, because §2 runs `npm run dev` and nothing here serves `dist/`; the check decays with repetition; and this is now the merged precedent for `EPIC-04` and `EPIC-05`, which are in the same position. **Chosen because it is the cheapest to reverse** on thin evidence, with no deadline in either direction — reversal is a superseding ADR plus one story, and nothing here makes it harder |

recovery still two navigations deep and advertised nowhere; **a mistyped address is told the same thing as a correct one and nothing detects it**, with the form staying visible as the whole mitigation; a mode added to the one screen a player types a password into; and a two-sentence string fixed before `EPIC-06` letter-fits anything. **Forecloses** any feedback about the address by rule rather than omission, and a *forgot your handle* flow for as long as `ADR-0031` §6.2 keeps the handle in the reset mail; **`#/forgot-password` is left open, not shut** — one table row and one branch, additive — and the phrase is fixed so no second `DEC` is needed for the word. Second half, deciding the same tickets: `DEC-049`/[`ADR-0056`](ADR-0056-a-throttled-sign-up-says-so-and-keeps-what-was-typed.md) §5 records that a `429` spends nothing, *"including `ADR-0036`'s offer, **which only 'Not now' dismisses**"*, which reads as *accepting does not spend it*; `STORY-0415`'s criterion *"It does not appear a second time after a second win"* reads as *being shown spends it* | [`ADR-0085`](ADR-0085-not-again-is-this-browser-and-an-answer-spends-the-offer.md) on 2026-08-27 — ***"not again"* is this browser, and an answer is what spends the offer.** **Derived, not chosen**: the roadmap's `v0.1` row — *"Two browsers, one room link, one complete duel, rematch. **No accounts.**"* — is the vision counting a player as a browser at exactly the stage the offer addresses, since it is shown only to a player holding no credential; and *Positioning*'s *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"* is what refuses a prompt that returns after the player answered. **Three measurements decided the first half.** `ADR-0036`'s stated reason does not hold on the shipped client: `device-id.ts` owns `pd.deviceId` in the same bucket `main.tsx` injects, so clearing storage takes the device id too, `ADR-0049` §4's `resolve` finds no live binding and **mints a fresh, empty profile** — the prompt is not resurrected, the profile is gone, and the returning browser sees no offer until *it* wins. The two answers are **indistinguishable for every player who can see the offer**, because it requires *no credential* and `ADR-0049` §1's `device_binding_live_player` gives such a player exactly one live browser; the only route to a second browser is signing in, which switches the offer off. And **two of `offerAccount`'s three terms are already browser-local** (`signedIn` is *"whether this browser holds a session token"*), so a travelling third term would give one predicate two ideas of who the player is. **The bit is written and read through the injected `Storage` and never sent**: no column, no `GET /api/me` field, no endpoint, no wire change, no migration. **Second half: an answer spends it, and nothing else does.** Both controls are answers — taking the offer and *"Not now"* — and each is permanent; a `429`, an abandoned sign-up, a rematch, a reload and the mere fact of having been rendered spend nothing; **an offer shown and never answered is made again after the next win**, because the prompt shares a screen with *Rematch* and `ADR-0036` §Alternatives refused silence for the reason *"a player who has never been told their coins are device-bound learns it by losing them"*. **Costs named rather than discovered**: the player who accepts and abandons is never re-asked, and it is the player most likely to convert; the offer **reappears on a second browser** and nothing can know it is the same human; a mis-tap on *Not now* is final by design; a player who tapped past it is asked twice; **one more storage key**; and `signedIn`'s browser-scoped meaning leaves a signed-out account holder offerable — **named, not solved**. **Forecloses** any server-side record that a player was ever offered an account — no funnel, no *asked-and-declined* metric — which is named because it is the first thing a growth argument asks for. **Amends** `ADR-0036` §Consequences' storage clause and `ADR-0056` §5's *"and by nothing else"*, leaving §5's holding and `ADR-0036` §Decision — the human's `DEC-025` call — byte-unchanged. **Chosen as the reversible direction**: a column is additive while `EPIC-07` hosts nothing, a shipped field is not. **Unblocks `STORY-0415`'s fifth, sixth and seventh tickets**; leaves `TASK-041501`–`TASK-041504` unchanged; hands the planner one clause for the story's third criterion; and **narrows `DEC-080`** to the key and the module. **Nothing in it was the human's** |
| DEC-078 | **The architect's** — what mechanically counts as a **gated** acceptance criterion, and what does `lint_tickets.py` do about the ones already written? Raised on 2026-08-26 after a run produced two families of defect a linter was expected to catch, with **both** candidate checks measured against the live backlog before being proposed and **both failing as written**: (a) *a criterion naming a file the `## Files` table excludes* — `TASK-041628`'s shape and six others — flags 194 tickets raw and 29 once narrowed to demand verbs, of which 28 are correct; (b) *a shell command named in a criterion must appear in `verify:`* — `TASK-041210`'s criterion 5, `TASK-041215`'s `grep -cE`, `TASK-041634`'s Scope — flags 25 unsettled tickets across 12 files and cannot land green without a twelve-file repair `TASK-000106` refuses | [`ADR-0084`](ADR-0084-a-criterion-that-speaks-in-shell-belongs-in-verify.md) on 2026-08-26 — **a criterion is gated when a `verify:` command exits non-zero if it is false, and nothing else gates anything.** Face (b) becomes a `lint_tickets.py` check scoped to `ready`/`in-progress`/`in-review`, matching by **word-subset of one `verify:` line** rather than substring — which is what dissolved the twelve-file blocker, since re-measuring showed the five spelling false positives were an artefact of the relation and the true count is **18 criteria across 11 files**, `backlog` × 16 and `ready` × 2. The sixteen retire at each ticket's own `backlog` → `ready` flip; `TASK-041219`'s two are repaired in the check's PR. Face (a) is **not** mechanised — re-measured at 206 raw, and every narrowing leaves the legitimate refusals dominant, because *"`CardSecrecyTest` passes with no change to the file"* is a refusal in a demand's grammar — and becomes a written rule in `tasks/README.md`: a criterion may demand new content only in a file the *Files* table edits, and one that cannot be met without a forbidden edit **is the next ticket**. The named cost is that `TASK-041628` would happen again today |
| DEC-077 | **The product owner's** — what does the product call the screen a player opens to reach an account from a browser that does not hold it, and therefore what is that screen's permanent slug? Raised on 2026-08-26 when `STORY-0412` was split. [`ADR-0076`](ADR-0076-a-screen-the-player-chose-has-an-address.md) §1 makes a slug *"the lowercase ASCII form of a word the product already says to a player"* and states that the ADR **coins no player-facing vocabulary**, so a screen needing a word the product does not yet say is a product question. The story settled the count `ADR-0076` §1 left to it — **two** — and found the first name already merged (`ADR-0050` §3, `ADR-0036` and `ADR-0056` §2 each say *account* to a player), so `TASK-041222` ships `#/account` with nothing invented.
