The second screen had only a **verb** in the merged record | [`ADR-0083`](ADR-0083-the-second-account-screen-is-sign-in-and-its-address-is-never-refused.md) — **the second account screen is *Sign in*, and its address is never refused.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-26, and **nothing is coined**: `ADR-0050` §3's merged player-facing text already says *sign in* to a player twice, and the vision's *Positioning* sentence (*"Lichess and Chess.com… Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"*) is what licensed the plain word over a themed one. **The heading is `SIGN_IN_HEADING = "Sign in"`** in `account-text.ts`, golden like every other string there. **The slug is `sign-in`** — a literal in `screen.ts`, never derived from the heading — and **the hyphen is the product's own spelling**, since `POST /api/auth/sign-in` is already written that way, so the client's address and the server's path now read the same character for character; `signin` and `sign_in` are this product's spelling of nothing. That **widens `ADR-0076` §1's *"a word"* to a hyphenated compound for this word only**, with the rule that survives it stated: `[a-z-]` only, never leading or trailing, and still the lowercase form of something the product already says. **The word is said twice, from one constant** — the screen's heading and the single door to it on the account screen, offered only when `signedIn` is false (`ADR-0060` §2's one-spelling-per-destination rule); no fourth door on the lobby; and `SIGN_IN_LABEL` stays a separate constant, because a control's verb is not a screen's name. **The address is refused to nobody**: a browser already holding a session token that opens `#/sign-in` gets the screen, the fragment is not replaced, and **holding a token is not a fourth branch** — `ADR-0076` §3's three store-owned branches still outrank it exactly as at `#/account`. The reason is that `signedIn` means *this browser holds a string*, nothing in this client reacts to a `401` (`TASK-041209`), and `ADR-0050` §3 ends every **other** device's session while those devices keep their dead token — so the browsers most in need of the screen are the ones a bounce would turn away, which is `ADR-0076` §2's forbidden shape, an address making a second claim about entitlement. **A successful sign-in lands on `#/account`**, not back on the sign-in screen, because `account-text.ts` authors no *you are signed in* sentence and the account screen's routes statement is the only confirmation the product has. Costs recorded rather than discovered: ***Sign in* is said three times in one flow and twice on one screen**, two constants holding one string, so tests must query by role and a screen reader hears it twice; **the first slug with a character outside `[a-z]`**, which makes `TASK-041226`'s `^[a-z]+$` criterion wrong and leaves every later screen the hyphen question; **the slug and the endpoint agree by two literals and nothing mechanical**, a fragment crossing no wire; **a signed-in browser can reach a working sign-in form** and swap identity at an address rather than behind a hidden door (legal, no coin moved — `ADR-0030` §6); **every sign-in pays one extra click** before the create and join controls; and **the word is fixed before `EPIC-06` letter-fits anything**. Chosen partly as the cheapest to unwind — two literals and a constant, in no mail, on no wire, bookmarked by nobody. **Unblocks `TASK-041226` and `TASK-041227`**, which need three changes folded in: the `^[a-z]+$` criterion widened, §5's landing rule owned by `TASK-041227` (which reaches `main.tsx`, outside its three-file budget), and the *Sign in* heading queried by role. **Folded in on 2026-08-26**, with one of the three landing differently: the criterion widened to `^[a-z]+(-[a-z]+)*$`, the heading is queried by role in `TASK-041227` — `TASK-041226` renders no DOM and says so rather than carrying a vacuous grep — but §5's landing rule became **`TASK-041229`** rather than a fourth file, because `ADR-0070`'s probe found the client gate green with either half alone, so no merged gate refuses the intermediate state and `atomic:` would have been a false claim. **Raises no `DEC`, and nothing in it was the human's** |
| DEC-076 | **The architect's** — where does the login handle `RecoveryMailer.sendPasswordReset(address, token, handle)` requires come from? **Nothing in this codebase can obtain a handle from a `PlayerId`**: `PasswordResets.issue` answers a `Boolean`, `RecoveryEmails.verifiedOwnerOf` answers a `PlayerId?`, `PostgresPlayerDirectory` resolves device ids only, and `Credentials` declares exactly `verify`, `verifyCurrent`, `create` and `holdsCredential` — with `verifyCurrent`'s own KDoc recording that a reverse lookup was deliberately **not** added, *"for no other reason than this one check."* `sendPasswordReset` has **no production caller** — the only call anywhere is `NoRecoveryMailerTest`'s literal `"handle"` — and `TASK-041626` is the first real one, with no later ticket in `STORY-0416` filling the gap, since `TASK-041630` passes `handle` through its decorator without sourcing it. The product half is settled and not reopened — [`ADR-0031`](ADR-0031-an-optional-verified-recovery-email.md) §1 names the reset mail as the single exception to *the handle appears in no response body, ever*. **Registered and answered in the same PR**, because an implementation attempt raised it rather than a planner (`DEC-064`/`DEC-065`'s pattern). Raised and answered on 2026-08-26 | [`ADR-0082`](ADR-0082-a-handle-is-read-from-a-proven-address-never-from-a-player-id.md) — **a handle is read from a proven address, never from a player id.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-26. **`RecoveryEmails` gains one member keyed by an address** — `resetRecipientOf(address: EmailAddress): ResetRecipient?`, answering `ResetRecipient(playerId, handle)` — and **there is no `PlayerId` overload and must never be one**. It is one statement joining `recovery_email` to `credential`, whose `WHERE` clause is `SELECT_VERIFIED_OWNER_SQL`'s **character for character** (the pinned `und-x-icu` fold, for the reason that constant's own comment already gives) and whose `c.kind = 'password'` matches `REWRITE_CREDENTIAL_SQL` verbatim. **The fence is the argument type, not the member name**: to get a handle you must already hold a **verified** recovery address, which is the exact secret the endpoint refuses to disclose — `ADR-0031` §5's own test for `verify-email`'s `409`, applied to a lookup. **A `JOIN`, never a `LEFT JOIN`**: unknown address, pending-only address and a verified address whose owner holds no `password` credential all answer `null`, so the third — unreachable under §3 — is answered rather than handed to the route as a null handle. **`verifyCurrent`'s refusal is upheld, not overturned**, and promoted from a KDoc sentence to a build failure: a test asserts **`Credentials` declares no member returning `String` or `String?`**, green today and reddening on exactly `handleOf(playerId): String?` in the one file anybody would write it. **`PasswordResets.issue` keeps its `Boolean`** — `ADR-0031` §5's two outcomes, seven merged assertions and four test doubles all untouched — which was the closest call: `Issued(handle)`/`Suppressed` is what `ClaimPendingResult`'s KDoc recommends, and it lost because `issue(playerId, newResetToken())` **is** a `PlayerId → handle` function with a side effect, and because the credential-less player leaves `Issued` no honest `String`. **The read is not inside `issue`'s transaction and need not be**: verified in source, the only `UPDATE credential` sets `secret_hash`, so a handle cannot go stale — the precise difference from §5's suppression window, which must share a connection. **A handle-less owner mints no token**, the route returning before `issue` so no row spends a window for a mail that cannot go. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **a read whose product is a login handle now exists and did not yesterday**, fenced by an argument type and a KDoc rather than by an impossibility proof; **`RecoveryEmails` reads a third table**, so its *"two recovery tables"* charter and its *"no member returns a `String` that could be one"* sentence are both amended; **`ResetRecipient` is a plain `data class`, so `"$recipient"` prints the handle** — deliberately not redacted, since §6.3 protects the address and inventing a rule the ADR did not make would also make every `assertEquals` failure unreadable, **with the trigger named** (the first log line anywhere on the reset path); **two reads of `recovery_email` now carry one `WHERE` clause in two constants** with nothing keeping them in step; the join adds a third caller to an unindexed `credential (player_id)` scan, with the index named as one later ticket; and **`kind = 'password'` as a literal becomes ambiguous** the day `DEC-027` admits a second kind carrying an identifier. **The gate is a tripwire, not a proof** — a handle read on another type, or one wrapped in a value class (`@JvmInline` reports the wrapper, not `String`), passes it. Chosen partly as the cheapest to unwind: one member and one `data class`, deleted in a commit. **No migration, no index, no wire change, no `recoveryRoutes` parameter, and `RecoveryMailer` byte-unchanged.** **Unblocks `TASK-041626`**, which gains the port read, a fixture whose verified owner holds a `password` credential, and a `theMailCarriesTheOwnersOwnHandle` test over **two** players with different handles; **`TASK-041630` needs one criterion widened** from two distinct argument strings to three. **Raises no `DEC`, and nothing here is the product owner's or the human's** |
| DEC-075 | **The architect's** — does the mailed recovery link survive a static host with no rewrite rule, and if it becomes a fragment route, what is the slug? [`ADR-0031`](ADR-0031-an-optional-verified-recovery-email.md) §4 mails `<baseUrl>/reset#token=…` — a **path segment** — and was right about the fragment for the token, which is never sent to any server. [`ADR-0076`](ADR-0076-a-screen-the-player-chose-has-an-address.md) §4 has since made the opposite call for **every** client address, on the express ground that *"a fragment never 404s… no host, proxy or rewrite rule has an opinion about it"*, which is the same call already merged in `room-link.ts`'s comment — and `EPIC-07` still has no file in `tasks/epics/`, so no host and no rewrite rule exists to depend on. A recovery link is the one URL in this product whose failure is unrecoverable: a `404` on `/reset` means the player never reaches the client at all, and `ADR-0031`'s Consequences make no recovery a **total, permanent** loss of the account. The verification link, `<baseUrl>/verify#token=…`, has the same shape and the same exposure. Raised by [`ADR-0077`](ADR-0077-no-sender-is-an-implementation-and-detachment-is-a-decorator.md), which transcribed §4 rather than reinterpreting it and put both links in **one function** | [`ADR-0081`](ADR-0081-a-mailed-link-is-a-fragment-route-and-the-token-is-the-segment-behind-the-slug.md) — **a mailed link is a fragment route, and the token is the segment behind the slug.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-25. **Both links become fragment routes on the client's single address**: `RecoveryLinks` returns `"$baseUrl/#/reset/$token"` and `"$baseUrl/#/verify/$token"`, so nothing depends on a rewrite rule from a host nobody has chosen. **The token has not moved out of the fragment** — it is now the second segment of one, which is *not* a URL path segment and is still never transmitted, never logged, never in a `Referer`; `ADR-0031` §4's entropy, hash at rest, one hour, single use and refusal of a query string stand **byte-unchanged**, and `TASK-041620`'s *token in a body, never in a URL* is untouched because the server still decodes it from a body and contains no `queryParameters`. **A recovery link contains no `?` at all**, kept absolute rather than conditional, which is why a query section inside the fragment (`#/reset?token=…`) lost the close call: the rule *a `?` is fine, but only after the first `#`* is broken by deleting two characters from a string that still looks ordinary. **The slugs are `reset` and `verify`, fixed here rather than left to `STORY-0417`** — the register's premise cut the other way, because these two addresses are **minted by the server into a mail** and a slug one module writes and another parses cannot be chosen later without reaching back into `RecoveryLinks`; neither word is coined here, `ADR-0031` §4 and `ADR-0077` §6 already wrote them, and `STORY-0417` keeps every other address `ADR-0076` §1 gives it. **`verify` and `reset` are answered the same way**: the failure is deterministic, so a second attempt mails the same dead link, and a failed verification is what *creates* the total-loss state under §3. **A stale or spent link is a screen that renders and a `400` on submission, never a routing outcome** — the client never inspects the token and must not, since `ADR-0080` deliberately left no liveness oracle; a missing token is an empty input, not an unknown address; and the sentence shown is `STORY-0417`'s, already owned. `screen.ts` gains `tokenFromHash`, matches on the **first fragment segment**, and the token is read once at mount before the address is replaced with `hashForScreen(screen)`. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **the two ends agree by two literals in two modules and nothing mechanical**, since a fragment crosses no wire and `protocol.gen.ts` cannot carry it — a divergence lands every mailed link on the lobby, silently; `#/duels/anything` now renders the record, a real widening of `ADR-0076` §7; **a reset link opened in a tab already seated is destroyed** by §3's store-outranks-the-address rule, at fifteen minutes' cost; `/reset` and `/verify` become dead addresses that a host with a rewrite rule will happily serve; and two player-facing words are fixed in a URL before `STORY-0412` names the screens. **Erred toward the `404` being unacceptable**: `ADR-0076`'s six costs are legible and survivable, while a `404` here is silent, deterministic, invisible to every test in this repository and permanent. **Blocks nothing today** — no sender is configured — and it is a one-function change, exactly as `ADR-0077` §6 promised. **Constrains `TASK-041633` (two literals) and `STORY-0417`; frees `EPIC-07` of any rewrite rule** |
| DEC-074 | **The architect's** — does a good reset token survive a `422`, and if so by what mechanism? [`ADR-0031`](ADR-0031-an-optional-verified-recovery-email.md) §5 gives `POST /api/auth/reset-password` a **`422` when the token was good and the new password fails policy**, which presupposes the endpoint knows the token was good *before* the policy is judged; §4 makes consumption **one** `DELETE ... RETURNING` inside the transaction that writes the password, with *"no read-then-write window"* — under which knowing the token was good **is** spending it. A conflict between two sections of a merged ADR, **not** in its *What this does not settle* | [`ADR-0080`](ADR-0080-the-password-is-judged-before-the-token-is-touched.md) — **the password is judged before the token is touched, so a refusal costs no link.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-25. **§5's precondition gives way and §4 stands byte-unchanged.** The handler runs three steps and no others: decode ⇒ `400`; `passwordIsLongEnough` **and** `passwordIsWithinTheWorkBound` ⇒ `422` with **no connection taken and no statement executed**; then `consume` ⇒ `204`/`400`. **A `422` never touches the row** — same `token_hash`, `issued_at` and `expires_at` — so the same link works on the next submission while it lives, and §5's fifteen-minute suppression still sees it, meaning a `forgot-password` pressed in frustration is still the complete no-op §5 designed. **The order is not new**: `ADR-0048` §2 already puts the maximum *"before Argon2 runs and before the identifier is looked up"*, and at this endpoint the token **is** the identifier; the minimum joins it because §2 makes reset one of the two endpoints it applies at and splitting the pair across the lookup would give one published rule two positions. **The register's disclosure worry was checked and runs the other way**: because the branch is chosen entirely by the caller's own password, the `422` is byte-identical for a live token, an expired one and a string the caller invented, so `400`-versus-`422` reports **nothing** about `password_reset` — every other order makes it a liveness report. `ADR-0048` §3 makes the policy *"a pure function of the presented secret"* and §7 publishes the rule, and `docs/protocol.md` already documents sign-up's identical `422`, so the two endpoints now mean one sentence — `ADR-0048` §6's *one meaning per code*, finally true. **The endpoint stays unbudgeted and this makes its cheapest request cheaper** (one `codePointCount`, no round trip); a token probe still costs one `DELETE … RETURNING` and is still spent by the finding, which is why `ADR-0031` needed no counter. **`consume(token, secret): Boolean` is unchanged** and needs no third value. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **a `422` no longer proves the link is alive and nothing else does either**, so a player can be refused twice for one attempt and `STORY-0417`'s form must move from *password refused* to *link expired* without contradicting itself; **a stranger holding no token can make this endpoint answer `422`**, which is licensed only while the policy stays a published pure function — **the day a breach corpus or any row-reading rule joins it** (`ADR-0048` leaves that open) this order becomes an unbudgeted oracle and the endpoint must be budgeted or the rule moved behind the lookup; a caller with both faults needs two round trips in a fixed order; and `ADR-0031` §5 now reads wrong on its own. **`TASK-041620` is unchanged and needs no re-cut** — the step lands in *front* of `consume` — with one fixture constraint: every request in `ResetPasswordRouteTest`, including the two expecting `400`, must carry a `newPassword` of 8–128 code points. **`TASK-041629` gains the check and loses one named test**: `aBadTokenStillAnswersFourHundredNotFourHundredAndTwentyTwo` asserts the reversed order and cannot be written, and what it defended is better asserted the other way — that the `422` for a fabricated token and for a live one are indistinguishable; `aRefusedPasswordLeavesTheTokenAsTheDecisionSays` resolves to a second request answering `204`. `TASK-041617` transcribes the corrected sentence, not §5's. **The losing branch needs no clock and cannot race**; the one hazard is a fixture pinning `java.time.Clock` (`ADR-0062` §5) far from the database's `now()`, which expires every token it mints. No migration, no config, no protocol version, no new type. **Unblocks `TASK-041629`. Raises no `DEC`, and nothing here is the product owner's or the human's** — the words a form uses are already `STORY-0412`'s under `ADR-0031` and `ADR-0048` §7 |
| DEC-073 | **The architect's** — what are the two numbers for each of the `POST /api/auth/recovery-email` and `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` attempt budgets, and does an over-budget attempt still count against its own window? Exactly `DEC-069`'s shape one endpoint pair later: [`ADR-0031`](ADR-0031-an-optional-verified-recovery-email.md) §5 fixes the mechanism, the key (remote address) and the answer (`202`, identical to success) and fixes **no numbers**, and neither shipped pair is obviously right here. `ADR-0074` chose `ADR-0022` §2's ten-a-minute for sign-in on an argument that does **not** transfer — it turned on shared-address collateral being *visible*, and here over budget is indistinguishable from success, so a throttled player is told nothing and cannot pace. `ADR-0031` §5 also already carries a **second, durable** limiter (no mail if a live token was issued in the last 15 minutes), so the answer must say what the address budget is still for once that exists | [`ADR-0079`](ADR-0079-five-to-attach-ten-to-forget-and-the-attach-budget-is-the-only-mail-cap.md) — **five to attach, ten to forget, and the attach budget is the only cap on the mail it causes.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-25. **`forgot-password`: `10` / `60000` ms. `recovery-email`: `5` / `60000` ms**, as `auth.forgotPasswordMaxAttempts` / `AUTH_FORGOT_PASSWORD_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `auth.forgotPasswordWindowMillis` / `AUTH_FORGOT_PASSWORD_WINDOW_MILLIS`, `auth.recoveryEmailMaxAttempts` / `AUTH_RECOVERY_EMAIL_MAX_ATTEMPTS` and `auth.recoveryEmailWindowMillis` / `AUTH_RECOVERY_EMAIL_WINDOW_MILLIS`, with `forgotPasswordLimits()` and `recoveryEmailLimits()` beside the two existing pairs and **a separate `AttemptBudget` instance each** (`ADR-0074` §1's reason, verbatim). **The register's premise was checked and then cut the other way**: invisible collateral is a reason to be *generous*, since a limiter nobody can perceive is one nobody can work around. **What decided the numbers is that §5's fifteen-minute rule covers one of the two mail paths.** `TASK-041613` puts it in `PasswordResets.issue`; `TASK-041607` fixes `RecoveryEmails.claimPending` as returning `Unit` and `TASK-041608` writes unconditionally, so **a verification mail goes out on every successful attach, for ever**. Hence: on `forgot-password` the address budget **adds nothing** against bombing one victim — the durable rule already caps that at four an hour across *every* source address — and its only unique job is an aggregate cap across distinct victims, which a second source address buys back and which is hard to aim, since causing one mail needs an address already verified here and nothing discloses which those are. It is therefore set where it cannot bite the one player in this product whose refusal is unrecoverable. On `recovery-email` the budget is **the only cap on outbound mail to a caller-chosen recipient**, and a **second door to the current-password guess** `ADR-0074` priced at ten a minute — so five, no refund, keeps the front door the cheaper one by two. **Sixty seconds on both**, because the window is the recovery time and `ADR-0074`'s burst-becomes-lockout interaction is worse where the lockout is invisible; it also holds `AttemptBudget`'s per-key list at a fifteenth of what sign-up's window accepts. **An over-budget attempt still counts** — one rule for every limiter in this system, no fork of a shared type — and here it is also the rule that *works*: `202` gives a sprayer no reason to pace, so counting caps a hammerer at one window's worth in total where not counting would hand them one window's worth every minute for ever. **Placement is per endpoint and was the half of the question that is not a number**: `recovery-email` admits **after** the `401`, the decode and `ADR-0078`'s syntax `400` and **before** the Argon2 verify (`ADR-0074` §2's reason; budgeting before identity would let unauthenticated traffic spend a signed-in player's budget); `forgot-password` admits **after the `202` is written**, the only budget in the system consulted after the response, because `admit` takes a `Mutex` and `TASK-041626` makes that ordering the timing defence. **The key is §5's and none of it is the architect's** — `origin.remoteAddress` alone, no `X-Forwarded-*` until `EPIC-07` installs the plugin. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **an attacker can switch off password recovery for everybody behind one address, silently and indefinitely, while each of them is told mail is on its way**; five is a per-person number on an aggregating key, so a classroom setting recovery up together waits a minute; `202` on the attach path now means one more thing; 300 verification mails an hour from one source is still a relay; the per-key list still grows with rate × window; and an operator now holds four limiters and eight numbers with nothing reporting which refused. **Leaves one residual, named and conditioned rather than deferred: the attach path has no per-account resend suppression**, which on the best reading of §5 is a defect against `TASK-041607`/`TASK-041608`/`TASK-041625` rather than a new question — a ticket for the planner, due before `EPIC-07` configures a sender. **Unblocks `TASK-041628`. Raises no `DEC`, and nothing here is the product owner's or the human's** |
| DEC-071 | **The product owner's** — which strings does `POST /api/auth/recovery-email` accept as an address, and what is the player told when one is refused? [`ADR-0031`](ADR-0031-an-optional-verified-recovery-email.md) §5 answers `400` for *"an address that is not syntactically an address"* and states no rule; §2 already settles storage and the fold, so only the acceptance predicate was open. That `400` is the **only** thing the endpoint ever tells a player about their address, and the cost ran one way: a rule that refuses a real mailbox denies that player recovery, which `ADR-0031`'s Consequences make a total, permanent loss of the account, its coins and its ladder place | [`ADR-0078`](ADR-0078-the-mail-is-the-only-real-check-on-an-address.md) — **the mail is the only real check, so the syntax rule refuses almost nothing.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-25, deriving from `docs/vision.md`'s *"One duel coin per win… a counter of duels won"* (which fixes the proportion: nothing here is worth a corpus, a blocklist, a network call or a bill) and its *Positioning* sentence (which decides what fills the gap), the same pair `ADR-0048` used one decision earlier. **The asymmetry was resolved on `ADR-0031`'s own text rather than on taste**: §3 requires verification before an address does anything and §2 notes that *"a player whose address is stored has, by construction, received mail at it"* — so the system already contains an exact deliverability check, and a syntax rule can only report earlier and can only be wrong in the expensive direction. **The rule is four clauses**: at least one `@`; the first code point is not `@`; the last is not `@`; no ASCII control character; at most **254** code points (RFC 5321's path limit, in the house unit `ADR-0029` §2 and `ADR-0048` §1 fixed). No separate minimum — `a@b` is the shortest thing that passes. **Clause four is not a syntax rule**: no `addr-spec` holds a control character in any position, so it denies no mailbox, and it is there because a line terminator inside an address is the one thing this predicate could hand `EPIC-07`'s unwritten transport that would harm somebody who is not a player. **The rule runs where an address enters and nowhere else** — `forgot-password` keeps its unconditional `202` and consults no predicate, which is `ADR-0048` §2's *never at the lookup path* one endpoint pair over, and is the single property that keeps a future tightening free. **Nothing is canonicalised**: `emailAddressOrNull` returns the input unchanged, departing from `ADR-0048` §5 and `ADR-0029` §2 because an address is a **delivery target** and §2 fixed the stored form as what the player typed. **Deliberately not checked**: deliverability, DNS/MX, whether the domain is spelled right, disposable-address lists, role addresses, plus-address stripping, unicode conversion, a dot in the domain, quoting, and any whitespace that is not a control character. **The refusal is `400` with an empty body**, indistinguishable from a failed decode, and the client says one sentence that mentions no mailbox, no domain and no other account; the one constraint on the silent path is that **a `202` may not be reported as recovery being on**, since §3 leaves `hasRecoveryEmail` false. Both fixture tables are in §6, each carrying the entry that distinguishes this answer from `.+@.+\..+`. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **the endpoint's only feedback now fires almost never**, so nearly every mistake is answered `202` and silence; **`Bob Smith <bob@example.com>` and a trailing space are accepted** and are undeliverable; a player can believe recovery is on when it is not; the predicate is **never an invariant over `recovery_email`**, which is the price of the reversibility; plus-addressing and normalisation variants let **two rows be one mailbox**; and a permissive rule hands `EPIC-07` a higher bounce rate. **`TASK-041601`'s parked catalog assertion now has its condition met** — the answer admits non-ASCII — and becomes a ticket for the planner. **Unblocks `TASK-041624` and `TASK-041625`** (the latter still needed `DEC-072`, since answered). **Nothing was the human's**: no clause needs a paid service, the one alternative that would is refused on that ground, and nothing is sent under any answer. Raises no `DEC` |
| DEC-072 | **The architect's** — what is the shape of the mail seam, and what are its failure, lifetime and observability semantics? `ADR-0031` §7 declared *"a build with no sender configured is a valid state"* without saying what the wiring holds in it; §5 required the `202` before any mail work with delivery *"on a detached coroutine"* and settled nothing about that coroutine's parent, a failed send after the token row is committed, retries, what is logged, or **what a test can await** — which decides whether *a mail was sent* is assertable at all. Also where `baseUrl` lives and what an unconfigured one does | [`ADR-0077`](ADR-0077-no-sender-is-an-implementation-and-detachment-is-a-decorator.md) — **no sender is an implementation, detachment is a decorator, and a test binds neither.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-25. **No sender configured is `NoRecoveryMailer`, a `public object` with two empty bodies — never a null**, so no route branches on configuration and the property `TASK-041627` exists to prove belongs to the type rather than to three handlers each remembering the same `if`. **Detachment is `DetachedRecoveryMailer(delegate, scope, log)`, a decorator over the same port**, so no route file contains a `CoroutineScope`, a `launch` or a `Job`; the two compose outermost-first. **The delivery scope is a supervisor child of the application's job**, built in `duelServer` beside `scheduleSweeps` — `ADR-0025`'s argument applied unchanged, so shutdown cancels every in-flight send, one failure cancels no sibling, and it is *not* the application scope itself, which also carries a ticker that never completes. **The server may exit with mail pending**; pending mail is cancelled, not drained, because a drain timeout is a number about a transport nobody has measured. **A failed send is logged once and nothing else happens**: the member name and `failure::class.simpleName`, with **no message and no stack trace** — a transport's exception is the likeliest place a recipient address ever reaches a log, and §6.3 admits no exception — **no `player_id`**, since the port deliberately carries none and §6.4's ceiling is not a floor, and **no success line**. **Nothing above the port is retried and no row is compensated**: retry policy is transport-shaped and stays `EPIC-07`'s *inside* the port, and a compensating delete would destroy live tokens for mail that actually arrived, since a relay that times out its acknowledgement throws what a relay that never delivered throws. **`baseUrl` is a `ServerConfig` field** — `server.baseUrl`, `BASE_URL`, defaulting to the Vite dev origin — **absent is the default, present-but-malformed refuses to start** like every other field, and **`RecoveryLinks` is the only place either URL is constructed**. **What a test can await, which was the load-bearing clause: the test binds an undecorated recording double**, so the send is an ordinary suspend call in the handler and both `assertEquals(1, mailer.sent.size)` and `assertEquals(emptyList(), mailer.sent)` are decidable with no join, no channel and no timeout — **absence is what forced the shape**, since no await proves a negative and four criteria in this story assert one. The Ktor test-engine mechanism was **measured, not assumed**. **No test asserts about a mail through `duelServer`**, the decorator is unit-tested on its own, and **§5's ordering is not gated and cannot be** — it stays a review criterion, as `TASK-041626`'s Proof already predicted. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **a lost reset mail costs the player fifteen minutes of silence they cannot distinguish from anything else**, the direct price of no retry; the failure log names a class and nothing else, so a broken relay must be reproduced outside the server; nothing in `STORY-0416` exercises the real composition; and `TASK-041627` is now bigger than its Files table. **Chose no transport and named the one clause that could not be answered provider-independently** — the retry policy — leaving it to `EPIC-07` rather than guessing. **Unblocks `TASK-041625`, `TASK-041626` and `TASK-041627`. Raises `DEC-075`** |
| DEC-054 | **The architect's** — does the web client grow URL-addressable routes and a working browser *Back*, and what carries them: the History API behind the store, a router library, or neither for now? Raised by [`ADR-0060`](ADR-0060-the-record-is-its-own-screen-and-the-lobby-is-the-door.md), which makes the duel record a screen with **no address**: nothing links to it, a reload lands on the first screen, and *Back* leaves the client. The answer had to settle what owns *which screen is showing*, what a route does to `ADR-0032`'s out-of-tree boot and single socket, what the Vite dev proxy and whatever `EPIC-07` deploys must serve so a deep link is not a `404`, and whether `?room=` becomes a route | [`ADR-0076`](ADR-0076-a-screen-the-player-chose-has-an-address.md) — **a screen the player chose has an address; a screen the server gave has none.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-25. **Yes, the client gets addresses**, and the address is a URL **fragment**: `/` for the first screen, `/#/duels`, `/#/leaderboard`, and one slug for each of `STORY-0412`'s screens — however many that story turns out to have, which is the story's call and not the ADR's. A slug is the lowercase ASCII form of a word the product **already says to a player**, written as a literal so a restyled heading cannot break a link, so the ADR coins no player-facing vocabulary. **The waiting screen, the table and the result screen get no address, ever**: all three are chosen by frames — `RoomJoined`, the first `Snapshot`, `DuelFinished` — and an address claiming a seat is a client asserting a game fact, false the moment the room is reaped; a reload while seated lands on `/` and the frames put the player back, per `ADR-0072`. **The store outranks the address**: `Lobby.tsx` keeps its branch order, a player whom a frame seats is shown the duel (`ADR-0060` §5) and the fragment is replaced with `/` so the address does not lie. **The fragment beats a path segment on a sentence already merged in `room-link.ts`** — a path segment 404s on a static host with no rewrite rule and `EPIC-07` has no file in `tasks/epics/` — and it beats a query parameter because a query reaches the server's access logs and would contaminate the invite link a host copies. `#/duels` rather than `#duels`, because a bare fragment is an element identifier the browser will scroll to. **What carries it is two owned files and no dependency**: a pure `screen.ts` and a `use-screen.ts` over `useSyncExternalStore`, the primitive `ADR-0032` §3 already chose; `pushState`/`replaceState` fire neither `popstate` nor `hashchange`, so a push is an assignment to `location.hash` and a replace notifies its own subscribers — named because that bug is silent. ***Back* is defined at every boundary**: a chosen screen pushes, so browser *Back* returns to the first screen **in the same document**; `ADR-0060` §4's in-page control **replaces**; *Back* on the first screen or on a typed-in address leaves the client, correctly; and **`DuelResult`'s `<a href="/">` and the waiting screen's *Back to the lobby* stay real page loads**, because they are store boundaries and routing them would ship `ADR-0075`'s four-field presence leak. **There is no address a player is not entitled to**: an address selects a screen, grants nothing and is not a capability — `#/duels` in another browser shows that browser's record — and an unknown fragment renders the first screen with no error. Costs recorded rather than discovered: the destination now has **two spellings**, undoing the tidiness of `ADR-0060` §2's single constant; **the screen survives a reload and its paging, filter and search do not** (`ADR-0057`), so the address promises more than it delivers; **two navigation authorities** held apart by prose and a branch order; a **second hand-rolled `useSyncExternalStore` source** to keep correct; the in-page and browser *Back* **coexist and are not the same operation**, so `ADR-0073` §5's collision is defined rather than removed; and **every screen story from here owes an address**. Chosen partly because it is the cheapest to unwind — two files and two `useState` flags, no host, no build, no dependency. No Kotlin, no frame, no protocol step, no deployment requirement. **Unblocks `STORY-0412`'s split.** Raises no `DEC` |
| DEC-070 | **The product owner's** — how long does the most recent *action the server took* stay on a player's screen, and what takes it off? [`ADR-0046`](ADR-0046-the-table-says-away-timed-out-and-back.md) §4 settles the six sentences and settles **which** mark is shown — *“showing the most recent one is enough”* — and says nothing about its lifetime. Four answers were each defensible and each a different product: it never clears; it clears on the next `Snapshot`, the way [`ADR-0043`](ADR-0043-a-rejection-closes-no-decision-point.md) clears a rejection; it clears when the rival's presence returns to `PRESENT`; it clears on the next `YourTurn` | [`ADR-0075`](ADR-0075-the-mark-lives-as-long-as-the-absence-that-produced-it.md) — **the mark lives as long as the absence that produced it.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-24, deriving from `docs/vision.md`'s *Positioning* sentence — the same one `ADR-0046` derived its string set from — and from `ADR-0046` §2's already-shipped rule that a presence line clears on a frame and *never on a timer, never on a fade*. **Two of the four candidates were eliminated on the server's own code, not on taste**: `AbsentSeats.kt` returns `listOf(Addressed(0, mark), Addressed(1, mark)) + next.outbound`, and `act` composes that outbound through `framesFor` = `broadcast + turnFor`, where `broadcast` emits a `Snapshot` to **both** seats for every applied action — so the mark and the `Snapshot` describing the mark's own action are consecutive frames in one delivery, and *clears on the next `Snapshot`* (or on the next `YourTurn`, which rides the same delivery) would clear the mark microseconds after it is set and `ADR-0046` §4 would never be read by a human being. `ADR-0043` §3's precedent does not transfer because neither `rejection` nor `Your rival is back.` arrives welded to a `Snapshot`. **The mark is a status, not a notice**: while a seat is absent `foldAbsent` replaces it at nearly every decision point, so it can only go stale when the server stops acting. **Exactly two frames take it off** — an `OpponentPresence` carrying `PRESENT`, and `DuelFinished` — as two keys in two existing reducer cases; every other frame leaves it untouched, and there is **no timer, no fade and no dismiss control**. It clears on the **frame**, not on a transition, so unlike `rivalReturned` it needs no bookkeeping: a `PRESENT` at a resuming client whose rival never left still clears it, because the mark is about whether the server is acting and not about whether a return happened. **The words do not change** — `ADR-0046` §4's six sentences stand, no seventh string is added. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **a mark can be older than the hand on screen** (the present player folds the button pre-flop and the turn never reaches the absent seat), which is the price of §4's *no action log* and is the cost being chosen; **a mark naming this client's own seat has no clearing frame but `DuelFinished`**, because `OpponentPresence` is recipient-relative and the wire has no *you are present again*, and a protocol step was deliberately not requested for a race whose window is one delivery; **`serverAction` clears on `DuelFinished` as a boundary guard**, not because that frame says anything about absence, so that no mark can survive into a rematch without the correctness resting on a three-link argument; and **the store still has no room boundary** — `rivalPresence`, `graceRemainingMillis` and `rivalReturned` are cleared by nothing at either boundary, unreachable today only because the way back to the lobby is a real navigation that rebuilds `initialState()`, and a ticket rather than a decision the day `DEC-054` lands. Forecloses the *shape*: if an action log ever ships the mark attaches to its line by `(handNumber, actionSequence)` and takes that line's lifetime, superseding this rather than extending it — which is why the whole frame stays in the store. **Unblocks `TASK-031314` and `TASK-031315`**, both now `backlog` behind `TASK-031313` like the rest of the chain. No Kotlin, no frame, no protocol step, no stored data. Raises no `DEC` |
| DEC-069 | **The architect's** — what are the two numbers of the **sign-in** attempt budget, and does an over-budget attempt still count against its own window? `ADR-0027` §6 fixed the mechanism, the key and the answer and fixed no numbers; `ADR-0055` §2's pair is sign-up's alone | [`ADR-0074`](ADR-0074-sign-in-is-ten-wrong-passwords-a-minute-reserved-before-the-hash.md) — **ten failed sign-ins per remote address per rolling sixty seconds**, `AUTH_SIGN_IN_MAX_ATTEMPTS` `10` and `AUTH_SIGN_IN_WINDOW_MILLIS` `60000`, on a second `AttemptBudget` instance. The budget is **reserved before the hash and refunded when the password was right**, because metering failure and protecting the pool cannot both be met by one `admit` call and the obvious peek-then-record shape lets concurrent requests from one address burst unbounded Argon2 work. An over-budget attempt **still counts**, as `ADR-0022` §2 and `ADR-0055` §1 both have it — the sixty-second window, not a forked type, is what keeps that from compounding into a lockout. Over budget answers exactly as a wrong password does; no `429`, no `Retry-After`, no wire change |
| DEC-068 | **The product owner's** — does the *waiting for your rival* screen offer a way out, and what does it say? A host who creates a room is remembered in it (`pd.roomCode`, written on `RoomJoined`) and has no control that leaves it: `RoomRegistry.resume` declines a `WAITING` room, `Room.join` answers a player who already holds a seat with `ALREADY_SEATED`, and `DuelSocket.replyToJoinRoom` answers that *exactly as a fresh seating would* — so a reload lands back on the same screen. The routes out are a rival joining, the room being reaped as idle after ten minutes (`ADR-0022`), or clearing browser storage | [`ADR-0073`](ADR-0073-the-waiting-screen-says-back-to-the-lobby-and-the-room-stays-open.md) — **the waiting screen says *Back to the lobby*, and the room stays open.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-23, deriving from `docs/vision.md`'s *Positioning* sentence — the same one `ADR-0046` derived its string set from — and from `ADR-0022`, which already gives a `WAITING` room one way to end and no other. **The screen gains a way out**, calling `ADR-0072` §4's `forgetRoom()` from an event handler. **The control reads `Back to the lobby`, byte-identical to the string `DuelResult.tsx` already renders** for the same action on the same memory: one phrase for one action, no new vocabulary. **It does nothing to the room** — the room stays `WAITING`, the host keeps seat 0, the code keeps resolving, and the idle timeout is still the only thing that ends it — and **exactly one line says so**: *The room stays open. That link still works for your rival, and it brings you back.* Every clause was checked in source: `Room.join`'s `WAITING` branch seats a rival, and the host's own follow of the link is `ALREADY_SEATED` answered as `RoomJoined(code, seat)`. **The line names no duration**, because the client owns no clock against a server window (`ADR-0072` §6); when the room is reaped, the already-shipped *No duel room has that code.* is the correction. **No confirmation of any kind** — the action destroys nothing, and a dialog would assert that it does. **Two strings are the whole addition**, and a third needs a new ADR, which is what makes `STORY-0314`'s *the words are the ADR's* criterion enforceable. Refused by name, each with its reason: *Cancel* / *Close the room* / *Delete the room* (claims the room is gone; `Room.join` will still seat a rival at that code), *Leave* (claims the seat is vacated; `Room.seatOf` still names the host, and `ADR-0072` §4 refused `leaveRoom` for a function nobody sees), *Back* alone (the lobby's two panel swaps say it and change nothing), *forfeit* / *give up* / *stand up* / *sit out*, and any duration. **`design/screens/create-duel.html`'s waiting frame gains the control and the line verbatim**, as `EPIC-06`'s work; `STORY-0314` does not wait on it. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **a host pulled into a duel after pressing this has a store that never saw `RoomJoined`** — `deliver` addresses by player id through `ConnectionDirectory.writerFor`, so the lobby tab gets the opening `Snapshot` and renders the table, but `duel-state.ts` sets `mySeat` and `roomCode` only on `RoomJoined`, so the result screen loses its seat and a reload does not rejoin; **a rival opening the link after the host walked away gets a duel with an absent seat**, and the host can lose a coin without seeing a card — both predate this control, and it makes neither worse except by making the route one press; and **two components must now be reworded together**, with nothing enforcing it. Forecloses one thing on purpose: **this control never quietly becomes a room-closing control** — an invite that dies with its host is a frame, Kotlin in `EPIC-02` and a protocol step, and it must arrive with different words. **Unblocks `STORY-0314`** and nothing else. Raises no `DEC` |
| DEC-067 | **The architect's** — after a `DuelFinished`, does this tab still remember the room it was seated in, and what forgets it so the lobby stays reachable? `boot.ts` forgets the code on that frame (`TASK-031009`), because the way on from the result is a reload (`TASK-030807`) and a tab that remembered would rejoin, be handed the same `DuelFinished`, and never reach the lobby. `ADR-0044` §5 needs the opposite — a reopened socket must re-`JoinRoom` to be handed the standing offer — so as merged a rematch survives no reconnect and the player's own press answers `UNKNOWN_ROOM` from an empty `RoomMembership` | [`ADR-0072`](ADR-0072-a-tab-remembers-its-room-until-the-player-leaves-it.md) — **a tab remembers its room until the player leaves it, and the way back is what forgets.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-23. **The client half moves, not the server's**: `boot.ts`'s `DuelFinished` branch is **deleted**, `pd.roomCode` means *the room this tab is seated in* and never *the screen this tab should show*, and exactly two things clear it — the player leaving, and `Failure(UNKNOWN_ROOM)` answering this tab's **own** rejoin, with `TASK-031010`'s `rejoining` guard kept verbatim. Not `DuelFinished`, not a timer, not the store. **The asymmetry was verified, not inferred**: `boot.test.ts`'s merged, green *sends no JoinRoom on the Welcome after a duel has finished* is a standing assertion that no socket this tab opens ever rejoins after a finish — a second `Welcome` being exactly what `reconnecting.ts` produces per attempt — so `DuelSocket.replyToJoinRoom`'s `for (player in resumed.room.rematchOffers)` loop is code that runs for nobody, and the case `ADR-0044` §5 was written for (an offer made while the opponent is inside their grace window) is the one that stays broken. **The surface is one member**: `DuelClient` gains `forgetRoom(): void` beside `store` and `send`, `DuelProvider` an **optional** third prop, `useForgetRoom()` sits beside `useSend()`, and `ADR-0032` §3's *event handlers only* extends to it verbatim — an effect would fire twice under StrictMode and would fire on the mount a rejoin just produced. Named for the mechanism on purpose: `leaveRoom` would be a lie, because nothing is sent, no seat is vacated and the open socket keeps its room — **the memory is about the next socket, never the current one**. **The way back stays an `<a href="/">`** and gains one `onClick`; `removeItem` is synchronous, so no `preventDefault` and no `window.location` are needed and `TASK-030807`'s reason for a navigation survives intact, with `DuelResult` still a function of its props (`onLeave`, handed down by `Lobby.tsx` exactly as `TASK-030910` hands down `onOffer`). **A stale code cannot strand a tab**: a boot holding a reaped code is refused, forgets under the `rejoining` guard, and lands at the lobby with *No duel room has that code.* — one round trip and one of `ADR-0022`'s ten budgeted joins, and **no client-side clock**, because a client never acts on a window the wire does not carry. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **a merged ticket is reversed and two green tests are deleted** — `TASK-031009` stays `done` and unrewritten on `ADR-0071`'s precedent, and §9 fixes the four replacement assertions so this is a reversal and not a weakening; **the way back is no longer a plain link**, so a modifier-click forgets the room in a tab that stays on the result screen; **a tab closed on the result screen comes back to it** until the player presses the way back or the room is reaped; **the two-tab adoption fight widens** from the duel to the whole finished-room lifetime; and the optional prop means a `main.tsx` that omitted it would compile and ship this exact defect, a hole bought to keep fourteen unrelated render sites out of the change and closed by review alone. Forecloses **the client deciding when a room has expired**. Rejected, each with its strongest case: shipping the rematch live-sockets-only (leaves `TASK-030913` asserting frames no client can receive); re-remembering on `RematchOffered` (makes the trap **intermittent** and misses every drop that follows `DuelFinished`); forgetting on every `UNKNOWN_ROOM` (re-opens the mistype bug, and a *live* finished room refuses nothing, so the lobby stays unreachable); a client timeout; a server auto-resume on `Welcome` (no way to say *not that room*); a `?lobby=1` marker in the invite's query string; and `sessionStorage`. **Blocks and unblocks no ticket** — `STORY-0309`'s fourteen apply frames to the store — but the transport half of its fourth acceptance criterion is now decided, and the story may not be called done without it. **Names one gap it does not close**: the *waiting for your rival* screen still has no way out, which is a screen affordance and a ticket of its own. Raises no `DEC` |
| DEC-066 | **The architect's** — `ADR-0028` §1 specifies `@SerialName("ActedForAbsentSeat")` verbatim; `ProtocolDiscriminatorTest.everyDiscriminatorIsShortAndUnique` has failed the build on any discriminator over **16** characters since `TASK-020210`, and that name is 18. Found by the `ADR-0070` §2 probe, not by reading: `Discriminator longer than 16 characters: [ActedForAbsentSeat]`. `OpponentPresence` is exactly 16 and passes. Shorten the `@SerialName`, rename the type, or raise the limit — three edits with different consequences on the wire and on a merged gate | [`ADR-0071`](ADR-0071-a-discriminator-is-its-kotlin-type-name.md) — **a discriminator is its Kotlin type name, and the name moves rather than the gate.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-23. **The type is renamed, not the annotation and not the gate**: `ActedForAbsentSeat` becomes **`ActedForAbsent`** (14), Kotlin type and `@SerialName` together, with `ADR-0028` §1's four fields, both `require` blocks, both recipients and every §5 emission point standing exactly as written — *the word that goes is the one the payload already supplies*, since the frame carries `seat`, exactly as its sibling `OpponentPresence` carries none and names none. **The invariant is stated for the first time**: a `ClientMessage`/`ServerMessage` subtype's `@SerialName` equals its Kotlin simple name character for character, and a divergence is a **defect** — `@SerialName` is not a licence to diverge but what makes moving the wire name deliberate rather than a refactor's side effect. It is **not gated and cannot cheaply be**: a descriptor carries no Kotlin name and `TASK-020210` forbade reflection by name, so it holds by review. **Divergence was rejected on a measured fact** — `typeNameOf` reads `descriptor.serialName`, so a shortened annotation renames the *client's* generated interface, `frames.ts`'s key, `docs/protocol.md`'s row and the ledger fingerprint, leaving the long name alive only in the server's own sources; and it would be this protocol's **first** divergence in sixteen types, which is the expensive one. **The 16 stands, unedited and explicitly unratified.** Looked for rather than assumed: `TASK-020210` gives no reason, `TASK-020718` restates it as *"stay short"*, the test's KDoc is silent on length, no ADR mentions it, and `TASK-020213`'s frame limits are in kilobytes — and the same JSON already carries the engine's `BettingRoundEnded` (17) and `UncalledBetReturned` (19) nested inside `Events`, so **16 is not a property of the wire** but a house rule on the envelope's fourteen names, which is the set every client switches on. It is a **style** gate, not a correctness one: the fully-qualified-name check already catches every defaulted discriminator. It is nevertheless not moved here, because *"the name I already wrote is two over"* is the one evidence that must never move a threshold — `ADR-0070` §4 forbids a weakened assertion as propagation, and a name renames again on any version step while a raised limit cannot come back down without renaming whatever grew past it. The trigger is written down: a frame whose clearest name **cannot** fit 16 without losing meaning, alternatives written out and rejected on what each costs a reader. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **twelve settled documents now name a type that will not exist** and are deliberately left as written (`ADR-0028`, `ADR-0044`, `ADR-0045`, `ADR-0046`, two answered rows here, two `BOARD.md` paragraphs, and six `done` tickets and stories), with only `ADR-0028` amended and `ADR-0071`'s Consequences §1 acting as the index a grep ends at; **eight live documents are corrected** where the rejected alternative would have cost none; **§1's invariant is enforced by review alone**; the name is **worse than the one `ADR-0028` chose**, by one word, on purpose; **16 is now defended without ever having been justified**; and `OpponentPresence` sits exactly on it, so this corner has no headroom left. Forecloses the divergence family permanently, *including* its legitimate use — renaming a Kotlin type while holding the wire still now costs a version step. **Unblocks `TASK-021402`** (`files_touched` unchanged at 13, `ProtocolDiscriminatorTest.kt` is **not** a fourteenth *Files* row, `status: ready`, `TASK-021401` having landed) **and with it `STORY-0214`, `STORY-0313` and `STORY-0405`**. Names no ticket and raises no `DEC` |
| DEC-065 | **The architect's** — `ADR-0069` §3's probe missed two more files on the same ticket, at the same place, one day later: probe **(b)** runs `compileTestKotlin` and `tsc` while §3's own justification sentence claims *"`:poker-server:check` compiles test sources and `npm run check` runs `vitest`"*, and its edit says *"each sealed hierarchy"* so an enum entry trips nothing. Are **(a)** and **(b)** two probes at all if both run full commands; what makes an enumeration **complete** rather than merely longer; and may a coder that has *empirically demonstrated* the whole set amend the *Files* table itself, or does that hand away `ADR-0069` §2's growth protection? Registered and answered in the same PR, because an implementation attempt raised it rather than a planner | [`ADR-0070`](ADR-0070-a-blast-radius-is-complete-only-when-the-gates-are-green.md) — **a blast radius is complete only when the gate set is green, and a merged gate may add the row that makes it so.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-23. **The obvious repair is not the fix.** Making **(b)** full would still miss both files: a red run of the gate set names a **prefix** — the probe's own `ServerMessage` variant breaks `DuelSocket.kt` in *main* sources, so `check` dies at `compileKotlin`, never reaches `test`, and never runs `ServerMessageHandshakeTest` or `TypeScriptDeclarationsTest` — which is exactly what happened in round one, where side **(a)** was already full. And **(b)**'s *edit* excludes `ProtocolError` by wording, so file 16 is invisible at any command depth. **Four procedures, one mechanism: every one was a copy** — a list of four files, of five, of twelve read out of two directories, and now of commands and of language constructs. A copy drifts from what it copies. So: **one probe**, every stub edit applied together, no hand-computed union; **its commands are the ones `.github/workflows/build.yml` runs, by reference and verbatim** — today root `./gradlew check -PrequireDocker=true`, `npm ci`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, recorded as a dated observation and not as the rule — so a check added to CI joins every probe with no ADR edited and **no document may publish a narrower probe command**; **the probe is a loop that ends on `exit 0`**, each red run's paths becoming *Files* rows and each getting the minimal propagation before the next run, because **there is no prefix of green**; and **the stub edit is the story's declared surface** — every declaration added, removed, renamed or re-valued, an enum entry included, carrying the fields the story states. `ADR-0069` §2 stands with **one bounded exception**: a coder may add a *Files* row and continue when all four hold — a merged gate fails naming the path (quoted in the PR), the ticket's own authorised edits are what make it fail, the edit is **propagation not decision** (no behaviour, no new test, **no assertion weakened or derived away**), and the full gate set then exits 0. Anything else is still a `DEC`. Both files added here hold **golden** assertions — nine hard-coded `ProtocolError` names, and the exact string `export type ClientMessage = …` — and deriving them instead of updating them would turn each into `x == x`, so the ADR forbids it explicitly. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **the probe becomes the most expensive step in planning** (a root `check` with Docker plus `npm ci`/`check`/`build`, iterated, before any design exists) and the pressure to narrow it is answered only by a rule; **the coder gains a unilateral edit of its own contract**, whose third condition is judgement read by a cheap reviewer; `files_touched` becomes as-implemented rather than as-planned; a probe run without a Docker daemon is quietly narrower than CI and the remedy is a declaration; and **four documents now answer one question in three days**. **Unblocks `TASK-021301`** (`files_touched: 17`, `status: ready`, the fifteen files already implemented unchanged) **and with it `STORY-0213`, `STORY-0214` and `STORY-0405`**. Names no new ticket and raises no `DEC`. `lint_tickets.py` is deliberately untouched — 17 rows still equal 17 |
| DEC-064 | **The architect's** — `ADR-0068` §5's enumeration missed three files and the first ticket it governed stalled at thirteen against a ceiling of twelve. What is the ceiling, must the enumeration widen to test sources and how is a planner to run it *before* sizing, and does the version-literal-in-fixtures category deserve a named mechanism? Registered and answered in the same PR, because an implementation attempt raised it rather than a planner | [`ADR-0069`](ADR-0069-the-blast-radius-is-probed-not-remembered.md) — **the blast radius is probed, not remembered, and a ticket's size is its own *Files* table.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-23. **The ceiling is deleted, not raised**: `MAX_FILES_TOUCHED_ATOMIC` is gone and there is no maximum on an `atomic:` ticket, because the set is monotone in a gate count nobody controls — it has been **four** (`ADR-0045` §4), **five** (`ADR-0047` §6), **twelve** (`ADR-0068` §5) and **fifteen** today, each step taken by an ordinary ticket that added a gate without knowing it was widening a bump. `ADR-0068` §5 said *"whatever replaces the five must not be another number"* while its own §3 wrote twelve into the linter, and **that contradiction is what stalled**. In its place the linter checks the one thing knowable at planning time: on a ticket declaring `atomic:`, **`files_touched` must equal the count of `create`/`modify`/`regenerate`/`delete`/`rename` rows in that ticket's own *Files* table** (≥ 4, unknown action = failure) — the residual `ADR-0068` §7 left unbuilt, built now and scoped to `atomic:` tickets only so the nine merged under-declaring tickets stay green. **What stops an atomic ticket is a file its *Files* table does not name, at any count** — the count-independent rule that actually fired here, promoted from ticket prose. **The enumeration becomes a probe**: `ADR-0068` §5's *"run the checks on the tree being changed"* is a **verification** procedure, unrunnable by a planner who has no changed tree, so its twelve-file list came from reading and reading stopped at the two `protocol/` directories. A probe is a throwaway one-line change made only to be read and reverted — `PROTOCOL_VERSION + 1` then `:poker-server:check` and `npm run check`; one throwaway sealed variant then `compileTestKotlin` and `tsc` — and it finds test sources because the compiler and `vitest` do not know which directory a file is in. **No count is written down anywhere again**, including here. **A version literal in a fixture is one of three things and only one of them is a literal**: the test whose subject *is* the number keeps its literal and there is exactly one per side (`ProtocolJsonTest`, and the client's derived `version.test.ts`); a test needing a *different* version writes `PROTOCOL_VERSION ± 1`, never an absolute number; every other fixture references the constant. Found while checking: **five client fixtures carry `protocolVersion: 2` and survive a bump silently** — `Welcome.protocolVersion` is generated as `number` so `tsc` sees nothing, and none of them reaches `connection.ts`'s one comparison — and `connection.test.ts`'s *"another version"* case writes the absolute `3`, which this bump turns into the current version. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **the guard surface narrows from ~fifteen incidental literals to two deliberate ones**, so if `ProtocolJsonTest` or `version.test.ts` were ever weakened nothing else would notice; **the only mechanical brake on an atomic ticket's size is gone** — a forty-row table with `files_touched: 40` lints clean; the probe costs two full `check` runs and must be reverted; the probe is complete only for gates that exist when it runs; **five fixtures stay stale until a separate ticket lands**; and the rules move twice in two days, so the trail holds *twelve* and *no number* one day apart. **Unblocks `TASK-021301`** (`files_touched: 15`, `atomic:` at seven gates, `status: ready`, the twelve files already implemented at `c904503` unchanged) **and with it `STORY-0213`, `STORY-0214` and `STORY-0405`**. **Names one ticket** — the five silently-stale client fixtures — and raises no `DEC`. Declined with a trigger: generating `Welcome.protocolVersion` as the `ProtocolVersion` literal alias |
| DEC-063 | **The architect's** — a `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump is irreducibly **twelve** files and a schema-2 ticket is capped at **three**; which gives, and how is a bump ticket written? | [`ADR-0068`](ADR-0068-an-atomic-ticket-names-the-gate-that-forbids-splitting-it.md) — **the cap gives, narrowly: an atomic ticket declares up to twelve files and names the merged gate that forbids splitting it.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-23. **The gate outranks the budget, always** — `ProtocolVersionLedgerTest`, `ProtocolDocumentationTest`, `verifyProtocolTypes`, `verifyDuelScript`, Kotlin exhaustiveness and TS1360 do not move, are not weakened and gain no escape hatch, which forecloses the *split the gates* family permanently: a lint rule about markdown frontmatter is not a reason to let a wire shape and the number naming it be separated by a commit boundary. **`files_touched` becomes a fact** — the count of `create`/`modify`/`regenerate` rows in the ticket's *Files* table, never smaller than that table — and schema 2 gains **one optional key, `atomic:`**, naming the gate in one line; without it the range stays `1..3` unchanged, with it `4..12`, and `atomic:` below four is rejected so the key means exactly one thing. **Twelve is a tripwire, not a budget**: it is the largest atomic unit this repository has demonstrated, so a thirteenth file becomes a decision rather than an escalation. A gate is something that fails an exit code — a compiler rule, a merged test, a verify task, a database constraint; *"they belong together"* and **a scope that grew after the ticket was written** earn nothing. **`ADR-0047` §6's "five artifacts" is wrong by seven and is replaced by a procedure, not another number** — a bump carries `PROTOCOL_VERSION` plus every file a merged gate derives from it, enumerated by running `:poker-server:check` and `npm run check`, which is twelve as of 2026-08-23 and dated so a stale list is recognisable; `ADR-0047` is not rewritten and `docs/protocol.md` is corrected in place. Measured rather than asserted: **683 schema-2 tickets, ten under-declare, nine of them merged that way**, every one naming its extra files in the open — the defect is unrepresentability, not dishonesty — and nine share one mechanism (a gate propagating a change mechanically) while `TASK-020717` simply **grew**, which is why a blanket cap raise was rejected: it would license the tenth as readily as the nine. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **the linter checks presence and a range, never truth**, so `atomic: <anything>` unlocks twelve for anyone willing to type a sentence; **twelve has zero headroom for the ticket that motivated it**, so a thirteenth file stalls the same critical path again; **the nine merged tickets are not corrected**, leaving nine permanently false fields in the trail; five places must learn the key; `files_touched` now means two things at once; and a bump ticket is a legitimately large unit for a cheap model — previously impossible, now merely expensive. **Unblocks `TASK-021301`** (`files_touched: 12`, `atomic:` set, `status: ready`) **and with it `STORY-0213`, `STORY-0214` and `STORY-0405`**. Names no ticket and raises no `DEC` |
| DEC-003 | When the big blind cannot cover its own blind, does the bar for the round stand at the nominal big blind or at the amount actually posted? | [`../duel-rules.md`](../duel-rules.md) — the bar is what was actually posted, so a big blind all-in for 60 at 50/100 leaves 60 to match and the small blind owes 10 more rather than 50. Most cardrooms hold the bar at the full blind; we do not, because the number a player faces should be genuinely at stake. A rules call, not an architecture one, so it is written into the rules rather than an ADR |
| DEC-004 | Whose cards are shown at showdown, and does the loser's hand ever become public? | [ADR-0008](ADR-0008-loser-mucks-at-showdown.md) — the loser mucks: only the winner's hand is revealed, and a mucked hand appears in no event, for anyone |
| DEC-005 | Where does a match-level event live? | [ADR-0009](ADR-0009-match-events-are-their-own-hierarchy.md) — its own `MatchEvent` hierarchy |
| DEC-006 | Where does event-log serialisation live, and in what format? | [ADR-0010](ADR-0010-engine-takes-a-serialization-dependency.md) — kotlinx.serialization, inside the engine, behind a narrowed guard |
| DEC-007 | How are the TypeScript protocol types generated, and what stops the checked-in output drifting? | [ADR-0020](ADR-0020-typescript-protocol-from-serial-descriptors.md) — an owned emitter over the `SerialDescriptor`s; a byte-comparing verify task on `check` fails CI on drift |
| DEC-010 | Do room and lobby messages belong to STORY-0202's protocol, or extend the sealed hierarchies? | [ADR-0017](ADR-0017-the-server-says-when-a-duel-ends.md) — later stories extend the existing hierarchies |
| DEC-011 | A device opens a second socket while one is live — refuse, adopt, or allow both? | [ADR-0018](ADR-0018-a-second-socket-adopts-the-seat.md) — the new socket adopts the seat, the old is closed |
| DEC-013 | Is a per-room `Mutex` enough once a duel runs inside the room? | [ADR-0016](ADR-0016-a-room-is-serialised-by-its-own-mutex.md) — the mutex stays; no actor |
| DEC-014 | Does the `duel` table gain a `hands_played` column? | [ADR-0019](ADR-0019-the-duel-table-records-hands-played.md) — yes, now, via V2 while the table is empty |
| DEC-015 | How does the end of a duel reach a client? | [ADR-0017](ADR-0017-the-server-says-when-a-duel-ends.md) — a new `ServerMessage.DuelFinished` |
| DEC-012 | Is holding a room code sufficient authorisation to take the second seat, or does joining need rate limiting or host confirmation? | [ADR-0022](ADR-0022-the-room-code-is-the-invite.md) — the code is the invite (the human's call); `RoomRegistry.join` budgets failed attempts at 10 per player per minute |
| DEC-016 | What names the opponent in a result line? | [ADR-0021](ADR-0021-a-profile-gains-a-display-name.md) — a profile gains a player-chosen display name (the human's call); nullable `player.display_name`, joined into the read path; product rules split to `DEC-017` |
| DEC-020 | What does an absent seat do at a decision point where `Fold` is illegal? | [ADR-0023](ADR-0023-an-absent-seat-checks-when-nothing-is-owed.md) — it checks; fold only when facing a bet, the action read from the engine's `legalActions`; `poker-engine` unchanged; amends ADR-0013 |
| DEC-021 | How is design work run — conversationally, or through the ticket lifecycle? | [ADR-0024](ADR-0024-design-follows-the-code-workflow.md) — ordinary ticketed work; `design/` is canonical and claude.ai/design is a render surface |
| DEC-019 | What drives `RoomRegistry.reap()` and `expireGracePeriods()` in production, with what period, scope and failure behaviour? | [ADR-0025](ADR-0025-one-ticker-coroutine-drives-both-sweeps.md) — one ticker coroutine on the application scope in `module()`; one configured period (`sweepPeriodMillis`, default 1 s), fixed delay, expiry then delivery then reap; a throwing pass is logged and retried next tick, only cancellation at shutdown ends the loop |
| DEC-022 | What is `web-client`'s toolchain, and how do its checks enter CI? | [ADR-0026](ADR-0026-vite-and-npm-drive-the-web-client.md) — Vite + npm on Node 24 (pinned once, in `web-client/.nvmrc`; CI governs); Vitest, ESLint + Prettier with `src/protocol/protocol.gen.ts` ignored by path in both; Vite's proxy carries `/api` and `/ws` to Ktor on 8080; a parallel `client` CI job runs `npm ci`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, and Gradle stays JVM-only |
| DEC-028 | What is the credential, session and handshake model once a device id is no longer the only credential? | [ADR-0027](ADR-0027-the-session-outranks-the-device-id.md) — a session token outranks a device id and never falls back to it; the token is opaque, stored as its SHA-256 with a 30-day absolute expiry, and presented in `Hello.sessionToken` on the socket and `Authorization: Bearer` over HTTP; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` moves to 3 once, in STORY-0405. What a credential *contains* stays with DEC-027 |
| DEC-018 | Does a player see anything while the duel is paused around them? | [ADR-0028](ADR-0028-the-wire-names-an-absent-opponent.md) — yes: `OpponentPresence` carries PRESENT/AWAY/ABSENT plus a remaining-millis countdown the client renders but never acts on, and `ActedForAbsentSeat` marks **every** action the server took for an absent seat, check as well as fold. Retracts the wire half of ADR-0023's indistinguishability. |
| DEC-026 | What does a claim convert, and what happens when a device with an anonymous profile signs into a different account? | [ADR-0030](ADR-0030-a-claim-adds-a-credential-and-moves-nothing.md) — a claim attaches a credential to the `player` row already resolved (no second profile, no copied `duel_result`, no `UPDATE player`); signing into another account writes only an `auth_session`, leaving `player.device_id` pointing at the anonymous profile, so sign-out restores it by subtraction. No schema change. Amends ADR-0027 |
| DEC-027 | Does an account carry an email, what may it be used for, and can a password be recovered without one? | [ADR-0031](ADR-0031-an-optional-verified-recovery-email.md) — an optional address in its own `recovery_email` table holding **only verified** addresses, feeding a single-use one-hour reset token that revokes every session when consumed. A player signs in with a lowercase login **handle**, never the display name and never the email. Declining the email means no recovery path at all |
| DEC-033 | How does React read the duel store, and where does the single `Connection` live? | [ADR-0032](ADR-0032-react-subscribes-to-a-store-it-does-not-own.md) — one `DuelStore` and one `Connection` per tab, created by framework-free boot wiring (`bootDuelClient` in `src/store/boot.ts`) that `main.tsx` calls once, outside the tree; components read through `useDuelState()` (`useSyncExternalStore` from React core — no store library) and send through `useSend()` from event handlers only; message-triggered sends are boot reactions, so `JoinRoom` after `Welcome` is exactly-once under StrictMode by construction, and no screen ever holds the `Connection` |
| DEC-034 | Do a component's internal em ratios — the wordmark lockup's four — fall under ADR-0024 §2's "every size is born in the sheet", or may they live in the canonical card behind a drift-gate clause? | DEC-035 | How does `design/check-drift.sh` read a CSS value? (Registered by #523 in **both** registers; `ADR-0034` recorded the answer here but struck neither open row, and its header's claim that the row lived only on the board is wrong — an ADR is immutable, so the correction lives here. Both open rows are struck by the change that adds this line) | [ADR-0034](ADR-0034-the-value-gate-reads-css-string-aware.md) — the awk regex `--pd-NAME:[^;]*;` is deleted and the reader becomes a third stock-perl walker, `VALUES`, scoped to `<style>`/`.css` regions, comment-stripping, string- and escape-aware, ending a declaration at a top-level `;` or its block's `}`, collapsing in-string whitespace so a wrapped string equals its one-line spelling — and refusing, by name, any shape it cannot read rather than returning a partial set. Two premises of the DEC's registration are corrected there: `ADR-0024` carries no stock-tools constraint, and the gate already runs two quote-aware perl walkers |
| DEC-001 | What exactly is one duel? | [ADR-0035](ADR-0035-a-duel-is-a-freezeout.md) — a freezeout: 100 BB, blinds rising every 10 hands, one player holds every chip. **The human's call.** The numbers stay `DuelFormat` configuration and the escalating schedule stays load-bearing, since `TASK-010715`'s termination property depends on it. Fixed-length duels remain implemented and available as configuration |
| DEC-009 | Can a duel be watched, and if so what may a spectator see and when? | [ADR-0040](ADR-0040-a-duel-may-be-watched-without-hole-cards.md) — live, with no hole card that showdown has not revealed. **The human's call.** A spectator has no seat, so the projection gains a third entry point in the engine beside `PlayerView.of` and `visibleTo` — never a sentinel seat and never a filter in transport — carrying its own leak property. Nothing is built; no epic owns spectating yet |
| DEC-017 | Is a display name refused when it is set, and can one ever be taken away? | [ADR-0038](ADR-0038-a-name-is-screened-when-set-and-can-be-taken-away.md) — a blocklist screens at set time, an operator may force-rename afterwards, and a name taken away is **retired forever** rather than released. **The human's call.** Uniqueness gains two more sources of truth; `ADR-0029`'s permanence becomes permanence *to the player*. Homoglyph impersonation is explicitly not solved. Unblocks `STORY-0410` |
| DEC-025 | Is an account ever required to play, or is anonymous play permanent? | [ADR-0036](ADR-0036-an-account-is-offered-never-required.md) — never required; anonymous play stays fully ranked, and the client offers an account after the player's **first win**, dismissible permanently. **The human's call.** `EPIC-04` gains one prompt story and gates nothing |
| DEC-029 | May a player delete their account, and what becomes of the opponent's history rows? | [ADR-0039](ADR-0039-v01-offers-no-account-deletion.md) — no deletion in v0.1, recorded as a position rather than left implied. **The human's call.** `STORY-0403`'s schema must keep both answers reachable, which forbids denormalising a display name into `duel_result` — the constraint with a real cost |
| DEC-030 | After a credential is attached, may the device id still sign in? | [ADR-0037](ADR-0037-the-device-is-a-credential-until-revoked.md) — yes, until the player revokes it from the account screens; revoking does not end the revoking session, and is offered only once a credential exists. **The human's call**, framed as risk acceptance. The account screens must state which routes are live. Amends `ADR-0030` |
| DEC-031 | May a player ever sign in with a third-party account, or is a handle and password the only credential? | [ADR-0041](ADR-0041-a-handle-and-a-password-are-the-only-credential.md) — handle and password only for v0.1 and v0.2, and the account screens are designed for one credential. **The human's call.** Explicitly *not now* rather than *never*: `ADR-0027`'s `credential.kind` is untouched, so adding a provider stays additive in the schema and costs a screen redesign |
| DEC-036 | How does an unattended run report its status to the human, on what schedule, and what makes a report survive the agent that would have written it? | [ADR-0042](ADR-0042-the-run-reports-itself-every-two-hours.md) — Telegram over stdlib Python; reports composed from **repository state** rather than the agent's memory, so a dead agent is not a missing report; an in-session two-hourly cron; the stop report carried by a `Stop` hook. **The human's call** on all three open points, taken 2026-08-15. The cost recorded rather than discovered: the heartbeat dies when Claude Code is quit, which `STORY-1104` exists to reverse in one command |
| DEC-037 | After a `Rejected`, is the decision point still open on the client, and when does a rejection stop being shown? | [`ADR-0043`](ADR-0043-a-rejection-closes-no-decision-point.md) — a `Rejected` closes nothing: the reducer keeps `pendingTurn`, clears `rejection` on the next `YourTurn`/`Snapshot`/`DuelFinished`, and counts refusals in a new `rejectionCount` so the bar's existing remount key lifts its in-flight lock. The server is **not** changed and `YourTurn` keeps meaning *a new decision point opened*, because `guard` proves the identity the client already holds is still valid after a rejection. The cost recorded rather than discovered: `DuelState` gains its first field the server did not send, and the remount discards the amount the player had dialled.
