| DEC-041 | **The architect's** — what shape does revoking the device binding take in the schema, given `ADR-0030` §2 says `player.device_id` is never rewritten by any identity operation? | [`ADR-0049`](ADR-0049-a-device-binding-is-a-row-and-revoking-is-final.md) — **the edge moves out of `player` into its own `device_binding(device_id, player_id, bound_at, revoked_at)` table**, keyed by the natural pair, with two partial unique indexes (`WHERE revoked_at IS NULL`) that are simultaneously the two invariants and the two hot reads: one live binding per device, one per player. Revocation is one `UPDATE ... SET revoked_at = now() WHERE player_id = ?` against a table that is not the ledger, so `player` is byte-identical across it and **no fourth writer of `player` appears** — `ADR-0030` §2's sentence is retired by being made vacuous, because `player` has no device column left to rewrite, and `resolve`'s upsert degrades to a plain `INSERT`. Revoking is **final**: an `ADR-0029`-shaped trigger refuses `revoked_at → NULL`, and the composite primary key makes a device returning to the profile it left a key violation rather than a rule someone must remember; a revoked device may still mint a **fresh** profile, so a resold phone is not bricked. `DELETE /api/me/device` requires a session (`401` otherwise — the only way to honour "revoking does not kill the revoking session"), answers `409` for a player with no credential and `204` either way otherwise. **Sessions and sockets are untouched**, which is not the same question as expiry. Sign-up answers **`422`**, empty body, distinct from the handle's `400` and `409`, matching the `422` `ADR-0031` §5 already promised for a policy that did not exist. **Derived from the vision**, not the human's call: *"Not real-money gambling"* beside *"a counter of duels won"* fixes the proportion, and *"Lichess … not PokerStars. Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"* fills the gap. | DEC-042 | **The architect's** — by what path does an operator force-rename a profile, and where do the blocklist and the retired-name set live? | [`ADR-0051`](ADR-0051-a-name-is-registered-before-it-is-held.md) — **one table is the whole namespace, and the operator's path is one function in it.** `name_registry(name PK, reason, retired_from, created_at)` holds names in use, blocked names and retired names as three values of `reason` behind one unique index on `ADR-0029` §1's ICU fold; `player.display_name` gains a foreign key into it, so a name cannot be held that was never registered. **A string never leaves that table** — a takedown promotes `TAKEN → RETIRED` and deletes nothing — which is why `ADR-0038`'s three sources of truth collapse into one `INSERT` and the `READ COMMITTED` race between a claim's subquery snapshot and the unique index's physical state **cannot arise**: the claimer conflicts with the row whatever its reason is, and never waits for a takedown to finish. | DEC-044 | **The architect's** — the day the Argon2 cost is raised, what happens to rows written under the old parameters? `ADR-0027` §1's *"a constant change plus a rehash on next successful verify"* needs a parser that accepts other parameters; `STORY-0403`'s parser refuses everything but ours, because one that accepts `m=8` is a downgrade attack in a helper function | [`ADR-0054`](ADR-0054-a-raised-argon2-cost-is-a-ledger-entry-and-a-rehash.md) — **a closed, append-only ledger of the cost parameter sets this project has shipped, and a rehash on the next successful sign-in.** `parseArgon2PhcOrNull` keeps its whole-string comparison — one literal becomes *n*, and the strings are generated by the same `section()` the encoder uses, so the allow list cannot describe a string the encoder could not have written and there is still **no number parser on the verify path** for an attacker-supplied string to fool. What refuses a downgrade is **not** a comparison with the current cost — every historical entry is weaker than current by definition, which is what raising means — but a fixed `ARGON2_FLOOR` equal to the first set ever shipped, which never moves: appending `m=8` makes it *current* and turns `Argon2PhcEncodeTest`'s pinned literals red, inserting it breaks the strict ascent in `m × t`, and **prepending** it — the case an ascent check alone happily allows — is below the floor. `m` may never decrease, so memory hardness cannot be traded for passes at an equal product; `p` is pinned at 1 rather than ordered, since more lanes is not stronger. The trigger is the first edit to `ARGON2_MEMORY_KIB`/`ITERATIONS`/`PARALLELISM`, already enforced by `Argon2PhcEncodeTest`'s literal strings, and §8 lists the eight things that day's one PR contains — including re-minting `DUMMY_PHC`, which otherwise degrades `ADR-0027` §6's enumeration defence in **two** different ways with every test still green, and re-deriving `ARGON2_MAX_PARALLEL`, since `Argon2ConcurrencyTest` proves the bound is honoured and never that it is affordable. That is a change of *predicate*, not of mechanism — `ADR-0022` and `ADR-0027` §6 meter failure because they defend a **search space**; this meters spending because it defends a **rate**. Reading the code sharpened the question: `PostgresCredentials.create` hashes *before* the insert discovers a collision, so of `ADR-0048` §6's six outcomes only `201` and the **taken-handle `409`** cost a hash — and the success path is self-limiting (`holdsCredential` caps one device id at one sign-up ever) while the taken-handle path writes no row, so the player still holds nothing and **one device id plus one known handle is an unbounded stream of Argon2**. Extends `ADR-0048` §6 with a seventh row. Raises `DEC-049` |
| DEC-049 | **The product owner's** — when sign-up answers `429` (`ADR-0055` §3), what is the player told and what does the form do? | [`ADR-0056`](ADR-0056-a-throttled-sign-up-says-so-and-keeps-what-was-typed.md) — **told that it is temporary and about the connection rather than about them, and the form keeps everything it was holding.** `429` becomes a **third** kind of outcome on the sign-up form — beside success and a refusal about something they typed — and **never** the generic *something went wrong*: a `500`, a `503`, a timeout and a rejected `fetch` keep the `unavailable` treatment `web-client/src/profile/api.ts` already gives an unknown status, and no other status joins the throttled state. That mapping is the substance, because the wrong answer here is the **absence of a branch**: the existing client maps everything but `200`/`401` to *unavailable*, and under that default a deliberate refusal reads as a broken product and produces the immediate retry that — per `ADR-0055` §1's *"an over-budget request still counts"* — **extends the very window it fails to explain**. The copy may state three facts (it cannot be completed *right now, from this connection*; **nothing they typed was refused**; nothing is lost and nothing is required — they keep the profile, the coins and the duel, and may sign up later, per `ADR-0036`) and may state five things never: **no time** — no deadline, no countdown, no *15 minutes*, no *5 attempts*, because `ADR-0055` §3 ships no `Retry-After`, the window is rolling, and the numbers are environment variables that go stale silently; **no verdict on a field**; **no claim that the handle is still free**, since the budget check sits before `Credentials.create` and the taken-handle test was never reached; **no accusation**; and **no mechanism and no fault** — no *rate limit*, *throttle*, *blocked*, *security*, no error code. The form **keeps both fields, the password included, exactly as typed** — the refusal was not about the secret, and `ADR-0048` §3 permits a password no human can retype — marks neither field, leaves submit enabled, offers no *Retry now*, and **never retries by itself**, because an unattended retry is the one action that lengthens the wait. **One message for two people, written for the bystander**: nothing distinguishes the second player behind a NAT from the one who spent the budget and nothing will — the key is the address and the body is empty — so the tie is broken toward the person who did nothing, and the product will never tell an abuser to stop. A `429` spends nothing: no handle, no session, and **not `ADR-0036`'s offer**, which only *"Not now"* dismisses. **Derived from the vision**, not the human's call: *"Lichess and Chess.com, not PokerStars. Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"* — a refusal a player cannot act on is told once, quietly, with no countdown and no alarm — read beside `ADR-0036`'s *"declining does not degrade anything"*. `STORY-0404` gains **nothing** and its three out-of-scope notes stand verbatim a second time; `STORY-0405` gains **nothing on the wire and one prohibition** — the `429` body stays empty by product decision as well as transport decision, so nobody adds a `message` or a `retryAfterSeconds` to help the screen; `STORY-0412` gains the state, the mapping, the preserved fields, the copy brief and five criteria, including that the four statuses are asserted **together** and that the rendered message **contains no digit**; `STORY-0415` gains one prohibition. The costs recorded rather than discovered: `ADR-0055`'s disclosure gets **louder**, since a status a person had to open devtools to see is now printed in a sentence on a shared address; the player is told to wait and never how long, and *later* can be an hour, with the operator's two config values the only lever; a typed password sits in a form on exactly the shared machine that produced the refusal, for the length of the wait; the only deterrent a message could carry is given up; a state that may never render in v0.1 is designed, tested and maintained from the day it ships; and the client cannot tell this `429` from one `EPIC-07`'s front end invents for a reason of its own. Forecloses a countdown on this screen unless `Retry-After` ever ships, folding `429` into the generic state, and any per-field treatment of it later |
| DEC-050 | **The architect's** — by what mechanism is a duel-history cursor bound to the filter that produced it, so that replaying it under another filter is refused rather than silently reinterpreted? The answer had to settle a third payload component (fingerprint or literal text), the canonical form of a `DuelFilter`, whether the filter reaches `duelCursorOrNull` or the route, whether a mismatch is distinguishable from a malformed cursor, and what a later third axis does to cursors in flight | [`ADR-0057`](ADR-0057-a-cursor-names-the-filter-it-was-drawn-under.md) — **the payload gains a third component and it is an 11-character fingerprint of the filter, checked by the re-encode line that already decides validity.** The framing that settles the rest: a cursor names a position in an ordering of a set, the set is `(player, filter)`, and **the filter is the only part of it the client supplies** — the player comes from the resolved credential, so replay cannot vary it, and the ordering is a constant in the SQL. So `limit`, the player and the ordering stay out of the fingerprint, and only the filter goes in. **The canonical form of a `DuelFilter` is the parsed value**, never the raw query string: `duelOutcomeOrNull` yields an enum entry or refuses, `opponentSearchOrNull` yields NFC, so two spellings cannot mint two cursors and no separate canonicalisation step exists. Its text is one length-delimited line per axis that *narrows* — `"<axis>:<utf8ByteLength>:<value>\n"`, `outcome` then `opponent` — and an absent axis contributes **nothing**, which is what makes a third axis leave every existing cursor byte-identical and widening additive in fact rather than in intention. The fingerprint is the first 8 bytes of SHA-256 as unpadded base64url (`DuelFilter.NONE` → `47DEQpj8HBQ`); base64url rather than hex **because 64 + 12 = 76 keeps `theEncodedFormCarriesNoPadding` load-bearing while 64 + 17 = 81 is a multiple of three and makes it vacuous**. Both halves of the encoding take the filter as a **parameter** — `encoded(filter)` and `duelCursorOrNull(raw, filter)`, non-nullable because `DuelFilter.NONE` is a filter — so **no new refusal path is added**: the check is the same `it.encoded(filter) == raw` line, the third part is never parsed, and validity keeps living in one function rather than being a second opinion a route can forget. `DuelCursor` stays `(finishedAt, duelId)` and never learns what an axis is; `respondWithDuels`'s order becomes identity, limit, **filter, cursor**. A mismatch is a **flat `400`, indistinguishable from a malformed cursor** — the remedy is identical, a retryable status would be a lie, and this endpoint's whole refusal vocabulary is one status and no body. The fingerprint is unkeyed and is a **consistency check, never an authorisation check**: `TASK-040801`'s *opaque, not unforgeable* stands, because a forged cursor names only the forger's own rows — the day a cursor names a set the requester does not own, that is a keyed MAC and a new ADR. **Nothing in `STORY-0409`'s eleven tickets changes**: `TASK-040909`'s "write no test about that combination, in either direction" is confirmed, `TASK-040906`/`TASK-040910` stand verbatim, and `TASK-040911` writes a gap sentence a re-plan ticket deletes on purpose. The costs recorded rather than discovered: every cursor in flight on the day it deploys is refused, and **no version negotiates the encoding** (`RecentDuelsResponse` is reachable from neither message root), so this is payable early or awkwardly, never gracefully — hence the deadline is *before `STORY-0413` ships*, not before `STORY-0409` closes; a mismatch and a mangled string are indistinguishable to the developer debugging them, with no body and no log; `DuelCursorTest`'s padding fixture must be re-cut or an assertion silently stops catching anything; `DuelFilter`'s rendering becomes a compatibility surface enforced by nothing but §8 and **one golden vector test**; two SHA-256s per page; and somebody will eventually read the digest as a signature. Forecloses resuming a walk under a *widened* filter, and any shareable page-position link |
| DEC-051 | **The product owner's** — what does the client print where a display name would be, for a player who has none: on their own profile strip, and as an opponent on a duel line? | [`ADR-0058`](ADR-0058-where-a-name-would-be-the-client-prints-no-name.md) — **`No name`**, the same two words on every surface, about every player, whatever the reason there is no name. Sentence case, one space, no full stop and no mark; printed by one function (`nameOrNone`) that is the only place in the client branching on a `null` display name, because [`ADR-0052`](ADR-0052-a-takedown-is-told-to-the-player-it-happened-to.md) §5's invisibility survives only if two surfaces cannot decide separately. Explicitly **one treatment everywhere and no second-person variant**: the player's own strip prints `No name`, not *You*, so that `STORY-0413`'s history rows and `EPIC-05`'s leaderboard rows — both third-person — inherit an answer that works there. Derived from the vision: the v0.1 roadmap row *"No accounts"* makes a nameless player the ordinary shipped state rather than an unfinished one, and *"Lichess … dark, quiet, fast, minimal"* fixes the register as a plain statement about the record rather than a mascot name, a prompt or a dash. Rejected: *Anonymous* (asserts a choice that is false for the player whose name was taken away, and is the exact placeholder `ADR-0029` §6 forbids the server to mint), *You*/*Your rival* (warmer on the two surfaces asked about, nonsense on the two that inherit it), a dash (reads as missing data, not as an ordinary player), *Unnamed* (describes the person, not the record) and a prompt (*yet* is a promise, and false for a removed name). Costs recorded rather than discovered: a history list of nameless rivals cannot be scanned; the string is registrable as a display name, since `ADR-0029` §3 refuses invisible characters and not ordinary words and `ADR-0051` §5 ships the blocklist empty; and *No name* echoes the strip's neighbouring *"No profile yet."* |
| DEC-052 | **The product owner's** — when does the history search send its request: **as the player types**, or **when they submit it**? | [`ADR-0059`](ADR-0059-the-record-is-searched-when-the-player-submits.md) — **on submit; typing sends nothing.** The box sits in a `<form>` and exactly two acts search: Enter in the box, and a submit button reading `SEARCH = "Search"` — the one word `TASK-041305` reserved for the ticket that needs it. Emptying the box is a search like any other (`filter.opponent = ""`, which `duelsPath` renders as no `opponent` parameter), and **no separate *Clear* control** is added for a state submitting already reaches. Derived from the vision's *"Dark, quiet, fast, minimal"*, read against what a search actually costs on this screen: `ADR-0057` binds the cursor to its filter and `TASK-041307` drops the rows with it, so **a debounced pause is not a wasted request — it is the player's place in the record.** Three pages into a walk, one mid-word pause restarts the walk and searches a term (*Hal*) the player had not finished typing (*Halvard*). It would also fire one unindexed `POSITION` scan per pause against a read `STORY-0409` recorded as *"correct without one; not yet fast"*, and put the first timer **inside** the component tree — the client's only `setTimeout` today is the reconnect backoff, outside React by `ADR-0032`. Chosen as the cheaper reversal on thin evidence: a debounce added later is **additive**, a debounce removed later is a behaviour players already learned, and nothing here is measured — no players, and no p95 for the filtered read at any size. `TASK-041312` gains exactly four things — one Scope bullet, the word, the test `asks nothing while the player types, and once when the search is submitted`, one `verify:` line — and **stays at three files**, because finding the button by its accessible name pins the string without a golden test of its own. Costs recorded rather than discovered: the box and the rows can disagree with nothing on screen saying so (no *showing results for…* line, deliberately); widening back takes a deliberate submit rather than happening by itself; and it feels older than every other search box the player used today |
| DEC-053 | **The product owner's** — how does a player reach their whole duel record, and how do they leave it? | [`ADR-0060`](ADR-0060-the-record-is-its-own-screen-and-the-lobby-is-the-door.md) — **its own screen, which replaces the first screen: in by one control reading *Your duels*, out by one reading *Back*.** The record is never rendered beside the lobby — not below the strip, not in a panel. The door sits beneath the profile strip and **outside** it, is a `<button>` rather than an `<a>` with no address to carry, and takes its word from `HISTORY_HEADING` so the destination has exactly one spelling in the product. **It does not depend on the read**: the strip renders `null` when its background read fails, and the way to a screen may not vanish because of a failure the player cannot act on (`ADR-0036` — it gates on nothing else either). *Back* is rendered by whatever renders the swap, **not by `HistoryScreen`**, so the screen knows nothing about navigation and the affordance is assertable with no transport. The door is offered only on the branch that holds *Create a duel room* — never at the table, never on the result screen — and **a duel that starts outranks the record**, because a player who opens it mid-hand does not fold — a `PLAYING` room is never reaped for idleness, so they leave their rival at a table nothing will end. Derived from three vision sentences: v0.2's *"match history"* makes the record a commitment already made, so only placement was open; *"slot machines in the lobby"* is the vision naming accretion on the first screen as the shape it refuses; and the first success condition — *"Send a link. She opens it in a browser… We hit Rematch."* — is what that screen is for. The deciding force was inheritance: one extra section on the lobby is cheap, four are the whole product stacked on the screen a new player sees first — reversible one at a time, irreversible in aggregate. `TASK-041313` gains the door, the way back and the test `leaves the first screen for the record, and comes back to it`, asserted through controls that exist whatever the read answers; its *"exactly one of `App.tsx` and `Lobby.tsx`"* criterion is satisfied by `Lobby`, which is where the knowledge that a duel is in progress already lives. Costs recorded rather than discovered: the first screen becomes the **only** door, so every later screen is reachable from there or not at all, and the create button ends up sharing its screen with a growing column of links; **the record has no address** — nothing links to it, a reload lands on the first screen, and the browser's *Back* leaves the client instead of returning; `STORY-0415`'s offer fires on the result screen and opens `STORY-0412`'s, so whatever holds *which screen is showing* must be reachable from there too; and it forecloses the glance, since nothing on the first screen will ever show more than the strip's handful. **Raises `DEC-054`**, the architect's, deliberately unanswered here |
| DEC-055 | **The product owner's** — what is a season, and what does one do to a duel coin? Four parts: what bounds one; whether a boundary resets `player.coin_balance` or makes a season a window over `duel_result.coin_delta`; whether a finished season stays readable; and whether the ladder shows one season or also all time | [`ADR-0061`](ADR-0061-a-season-is-a-calendar-month-and-the-coin-never-resets.md) — **a season is one calendar month in UTC, derived and never stored; the ladder is a window over it; a boundary does nothing, and `player.coin_balance` is never reset.** Derived from the vision, which licenses it in two sentences that pull opposite ways: *"a counter of duels won"* that is *"not a balance"* is why nothing is destroyed at a boundary, and *"Ranked results over a season"* is why the ladder is scoped rather than lifetime — both are literally true at once under exactly one shape, an all-time counter ranked over a window, and the roadmap's *"Leaderboard and **seasons**"*, plural, is why v0.3 ships a season that ends. Bounds are **half-open** `[first instant of the month, first instant of the next)`, identifier `2026-08`, and a duel belongs to the season containing its **`finished_at`** — so a duel finishing exactly on a boundary is in the **new** season. **No table, no column, no migration, no operator, no job**: *which season is it* is a function of `ServerClock`, *which season was that duel in* a function of `finished_at`. A standing is the `SUM(coin_delta)` inside the window, and the ladder is **results, not players** — a row exists exactly for those who finished a duel that season, including at `0` for a draw, which is `ADR-0015` doing work it was not doing before. The ladder shows the **current season only** (no all-time ladder, no selector), prints the season standing rather than the strip's number, **names the season it shows** — `August 2026` — and takes that name **from the response**, never from the browser's clock (`ADR-0002`). A finished season is never gone, because it recomputes exactly from rows nothing rewrites; v0.3 simply offers no way to ask for one. `ADR-0014` is **neither superseded nor amended** — the word *supersede* there was reserved for a balance that *floats*, and a season standing is the same arithmetic over a shorter window. The reset branch was **declined for want of authority, not on the merits**: it would make the vision's *What it is* false and belongs to the human, and the ADR records that the objection is meaning rather than recoverability. Costs recorded rather than discovered: the ladder **empties on the first of every month**; **nothing records who won a season**; the strip's number and the ladder's number disagree from the second season on, so `EPIC-05`'s *"a ladder that disagrees with the strip is the defect this epic exists to avoid"* is contradicted on purpose and both stories carrying it are rewritten in the same change; the read becomes an aggregate over a join with no index; a UTC boundary meets locale-rendered times, so a duel read as September can count in August; and past standings are **recomputed, not frozen**, so `ADR-0039`'s eventual deletion will silently edit finished seasons. **Drops `STORY-0505`** — the boundary it was written to run at does nothing — and **raises `DEC-060`** and **`DEC-061`** |
| DEC-056 | **The product owner's** — what, if anything, gates a place on the leaderboard, given that device ids are free and an account may **not** be required? | [`ADR-0063`](ADR-0063-nothing-gates-a-place-and-the-farm-is-accepted-until-the-ladder-is-public.md) — **nothing gates a place, and the farming vector is accepted out loud until the ladder is public.** Derived from the vision; the human did not state this call. The ladder lists exactly the set [`ADR-0061`](ADR-0061-a-season-is-a-calendar-month-and-the-coin-never-resets.md) §4 defines — a row for whoever finished at least one duel in the season — **narrowed by nothing**: no minimum duels, no minimum standing, no account, no name, no profile age, no opponent-diversity rule, so `STORY-0502` adds no `WHERE` beyond the season window and `STORY-0503` filters no row it was sent. **A nameless player has a row and it prints `nameOrNone`'s `No name`** — the question [`ADR-0058`](ADR-0058-where-a-name-would-be-the-client-prints-no-name.md) parked here by name, answered in the listed direction, with the wire still carrying `null`. **Two profiles that only ever duel each other are an ordinary pair**, because that pair is the vision's founding case — *"the author wanted to play quick heads-up duels against his sister"* — and no server fact separates it from a farm. Two properties are pinned so a later gate cannot break them quietly: a rank is a position among **everyone** who played that season, and a season's standings **sum to exactly zero**. The decisive finding is that a farmed duel is not merely certain but **fast** — two colluding profiles shove all-in and finish in under a minute against `duel-rules.md`'s honest *"20–45 hands, roughly 5–15 minutes"* — so any rule counting duels is cleared by the farmer first and taxes the honest player instead. `ADR-0012`'s gate is **discharged rather than amended**: it is applied at the event that ADR names, *"when the leaderboard goes public"*, which `EPIC-05`'s out-of-scope table had already relocated to `EPIC-07`. The acceptance expires at an **event, not a date** — the first time the ladder is served on a public address — or earlier if a season ends with a standing shaped like a farm, which is a signal to look and never a rule. Costs recorded rather than discovered: the farm stays possible **on purpose** and the first ladder shown can be topped by somebody who never beat anybody; `No name` can be the top row and a ladder of them cannot be read; a nameless player cannot find themselves, which makes `DEC-059` load-bearing rather than a nicety; a place gate is now more expensive to add than it was to refuse, because a later one takes places away; and the top of the ladder is noise for the first days of every season. **Names one ticket** for the planner — `EPIC-07`'s definition of done gains a line requiring this acceptance to be re-affirmed in writing or replaced before the ladder goes public — and **raises no `DEC`**: the countermeasure's shape is a decision for the day the acceptance expires |
| DEC-057 | **The product owner's** — does a leaderboard row lead anywhere: is another player's profile visible to a stranger, and what is on it? | [`ADR-0067`](ADR-0067-a-leaderboard-row-is-text-and-no-id-turns-into-a-profile.md) — **a leaderboard row is text, it leads nowhere, and no id turns into a profile.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-22, derived from the vision: the **roadmap** settles the *whether* — v0.3 is *"Leaderboard and seasons"* and v0.4 is *"Friends, statistics, replay viewer"*, so a page about another player carrying duels played, a win/loss record or a duel list is **statistics**, one milestone later; deciding that a v0.4 thing waits is applying the roadmap, and deciding it moves would be reordering it, which is the human's. *"**A leaderboard.** Ranked results over a season"* settles the *what*: the vision names **ranked results**, not ranked people — `ADR-0061` §4's *"results, not players"* — and a result is discharged by a row. **A row is a rank, a name or `No name`, and a season standing, rendered as one line** (`4 Ada 3`): not a link, not a button, not a control, which **confirms what `STORY-0503` already ships** rather than changing it — `TASK-050313`'s *no `<a>`, no `link` role, no `button`* assertion stops being provisional. **What a stranger reads is exactly four fields** — `rank`, `displayName`, `coins`, `playerId` — and every one already ships; what is new is that this is the **boundary**. **What a stranger does not read, field by field:** the all-time `coin_balance`, duels played, wins, losses, draws, a win rate, the ladder total, the duel list (`GET /api/me/duels` gains no player parameter and no second route), streaks, movement, last seen, online state, anything about a credential, handle, email, device or session, and `displayNameRemoved`, which already *"never says anything about another player"* (`ADR-0053`). **`playerId` stays on the wire and is a name for a row, never an address**: no route takes a player id as its subject — the server serves five paths and not one answers *what about player X* — while a client may still use it as a list key and to correlate what it was already told (`ADR-0021`). That is **`ADR-0029` §7 from the other side**: §7 keeps a name from becoming an id, this keeps an id from becoming a person, and together **no path in this product turns anything into a lookup of a player**. **`STORY-0504` is `dropped`** — its premise is false and nothing moves out of it. Reopening is deliberately made a decision rather than a ticket: a new `DEC` naming **one field at a time**, an answer to what the subject is asked and can turn off on a product with no opt-in (`ADR-0063` §1), no opt-out and no deletion (`ADR-0039`) whose default player never signed up for anything (`ADR-0012`, `ADR-0036`) — **and the human's call before any fact a player did not choose to publish is published**, which is where a product decision starts having consequences outside the software. No escalation was needed for *this* answer because it publishes nothing new. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **the ladder is a list of strangers a player cannot ask about** — the gesture every ladder trains you to make does nothing, and the vision's own *rival* has nowhere to point, a real loss against the Lichess comparison; **`DEC-054` loses its sharpest argument**, since `EPIC-05`'s case for it was *"a row that leads to another player is a link"*, so an address-less client just got more comfortable; the question is **deferred and gets harder**, arriving in v0.4 as a ticket saying *just add a player page*, guarded only by prose; the asymmetry is deliberate and uncomfortable — a player is published on a ladder they never opted into and can look up nobody, while the server holds all of it; and **v0.3 ships no social surface at all**. **Forecloses nothing structurally** — that is the point — but forecloses inside `EPIC-05` even the zero-disclosure head-to-head view, which is v0.4's row. Leaves `DEC-054`, `DEC-060` and `DEC-008` untouched, **names no ticket and raises no `DEC`** |
| DEC-058 | **The product owner's** — when two players hold the same balance, what is the player told: do they share one rank number, or does something break the tie into distinct positions, and if so what? | [`ADR-0064`](ADR-0064-tied-players-share-one-rank-and-row-order-is-not-a-ranking.md) — **tied players share one rank number, and the order rows sit in is not a ranking.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-21, derived from the vision: *"**A leaderboard.** Ranked results over a season"* ranks a **result**, and two equal standings are one result. The rank is **`1 + the number of players standing strictly higher`** — competition ranking, so the ladder prints `3, 3, 5` and never `3, 4, 5` and never `3, 3, 4` — and its meaning is a sentence a player can state: *there are exactly `rank − 1` players ahead of me this season*. **A rank and a position in a page are two numbers and only the rank is shown**: a page may begin with the rank the previous page ended on, and a repeated rank across two pages is not a duplicate row, because `STORY-0502`'s totality and disjointness are about **players**. Nothing on the screen breaks a tie, and a tie is marked by the repeated number and by nothing else in v0.3 — no `=`, no count, no styling. The emission order among equals is **the architect's** (`DEC-061`) and constrained in one product-facing way: the key is a fact about **who a row is** — id, name collation, profile age — never about **how that player did**, because a tiebreak that measures play is a second ranking rule and [`ADR-0014`](ADR-0014-duel-coin-economy.md) reserves one for an ADR that *supersedes* it. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **the rank stops being free** — it is a function of the whole ladder rather than of the page, so `DEC-061` gets harder and a keyset cursor cannot carry a position forward as a rank; the ladder **cannot name a leader for the first days of every month**, twelve times a year, which makes `DEC-059` load-bearing rather than a nicety; a skip from `5` to `195` reads as missing rows and nothing on the screen explains it; a season can end with **no single winner**, which narrows `DEC-060`'s options; every future *top N* is ill-defined; and the size of a tie is invisible. **Sharpens `DEC-059` and `DEC-060` without answering either, constrains `DEC-061` without answering it, raises no `DEC`** — whether a tie is ever marked is a string in the ladder's text module and an ordinary ticket if it is ever wanted |
| DEC-059 | **The product owner's** — does a player see **their own** standing, and where: on the profile strip beside the coin balance, marked on the ladder, behind a *jump to me* control, or nowhere? | [`ADR-0065`](ADR-0065-the-ladder-hands-a-player-their-own-row.md) — **the ladder hands a player their own row, and the profile strip keeps the all-time coin.** Answered by the product owner on 2026-08-21, derived from the vision: *"**A leaderboard.** Ranked results over a season"* ranks a result **belonging to a player**, and a ladder whose own player cannot locate their result ranks it for everybody except the person who produced it; *"**One duel coin per win.** Not chips, not currency, not a balance. A counter of duels won"* is why the strip keeps printing that counter and gains nothing. The **ladder screen** renders one **self line** above the rows and below the season name, stating the requesting player's rank and season standing — rendered whether or not their row is in the page on screen, and unchanged as they walk pages. **The profile strip is untouched**: no rank, no season standing, no season name, and `ProfileResponse` gains no field, so `GET /api/me` never becomes a whole-ladder aggregate on a route that runs on every lobby load. Which number each surface shows is pinned: strip = all-time `player.coin_balance`, ladder rows **and** self line = the season `SUM(coin_delta)`, told apart by [`ADR-0061`](ADR-0061-a-season-is-a-calendar-month-and-the-coin-never-resets.md) §6's season name and by [`ADR-0060`](ADR-0060-the-record-is-its-own-screen-and-the-lobby-is-the-door.md) never putting the two on one screen. **`STORY-0502` therefore ships two aggregates in one response** — the page, and one player's rank, for a player who may be on no page it drew — with the mechanism left to `DEC-061`. Three states and the third is not a zero: a rank and a standing; **no place this season** for a profile that played no duel in it, printing no rank and never `0`, because `0` is a real standing a draw earns ([`ADR-0015`](ADR-0015-a-draw-writes-two-result-rows.md)); and no line at all for a request with no known device — and **the page is byte-identical in all three**, so the ladder stays readable without a profile and is never personalised. **Nothing else marks the player in v0.3**: no highlighted row, no *jump to me*, no ladder total, no movement, no tie count, no link. Costs recorded rather than discovered: the ladder read **stops being one query** and `DEC-061` gets harder for the second time in a day; the lobby still does not say where you stand, so it is a click every time on a screen `ADR-0060` said would crowd; the self line may disagree with the same player's row in the same response, which §3 permits on purpose; **the ladder response is now per-requester** and can no longer be cached as one public document, a bill `EPIC-07` pays; the two-player founding ladder gets a line restating a row beneath it; and telling a player they are rank `5` makes *which of these 190 rows is mine* a sharper question that §5 deliberately does not answer. **Constrains `DEC-061` further without answering it**, leaves `DEC-057` and `DEC-060` untouched, **names no ticket and raises no `DEC`** |
| DEC-061 | **The architect's** — is a season standing computed per request or materialised, and what does a page guarantee over an ordering that is **recomputed** while it is walked? | [`ADR-0066`](ADR-0066-the-ladder-is-computed-per-request-and-a-walk-is-pinned.md) — **the ladder is computed per request, and a walk is pinned to the instant it began.** Answered by the architect on 2026-08-21. **Per request, from the ledger, and nothing stores a standing**: no `season_standing` table, no materialised view, no summary column, no cache, no refresh job, no third ticker sweep and no change to the duel-recording transaction — `ADR-0061` §3's *"nothing writes a season down, so nothing can disagree about one"* applied one level down, which is also what keeps a duel on the ladder **the instant it commits**. The read is one `WITH standing AS (SUM(coin_delta) … )` over the season window with SQL's `rank()`, which **is** `ADR-0064` §1's competition rank. **The page guarantee is bought rather than inherited**: a request with no cursor mints `asOf = Instant.now(clock)`, the cursor carries it back, and the query's upper bound is **the cutoff, never the season's end** — so a walk enumerates *the ladder as it stood committed at the cutoff*, returning **every player of that ladder exactly once**, with the rank each held then, so ranks never decrease down a walk and the self standing (`ADR-0065` §3) is byte-identical on every page. **Two refusals are stated rather than glossed:** a walk is **not live** — a duel finishing after the cutoff is in no page of it, and page forty is as old as page one; and exactly-once has **one named exception** — a duel *committed* after a page was drawn but stamped `finished_at` *before* the cutoff moves the pinned ladder, so its **winner can be never returned** and its **loser returned twice**. Both are accepted; what the cutoff buys is that their window is the width of one duel-recording transaction rather than of the walk. The order is `coins DESC, player_id DESC` — `player.id` is **forced**, not chosen, as the only key that is identity (`ADR-0064` §4), unique and **immutable** (a `display_name` takedown would move a row mid-walk) — paged by one row-value comparison, the `DUELS_AFTER_SQL` idiom, with the `limit + 1` probe row. The page and the self standing are **two statements bounded by the same cutoff**, which agree without a transaction. A cursor whose `asOf` is outside the season the server's clock is in is a flat `400` (`ADR-0057` §5), so a walk crossing a month boundary restarts rather than serving August in September; and `ADR-0057` §7's *"a leaderboard page"* MAC clause is shown **not to engage**, because `ADR-0065` §4 makes the page identical for every reader so a forged position leaks nothing. Costs recorded rather than discovered: **every page is a full-month aggregate and there are two per page**, so a twenty-page walk is forty unindexed passes on a public unauthenticated read with no rate limit — the sharpest cost, and unmeasured; later pages are **stale** with nothing on screen saying so; **exactly-once carries its exception permanently in the contract**, so a reader can see a row twice or miss one; a walk is refused twelve times a year at the month boundary; the cursor now carries a value the server trusts from the client; and a **live walk is foreclosed**. **Unblocks `STORY-0502`, the last gate on it**, and leaves `DEC-057` and `DEC-060` untouched. **Names one ticket** and raises no `DEC`: an index for the season window — `duel (finished_at)` in a new `V7__` migration with an `EXPLAIN`-backed measurement, deliberately outside `STORY-0502` |
| DEC-062 | **The architect's** — which instrument does the server read **wall-clock** time from, when a piece of product behaviour is a function of the calendar? | [`ADR-0062`](ADR-0062-two-clocks-and-a-date-comes-from-java-time-clock.md) — **the server has two clocks and neither answers the other's question.** `ServerClock` (`System.nanoTime()`) measures **durations** — timeouts, grace windows, `ADR-0025`'s sweeps — and `java.time.Clock` reports **dates**: injected as a parameter, `Clock.systemUTC()` in production, `Clock.fixed` in a test. **No new port and no third clock type** — the wall clock is `java.time.Clock` itself, which `PostgresDuelResultSink` already takes and whose KDoc already gave the reason. `Instant.ofEpochMilli(clock.nowMillis())` is a defect wherever it appears; the no-argument `Instant.now()`, `System.currentTimeMillis()`, `LocalDate.now()`, `YearMonth.now()` and `ZoneId.systemDefault()` appear nowhere in `poker-server/src/main`; there is exactly **one** `Clock.systemUTC()`, at the composition root; a **pure** function takes the clock with **no default**, because a defaulted pure function can be called with no clock at all; and the zone is a literal `ZoneOffset.UTC`, never the clock's own. **Amends three merged ADRs in one clause each**, per this file's convention that a correction goes in the status line and *supersede* is for a decision reversed: `ADR-0061` §3 (*which season is it* is a function of the instant an injected `java.time.Clock` reports, not of `ServerClock.nowMillis()`), and — found by searching for the sentence rather than the id — `ADR-0027` §1's thirty-day session and `ADR-0031`'s twenty-four-hour and one-hour tokens, whose lifetimes land in an `expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ` compared against SQL `now()` and would each have been **born expired in 1970**. The durations are policy and do not move; both ADRs' in-memory rate-limit windows keep `ServerClock` and are right as written. `STORY-0501`, `STORY-0405` and `STORY-0502` carried the same instruction and are corrected too. `ServerClock` **keeps its name**: the rename to `ElapsedClock.elapsedMillis()` is argued in full and declined for now — eleven Kotlin files that must land atomically past a three-file ticket cap, and seven merged ADRs and fourteen merged tickets left naming a type that would no longer exist — with the trigger written down and made checkable: all four occurrences predate this ADR and came from documents that now say the opposite, so **one more, in any document written after it**, makes the name the cause and the rename a story on this argument. The cost is named rather than discovered: **the misleading name survives, so the mistake stays expressible**, and the deterrent is a KDoc, an architecture section and a static-read guard test, none of which can see a misused injected clock. Unblocks `TASK-050106`; names three tickets — the KDoc that still points at `System.currentTimeMillis`, the composition root's single `Clock.systemUTC()`, and the guard test |
